# THROTTLE COUNTY - MASTER BUILD PROMPT (500 paragraphs)

This message contains the COMPLETE game design and build specification for a Roblox game, written as 500 numbered paragraphs in 21 sections. Because you are receiving all 10 parts at once, skip the part-by-part acknowledgements described in Section 1. Read everything first. Then confirm you have paragraphs [1] to [500] and begin Phase 1 exactly as described in the build roadmap in Section 21.

---

## SECTION 1 - MISSION, YOUR ROLE & HOW WE WORK (Paragraphs 1-20)

[1] You are the entire senior team on this project at once: a senior Roblox engineer who writes production Luau with `--!strict` and typed module exports, a technical artist who owns materials, lighting and asset specs, a vehicle-dynamics programmer who builds the raycast suspension, tire and balance models, a level designer who lays out a 16,000 x 16,000 stud open world, and a game designer who tunes progression and economy. The bar is a front-page Roblox experience that holds up next to a studio release: true-to-scale machines, physics-driven riding, dense handcrafted regions and a clean Output window. The user is a solo creator, possibly new to Studio, who pastes your files exactly as delivered, so every file must run as written and every instruction must be followable by a beginner, with no hidden steps and no missing pieces.

[2] The game is THROTTLE COUNTY, a working title the user may rename, so the title lives in exactly one `GAME_TITLE` constant. It is an open-world, multiplayer, realism-first riding game built on bikelife culture, motocross, enduro, street riding, jobs, economy and customization, running 30-player servers on PC, Mac, phones, tablets and Xbox/PlayStation gamepads. The roster is 53 machines plus a trailer and a hitch carrier: pedal bikes, e-bikes, electric dirt bikes and motorcycles, gas dirt, pit, enduro, trials and supermoto bikes, street bikes, quads, a UTV, a snowmobile, a jet ski and haulers (see [126]-[205]). Ten regions fill a 4.5 x 4.5 km map at 1 stud = 0.28 m with `Workspace.Gravity` = 35 studs/s^2. Content targets the Mild rating: crashes ragdoll the rider with no blood, gore, injury or death.

[3] First minutes are the session-start acceptance target ([344] pacing, [449] steps). New player: spawns at the Old Town spawn plaza (0, 15, 0) beside Throttle Works with $2,500 and the Kurb Street BMX; a skippable, device-aware tutorial ([106]) teaches F mount, W pedal, A/D steer and lean, S/Space brakes and Mouse-Y or Up/Down weight shift into a manual, and the phone GPS leads to a `JobPickup` restaurant. By minute 10: 2-3 deliveries, Rep L2, one bail (B), one reset (hold R 1.5 s), and enough Cash for a Glide E-Board ($1,800) or Corsa Zip 50 ($1,900). Returning player: within 60 s of the [479] loading screen they stand at their chosen home spawn ([462]) with every vehicle's damage, fuel or charge and odometer intact, one What's New card ([484]) and one daily Rewards toast ([364]); nothing else blocks riding.

[4] Journey targets, checked by `/econsim` ([346]) and playtests. First hour: $4,000-6,000 per hour from Food Delivery and Parcel Courier, about Rep L5 ([341]), a Rascal 110 ($3,500) or Corsa Riva 150 ($4,800), a first Pumpline refuel, pump-track and foam-pit sessions and a first casual race, with a new goal (level, purchase, region or challenge) every 10-15 minutes and no grind wall. First week, 10-20 hours: Rep L18-25 at $15,000-25,000 per hour; a Volt Wasp X ($14,000) or Kensei K2-250 ($15,000); the Maple Heights House ($120,000); Bike Transport with a Hauler HD and 3-Rail Bike Trailer; a crew, Skyline Airfield 1/4-mile passes, Pinecrest singletrack, a Red Mesa lakebed top-speed run and one PI class gained by upgrades. Long term: L50 near 76 hours, L100 near 300, with mastery, records and seasons ([343], [363]) keeping late sessions goal-driven.

[5] Change requests. When the user asks for something new mid-build ("add helicopters", "use the real brand's logo"), classify it before writing code and answer in one line. (a) IP: real brands, models, logos or likenesses are refused and offered as a fictional "inspired by" equivalent ([19]). (b) Policy: anything raising the Mild label, adding paid randomness or linking off-platform is refused with the reason ([15], [469]). (c) Content inside an existing system (vehicle, part, job, race, region feature) is accepted as a data-first [498] recipe: built now if it touches only current-phase files, otherwise scheduled into its owning phase. (d) A new system is logged as `Backlog B<n>: <request> - target phase <k>` in the [11] package and built when that phase arrives. Never abandon the current phase's tests for a request, and never drop one silently.

[7] Paste protocol. The user pastes this prompt in 10 parts: Part 1 = [1]-[45], Part 2 = [46]-[100], Part 3 = [101]-[150], Part 4 = [151]-[205], Part 5 = [206]-[250], Part 6 = [251]-[295], Part 7 = [296]-[340], Part 8 = [341]-[390], Part 9 = [391]-[450], Part 10 = [451]-[500]. After each of Parts 1-9 you reply with exactly one line, "Part N received - send part N+1." (for example "Part 3 received - send part 4."), with no summary, no questions and no code. The single exception: if the numbering inside a part has a gap, reply "Part N is missing [a]-[b] - please re-paste part N." After Part 10 you confirm "All 10 parts received: paragraphs [1]-[500] complete." and begin Phase 1 in that same reply.

[8] Build protocol. Deliver one phase at a time, in this order: (1) Foundation; (2) Physics core + one test bike (Volt Wasp X); (3) Powertrains + audio core; (4) Full roster + VehicleBuilder placeholders + garage/dealer basics; (5) Rider animation, crashes, damage, wear; (6) World blockout + road graph + streaming; (7) World art pass + lighting, weather, VFX; (8) Economy, jobs, properties; (9) Customization, tuning, dyno, PI; (10) Game modes, races, stunts, leaderboards; (11) Traffic, pedestrians, police; (12) UI polish, settings, social, monetization, safety, anti-exploit; (13) Optimization, QA, launch. Exact file lists and exit criteria live in [476]-[500]. Each phase must leave a place that boots cleanly and passes its own and every earlier phase's tests. Never pre-build later-phase features; expose typed extension points instead. When a phase changes an earlier file, re-deliver that whole file marked "REPLACES".

[9] File format. Every file is preceded by four header lines: a counter ("File 3 of 9"), the exact Explorer path (`ReplicatedStorage/Shared/Units`), the class (Script, LocalScript or ModuleScript), and a one-line purpose naming the modules it requires. File names in this prompt map to classes: `.server.luau` = Script, `.client.luau` = LocalScript, plain `.luau` = ModuleScript, and in Studio the instance Name drops the suffix (`Main.server.luau` becomes a Script named `Main`). The complete file follows in a single fenced Luau code block whose first line is `--!strict`. Never write "..." as an omission marker (Luau varargs are fine), "rest of code here", "same as before", "add your logic", TODO stubs or empty function bodies: a file is either complete or not delivered.

[10] Long output. When a reply approaches your output limit, stop only at a file boundary, never inside a code block, and end with one line: "Paused after file 4 of 9 (`ReplicatedStorage/Shared/Net`). Type continue for file 5." When the user types "continue", start the next file's header immediately, with no recap and no repeated files. If one file cannot fit in a reply, it was designed too large: split it at design time into cohesive ModuleScripts with typed interfaces (for example, tire curves separate from the suspension solver) and keep each file under about 600 lines. If a reply is still cut off mid-file, the user types "continue" and you re-send that entire file from `--!strict`, never a fragment to splice.

[12] "No errors" means: on a fresh Baseplate place, after the user follows your setup steps exactly, both Play solo and the [11] local server test (2 clients, 4 from Phase 8) run 10 minutes through the phase test checklist with zero red errors and zero yellow warnings in the Output window (third-party plugin messages excluded), and the Script Analysis window shows zero errors and zero warnings under `--!strict`. The one permitted yellow line is the single placeholder-asset summary defined in [13]. Get there with nil guards on every `FindFirstChild` result, `WaitForChild` always given a timeout and a handled nil (an "Infinite yield possible" warning is a failure), and `pcall` with retry and exponential backoff around every web call. You cannot run Studio, so never write "tested", "verified" or "works"; write "Not run in Studio - written to pass T2.1-T2.12."

[13] Asset honesty. As a text model you cannot create 3D meshes, textures, sounds or animations, and you cannot upload anything. For every vehicle you deliver the `VehicleBuilder` placeholder, generated from config `Dimensions` with all canonical attachments (`ChassisRoot`, `CoM`, wheel, `SteerPivot`, seat, grip, peg, exhaust and light attachments per [206]-[230]) so it is drivable today, plus a written modeling spec an artist can follow (at most 20,000 triangles per MeshPart, textures at most 1024x1024). A real model placed at `ReplicatedStorage/Assets/VehicleModels/<VehicleId>` replaces the placeholder with zero code changes. Never invent or recall asset IDs: every ID sits in `Config/AssetIds` or `Config/SoundIds` as 0 with a comment describing the asset, code skips 0 or missing IDs without throwing, and `Logger` warns once per session with a count, such as `AssetIds: 41 placeholder IDs skipped`.

[14] Manual Studio steps. Tag every action only the user can perform "MANUAL STEP" in the setup list and say what breaks without it: publishing the place (File, Publish to Roblox), without which DataStore and ProfileStore calls fail; Game Settings, Security, Enable Studio Access to API Services; setting `Workspace.Gravity` = 35 and the Studio-only properties `Lighting.Technology` = Future, `Workspace.StreamingEnabled` with its streaming values, `Workspace.SignalBehavior` and `Terrain.Decoration` in the Properties panel; inserting the official ProfileStore module by loleris into `ServerScriptService/Server/Packages` (never retype third-party libraries from memory); importing meshes with the 3D Importer; uploading sounds and images through the Asset Manager; publishing animations from the Animation Editor; and creating game passes and developer products on the Creator Hub, then pasting each ID into its config field. Code must detect an unpublished or API-disabled place and fall back to session-only data instead of erroring.

[15] Priority order. When goals conflict, resolve them in this fixed order: 1 stability and no errors, 2 Roblox policy compliance (Community Standards, the Mild content target, text filtering, no paid random items), 3 performance on mobile, 4 realism, 5 feature count. A realism feature that pushes a mid-range phone past the frame budget in [476]-[500] gets a cheaper mobile path or a quality toggle. A feature you cannot make error-free in the current phase is cut and listed as deferred, never shipped half-working. No realism or feature gain justifies a policy risk. Record each decision in one line using the form "Tradeoff: <what changed> (priority 3 over 4)" inside the end-of-phase package.

[16] User commands. "redo paragraph N": re-read [N] (ask for a re-paste if it is out of your context), find every delivered file it governs, and re-deliver each one complete with a 3-line change summary. "fix" with no details: fix the most recently reported problem; if none was reported, ask exactly "What did you see? Paste the Output text or describe it." "explain": answer in plain, short sentences, with no new code unless asked. A pasted Output error such as `ReplicatedStorage.Shared.Net:57: attempt to index nil with 'FireServer'`: read the script path and line number, trace the root cause (why it is nil, not only where), deliver every corrected file in full, explain the cause in 2-3 sentences, and add a test step that proves the fix. Never hide an error with `pcall`, a silent nil skip or deleted code.

[17] Defaults over questions. Do not open a phase with questions. Ask at most one question per phase, and only when canon is silent and the answer would force a rewrite later; a game rename is not such a case, because it touches one constant. Otherwise pick a default consistent with canon, keep building, and record it in the end-of-phase list in the form "A<phase>.<n>: <decision> - <reason>", for example "A1.2: Logger prefixes every line with the calling module name - faster triage of pasted errors." The user changes any assumption by typing "change A1.2 to <new value>", and you re-deliver every affected file complete. An assumption never overrides a numbered paragraph, and it is re-listed in each later phase that depends on it.

[18] "Professional, no basic stuff" is testable. Proportions: wheelbase, seat height, wheel diameter and overall length match config `Dimensions` within 2 percent after `Units` conversion (a 1.26 m wheelbase is 4.50 studs). Materials: final MeshParts use PBR `SurfaceAppearance` (`ColorMap`, `NormalMap`, `RoughnessMap`, `MetalnessMap`); placeholders and world surfaces use fitting built-in materials or tuned `MaterialVariant`s, never default SmoothPlastic. Audio: never one pitch-shifted loop; combustion engines crossfade 3+ RPM-band layers by RPM and load, electric drives layer motor whine, drivetrain and tire noise ([251]-[270]). Riding: all motion comes from simulated forces ([46]-[75]); animations never move the vehicle, so wheelies, stoppies and crashes emerge from physics. UI: one design system, gamepad- and touch-navigable everywhere ([431]-[450]). Map: every 200 x 200 stud cell of playable land holds authored detail (props, foliage, terrain shaping, riding features); open spaces like the dry lakebed are framed by landmarks ([271]-[320]).

[19] IP rule. Every in-game brand and model is fictional and only "inspired by" a real machine for proportions, specs and character. Real brand names, model names and logos never appear anywhere in the game: not in display names, VehicleIds, decals, textures, asset or sound names, UI strings, rider gear, tire sidewalls, sponsor stickers, code identifiers or code comments. Use only the canon fictional names (Ridgeline, Volt, Talon, Zephyr, Ryujin, Kensei, Austral, Liberty, Veloce and the rest in [126]-[205]) and design liveries that do not copy any manufacturer's trade dress. Why: Roblox removes content after rights-holder DMCA complaints, and repeat strikes can take the experience down or moderate the account. Acceptance: a search of every delivered file for each real name cited in this prompt finds no reference to a real product (ordinary words like "session" or "zero" do not count).

[20] Memory discipline. Long conversations push early paragraphs out of your context, so at the start of every phase, before any code, write one line "Re-read: [a]-[b], [c]-[d]" listing the governing ranges, then re-read them: always [1]-[45] (protocol, architecture, code standards) plus the phase's own sections, for example Phase 2 = [46]-[75] physics, [101]-[125] controls and the Volt Wasp X stat block in [126]-[150]; Phase 6 = [271]-[320] map and the streaming values in [476]-[500]. If an exact value you need (a price, a spec, a module name, a coordinate) is no longer visible to you, do not reconstruct it from memory: stop and ask "Please re-paste Part N ([a]-[b])", using the part table in [7]. Canon values always beat your recollection.

## SECTION 2 - TECHNICAL FOUNDATION, ARCHITECTURE & CODE STANDARDS (Paragraphs 21-45)

[21] Before Phase 1 code runs, tell the user to set these by hand. Game Settings (publish first): Security > Enable Studio Access to API Services ON, or ProfileStore and every DataStore call fails in Studio; Avatar settings (Game Settings > Avatar or the newer Avatar Settings window) Avatar Type R15, which rider IK assumes; Places > edit this place > Max Players 30. Properties panel: `Workspace.Gravity` 35, `Workspace.StreamingEnabled` true (radii in [476]-[500]), `Workspace.SignalBehavior` Deferred, `Lighting.Technology` Future, `StarterPlayer.CharacterUseJumpPower` false, `StarterPlayer.CharacterJumpHeight` 1.8. Never assign Studio-only properties from scripts. At boot `Main.server.luau` reads Gravity, `Players.MaxPlayers` and both jump properties and logs one WARN per mismatch (`Workspace.Gravity is 196.2, expected 35`); Studio-only values are read inside `pcall`, and an unreadable one logs one INFO reminder to verify it. Acceptance test: a default place boots fully with exactly one warning per wrong setting.

[22] Support two workflows that produce an identical Explorer tree; in your Phase 1 reply offer both and use pure Studio unless the user picks Rojo. Pure Studio: create every script by hand, use Team Create with Collaborative Editing, and publish a place version after each phase. Optional pro workflow: Rokit-managed `rojo`, `wally`, `selene` and `stylua`; a Rojo `default.project.json` mapping `src/shared` to `ReplicatedStorage/Shared`, `src/server` to `ServerScriptService/Server` and `src/client` to `StarterPlayer/StarterPlayerScripts/Client`; `wally.toml` dependencies installed into `Packages` (synced to `ReplicatedStorage/Packages`) and `ServerPackages` (synced to `ServerScriptService/Server/Packages`); Git with one commit per phase, ignoring `Packages/`, `ServerPackages/` and `sourcemap.json`. Mapping in both workflows: `Main.server.luau` is a `Script` named `Main`, `Main.client.luau` a `LocalScript` named `Main`, every other `.luau` file a `ModuleScript` without the extension. ProfileStore is one ModuleScript copied from loleris's official source into `Server/Packages`.

[23] Build exactly this Phase 1 skeleton. A new root folder or a renamed canonical module is a defect; later sections add subfolders only inside these roots (such as `Shared/VehiclePhysics` in [46], `Shared/RoadGraph`, `Shared/UI`, `Config/NpcPaths`). Each Service lives in `Server/Services` and each Controller in `Client/Controllers`, named exactly as listed in [24] and [25]. Never author `ReplicatedStorage/Remotes` by hand: the server `Net` module creates it at boot. `ServerStorage/ServerAssets` never replicates; clients see a template only after the server clones it into Workspace. A MeshPart model at `Assets/VehicleModels/<VehicleId>` overrides the VehicleBuilder placeholder with zero code changes ([206]-[230]).
```
ReplicatedStorage
  Shared/  Units Types Signal Trove Net NetDefs Logger Util VehicleBuilder
  Shared/Config/  Vehicles/<VehicleId> Upgrades Economy Jobs Races Weather AssetIds SoundIds
  Packages/ (shared third-party)   Remotes/ (runtime, created by Net)
  Assets/  VehicleModels/<VehicleId>  RiderGear/  VFX/  UI/
ServerScriptService
  Server/  Main.server.luau  ConfigValidator  DevTools (Studio only)
  Server/Services/  DataService ... AnalyticsService (17 modules)
  Server/Packages/  ProfileStore
ServerStorage
  ServerAssets/  (server-only templates)
StarterPlayer
  StarterPlayerScripts/Client/  Main.client.luau  DevToolsClient (Studio only)
  StarterPlayerScripts/Client/Controllers/  InputController ... SettingsController (12 modules)
```

[24] `Main.server.luau` is the only server Script. Sequence: require `Logger`; run the [21] settings check; run `ConfigValidator` ([39]); call `Net.InitServer()` so remotes exist before Services bind; require Services in exactly this order: DataService, PlayerService, VehicleService, EconomyService, ShopService, GarageService, CustomizationService, JobService, RaceService, EventService, TrafficService, WeatherService, TimeService, CrewService, MonetizationService, AntiCheatService, AnalyticsService; freeze the registry ([27]); call each `:Init(registry)` in order, then each `:Start()` in order inside its own `task.spawn`. Every require, Init and Start runs through `xpcall(fn, debug.traceback)`; a failure logs `[Boot][ERROR] EconomyService:Init - <message + traceback>`, skips that module's remaining steps, and boot continues. Init runs in a coroutine; if it is not `dead` after one resume, log `EconomyService:Init yielded`. Finally set ReplicatedStorage attribute `ServerReady` and log `Boot 17/17 OK in N ms` (or `Boot 16/17, failed: EconomyService`). Acceptance test: an injected EconomyService:Init error leaves the other 16 Services booted.

[25] `Main.client.luau` is the only client LocalScript (sole exception: `ReplicatedFirst/Loading.client.luau`, [479]) and mirrors the server bootstrap. Sequence: wait for the `ServerReady` attribute on ReplicatedStorage (check `GetAttribute`, then `GetAttributeChangedSignal`; WARN every 10 s while waiting, never give up); call `Net.InitClient()`, which waits up to 30 s for `ReplicatedStorage.Remotes` and verifies every NetDefs entry exists; require Controllers in this order: InputController, VehicleController, CameraController, AudioController, RiderAnimationController, HUDController, UIController, PhoneController, MapController, WeatherFXController, TrafficController, SettingsController; Init all, then Start all, with the same `xpcall` isolation, yield detection and one-line failure logs. Finally fire `Player_Ready` so PlayerService knows this client can receive data. StarterPlayerScripts never re-run on respawn, so Controllers subscribe to `LocalPlayer.CharacterAdded` and rebind character references every life. Acceptance test: an error thrown in MapController:Start leaves riding, HUD and audio fully functional.

[26] Every Service and Controller follows one template: `--!strict`, cached services, requires of `Shared` and `Packages` modules only, a module table whose `Name` equals the file name, `:Init(registry)`, `:Start()`, then `return`. Init is wiring only: store dependencies from the registry, build internal tables, create the module's own `Signal`s and `Trove`s, and connect to earlier modules' Signals; it finishes in under 5 ms with no yielding, remote firing, web calls or Instance creation. Start connects `Players.PlayerAdded` (then loops over `Players:GetPlayers()` to catch anyone who joined earlier), binds Net handlers and spawns loops with `task.spawn`. Require-time code only defines locals and functions. Public methods are PascalCase with colon syntax (`VehicleService:SpawnFor(player, serial, bayCFrame)`, [49]), private helpers are local functions, and public events are Signal fields such as `EconomyService.CashChanged`. Per-player state always has matching cleanup in `Players.PlayerRemoving`.

[27] Dependency injection uses one registry table built by each bootstrap: `registry[module.Name] = module` after every successful require, then a metatable whose `__index` errors on unknown names (catching typos), then `table.freeze` before the first Init. Services and Controllers never `require` each other; they read dependencies in Init (`DataService = registry.DataService`) and cast them to small interface types from `Types` (such as `IEconomyService`). Direction rule: a module may call modules earlier in its boot order, never later ones; to inform later modules it fires a Signal they connect to in their Init (DataService fires `ProfileLoaded`, EconomyService listens). Shared layers, each requiring only lower layers: 0 `Units`, `Logger`, `Signal`, `Trove`; 1 `Types`, `Util`; 2 `NetDefs`, `Config`; 3 `Net`; 4 `VehicleBuilder`. Acceptance test: Find All for `require(` shows Services and Controllers required only by the two `Main` scripts.

[28] `ReplicatedStorage/Shared/Units.luau` is the only file allowed to contain conversion factors, and it returns a frozen table. Constants: `STUD_M = 0.28`, `M_TO_STUDS = 1/0.28` (3.5714), `GRAVITY = 35` studs/s^2 (9.80 m/s^2), `RMU_KG = 21.952`, `KG_TO_RMU = 1/21.952`, `KMH_TO_SPS = 1/(3.6*0.28)` (0.99206), `MPH_TO_SPS = 0.44704/0.28` (1.59657), `HP_TO_KW = 0.7457`, `N_TO_FORCE = M_TO_STUDS*KG_TO_RMU` (0.16269 RMU*stud/s^2 per newton), `NM_TO_TORQUE = M_TO_STUDS^2*KG_TO_RMU` (0.58104). Helpers: `mToStuds`, `studsToM`, `mmToStuds`, `kmhToStudsPerSec`, `studsPerSecToKmh`, `studsPerSecToMph`, `kgToRMU`, `rmuToKg`, `hpToKW`, `newtonsToRobloxForce`, `nmToRobloxTorque`, `newtonSecondsToImpulse`. Configs stay SI; conversion happens once when a vehicle spawns. Acceptance test: `kmhToStudsPerSec(75)` = 74.405, `kgToRMU(56)` = 2.551, `newtonsToRobloxForce(1000)` = 162.69 and `nmToRobloxTorque(100)` = 58.10 (all within 0.01), and Find All for `21.952` hits only `Units.luau`.

[29] `Shared/Types.luau` contains only `export type` declarations and returns an empty frozen table. Required exports: `VehicleConfig` with one nested type per field group (`IdentityConfig`, `EconomyConfig`, `DimensionsConfig`, `MassConfig`, `ChassisConfig`, `SuspensionConfig`, `TiresConfig`, `PowertrainConfig`, `AssistsConfig`, `AudioConfig`, `VisualConfig`, `SeatsConfig`); `PowertrainKind = "Pedal" | "Electric" | "Gas2T" | "Gas4T" | "Diesel"`; `PlayerProfile` (at minimum `DataVersion`, `Cash`, `RepXP`, `Level`, `OwnedVehicles: {[string]: OwnedVehicle}` keyed by Serial, `Settings: PlayerSettings`); `OwnedVehicle` (at minimum `VehicleId`, `Upgrades`, `Paint`, `Odometer_km`, `DamageState`, `TotalSpent`; full schema and migrations in [451]-[475]); `VehicleState` (the decoded packet from [36]); `NetDef`; `Registry`; and interface types such as `IDataService`. Every config module is annotated `local config: Types.VehicleConfig = {...}`, so a missing or mistyped field becomes a Script Analysis error before the game runs, and `ConfigValidator` ([39]) catches anything the type checker misses.

[30] `Shared/Signal.luau` is the single event primitive for same-machine communication (wrapping the Wally package `sleitnick/signal` behind this path is acceptable). API: `Signal.new()`, `:Connect(fn)` returning a connection with `:Disconnect()` and `Connected`, `:Once(fn)`, `:Wait()` (never inside Init), `:Fire(...)`, `:DisconnectAll()` and `:Destroy()`. Fire snapshots the handler list, so disconnecting during a fire is safe, and runs each handler in its own thread via `task.spawn` with a reused runner thread, so one erroring handler never blocks the others. Never use `BindableEvent` or `BindableFunction` between modules: they copy tables, strip metatables and allocate on every fire. Signals never cross the client-server boundary; that is the job of `Net`. Acceptance test: firing a Signal with three handlers where the second throws still runs the first and third, and the error appears once in Output with its traceback.

[31] `Shared/Trove.luau` owns cleanup: `Trove.new()`, `:Add(obj, method?)`, `:Connect(signal, fn)`, `:Clone(instance)`, `:Extend()` for child troves, `:Remove(obj)`, `:Clean()` and `:Destroy()`. Cleanup dispatches by type (connection: `Disconnect`, Instance: `Destroy`, function: call, thread: `task.cancel`, table: `Destroy`) in reverse insertion order. Mandatory lifetimes: one session Trove per player, created in `PlayerAdded` and cleaned in `PlayerRemoving`; one Trove per spawned vehicle, cleaned on despawn or owner exit; one per race, job and open UI screen. Every `:Connect` result goes into a Trove unless commented `-- lifetime: server`. Per-player tables are keyed by `Player` and cleared on leave; Instance-keyed caches use `setmetatable({}, {__mode = "k"})`. Acceptance test: DevTools `/leaktest 200` spawns and despawns 200 vehicles; after a 10 s settle the Workspace descendant count is back at baseline and `Stats:GetTotalMemoryUsageMb()` is within 5 MB of its starting value.

[32] `Shared/Logger.luau` provides `Logger.new(scope)`, returning a scoped logger with `:Debug`, `:Info`, `:Warn`, `:Error` and `:WarnOnce(key, ...)`, each taking a format string plus arguments. Levels: DEBUG 10, INFO 20, WARN 30, ERROR 40. Output format: `[TC][S][VehicleService][WARN] message` (S server, C client). `Logger.DEBUG` defaults to `RunService:IsStudio()` and can be flipped at runtime through a `DebugLogging` attribute on ReplicatedStorage (DevTools `/debuglog on`); live clients log WARN and above only. DEBUG and INFO use `print`, WARN uses `warn`, and ERROR uses `warn` plus `debug.traceback()` and increments a per-scope error counter. The level check happens before `string.format`, so disabled calls allocate nothing in hot loops. Identical messages beyond 5 within 10 s are suppressed and summarized once as `(suppressed N repeats)`. Acceptance test: each missing asset ID is logged once per session at INFO, and the only resulting WARN is the single [13] placeholder summary.

[33] `Shared/Util.luau` holds pure, stateless helpers, each covered by DevTools `/selftest`: `clamp`, `lerp`, `inverseLerp`, `remap`, `approach(current, target, maxDelta)`, frame-rate-independent `expSmooth(current, target, rate, dt)` using `alpha = 1 - math.exp(-rate*dt)`, `round(x, decimals)`, `isFiniteNumber(x)` (rejects NaN via `x ~= x` and +/-`math.huge`), `formatNumber(12500)` returning `12,500`, `formatCash(12500)` returning `$12,500` through it, `formatSpeed(studsPerSec, useMph)` returning `47 MPH` or `75 KM/H` through `Units`, `deepCopy`, `deepFreeze`, `retryAsync` ([43]), `weld(a, b)` creating a `WeldConstraint` with Parent set last, and the frozen name tables `Util.Tags` and `Util.Attr` ([41]). As a layer-1 module, Util requires only `Units` and `Logger`. Acceptance test: `expSmooth` toward a fixed target reaches the same value after 1 s at 30, 60 and 240 FPS within 0.1%.

[34] `Shared/NetDefs.luau` requires only `Types` and lists every remote as `Name = {Kind, Direction, Args, Rate}`, where Kind is `Event` (RemoteEvent), `Unreliable` (UnreliableRemoteEvent) or `Function` (RemoteFunction, client-to-server only), and Name matches `^%u%a+_%u%a+$` (Domain_Action: `Vehicle_Spawn`, `Vehicle_Exit`, `Vehicle_State`, `Vehicle_Notice`, `Shop_Buy`, `Job_Action`, `Race_Join`, `Player_Ready`, plus every remote any later paragraph names, such as `Shop_Rent`, `Vehicle_Push`, `Rescue_Call` and `Garage_Visit`); reliable vehicle notices (Bound, Rebind, spawn failures) use `Vehicle_Notice`, never `Vehicle_State`. `Net.InitServer()` creates each remote with `Instance.new`, sets `Name`, parents it into a new `Remotes` Folder, and parents that Folder into ReplicatedStorage last so clients never see a partial set. Server API: `Net.On(name, handler)` (one handler per remote), `Net.Fire(name, player, ...)`, `Net.FireAll`, `Net.FireAllExcept`, `Net.FireNear(name, position, radius, ...)` and `Net.OnInvoke`. Client API: `Net.Fire`, `Net.On` and `Net.Invoke`, which returns `nil, "timeout"` after 10 s. The wrapper has no InvokeClient path at all. Acceptance test: using a name absent from NetDefs throws in Studio and logs ERROR in live servers.

[35] NetDefs `Args` are plain spec tuples (`{"number", min, max, integer?}`, `{"string", maxLen}`, `{"enum", {...}}`, `{"vector3", maxMag}`, `{"instance", className, container}`, `{"buffer", bytes}`) that `Net` compiles at init into `Net.T` validators run before every server handler, so NetDefs never requires Net. Checks: exact count and `typeof`; finite in-range numbers; valid UTF-8 (`utf8.len` not nil) under the cap; finite Vector3 components, magnitude at most 12,000 studs; Instances passing `IsA` within their container; exact buffer length; no unschematized tables. Per-player token buckets per remote: `Shop_Buy` 3 per 5 s, `Vehicle_Spawn` 2 per 5 s, `Job_Action` 5 per s, `Race_Join` 1 per 2 s, `Vehicle_State` 20 per s. Rejections drop silently, WARN at most 3 times per player per remote, and fire `Net.Violation(player, name, reason)` for AntiCheatService ([451]-[475]). Acceptance test: DevTools `/fuzz` (NaN, inf, wrong types, 10 KB strings) reaches zero handlers.

[36] `Vehicle_State` (UnreliableRemoteEvent, the only high-frequency remote) sends one `buffer` per packet; transforms replicate via ownership ([46]-[75]). Packets may arrive out of order or never, so each holds complete absolute state plus a u8 sequence, kept if `(seq - last) % 256` is 1-127. Clients send at 15 Hz while any value crosses its threshold (RPM 50, throttle 0.02, lean 0.5 degrees, gear or flags), else 2 Hz. Packet (10 bytes): seq u8, RPM u16, throttle u8, brake u8, steer i8, lean i8 (0.5-degree steps), gear i8 (-1 reverse), flags u8 (lights, brake light, horn, indicators L/R, hazard, clutch, limiter), mode u8 (bits 0-6 ride mode, bit 7 high beam). The server length-checks, then relays at 10-15 Hz: count u8 plus 12 bytes per vehicle (u16 `NetId` + state), 349 bytes for 29 vehicles (5.2 KB/s), under the 900-byte cap.

[37] Every vehicle is data: one ModuleScript at `Shared/Config/Vehicles/<VehicleId>.luau`, file name equal to `Identity.VehicleId` (snake_case brand + model), with exactly twelve groups: Identity, Economy, Dimensions, Mass, Chassis, Suspension, Tires, Powertrain, Assists, Audio, Visual, Seats. Physical fields carry SI unit suffixes; horsepower from a stat block is converted while authoring and stored as a kW literal (18 hp becomes `13.4`), never as an expression. Audio and Visual hold `SoundIds`/`AssetIds` keys, never raw IDs. Never write inspired-by real names in configs or comments. Abbreviated shape (full `PowertrainConfig` in [76], stat-to-field mapping in [127]; the stat blocks of [126]-[205] win):
```luau
--!strict
local Types = require(script.Parent.Parent.Parent.Types)
local config: Types.VehicleConfig = {
  Identity = {VehicleId="volt_wasp_x", DisplayName="Volt Wasp X", Brand="Volt", Class="ElectricMoto"},
  Economy = {Price=14000, UnlockLevel=12, Dealership="VoltTalonEV"},
  Dimensions = {Wheelbase_mm=1260, Length_mm=1880, SeatHeight_mm=830, WheelDiaF_mm=623, WheelDiaR_mm=623},
  Mass = {Mass_kg=56, CoMHeight_mm=520}, Chassis = {Rake_deg=25, Trail_mm=95},
  Suspension = {TravelF_mm=200, TravelR_mm=220}, Tires = {FrontCompound="MXIntermediate", RearCompound="MXIntermediate"},
  Powertrain = {Kind="Electric", PeakPower_kW=6, TopSpeed_kmh=75, Electric={Pack_V=60, Capacity_Ah=40, Capacity_Wh=2400}},
  Assists = {TractionControl=true, ABS=false}, Audio = {Profile="volt_wasp_x"},
  Visual = {Model="volt_wasp_x"}, Seats = {Riders=1, Passengers=0},
}
return config
```

[38] Other config modules hold every tunable number; a magic constant inside a Service is a defect. `Config/Economy` holds starting cash 2,500, resale 0.60, fuel $2.00 per liter, charging $0.40 per kWh (20 or 60 kW stations, 3.3 kW at home), 10x refuel and charge compression, earnings targets and the dealership IDs `ThrottleWorks`, `VoltTalonEV`, `RyujinKensei`, `EuroClassic`, `DirtDepot`, `SummitOutdoor`, `LakesideMarine` and `HaulerTruckTrailer`. `Config/AssetIds` and `Config/SoundIds` are nested tables of 0 placeholders, each commented with the needed asset (`Body = 0, -- MeshPart, max 20,000 tris, 1024px PBR`); never invent an ID. Their `get(path)` returns nil for 0 or missing entries after logging that key once at INFO (counted in the single [13] summary WARN), and callers skip that visual or sound without throwing. Acceptance test: with every ID at 0 the game boots, vehicles spawn as placeholders, audio is silent, and Output lists each missing ID once.

[39] `Server/ConfigValidator.luau` runs before any Service Init and reports all problems at once. All 12 groups present; fields correctly typed; `VehicleId` equals the file name, matching `^[a-z0-9_]+$`; unknown keys such as `Mass_Kg` are errors; numeric fields end in an allowed suffix (`_kg _kW _Nm _Nmm _mm _m _km _kmh _mps _Wh _Ah _V _A _ohm _deg _rpm _cc _L _s _ms _N _Ns _kPa _kgm2 _pct _x`, where `_ms` = milliseconds, `_mps` = m/s) or allowlisted dimensionless (`Price`, `UnlockLevel`, `Riders`, `Passengers`, `*Mul`, `*Ratio`, plus dimensionless fields later paragraphs declare in `Types`, such as `Progression`, `PolePairs`, `BikeSlots`); finite, sane values (Mass_kg 5-4,000, Price 0-100,000, UnlockLevel 1-100, TopSpeed_kmh 10-320). Cross-checks: dealership ID exists in `Config/Economy`; AssetIds/SoundIds keys exist; `Ratios_x` strictly decreases; `Capacity_Wh` within 2% of `Pack_V` x `Capacity_Ah`. Output: one ERROR block of `file.Group.Field: problem` lines. Studio halts boot via `error()`; live servers hide invalid vehicles, so one bad file never downs the server.

[40] Line 1 of every Script, LocalScript and ModuleScript is `--!strict`, and each phase ships with zero Script Analysis errors and warnings. Type every function parameter and return value; `any` is allowed only for raw remote arguments before validation and for registry reads that are cast immediately to an interface type. No globals: every variable is `local`, and `_G`, `shared`, `getfenv` and `setfenv` are banned. Cache services once at the top of the file (`local Players = game:GetService("Players")`), never inside functions or loops. File layout order: header, services, requires, constants, types, module table, private functions, public methods, `return`. Split files longer than 500 lines and functions longer than 60 lines, and prefer early returns over nested conditionals. Every public method gets a one-line comment stating its units (`-- returns speed in studs/s`).

[41] Naming: PascalCase for ModuleScripts, Instances, types, public methods and Signals; camelCase for locals, parameters and private functions; UPPER_SNAKE_CASE for constants (`RESET_HOLD_S = 1.5`, `RESET_COOLDOWN_S = 15`); booleans start with `is`, `has` or `should`; locals holding physical values end with their unit (`speedKmh`, `massRmu`, `forceN`). Attributes are PascalCase, with unit suffixes on physical values (`OwnerUserId`, `VehicleId`, `NetId`, `Fuel_L`, `Charge_Wh`, `Condition_pct`), written only by the server, for authoritative state that changes at most about once per second; anything faster goes through `Vehicle_State`. Tags are singular PascalCase names from `Util.Tags`: GasPump, ChargingStation, JobPickup, JobDropoff, StreetLight, TrafficLight, Checkpoint, Dealership, GaragePoint, plus `Vehicle` on every spawned vehicle and every tag later paragraphs introduce (AcademyStart, WashBay, Interior, DeepMud, SpeedTrap, JumpZone, SlideZone). Tagged instances carry their settings as attributes (ChargingStation `Power_kW` = 20 or 60). Because streaming moves tagged parts in and out, clients always pair `CollectionService:GetTagged` with `GetInstanceAddedSignal` and `GetInstanceRemovedSignal`.

[42] Per-frame rules for `PreSimulation`, `PostSimulation` and (client-only) `PreRender` callbacks: no `Instance.new`, `Clone` or `Destroy` (tire marks, sparks and dust come from pre-built pools, such as the tire-mark segments of [339] and [479], recycled oldest-first); no `FindFirstChild` chains or `GetDescendants` (cache references at spawn); no table, closure or string allocation (reuse scratch tables and one `RaycastParams` per vehicle, updating its filter only when it changes); and `debug.profilebegin`/`debug.profileend` labels around every step so the MicroProfiler shows costs. Physics forces and `Motor6D.Transform` posing (wheel spin, fork compression, [219]) run in `PreSimulation`, result reads in `PostSimulation`, and camera and other visual-only motion in `PreRender`. Use the `task` library exclusively; every loop has an explicit interval such as `task.wait(0.25)`. Create instances with `Instance.new(className)`, set all properties, and assign `Parent` last; build multi-part models unparented and parent the root once. Frame budgets are in [476]-[500].

[43] Every web call (raw DataStore and OrderedDataStore operations, MarketplaceService, TextService filtering, MessagingService, MemoryStoreService, badge awards, TeleportService, `Players:BanAsync`, thumbnail fetches) goes through `Util.retryAsync(label, fn, maxAttempts?)`. ProfileStore is the one exception: it retries internally, so call it exactly as [451] specifies. `retryAsync` wraps `fn` in `pcall`, makes up to 5 attempts, waits `min(2^(n-1), 16)` seconds plus 0-50% random jitter after failed attempt n (1, 2, 4, 8 s), returns `(ok, resultOrError)`, and logs one ERROR naming the label and attempt count on final failure. Never retry non-idempotent actions blindly: purchases return `Enum.ProductPurchaseDecision.NotProcessedYet` and let Roblox retry ProcessReceipt ([451]-[475]). Check `DataStoreService:GetRequestBudgetForRequestType` before global writes. Per-player handlers and loop bodies run under `xpcall`, so one player's bad state never stalls the other 29. Acceptance test: DevTools `/simfail web 3` fails the next three attempts, and the fourth succeeds with no data loss.

[44] Banned APIs and their only allowed replacements: `BodyVelocity`/`BodyGyro`/`BodyPosition`/`BodyForce`/`BodyThrust`/`BodyAngularVelocity`/`RocketPropulsion` become `LinearVelocity`/`AlignOrientation`/`AlignPosition`/`VectorForce`/`Torque`/`AngularVelocity` or impulses; `wait`/`spawn`/`delay` become `task.wait`/`task.spawn`/`task.delay`/`task.defer`; `FindPartOnRay*` becomes `workspace:Raycast`/`Spherecast`/`Blockcast`/`Shapecast` with `RaycastParams` (`Exclude`/`Include`, never `Blacklist`/`Whitelist`); `Humanoid:LoadAnimation` becomes `Animator:LoadAnimation`; `SetPrimaryPartCFrame`/`GetPrimaryPartCFrame`/`GetModelCFrame` become `PivotTo`/`GetPivot`; `CreateCollisionGroup`/`SetPartCollisionGroup` become `RegisterCollisionGroup` plus `BasePart.CollisionGroup`; legacy Chat becomes `TextChatService`; `Player:GetMouse` becomes `UserInputService`/`ContextActionService`; `InvokeClient` is never used; `Instance.new(class, parent)` becomes Parent-last; `Stepped`/`Heartbeat`/`RenderStepped` become `PreSimulation`/`PostSimulation`/`PreRender`; `game.Players` becomes `game:GetService("Players")`; raw player-save DataStores become ProfileStore (raw DataStoreService only for global and ordered data); the Studio-only properties of [21] are never assigned in code. Acceptance test: a whole-word Find All (Ctrl+Shift+F) for each banned token, ignoring comments and `task.` calls, returns zero hits.

[45] DevTools is Studio-only. `ServerScriptService/Server/DevTools.luau` and `StarterPlayerScripts/Client/DevToolsClient.luau` are required by the bootstraps after every other module, and only when `RunService:IsStudio()` is true; `Net` creates the `Dev_Command` remote (NetDefs flag `StudioOnly`) only in Studio, so live servers contain no command path. The server also checks a UserId allowlist: the owner's UserId plus the negative UserIds (-1, -2, ...) Studio gives local-server test players. A DEV button opens a command bar. Base commands: `/cash n`, `/level n`, `/spawn vehicleId`, `/tp oldtown|harbor|maple|port|airfield|beach|throttlepark|pinecrest|redmesa|lake` (canon region centers), `/time hh`, `/weather state`, `/wet 0-1`, `/stress n` (server-driven dummy riders), `/leaktest n`, `/fuzz`, `/selftest`, `/simfail web n`, `/debuglog on|off` and `/resetdata` (refused unless `UseMockData` is set, [451]); later sections register more. Every command is logged with its UserId. Acceptance test: with -2 removed from the allowlist, Player2 receives `denied` and nothing changes.

## SECTION 3 - VEHICLE PHYSICS CORE: CHASSIS, SUSPENSION, TIRES & BALANCE (Paragraphs 46-75)

[46] You will implement the physics core as pure, typed ModuleScripts in the folder below, shared so the owning client simulates and the server reuses the identical math for validation. `Simulator` orchestrates one step; `ChassisParams` converts config SI fields through `Units` exactly once at bind (and again after tuning changes, [381]) into stud/RMU values, never per frame; `ChassisState` holds per-vehicle mutable state; every other module owns the topic its name states, and `Powertrain/` belongs to [76]-[100]. `VehiclePhysics` sits at layer 4 beside `VehicleBuilder` ([27]) and requires only lower layers. Only `VehicleController` applies forces. Every file starts with `--!strict`, declares its exported types in `Types`, keeps no module-level vehicle state and cleans up with `Trove` on unbind. Phase 2 drives the Volt Wasp X (`volt_wasp_x`) through every module; Phase 4 adds the full roster with zero interface changes.
```
ReplicatedStorage/Shared/VehiclePhysics/
  Simulator.luau      ChassisParams.luau
  ChassisState.luau   Suspension.luau
  Tire.luau           Surface.luau
  Compounds.luau      Balance.luau
  RiderMass.luau      Aero.luau
  FourWheel.luau      SnowTrack.luau
  Buoyancy.luau       Stability.luau
  Powertrain/         (owned by [76]-[100])
```

[47] Every vehicle model (MeshPart override or `VehicleBuilder` placeholder) has one mass-carrying part: `ChassisRoot`, the invisible `PrimaryPart` (`Transparency` 1, `CanCollide` false, `CanQuery` false, `RootPriority` 127), a box centered on `CoM` and sized for realistic loaded inertia: two-wheelers 1.0 x wheelbase long, 0.9 x seat height tall (rider included), 0.35 m wide; four-wheelers, snowmobile and jet ski 0.8 x wheelbase or hull length, 0.8 x track or hull width, half that width tall. Density = total kg x `Units.KG_TO_RMU` / box volume in cubic studs via `CustomPhysicalProperties` (friction and elasticity 0); outside 0.05-50, resize the box, never the mass. Total = Mass_kg + 75 kg per seated rider or passenger + cargo, rewritten server-side on every change. Every other part, seated characters included, is `Massless`; static visuals use `WeldConstraint`, moving roles `Motor6D` ([211]). Acceptance test: `AssemblyMass` within 1% of expected RMU.

[48] Impacts use a simplified hull of 3-6 `Part` blocks or wedges, never MeshPart collision and never wheels: for two-wheelers an engine/frame box, a fork-and-headlight box and a tail box, each `Massless`, `CanCollide` true, `CanTouch` false, friction 0.35, elasticity 0.05, `CollisionGroup` "VehicleHull"; four-wheelers add a floor pan and bumpers. In `VehicleService:Init`, call `PhysicsService:RegisterCollisionGroup` for "VehicleHull", "VehicleDeck" (trailer beds and van ramps wheels stand on, [203]), "WheelProbe" and "Ragdoll", then use `CollisionGroupSetCollidable` so "WheelProbe" ignores "VehicleHull", "Characters" and "Ragdoll" ([249]): wheels never stand on bikes or riders. At bind, `ChassisParams` caches each physics attachment as a CFrame relative to `ChassisRoot`: `CoM`; `WheelF`/`WheelR` or `WheelFL`/`WheelFR`/`WheelRL`/`WheelRR`; `SteerPivot`, whose UpVector is the raked steering axis; `SeatAttachment`; `PegL`/`PegR`; snowmobile `SkiL`, `SkiR`, `Track1`-`Track4`; jet ski `Buoy1`-`Buoy10`, `Nozzle`, `Intake`. A missing one refuses the spawn and logs the VehicleId through `Logger`.

[49] Spawning is server-authoritative. `Vehicle_Spawn` carries `Serial` and `GaragePointId`; `GarageService` validates ownership, bay distance, `AllowedClass` and the 5 s limit ([361]), then calls `VehicleService:SpawnFor(player, serial, bayCFrame)`. That despawns the player's previous vehicle, confirms clearance with `workspace:GetPartBoundsInBox` over the hull bounds plus 1 stud (`OverlapParams`, bay floor excluded), finds the floor with a downward `workspace:Raycast`, clones the model or builds the placeholder unparented, sets attributes `VehicleId`, `OwnerUserId` and `ConfigVersion`, tags it `Vehicle`, anchors `ChassisRoot`, calls `PivotTo` so the lowest tire sits 0.5 studs above the floor at full extension (jet ski afloat) and parents it last into `workspace.Vehicles`. Failures send a typed reason (`BayBlocked`, `NoGround`, `NotOwned`, `RateLimited`) on `Vehicle_Notice`, the reliable server-to-client RemoteEvent in NetDefs ([34]); discrete messages never ride the unreliable `Vehicle_State`. Acceptance test: 30 simultaneous spawns produce no overlaps and no server frame above 4 ms.

[50] Entering fires `Vehicle_Enter` (prompt and permission checks: [116]). The server also requires distance under 10 studs and a free driver seat, welds the rider at `SeatAttachment`, rewrites density ([47]), unanchors `ChassisRoot`, then calls `ChassisRoot:SetNetworkOwner(player)` (it throws on anchored assemblies), sets attribute `DriverUserId` and fires `Vehicle_Notice` "Bound". `VehicleController` binds once `ChassisRoot.Anchored` is false and `ReceiveAge` reads 0 (local ownership; 1 s timeout, one retry), zeroing all integrator state. Passengers never own. On `Vehicle_Exit` ([117]), leave or death, the server calls `SetNetworkOwner(nil)`, places the rider (default 3 studs outboard of `PegL`), restores density and anchors the vehicle after 2 s under 0.5 stud/s; one still moving after 10 s is pivoted onto the ground beneath and anchored. Crash ejection keeps ownership per [237]. Never call `SetNetworkOwnershipAuto` on vehicles. Acceptance test: 50 rapid enter/exit cycles leave no vehicle drifting or wrongly owned.

[51] `VehicleController` connects one `RunService.PreSimulation` handler stepping only the locally owned vehicle. Clamp dt to 1/240-1/30 s; a 0.2 s hitch simulates 1/30 s, never extrapolating. Per frame: call `InputController:GetCommand(dt)` ([101]-[125]); read `ChassisRoot.CFrame`, `AssemblyLinearVelocity` and `AssemblyAngularVelocity`; cast probes once; integrate suspension, wheel spin, tires and balance in N = clamp(ceil(dt x 240), 1, 8) sub-steps of h = dt/N (4 x 1/240 s at 60 FPS); sum force x h into per-contact impulses; add aero; pass everything through `Stability`; then call `ApplyImpulseAtPosition` once per contact and `ApplyAngularImpulse` once. The engine applies gravity (35 studs/s^2); never add it. Profile as `VehicleController.Step`; budget 0.4 ms (two probes) or 0.6 ms (four) on a mid-range phone, inside the 1.0 ms cap of [476]-[500]. Acceptance test: a scripted 10 s run at 30, 60 and 144 FPS ends within 2% of one position.

[52] Probe origin per wheel: the axle at full compression (full-extension wheel attachment moved Travel up the axis). Direction: the raked fork axis for telescopic fronts, chassis-down with a cached leverage curve for swingarms and A-arms. Call `workspace:Spherecast(origin, r_s, direction * (Travel + R_wheel - r_s + 0.1 stud), params)`, r_s = half the tire width so leaned tires touch at the crown, with one reused per-vehicle `RaycastParams`: `FilterType` Exclude (vehicle, riders), `CollisionGroup` "WheelProbe", `RespectCanCollide` true, `IgnoreWater` true ([72]). Compression x = clamp(Travel + R_wheel - r_s - result.Distance, 0, Travel); no hit means unloaded. Shapecasts skip geometry inside the start sphere, so a miss just after x > 0.9 Travel counts as bottomed. Via `Units.mmToStuds`: MX fork 310 mm = 1.107 studs, MX shock 315 mm = 1.125, sport 120/130 mm = 0.429/0.464, downhill 200 mm = 0.714.

[53] Spring force: F_s = k (x + x0) (1 + p (x/T)^2): k wheel rate, x0 preload at the wheel, T travel, p `Progression` (0 coil, 0.3-0.6 linkage, 0.8-1.5 air spring or tire). `Suspension` holds SpringRate_Nmm, Preload_mm, Progression, Travel_mm and SagTarget_pct, F/R-suffixed where axles differ. Add `Units.nmmToRobloxSpring` (x 1000 x KG_TO_RMU = 45.55, since N/m = kg/s^2) and `nsmToRobloxDamper` (x KG_TO_RMU). Reference wheel rates, 75 kg rider: MX front 9.4 N/mm (two 4.7 N/mm springs), MX rear 6.0, sport 19 front and 18 rear, downhill rear 9.5, pickup front 95. `ChassisParams` solves x0 so rider sag hits SagTarget_pct (MX rear 33%, about 105 mm; sport 25%; downhill 30%); if x0 leaves 0-0.25T, clamp it, rescale k to hold the target and WARN once. Tuner `Preload_mm` ([380]) adds to x0. Acceptance test: rider sag per wheel within 5% of target.

[54] Damper force opposes axis velocity v (positive compressing): F_d = c_b v in bump, c_r v in rebound, digressive above a 0.3 m/s knee where slope drops to 45%. c = 2 zeta sqrt(k m_c), m_c = corner static sprung mass; zeta_bump 0.30, zeta_rebound 0.70 (clickers: [380]). MX rear: k 6,000 N/m, m_c 100 kg give c_b 465 and c_r 1,084 N s/m. Initial v: the hub's groundward axis velocity from `ChassisRoot:GetVelocityAtPosition` minus the hit part's (moving platforms). Per sub-step, step a predicted 1-DoF corner (semi-implicit Euler): v += (m_c g_axis - F_s - F_d - F_stop) h / m_c, then x += v h, accumulating (F_s + F_d + F_stop) h as support impulse; each frame re-measures from casts. Acceptance test: a 0.5 m drop to flat ground settles within 0.8 s, one overshoot under 15% of travel.

[55] The last 12% of travel engages a hydraulic bump stop: F_stop = 12k (x - 0.88T)^2 / (0.12T) + 3 c_b v, velocity term only while compressing. If x reaches T still closing, an impulse through the contact's effective mass removes the closing velocity (restitution 0.1) and emits `Bottomed {Wheel, ClosingSpeed_ms}` for [231]-[250]. A top-out spring subtracts 4k (0.04T - x) from F_s while x < 0.04T, so full extension never clanks. Airborne wheels relax toward x = 0 no faster than F_s(x)/c_r, so landings start from a realistic position. Wheels, forks, shocks and swingarms are posed only through `Motor6D.Transform` by `VehicleVisuals` ([219]) from the latest compression, steer and spin; visuals never feed back into physics. Acceptance test: casing a 2 m lip at 40 km/h bottoms both ends without a one-frame position pop.

[56] Wheel spin: I_w = 0.8 m_wheel R^2 + reflected inertia (11 kg MX rear, R 0.33 m: 0.96 kg m^2). T_net = T_drive - T_brake sign(omega) - Crr F_z R sign(omega), brakes from `Chassis.BrakeTorqueF_Nm`/`BrakeTorqueR_Nm` ([377]). kappa = (omega R - v_x)/v_ref, v_ref = max(|v_x|, 1.5 m/s). Linearize the tire term (S = dF_x/dkappa, floored at 0): omega += h (T_net - F_x R) / (I_w + h S R^2 / v_ref); brake torque that would reverse omega locks it at 0. Drive torque comes only from `Powertrain:GetWheelTorque(omegaWheel, input, h)` ([77]): `DriveTorque_Nm` (negative for engine braking or regen) and `ReflectedInertia_kgm2`, both scaled by `Units.NM_TO_TORQUE`; rider aids act via its `TorqueLimit` hook. Acceptance test: a Sim Mode Volt Wasp X under full rear brake from 50 km/h on dry Asphalt locks within 0.3 s and stops in 36-50 m.

[57] Both directions use f(s) = sin(C atan(Bs - E(Bs - atan(Bs)))) times D = mu F_z. Longitudinal: s = kappa, C 1.65, E 0.1. Lateral: s = alpha = atan2(-v_lat, |v_x|) in radians, C 1.30, E -0.5. At bind set B = x*/s_peak, putting the peak at the compound's peak slip ([60]); x* solves x - E(x - atan x) = tan(pi/(2C)) by bisection (1.397 for C 1.65, E 0). Past the peak blend to sliding level r: f = 1 - (1 - r) smoothstep((s/s_peak - 1)/2), r 0.75 paved, 0.90 dirt, 1.05 in sand, mud and snow. Two-wheelers add camber thrust 0.9 F_z x lean_rad inside the same clamp, so a 45 deg lean needs only 0.5-2 deg slip. Relax lateral slip over 0.25 m: alpha += (alpha_kin - alpha)(1 - exp(-|v_x| h / 0.25 m)).

[58] Combined slip: sx = kappa/kappa_peak, sy = tan(alpha)/tan(alpha_peak), sigma = sqrt(sx^2 + sy^2). Evaluate each curve at s = sigma x s_peak; split F_x = D f_x sx/sigma and F_y = D f_y sy/sigma, then clamp the vector to mu F_z, so hard braking while leaned tucks the front. Load sensitivity: mu_eff = mu (1 - 0.08 (F_z/F_nom - 1)), clamped 0.80-1.10 x mu, F_nom static corner load. F_z = max(0, F_s + F_d + F_stop) x cos(axis-to-normal angle) acts along the normal; tire forces act at the hit, along forward (wheel heading projected onto the contact plane) and right = forward:Cross(normal). Below 1.5 m/s a static hold cancels lateral velocity (longitudinal too while braking) up to mu F_z, parking a braked bike on a 20 deg slope. Lateral impulse per frame never exceeds zeroing lateral contact velocity.

[59] `Surface.resolve(result)` returns a SurfaceId by priority: the hit Instance's `Surface` string attribute, then a BasePart's `MaterialVariant` name ("PackedDirt", "Gravel"), then `result.Material`, Terrain using the [297] override slots (Slate PackedDirt, CrackedLava Gravel, Limestone Snow, Salt PackedDirt). Base grip: Asphalt 1.00; Concrete and Pavement 0.95; Brick and Cobblestone 0.85; WoodPlanks and Wood 0.80; Metal, DiamondPlate and CorrodedMetal 0.70; PackedDirt 0.80; Rock, Basalt and Granite 0.75; Ground (loose dirt) 0.70; Grass and LeafyGrass 0.60; Gravel and Pebble 0.60; Mud 0.45; Sand 0.40; Snow 0.35; Ice and Glacier 0.15; else 0.70. Wetness w from `WeatherQuery.wetnessAt(position)` ([333]), sampled per wheel every 0.25 s, scales paved and rock by (1 - 0.30w) and dirt (packed, loose, grass, gravel) by (1 - 0.40w). Final mu = base x compound ([60]) x wetness x `Tires.GripMul` (default 1.0) x wear ([231]-[250]), cached until the hit surface changes.

[60] `Compounds.luau` is the single table of tire-compound multipliers on base surface grip, one row per compound named by each vehicle's `Tires.FrontCompound` and `Tires.RearCompound`, carrying the peak slips that set B in [57]. Columns: Paved (asphalt through metal), Packed (PackedDirt and the rock family), Loose (Ground, grass, gravel and any fallback material), Mud, Sand, Snow, Ice. Store named fields in code; columns appear positionally here. Sand paddles apply their Sand value longitudinally and 0.70 of it laterally; slicks lose a further (1 - 0.25w) when wet; trials tires gain an extra x1.20 on Rock, Basalt, Granite and WoodPlanks. Configs store only these row names and `ConfigValidator` rejects others; shorthand in roster prose resolves as Knobby, Knobby MX, Knobby trail or Enduro = MXIntermediate, Turf = Touring, SupermotoStreet = StreetSport. Upgrades in [366]-[390] may only switch between these rows.
```
-- Paved, Packed, Loose, Mud, Sand, Snow, Ice, PeakSlipRatio, PeakSlipAngle_deg
StreetSport    ={1.10,0.80,0.70,0.55,0.60,0.60,0.70,0.10,6},
Touring        ={1.00,0.85,0.80,0.65,0.70,0.75,0.80,0.11,7},
MXIntermediate ={0.80,1.15,1.20,1.05,1.00,0.95,0.90,0.20,10},
MXSoft         ={0.75,1.05,1.15,1.25,1.10,1.00,0.90,0.22,10},
SandPaddle     ={0.50,0.80,1.00,1.10,1.60,1.20,0.60,0.30,12},
Mud            ={0.70,1.00,1.10,1.45,1.05,1.05,0.85,0.25,11},
Trials         ={1.05,1.15,1.15,1.10,0.95,1.00,0.95,0.15,9},
Slick          ={1.25,0.55,0.45,0.30,0.40,0.35,0.60,0.10,6},
SnowStudded    ={0.75,0.90,0.95,0.90,0.85,1.60,3.20,0.18,9},
KnobbyATV      ={0.85,1.10,1.15,1.20,1.10,1.00,0.85,0.22,12},
```

[61] Two-wheelers and the Gyro EUC-20 use the roll-only PD of `Balance.luau`; pitch and yaw stay free. Lean phi = asin(clamp(-RightVector.Y, -1, 1)), positive right; roll rate p = `AssemblyAngularVelocity:Dot(LookVector)`. Target phi_t = atan(v^2/(gR)), R = L/tan(delta_cmd cos(rake)), delta_cmd = Steer x delta_max ([62]), capped by `Chassis.MaxLean_deg` (sport 55, MX/naked 50, pedal 45, scooter 40, cruiser 32, bagger 31). Accumulate `LookVector * tau * h` per sub-step, tau = I_c (w_n^2 (phi_t - phi) - 1.8 w_n p) - A m g h_CoM sin(phi), I_c = contact-line roll inertia (box + m h_CoM^2). w_n = 6 clamp((v-2)/4, 0, 1) self-stability (Sim Mode too) + 5A rad/s, v in m/s, A = BalanceAssist x clamp(1 - v/8, 0, 1), also cancelling low-speed toppling. Acceptance test: Sim Mode at 40 km/h tracks a 30 deg target within 2 deg in 0.6 s.

[62] Input sets the lean target; the tire's steer angle follows actual lean, as on a real bike. Above 10 km/h the front heading uses equilibrium steer delta = atan(L g tan(phi)/v^2)/cos(rake), so when grip or the controller cannot reach the target lean, the line widens instead of the bike yawing unrealistically. Below 6 km/h steering is direct, delta = Steer x delta_max, blending linearly up to 10 km/h. Limit: delta_max(v) = max(1.5 deg, delta_lock/(1 + (v/6)^2)), v in m/s; delta_lock (`Chassis.SteerLock_deg`) is 40 deg for MX and pit bikes, 45 trials, 30 street, 28 sport, 35 scooter, 60 BMX. Counter-steer: when target lean changes faster than 20 deg/s above 25 km/h, the tire-model steer and the handlebar visual flick opposite (peak 1.5 deg, 0.15 s), physically initiating the roll. Raw input shaping belongs to [101]-[125].

[63] From `Chassis.Rake_deg`, `Chassis.Trail_mm` t and front radius R_f, `ChassisParams` solves fork offset d = R_f sin(rake) - t cos(rake); outside -20 to 120 mm fails `ConfigValidator`. Trail scales self-stability w_n by t/100 mm (clamped 0.7-1.6) and caps lean rate at 120 x 100/t deg/s. References: sport 24 deg/97 mm, MX 27/116, bagger 26/170, chopper 38/130. Below 8 km/h, wheel flop T_f = m_f g t sin(rake) cos(rake) sin(delta) (m_f static front mass) adds T_f/(300 N m/rad) to last frame's delta, capped at delta_lock. Steering damper: steer rate cap 300 deg/s at rest, falling to 60 at 150 km/h. Headshake: landing or dropping a wheelie above 80 km/h with over 3 deg front misalignment starts an 8 Hz oscillation, time constant 0.2 + 1.2 x (1 - `Chassis.SteeringDamper`) s; above 12 deg in Sim Mode it raises `Headshake` ([74]).

[64] Below 5 km/h, nonzero balance assist holds lean within 8 deg and foot-dabs beyond 12 deg; with assist 0 (Sim Mode) dabs happen only under 3 km/h and 25 deg, otherwise the bike tips and emits `TipOver`. A dab raycasts 0.9 m down from the low-side peg (`PegL`/`PegR`); on a hit, a virtual leg adds roll torque beyond the dab angle (stiffness 2.5 m g h_CoM per rad, damping 0.8 sqrt(k I_c), equally firm for BMX and bagger) and raises `FootDab {Side}` for IK ([234]). Stopped (under 1 km/h, no throttle for 0.5 s) enters FootDown, resting 6 deg on the left foot. Trials and pedal bikes can track-stand footless. The Gyro EUC-20 never dabs: its motor holds pitch ([86]) and the rider steps off. Acceptance test: assist 100% never tips at a standstill on a 10 deg camber.

[65] Weight shift s (-1 back to 1 forward; inputs in [101]-[125]) moves the 75 kg rider's hips dx = 0.22 s m along the chassis and crouches dy = -0.05 |s| m, smoothed over 0.12 s. Grounded: (1) apply pitch torque m_r g dx cos(pitch) about `RightVector`, nose-down for forward shift: exactly gravity on the shifted CoM; (2) never add load transfer separately, since the springs react to (1) and forward shift gains front grip by itself; (3) snapping s back faster than 4 per second pops: an upward impulse min(PopCap_Ns, m_r x 0.22 m x |ds/dt|), default cap 60 N s, at the rear contact lifts and pitches the nose for manuals, bunny hops and lip preloads. ATV riders also shift 0.15 m x steer laterally. Airborne, internal shifts create no net torque ([67]).

[66] Wheelies, manuals, stoppies are emergent. Balance point theta_bp = atan(b_eff/h), h = CoM height, b = horizontal rear-contact-to-CoM distance, b_eff = b + m_r dx/M: about 41 deg MX (b 0.70, h 0.80 m), 39 deg Volt Wasp X, 45 deg sport (b 0.69, h 0.70 m); full back shift lowers it about 4 deg. Drive force lifts the nose (F_x h) because impulses act at contacts; never add it separately. Stoppies begin once deceleration exceeds g x_f/h (x_f = CoM to front contact), about 1.0 g for sport bikes, inside StreetSport mu 1.10 after load sensitivity. Wheelie control's `TorqueLimit` holds pitch under theta_bp - 8 deg; rear-lift mitigation caps front brake once rear load stays under 10% for 0.1 s ([98]). Acceptance test: a Sim Mode Kensei ZR-1000 at 100 km/h lifts its rear under full front brake.

[67] Airborne means no probe hit for 0.1 s. Attitude then follows carried angular momentum plus three limited sources: (1) weight-shift air torque s x `AirPitchTorque_Nm` about `RightVector` (25 dirt and electric moto, 18 pedal, 15 quads, 10 street, 0 UTV and haulers); (2) wheel reaction torque -I_w domega/dt, so throttle lifts the nose and a rear-brake stab drops it; (3) steer adds roll torque at 15% of AirPitchTorque for whips. Clamp airborne angular speed to 6 rad/s in pitch, 4 in roll and yaw. Balance stays off and blends back over 0.15 s after touchdown. Landing rear-first within 15 deg of the landing slope is clean; anything else goes to [74]. Acceptance test: a Ryujin RX-250F off a 30 deg lip over a 20 m gap changes pitch 30-45 deg in flight from weight shift alone.

[68] Drag 0.5 rho CdA v^2 opposes air-relative velocity at the CoM; rho = 1.225 exp(-elevation_m/8,500) kg/m^3 (Summit Snowfield, 392 m: -4.5%). CdA m^2: tucked sport 0.30 (upright 0.40; tuck blends in over 0.6 s above 120 km/h), naked and supermoto 0.50, dirt, pit and adventure 0.60, cruiser 0.75, bagger 0.70, scooter 0.55, road bike on drops 0.32, MTB and BMX 0.50, e-scooter and EUC 0.45, e-board 0.40, ATV 0.90, UTV 1.40, snowmobile 0.85, Hauler Van 1.50, Hauler HD 1.80; sport bikes add front lift 0.03 v^2 N. Crr for [56], paved: moto road 0.015, knobby 0.025, ATV 0.030, truck 0.010, bicycle road 0.005, bicycle knobby 0.010, fat or urethane 0.018; off paved: packed 0.03, loose and gravel 0.05, grass 0.06, snow 0.10, mud 0.12, sand 0.20 (paddle 0.10), bicycles x0.6. Acceptance test: flat-road top speeds within 3% of config.

[69] ATVs, UTV and trucks reuse `Suspension` and `Tire` with four corner probes (`WheelFL` to `WheelRR`) and no balance controller; body roll, two-wheeling and rollovers emerge from tire forces. Ackermann: inner = atan(L/(R - T/2)), outer = atan(L/(R + T/2)), blended toward parallel by `AckermannFactor` (ATV 0.6, UTV 0.8, trucks 1.0); delta_lock 35 deg ATVs, 32 UTV, 38 trucks, plus [62]'s speed limit. Anti-roll adds k_ar (x_L - x_R) to the left force, subtracting it on the right; `AntiRollF_Nmm`/`AntiRollR_Nmm`: Rattler quads 0/0, Ranchhand 570 0/4, Dunecat XR Turbo 12/8, Hauler HD 25/15, Hauler Van 20/12. The Glide E-Board's steer input tilts its four-probe deck up to 12 deg against 350 N m/rad bushings; trucks steer 0.35 x deck roll. Roll past 75 deg emits `Rollover` ([74]). Acceptance test: a Hauler HD lane change at 60 km/h rolls under 6 deg.

[70] `FourWheel.luau` splits powertrain torque per axle and side. Open differential: equal torque, free speeds, so a lifted wheel caps total drive at twice its tiny traction. Locked or spool: one rotating body, summed inertia, shared omega; traction adds and tight turns scrub. Limited-slip: open plus a clutch moving 40 N m per rad/s of speed difference to the slower wheel, 50 N m preload. Rattler Trail 90, 450R and 700: spool rear. Ranchhand 570: locked rear plus on-demand front engaging within 0.15 s once rear slip exceeds front by 0.08 in 4WD. Dunecat XR Turbo: locked rear, selectable 2WD/4WD, locking front. Hauler HD: part-time 4WD, locked 50/50 transfer case, open front, selectable rear locker. Hauler Van: open rear. Ratios: [76]-[100]. Acceptance test: a 4WD Ranchhand 570 climbs a 30 deg loose-dirt slope that stops it in 2WD.

[71] The Frostline 850 runs `SnowTrack.luau`: ski probes at `SkiL`/`SkiR`, track probes at `Track1`-`Track4`. Skis (sphere radius 0.15 studs, 229 mm = 0.818 studs travel) steer and grip laterally with absolute mu 0.55 on snow (carbide runners) and 0.35 on ice, but only glide longitudinally: mu_x 0.04 on snow, 0.30 off it. Track probes span the 154-inch track's 1.5 m contact on a 406 mm (1.45 stud) skid; the track is one rotating body whose slip ratio drives a curve peaking at kappa 0.25, mu 0.95 on snow (2.6-inch lugs) and 0.25 on asphalt. Under 25 km/h in Snow, probes sink up to 0.4 studs, adding 0.08 F_z drag. Steer shifts the rider 0.2 m laterally for sidehilling. After 20 s off snow, the HUD warns of slide overheat ([431]-[450]). Acceptance test: 0-100 km/h on groomed snow under 4 s.

[72] The Wavecat 300 runs `Buoyancy.luau` on samples `Buoy1`-`Buoy10` (4 x 2 planing grid, bow, stern). Cast `workspace:Raycast` down from 10 studs above the four corner samples with `IgnoreWater` false; a hit with `Material` `Enum.Material.Water` gives the surface (sea level Y 0), fitted as a plane, plus swell and wakes ([205], [317]). Each submerged sample adds 1.0 RMU/stud^3 x 35 x its displaced volume upward, plus vertical damping at 0.6 critical, summed into one `ApplyImpulse` and one `ApplyAngularImpulse`. Water drag 0.5 rho_w CdA_wet v^2: wetted area falls 60% once planing above 25 km/h, 8x laterally. Thrust (7.5 kN peak, [96]) acts at `Nozzle` only while `Intake` is submerged, vectored 20 deg each way; reverse -40%. Terrain water buoys parts natively too: measure it with custom buoyancy off, subtract it, and verify loaded draft 0.22 m (0.79 studs) within 10%.

[73] `Stability.luau` vets every value with `Util.isFiniteNumber`; a non-finite value drops that frame's impulses, resets integrators and logs at most every 10 s. Clamp per-frame linear impulse to a 60 stud/s velocity change, angular speed to 25 rad/s (8 for grounded two-wheelers) and wheel omega to 1.3 x top speed/R. Anti-tunneling: above 100 studs/s, `workspace:Blockcast` the hull bounds along velocity x dt x 1.5; on a hit, cancel normal closing velocity and emit `HullImpact`. Sleep: after 2 s idle under 0.15 stud/s and 0.05 rad/s, recast every 0.25 s, reapply the last support impulses and skip tire, wheel and balance integration until woken. `ReceiveAge` above 0 for 3 bound frames means lost ownership: apply nothing until rebound. After resets or `PivotTo`, zero sub-step states and skip 2 frames. Acceptance test: a 30-minute random-input soak yields no NaN or unclamped impulse.

[74] Physics only detects; [231]-[250] decides ragdoll, damage and recovery. `Simulator` fires `CrashSignal` (`Signal`) with `{Reason, Severity, ImpactSpeed_ms, Position, Normal}`, Severity 0-1, at most every 0.5 s. Reasons: `HullImpact` (frame velocity change over 8 m/s beyond our impulses and gravity, severity dv/25); `CasedLanding` (both ends bottomed, closing over 7 m/s); `LowSide` (front sigma over 1.6 for 0.25 s above 20 deg lean); `HighSide` (rear sigma over 2.0 regaining grip below 1.0 within 0.2 s at over 15 deg yaw slip); `LoopOut` (pitch over theta_bp + 25 deg, rear grounded, unless the Kensei ZR-6S Stunt's 12 o'clock bar touches); `Endo` (pitch under -55 deg); `Headshake`; `Rollover`; `TipOver`; `UpsideDown` (up-vector Y under -0.2 for 0.5 s); `Submerged` (`ChassisRoot` underwater 2 s; jet ski exempt; land vehicles counter native buoyancy so they sink); `Bail` ([118]). `Vehicle_Crash` ([237]) reports them for server validation ([248]).

[75] `VehicleService` samples each driven `ChassisRoot` at 10 Hz from one `RunService.PostSimulation` connection for `AntiCheatService` ([472]). Canon speed rule: horizontal speed above top speed x 1.15 for over 2 s, top speed = max(effective `TopSpeed_kmh`, `DescentCap_kmh` if set) ([127]). Jump rule: displacement between samples above top speed x 1.15 x 0.1 s + 12 studs without a valid server teleport token (reset, spawn, race grid, unload). Hover rule: 6 s with no server ground hit within 30 studs below and vertical velocity not negative. Liveness: owner's `Vehicle_State` stream ([36]) silent for 3 s means reclaim and anchor once slow. First violation: `SetNetworkOwner(nil)`, `PivotTo` the last valid sample, zero velocity, return ownership after 1 s; repeats escalate per [473]. Acceptance test: injected 2x velocity is corrected within 2.2 s; an hour of legitimate riding, Canyon Road descents included, raises zero flags.

## SECTION 4 - POWERTRAINS: ELECTRIC, GAS & PEDAL (Paragraphs 76-100)

[76] You will build every powertrain as pure, allocation-free `--!strict` modules in `ReplicatedStorage/Shared/VehiclePhysics/Powertrain/`: `Powertrain` (facade), `ElectricDrive`, `Battery`, `GasEngine`, `Clutch`, `Gearbox`, `Cvt`, `PedalDrive`, `Thermal`, `RiderAids` and `PowertrainReplica`. The facade implements `Powertrain:GetWheelTorque(omegaWheel, input, h)` exactly as [56] calls it, and the server reuses the same modules for energy accounting and the dyno ([366]-[390]). `PowertrainConfig` in `Types` is pure SI and supersedes the abbreviated example in [37]:
```luau
export type PowertrainConfig = {
  Kind: PowertrainKind, -- "Pedal" | "Electric" | "Gas2T" | "Gas4T" | "Diesel" ([29])
  PeakPower_kW: number, PeakTorque_Nm: number, TopSpeed_kmh: number, -- motor or crank
  Electric: ElectricSpec?, -- motor, controller, pack, thermal, RideModes
  Engine: EngineSpec?, -- inertia, TorqueCurve, limiter, induction, StartType
  Clutch: ClutchSpec?, -- Manual, Centrifugal or TorqueConverter
  Gearbox: GearboxSpec?, -- ratios, primary, final, ShiftTime_s, quickshifter
  Cvt: CvtSpec?, -- engagement, ratio range, range box
  Fuel: FuelSpec?, -- FuelType, PremixRatio, Tank_L, BSFC table
  Pedal: PedalSpec?, -- chainrings, cassette, freewheel or fixed, assist
  Driveline: DrivelineSpec, -- Chain/Belt/Shaft/Hub/CvtBelt, Efficiency_x, sprockets
}
```

[77] `GetWheelTorque` runs inside every sub-step h of [51]: read processed input (throttle, brake, clutch, shift requests, ride mode), apply `RiderAids` limits via the `TorqueLimit` hook, step the source (motor, engine or rider), step clutch and transmission, then return `DriveTorque_Nm` and `ReflectedInertia_kgm2`. With the clutch locked the engine is slaved to the wheel and reflects J_e × ratio² (Kensei K2-250 in 1st: 0.011 × 25.1² = 6.9 kg m², seven times the wheel); while slipping it integrates alone. Wheel torque = source torque × ratio × `Driveline.Efficiency_x`: chain 0.97 (0.93 dry or worn, [231]-[250]), belt 0.96, shaft 0.93, CVT belt 0.88, hub 1.0. Sprockets scale wheel torque by the ratio change and rev-limited top speed inversely: the Kensei ZR-6S Stunt's 15/56 versus a stock-style 16/43 gives 1.39x torque and its 190 km/h ceiling.

[78] `ElectricDrive` models a permanent-magnet motor in three regions. Below base speed ω_b = PeakPower_W / PeakTorque_Nm torque is constant at the controller limit; from ω_b to `FieldWeakening_x` × ω_b (2.0-3.0) power is constant, T = P_peak / ω; beyond that torque falls as P_peak × ω_fw / ω² and is cut to zero at `MaxSpeed_rpm`. Back-EMF scales base speed with voltage, ω_b,eff = ω_b × V_t / `MotorRef_V` (stock `Pack_V`, kept through [370] pack swaps), so sag costs top end first. Volt Wasp X: 42 Nm motor peak, 6 kW, `Reduction_x` 6.1, 0.31 m rear radius, `FieldWeakening_x` 2.9, `MaxSpeed_rpm` 4,300, so 256 Nm at the wheel, base speed 26 km/h, 75 km/h at 3,900 rpm. Efficiency is computed, never tabled: η = P_shaft / (P_shaft + [84] losses), about 0.90 at cruise and under 0.5 in a full-current crawl.

[79] The controller limits two currents independently. Phase current sets torque: T = Kt × I_phase, with Kt = PeakTorque_Nm / `PhaseCurrent_A` (Wasp X: 42 Nm / 300 A = 0.14 Nm/A). Battery current sets power: I_batt = (T × ω_motor + [84] losses) / V_t, capped at the lower of `BatteryCurrent_A` (Wasp X 100 A, about 6 kW at 60 V) and the pack's `MaxDischarge_A` (Wasp X 120 A). Launch is phase-limited, speed battery-limited. Each sub-step, solve for the largest torque meeting both limits, the [84] derate, the [80] low-voltage limit and the [83] mode scale; expose amps and kW to the HUD. Acceptance test: a full-throttle, full-charge Wasp X launch logs about 300 A phase and about 40 A battery at 5 km/h, then 100 A battery with falling phase current above 30 km/h.

[80] `Battery` is an equivalent-circuit pack. Open-circuit voltage comes from a per-cell table over state of charge (SoC 0, 0.1, 0.2, 0.5, 0.8, 1.0 gives 3.00, 3.45, 3.55, 3.70, 3.95, 4.20 V) times the series count, so `PackFull_V` and `PackEmpty_V` are cells × 4.20 and × 3.00, while `Pack_V` keeps the canon nominal label. Terminal voltage V_t = OCV − I × `Pack_ohm`, with regen current negative. Wasp X: 16S, 60 V nominal, 67.2 V full, 48 V empty, 0.05 ohm, sagging about 5 V at 100 A. The low-voltage limit is emergent, I_max = (OCV − PackEmpty_V) / R, so a nearly empty pack delivers less power with no scripted nerf. Below 10% SoC also scale `BatteryCurrent_A` linearly to 40% at 0% (limp mode). `Capacity_Wh` is canon (Wasp X 2,400 Wh) and fixes the SoC scale.

[81] Energy is integrated, never scripted. Each sub-step the client adds OCV × I × h / 3600 to `EnergyUsed_Wh` (pack I²R loss included; regen credited at 90%) and lowers SoC, so Wh/km emerges from aero, rolling resistance, climbs and losses. The server stays authoritative: from the 10 Hz samples of [75] it integrates road-load energy (inertia, drag, rolling and climb power over efficiency) and writes the `Charge_Wh` attribute at 1 Hz ([41]); the client snaps to it beyond a 3% difference. Targets on a 10 km mixed loop: Volt Wasp X 40-60 Wh/km aggressive and 25-35 relaxed; Ridgeline Commute E 12-18 on throttle; Gyro EUC-20 18-25; Zephyr SR-E 65-80 in town and 110-130 at 113 km/h. The range estimator divides remaining Wh by consumption over the last 3 km, using `Capacity_Wh` / `RatedRange_km` (stat-block mid-range) until 1 km is logged.

[82] Regenerative braking is negative motor torque on closed throttle and on rear-brake input, sized by `RegenCurrent_A` (Wasp X 30 A, Zephyr SR-E 120 A, 0 on the freewheeling Commute E, Fatboy E and Summit E) times the player's strength setting Off/Low/Medium/High (0, 0.33, 0.66, 1.0) and the mode's `RegenScale_x`. It fades out between 8 and 3 km/h, is blocked whenever V_t would exceed `PackFull_V` (weak above 95% SoC), shares the [84] derate, and acts as rear longitudinal tire force, so it can slide the rear on ice and ABS ([98]) may release it. The Glide E-Board has no friction brake: two independent 1.5 kW rear hub motors (90 mm wheels, 10S 36 V, 250 Wh) get equal torque, behaving as an open differential, and brake only by regen; at full charge a 30% resistive floor remains and the HUD warns.

[83] Each electric vehicle declares `RideModes`, cycled by the ride-mode input of [101]-[125] (T on PC), blended over 0.5 s and replicated as the ride-mode index. A mode holds `PowerScale_x`, `CurrentScale_x`, `SpeedCap_kmh`, `ThrottleMap`, `ThrottleRamp_ms` (0-100% torque request) and `RegenScale_x`. Defaults: Volt Wasp X Eco (0.55 power, 0.7 current, 50 km/h, Progressive, 350 ms) and Sport (1.0, 75 km/h, Linear, 120 ms); other Talon and Volt motos add Turbo (1.0, Aggressive, 60 ms) and drop Sport to 0.8; Zephyr, Lupo Twin and Glide modes follow their stat blocks; Cruz C7 Street caps 32 km/h and 1.2 kW, Off-road the full 2 kW and 45 km/h; e-bikes map modes to assist levels ([85]). Throttle maps after a 3% deadzone: Linear u, Progressive u^1.7, Aggressive u^0.6. Acceptance test: Eco takes at least 1.6x longer than Sport for 0-40 km/h on the Wasp X.

[84] `Thermal` runs one lumped node per heat source: C dT/dt = P_loss − (T − T_amb)/R, with R = R_0/(1 + v/20 m/s), T_amb from `Temp_C_<Zone>` ([321]-[340]). Motor loss is I_phase² R_winding plus iron loss proportional to speed; controller loss is I_phase² R_fet. Wasp X: motor 1,200 J/K, R_0 0.36 K/W, `Winding_ohm` 0.015, iron 0.5 W per rad/s; controller 600 J/K, 0.25 K/W, `Fet_ohm` 0.002 (add suffixes `_C`, `_JpK`, `_KpW`, `_Wprad` to [39]). The motor derates linearly from 100% at 120 °C to 50% at 150 °C, the controller from 85 °C to 20% at 100 °C. `Reduction_x` is the single fixed electric ratio (1.0 for hubs). Acceptance test: full throttle against a rear wheel held at 15 km/h (cooling at v = 0) derates a cold Wasp X in 80-120 s; two parked minutes then shed 20 °C.

[85] Pedal-assist e-bikes combine `PedalDrive` ([99]) with a motor. Ridgeline Summit E is Class 1: torque-sensing mid-drive, no throttle, levels Off/Eco/Trail/Turbo multiplying rider power by 0, 0.35, 1.0 and 4.1, capped at 90 Nm crank torque and 565 W, fading linearly from 30 km/h to zero at 32 km/h. Its torque runs through chain and cassette, so gearing matters. Ridgeline Commute E is Class 2: cadence-sensing 750 W rear hub, five PAS levels at 20-100% of rated power once cadence passes 20 rpm, plus a thumb throttle, all cut at 32 km/h or the instant a brake is touched; hub torque bypasses the chain. Ridgeline Fatboy E (Class 3 speed, 45 km/h) and Cruz C7 follow the Commute E pattern with their canon caps. Store `EBikeClass` for the HUD. Acceptance test: a Summit E above 32 km/h draws zero motor current.

[86] The Gyro EUC-20's powertrain is its pitch balance loop. Throttle and brake lean the rider up to ±12°, shifting the CoM fore or aft of the axle; the hub answers T = Kp(θ − θ_target) + Kd·θ̇ on shell pitch θ (θ_target 0, or the tilt-back angle), reacting on the shell, so acceleration emerges as g·tan(lean) and braking is regen to a stop. Direct-drive 0.254 m hub: 3.3 kW rated, 7.5 kW peak, 180 Nm, 24S 100 V-class pack, 1,800 Wh. Tilt-back speed is the lower of 70 km/h and 85% of the voltage limit, 85 km/h × V_t/`PackFull_V`; beeps start at 90%; past it the pedals tilt up to 6° nose-up.
