Requested torque above 95% of available for 0.25 s fires `Cutoff`: a growing 6 Hz wobble for 0.4 s, then zero torque and the [231]-[250] crash flow.

[87] Charging is server-authoritative with no remote: each `ChargingStation`-tagged VoltCharge post carries a `ProximityPrompt` whose server-side `Triggered` starts a session once the player's own EV is parked within 12 studs. Power is the lower of the post's `Power_kW` ([41], 20 or 60) and the vehicle's `MaxCharge_kW` (60 Zephyr models, 20 other EVs); home `GaragePoint` sockets give 3.3 kW free. Above 80% SoC the rate drops to 40% (constant-voltage phase, [362]). Time runs 10x: each real second adds ten seconds of energy to `Charge_Wh`. EconomyService accrues $0.40 per kWh every 0.25 s tick and charges once per session ([362]), ending on the prompt, ride-off, walk-away or when the bill reaches Cash. References: Wasp X 10-80% at 20 kW about 30 s ($0.67), 10-100% about 52 s ($0.86); Zephyr SR-E 10-80% at 60 kW about 73 s ($4.84); Wasp X home charge from empty about 6 min.

[88] `GasEngine` integrates crank speed, J × dω/dt = T_combustion − T_friction − T_clutch, with `Inertia_kgm2` per engine: Kensei K2-125 0.006, Kensei K2-250 0.011, Ryujin RX-450F 0.020, Kensei ZR-1000 0.045, Liberty Glide Bagger 0.20, Hauler HD 1.10 with converter impeller. `TorqueCurve` rows {rpm, WOT_Nm, Closed_Nm} every 500 rpm use monotone cubic (Fritsch-Carlson) interpolation, never overshooting; part throttle blends T = Closed + a × (WOT − Closed), with airflow a from a butterfly map (30% throttle gives 60% airflow at low rpm). Throttle-body lag is first-order: τ 35 ms injected, 70 ms carbureted. An idle PI controller holds `Idle_rpm` (2T dirt 1,700, 4T dirt 1,900, street fours 1,200, V-twins 950, CVT quads/UTV 1,250, diesels 700; stat blocks win) with up to 12% airflow. `ConfigValidator`: peak T × ω of the warm table matches `PeakPower_kW` (canon hp × 0.7457) within 3%.

[89] `Limiter` modes: `"Hard"` (race 4T singles and sport fours, e.g. Ryujin RX-250F 13,500, Ryujin RS-600 16,000) cuts combustion above the limit and restores it 150 rpm lower, a 15-25 Hz bounce firing `Limiter` per cut; `"Soft"` (street twins, cruisers, diesels) retards over the last 300 rpm to a governed ceiling; `"None"` (most 2Ts) lets the table fall off past the powerband to zero net torque by its last row. Engine braking is the negative `Closed_Nm` column: four-strokes reach about −0.25 × peak torque at redline (big singles and V-twins −0.30), two-strokes about 40% of that because they lack intake-stroke pumping loss, and diesels add exhaust-brake torque in tow mode. It passes through the clutch like drive torque, so rear hop on aggressive downshifts emerges naturally; the Austral SM-700 slipper clutch caps back-torque at 35% of forward capacity.

[90] Stall: crank speed below 0.55 × `Idle_rpm` with clutch engagement above 0.6 in gear stops combustion, sets `EngineRunning` false and fires `Stall`; only compression drag remains. Auto-clutch opens at idle + 300 rpm, so stalls need the manual clutch or Sim Mode. Tapping throttle while stalled requests a restart. `StartType = "Electric"` engines (canon Ryujin Trail 230 and Austral EX-300, plus Rascal 110, Ryujin RX-250F, RX-450F, street bikes, quads, UTV, haulers, Frostline 850, Wavecat 300) crank 0.6-1.2 s in neutral or with the clutch pulled. Kick-only engines (Kensei K2-125, Kensei K2-250, Trialtek TR-300) fire `KickStart` for RiderAnimationController ([231]-[250]) per kick: success 0.70 warm, 0.40 cold, certain on the third, but 0.15 while a carbureted 2T is flooded (over 3 s of held throttle while stalled, cleared by 5 s closed). The Gopher 200 fires `PullStart` with the same odds.

[91] `Clutch` maps lever input l (0 released, 1 pulled) to engagement e = smoothstep((0.65 − l) / 0.30), a 35-65% friction zone that LeftShift, LB or auto-clutch can feather. Capacity T_cap = e × μ(T_clutch) × `Capacity_Nm` (1.4 × peak torque × primary). Below 5 rad/s of slip it locks while the torque needed to stay locked is under T_cap; otherwise it transmits T_cap × sign(slip). Slip power P = T × |Δω| heats a 1,500 J/K pack cooled at 6 W/K; above 250 °C μ drops 30% (`ClutchFade`), recovering below 180 °C. A dump releases from high rpm in under 80 ms, so flywheel energy ½Jω² spikes wheel torque. Acceptance test: a Kensei K2-250 dumped at 9,000 rpm in first, weight neutral, lifts its front past 30° within 0.7 s, and 30 s of slipping fades the clutch.

[92] `Gearbox` stores `Ratios_x` (1st first), `Primary_x` and `FinalDrive_x` from sprocket teeth; overall = Primary × Ratio[g] × Final. Kensei K2-250 reference: primary 3.15, ratios 2.07, 1.63, 1.31, 1.05, 0.85, final 50/13, rear radius 0.34 m, so 125 km/h is near 10,000 rpm in 5th. Each shift removes drive for `ShiftTime_s` (dirt 0.09, street 0.12, cruisers 0.16). The pattern is 1-N-2-3-4-5(-6): neutral comes only from an upshift in 1st below 8 km/h, and Rattler 450R and 700 take reverse by holding downshift 0.5 s in neutral, stopped. Outside Sim Mode every shift succeeds. In Sim Mode, clutchless upshifts without a quickshifter need throttle under 15% for 50 ms, clutchless downshifts need rpm within ±1,200 of the post-shift value, and a lazy loaded 1-2 finds false neutral 8% of the time; failures fire `MissedShift` and keep the gear (false neutral: N).

[93] The Rascal 110 is semi-automatic: a centrifugal clutch engages from 2,300 to 3,800 rpm, clutch input is ignored, and the rotary 4-speed N-1-2-3-4 returns from 4th to N only when stopped. Auto-shift (player assist, default on mobile, always on hauler automatics) upshifts above lerp(PeakTorque_rpm, 0.95 × Redline_rpm, throttle), downshifts when the lower gear lands under 0.90 × redline and rpm is below lerp(Idle + 800, 0.7 × PeakTorque_rpm, throttle), kicks down on a 50% throttle jump within 200 ms, holds gear airborne or above 35° lean, and waits 400 ms between shifts; two-strokes use powerband edges. Quickshifters (canon Kensei Street 900, Ryujin RS-600, Kensei ZR-1000; others per stat block) cut ignition on upshifts above 30% throttle and 5,000 rpm, 80 ms at 6,000 rpm to 45 ms at 14,000 ([200]). Autoblip (ZR-1000, listed bikes) blips 90-140 ms and fires `Blip`.

[94] `Cvt` serves the Corsa Zip 50, Corsa Riva 150, Rattler Trail 90, Ranchhand 570, Dunecat XR Turbo, Frostline 850 and torque-converter Gopher 200. A centrifugal clutch engages at `Engage_rpm` (Gopher 2,100, scooters 3,200, quads and UTV 1,600, Frostline 4,000) and locks 900 rpm higher. The variator moves between `RatioLow_x` and `RatioHigh_x` (Riva 150: 2.6 to 0.85, then an 8.6 final) to hold engine speed at lerp(Engage + 600, PeakPower_rpm, throttle) plus up to 800 rpm as wheel load nears the ratio's maximum wheel torque (torque-sensing secondary), so climbs and towing backshift. Ratio slews at most 1.2 per second; below engagement the belt freewheels, so engine braking is minimal. Ranchhand 570 and Dunecat XR Turbo add an H/L/N/R range box (L = 1.65 × H, reverse ≤ 15 km/h) shifted while stopped, and the ride-mode input toggles 2WD/4WD for [70].

[95] Two-stroke character is expansion-chamber resonance: T = T_base(1 + A·exp(−((rpm − rpm_res)/w)²)), rpm_res at the powerband centre, w = 0.35 × band width (Kensei K2-125 9,500-12,500 rpm, A 0.55; Kensei K2-250 7,000-10,500, A 0.45; Frostline 850 0.30; Austral EX-300 and Trialtek TR-300 0.15). `TorqueCurve` holds the warm stat-block curve; dividing out the warm term at bind gives T_base, so stock engines pass [88] while pipe heat and [373] pipes move the bump. rpm_res ∝ sqrt(T_pipe), and pipe gas heats from 750 K to 900 K over 60 s, so cold bikes hit 9% lower. Crossing the band's lower edge fires `PowerbandHit`. Premix: Kensei 2Ts 32:1, Trialtek TR-300 60:1; Austral EX-300, Frostline 850 and Corsa Zip 50 are oil-injected. Assists on, Pumpline auto-selects premix ($2.00/L); in Sim Mode a wrong fill seizes the engine after 2 km above 70% throttle ([231]-[250]).

[96] Dunecat XR Turbo boost b rises from 3,800 rpm to a wastegated 1.3 bar by 6,500 rpm at full throttle, spool τ 0.55 s at 4,500 rpm, 0.20 s at 7,500; torque blends `TorqueCurve` into `BoostCurve_Nm` by b/b_max, and lifting above 0.4 bar fires `BlowOff`. The Wavecat 300's gear-driven supercharger is lag-free, b = 0.9 (rpm / 8,000)²; its impeller absorbs 3.8e-4 ω² N m and gives [72] thrust 7,500 N × (rpm / 8,000)² × (1 − v / 38 m/s), governed at 108 km/h. Haulers use a torque converter (stall ratio 2.0, lockup above 45 km/h). Hauler HD: 1,420 Nm flat 1,600-2,200 rpm, 10-speed 4.70, 2.99, 2.15, 1.77, 1.52, 1.28, 1.00, 0.85, 0.69, 0.64, axle 3.55, 4-Lo 2.64. Hauler Van: 7-speed 4.38, 2.86, 1.92, 1.37, 1.00, 0.82, 0.73, axle 3.92. Towing on `Hitch` raises shift points 15%.

[97] Fuel burn is load-based: mass flow = max(BSFC(rpm, load) × P_brake, idle flow), best BSFC 4T 270 g/kWh, 2T 420, diesel 215; idle 0.15 L/h per 100 cc, diesels 0.3 L/h per liter; 0.745 kg/L gasoline, 0.832 diesel. `Tank_L` examples: Kensei K2-250 7.5, Ryujin Gremlin 125 6.0, Kensei Hayate 1300 20, Hauler HD 129. The server owns `Fuel_L` like `Charge_Wh`; the HUD shows L/100 km or MPG. Targets: Gremlin 1.8-2.4 L/100 km, Hayate 5.5-7.0 cruising, Hauler HD 14-17 empty and 24-30 towing, K2-250 4-5 L/h of motocross. Pumpline `GasPump`s bill $2.00 per liter at 38 L/min × 10 ([362]). Engine temperature reuses [84], heat = 30% of fuel power (43 MJ/kg): liquid-cooled engines warn at 110 °C and derate linearly to 70% at 120 °C, air-cooled heads derate above 230 °C, and engines under 50 °C lose 8% torque.

[98] `RiderAids` runs the factory electronics listed in each vehicle's `Assists` group, writing only through the [56] `TorqueLimit` hook and brake channel. Traction control levels 1-6 target rear slip from 0.30 down to 0.06, trimming torque every sub-step via ignition cut or motor current. Wheelie control levels 1-3 hold pitch under 22°, 14° or 8°. Ryujin RX-450F launch control modes 1-3 pin 9,500, 8,500 or 8,000 rpm at open throttle, softening ignition until 2nd gear. ABS releases brake force above 0.15 slip and reapplies at 10 Hz; cornering ABS caps it to the friction-circle share left at the current lean ([58]). Engine-brake control levels 1-3 crack the throttle on overrun, cutting engine braking 25-75%. Player assists ([113]-[115]) reuse these modules on every vehicle; Sim Mode switches them off, but factory electronics are vehicle equipment and keep their vehicle-menu level, Off included.

[99] `PedalDrive` treats the 75 kg rider as a critical-power engine: CP 250 W sustained, 900 W sprint and anaerobic pool W' = 6,500 J, drained above CP, refilled at 0.4(CP − P) below; empty, output caps at CP. Power peaks at 90 rpm cadence, times max(0.15, 1 − ((cadence − 90)/70)²); crank torque = min(P/ω_crank, 150 Nm) keeps standing starts finite; cadence = wheel rpm × rear/front teeth. Gearing: Kurb Street BMX 25/9; Ridgeline Fixie 48/16 fixed (pedals turn with the wheel, back-pedal braking to 0.25 g); Ridgeline Apex Road 50/34 × 11-30 12-speed; Ridgeline Gravity DH 36 × 11-25 7-speed; other single speeds per stat block. Freewheels coast when the wheel outruns the pedals. Acceptance test: the Apex Road holds 34-38 km/h at 250 W on the flat; the BMX spins out near 38 km/h at 140 rpm.

[100] Remote clients hear powertrains through the [36] `Vehicle_State` UnreliableRemoteEvent with no added bytes: the owner writes engine or motor rpm (cadence for pedal bikes), throttle, gear, clutch and limiter flags and the ride-mode index at up to 15 Hz. Locally, `Powertrain.Events` (`Signal`s) fires `Shift`, `MissedShift`, `Limiter`, `Stall`, `Start`, `KickStart`, `PullStart`, `Backfire`, `BlowOff`, `Blip`, `PowerbandHit`, `ClutchFade` and `Cutoff`; on gas engines a throttle drop from above 0.7 to below 0.1 above 60% of redline fires `Backfire` at 25% probability (`BlowOff` on the Dunecat). Remote clients run `PowertrainReplica`, interpolating rpm over 100 ms and deriving events from consecutive packets: a gear change is `Shift`, a limiter-flag rising edge `Limiter`, gas rpm falling to 0 `Stall`, plus the same backfire rule. AudioController consumes both streams ([251]-[270]). Acceptance test: at 30 players, remote audio follows a shift within 150 ms of packet arrival.

## SECTION 5 - RIDER CONTROLS, INPUT & CAMERA (Paragraphs 101-125)

[101] You will make `Client/Controllers/InputController` the only module that binds riding input; no other module calls `ContextActionService` or reads `UserInputService` events for riding. `InputController:GetCommand(dt)` builds a typed `RiderCommand` at most once per frame: `Throttle` 0..1, `FrontBrake` 0..1, `RearBrake` 0..1, `Steer` -1..1, `WeightShift` -1 (back) to 1 (forward) per [65], and `Clutch` 0..1. Discrete actions fire `Signal`s: `ShiftUp`, `ShiftDown`, `EnterExit`, `Reset`, `Bail`, `Horn`, `Lights`, `RideMode`, `CameraCycle`, `LookBack`, `Map`, `Phone`, `PhotoMode`. Actions belong to contexts `OnFoot`, `Riding`, `Menu`, `PhotoMode` and `Global`; switching context rebinds through `ContextActionService:BindActionAtPriority` at `Enum.ContextActionPriority.High.Value + 10`, returning `Enum.ContextActionResult.Sink` for consumed input. While riding, disable walking with `require(PlayerScripts.PlayerModule):GetControls():Disable()`, re-enabling it on exit. If the Input Action System (`InputContext`, `InputAction`, `InputBinding`) exists in the build, you may implement this interface on it without changing the public API.

[102] Add `Shared/Config/Controls.luau` to the [23] tree with the canon PC defaults: W throttle, S rear brake, Space front brake, A/D steer and lean, Mouse-Y or Up/Down arrows weight shift, LeftShift clutch (hold), E upshift, Q downshift, F enter/exit, R reset (hold 1.5 s, 15 s cooldown), C cycle camera, V look back, H horn, L lights, T cycle ride mode, B bail, M map, Tab phone, P photo mode. Up arrow or pushing the mouse forward moves weight forward; an Invert option flips it. Tell the user to set `StarterPlayer.EnableMouseLockOption` to false in Studio so Shift Lock never takes LeftShift, and call `StarterGui:SetCoreGuiEnabled(Enum.CoreGuiType.PlayerList, false)` so Tab opens the phone, whose Players app replaces the list. Acceptance test: every listed key triggers its action within one frame wherever it applies (R starts its hold ring).

[103] Gamepad defaults (Xbox labels): RT throttle (`ButtonR2`), LT front brake (`ButtonL2`), A rear brake, LB clutch (`ButtonL1`), B upshift, X downshift, Y enter/exit, left stick steer/lean (`Thumbstick1`), right stick Y weight shift and X camera orbit (`Thumbstick2`), R3 look back, L3 horn, D-pad Up lights, Down camera, Left/Right ride mode, RB held 1.5 s to reset, View phone (`ButtonSelect`), Menu pause (`ButtonStart`). Read sticks from `InputObject.Position.X/Y` and triggers from `Position.Z` (0..1). Set `GuiService.AutoSelectGuiEnabled = false` to free View. Roblox reserves Menu for its system menu, so pause means throttle 0 and 30% linked brakes from `GuiService.MenuOpened` until `GuiService.MenuClosed`. Canon gives the gamepad no bail key: default bail to holding Y for 0.6 s above 10 km/h, while a tap at or below 10 km/h exits. PlayStation glyphs follow [106].

[104] Mobile riding controls live in a `ScreenGui` with `ScreenInsets = Enum.ScreenInsets.DeviceSafeInsets`, shown while riding on Touch, with `GuiService.TouchControlsEnabled = false` hiding the default thumbstick and jump button. Sizes assume a 390 px-tall landscape phone, scaling with screen height 0.85-1.6x. Left thumb zone (left 40%, lower 60%): a floating steer stick (150 px base, 60 px knob) beside a vertical weight-shift slider (60 x 200 px) returning to center at 4 per second, so only deliberate swipes pop ([65]). Right zone: an analog throttle slider (96 x 200 px, snaps to 0, 8% bottom dead band), front brake (88 px) beside it, rear brake (72 px) above. Enter/exit, camera and phone: 56 px buttons, upper right. Targets stay at least 44 x 44 px `AbsoluteSize` with 12 px gaps; opacity 45% idle, 85% pressed, adjustable 20-100%; Left-Handed mirrors X.

[105] Track each finger with `UserInputService.TouchStarted`, `TouchMoved` and `TouchEnded` keyed by its `InputObject`, so steer, throttle and brakes work simultaneously; hit-test new touches against your own control rectangles, accepting them although `gameProcessedEvent` is true over your frames. Never use `ContextActionService` auto-created touch buttons for driving. Tilt-steer is optional, offered only when `UserInputService.AccelerometerEnabled` is true. Set `PlayerGui.ScreenOrientation = Enum.ScreenOrientation.LandscapeSensor` and flip the sign when `PlayerGui.CurrentScreenOrientation` switches LandscapeLeft/LandscapeRight. Roll is the signed screen-plane angle between `UserInputService:GetDeviceGravity().Position` and the calibrated neutral vector: 2 degree deadzone, 28 degrees for full Steer, gamma 1.3, 30 ms low-pass. Calibrate averages gravity over 0.5 s. A layout editor drags and resizes each control 75-150% and stores normalized positions; Stick-Y Weight Shift lets the steer stick's vertical axis replace the slider. Acceptance test: on a 6-inch phone no control ever triggers its neighbor.

[106] Hints and glyphs follow the last-used device. Watch `UserInputService.PreferredInput` with `GetPropertyChangedSignal`, falling back to `UserInputService:GetLastInputType()` and `LastInputTypeChanged` mapped to the same three `Enum.PreferredInput` values (KeyboardAndMouse, Gamepad, Touch). Debounce switches by 0.3 s and ignore mouse movement under 4 px and stick motion inside the deadzone, so a resting controller never flickers the HUD. Keyboard labels use `UserInputService:GetStringForKeyCode` (an AZERTY player sees Z for `KeyCode.W`); gamepad glyphs use `UserInputService:GetImageForKeyCode`, which returns Xbox or PlayStation art for the connected pad, with a text fallback. `ProximityPrompt` updates its own key art. For 10-foot viewing (gamepad active or `GuiService:IsTenFootInterface()` true), glyphs are at least 48 px and hint text at least 24 px at 1080p, all inside a 5% title-safe margin. Acceptance test: alternating keyboard, gamepad and touch on one device updates every hint within 0.3 s.

[107] Settings > Controls has tabs Keyboard & Mouse, Gamepad and Touch Layout, with Primary and Secondary slots per action. Capture listens to `UserInputService.InputBegan` and `InputChanged`, takes the first key, mouse button or axis past 0.5, times out after 5 s, and cancels on Escape, `GuiService.MenuOpened` or a 1 s hold of B (a B tap still binds B). Throttle and brakes accept an axis or a key; Steer and WeightShift accept an axis or a negative/positive key pair. Two actions conflict when they share an input and their contexts overlap: `Global` overlaps everything, while `OnFoot` and `Riding` do not, so F can enter on foot and exit while riding. Conflicts offer Swap (default), Replace or Cancel. Escape, Slash (chat), F9, F11 and `ButtonStart` are unbindable. Save is disabled while Throttle, either brake, Steer or EnterExit is unbound.

[108] Bindings are saved in the player's ProfileStore profile under `Settings.Controls`, with a version field and migrations per [451]-[475]. `SettingsController` sends changes over a new `Settings_Save` RemoteEvent in `NetDefs`, at most once per 2 s. The server checks every field before writing: action names must be in the `Config/Controls` allow-list, input names must be in a set built once from `Enum.KeyCode:GetEnumItems()` plus the mouse button and movement `Enum.UserInputType` items, each action has at most two bindings, numbers are clamped to their slider ranges, and the payload stays under 4 KB. An invalid field reverts to its default and never throws. On join, `InputController:Start()` binds the defaults right away and rebinds when the settings snapshot arrives through a `Signal`, so `Init` never waits. Acceptance test: Throttle rebound to Up arrow still works after rejoining on another PC.

[109] Analog conditioning runs in a fixed order: deadzone, rescale, response curve, sensitivity. The left stick uses a radial deadzone on the 2D vector (inner 0.12, outer 0.95), rescaled so output starts at 0 past the inner ring and reaches exactly 1.0 at the outer ring; steering takes `Thumbstick1` X only after the radial pass, so diagonals are never squared off. Triggers use an axial deadzone (inner 0.05, outer 0.97). The response curve is out = sign(x)*abs(x)^gamma, with defaults steer 1.6, throttle 1.3, brakes 1.2 and weight shift 1.0, each adjustable 1.0-2.5; deadzones adjust 0.05-0.30. The right stick's split axes follow [121]. Acceptance test: a raw deflection of 0.50 rescales to 0.458 and yields steer 0.287 at defaults, shown in a developer-only debug overlay.

[110] Digital inputs (keys, gamepad buttons such as A and LB, mobile brake buttons) never jump from 0 to 1: a rate limiter moves each command toward its target by at most dt/rise while magnitude grows and dt/fall while it shrinks; analog axes skip it. Full-swing rise/fall: throttle 0.22/0.10 s; rear brake 0.15/0.08 s; front brake 0.25/0.08 s (no tap locks the front). Steer rises in 0.18 s at rest, lengthening linearly to 0.40 s at 120 km/h, and returns in 0.12 s (0.06 s when the opposite key is pressed). Arrow weight shift: 0.30/0.25 s (4 per second, below the [65] pop); pressing the opposite arrow crosses at 8 per second, the keyboard pop. Clutch: 0.08/0.20 s, crossing the [91] friction zone in 60 ms, a deliberate dump. `WindowFocusReleased`, `TextBoxFocused` and context switches zero all held digital inputs.

[111] Analog smoothing is a one-pole low-pass, time constant at most 16 ms, and total smoothing delay in the input path stays under 50 ms ([110] ramps excluded). Input events update raw state on arrival, and `VehicleController` calls `GetCommand(dt)` at the start of its `PreSimulation` step ([51]), so commands are never a frame stale. Mouse weight shift: while riding, set `UserInputService.MouseBehavior = Enum.MouseBehavior.LockCenter` (re-asserted every `PreRender`; the Roblox menu and focus changes can release it) and `MouseIconEnabled = false`; each pixel of vertical `InputObject.Delta` changes WeightShift by 0.006 times sensitivity, clamped to ±1, and Spring Return (default) decays it toward 0 at 1.5 per second after 0.2 s without motion. The phone, map and menus free the cursor. Acceptance test: `os.clock()` stamps from `InputBegan` to the first throttle impulse show at most one frame plus ramp time.

[112] Steering adapts to speed without removing authority. Physics converts the conditioned `Steer` into steer angle and lean target with its own speed limits ([61]-[62]), so full input always requests full lean or full lock. This layer only softens the stick center as speed rises: gamma_eff = SteerGamma + 0.8 * S * clamp((v - 20) / 130, 0, 1), with v in km/h and S from a High-Speed Softness slider 0-2 (default 1). Below 20 km/h base gamma keeps U-turns precise; keyboard steering adds the speed-lengthened rise of [110]. Sim Mode keeps this curve, since it maps input. Acceptance test: at defaults a post-deadzone deflection of 0.20 yields Steer 0.076 at 20 km/h and 0.021 at 160 km/h, while full deflection yields 1.0 (the maximum lean request) at both.

[113] Player assists configure the [98] `RiderAids` modules and [90]-[93] clutch and gearbox logic, adding no physics; inapplicable ones grey out (auto-clutch on CVT and electric drives, TC on pedal bikes). Auto-clutch feeds the [91] lever max(manual Clutch, auto demand), keeping clutch-up wheelies; auto-shift uses [93] shift points, kickdown and airborne/lean holds plus a 400 ms lockout after manual shifts. Steering assist (default on) trims Steer once front slip angle passes 90% of the compound's peak ([57], [60]) and damps wheel flop below 10 km/h ([63]). Balance assist 0-100% drives [61] and [64]. Traction control Off/1-6 (default 3; targets +0.06 on loose ground), ABS On/Off/Off-Road (front only) and wheelie control Off/1-3 (default 1) reuse [98]. Defaults: PC and gamepad auto-clutch on, auto-shift off, balance 70%; mobile both on, balance 100%. Stronger of player and factory level wins; saved in `Settings.Assists`.

[114] The Rider Coach overlay (HUD toggle, default off) shows throttle, brake and clutch bars, steer and weight-shift markers, lean angle, front and rear slip-versus-peak meters ([57]-[58]) and a flash whenever traction control, ABS or wheelie control intervenes. Instant Replay: `VehicleController` keeps a 30 s client ring `buffer` at 20 Hz for the local vehicle and up to 7 replicated ones within 150 studs (position 3 x f32, quaternion 4 x int16, RPM, throttle, steer, lean, gear, two rider blend weights: 28 bytes per frame, about 135 KB). Opened from the [240] crash banner or phone Camera app below 2 km/h outside races, it holds the real vehicle as [123] does, poses anchored clones through the remote `VehicleVisuals` and [231] blend-track paths, replays engine audio from recorded RPM, and offers chase, `SpectatorCam` and free cameras, 0.25x-1x speed and scrubbing.

[115] Assist presets sit above the [113] toggles: Beginner (auto-clutch and auto-shift on, balance 100%, TC 4, ABS On, wheelie control 3, steering assist on), Rider (the [113] defaults, given to new profiles), Pro (auto-clutch only, balance 30%, TC 2, ABS Off-Road, wheelie control and steering assist off) and Sim; any edit shows Custom. Sim Mode turns every player assist off: gas bikes can stall, miss shifts and need restarts ([90]-[92]), factory electronics keep their vehicle-menu level ([98]), and input conditioning ([109]-[112]) stays because it maps devices, not skill. Presets and Sim Mode change only below 2 km/h outside an active race; lobbies record Sim Mode for Sim-only events and boards ([391]-[415]). The HUD shows a preset or SIM badge plus active-assist strip. Ride mode (T, D-pad Left/Right) cycles config modes only below 0.1 throttle ([76]-[100]).

[116] Enter flow: `VehicleService` parents a `ProximityPrompt` to each `SeatAttachment` (`ActionText` "Ride", `ObjectText` the display name, `KeyboardKeyCode` F, `GamepadKeyCode` `ButtonY`, `HoldDuration` 0, `MaxActivationDistance` 8 studs, `RequiresLineOfSight` false, `Exclusivity` OnePerButton); passenger-capable vehicles add "Ride Passenger" on `PassengerSeatAttachment`. The prompt is the on-foot EnterExit binding: `InputController` binds nothing to that key in `OnFoot` and rewrites both key codes locally after a rebind. Clients hide prompts on vehicles they may not use, and all vehicle prompts while riding; the client `Triggered` handler fires `Vehicle_Enter` ([50]), and the server ignores the server-side event. Beyond [50], the server checks `OwnerUserId` (or passenger permission), speed under 2 km/h, a living, non-ragdolled Humanoid and 1 s since the last enter, then creates the `HumanoidRootPart`-to-`SeatAttachment` `Weld` ([227], [232]), makes character parts `Massless` and sets `Humanoid.PlatformStand` true until exit. Acceptance test: nobody without permission rides a locked bike.

[117] Exit (F, Y tap, mobile button) works at or below 10 km/h; faster requests show "Slow down or bail". The server tries, in vehicle space: 3 studs outboard of `PegL` (the [50] default), then `PegR`, 5.5 studs behind, 5.5 ahead, then each 3 studs higher. A spot passes when a hip-height `workspace:Raycast` from the seat to it hits nothing, a downward `workspace:Blockcast` (2 x 1 x 1 studs, from 4 studs up, 12 long) finds non-water ground with normal Y at least 0.7, and `workspace:GetPartBoundsInBox` (the character's on-foot `GetExtentsSize()` from enter plus 0.2 studs, `OverlapParams.RespectCanCollide = true`, vehicle and rider excluded) is empty. Otherwise use the newest of 10 safe positions buffered each second while riding. Feet land 0.2 studs above the hit. Acceptance test: 500 scripted exits beside walls, containers, tunnels and the pier embed no rider.

[118] Reset (R or RB held `RESET_HOLD_S` = 1.5 s with a progress ring; mobile shows a Reset button when flipped or stuck) has a server-enforced 15 s `RESET_COOLDOWN_S` countdown. It is allowed under 8 km/h, when chassis up-vector Y stays below 0.3 for 1 s, or when throttle above 0.3 yields under 1 km/h for 3 s. On `Vehicle_Reset` the server reclaims ownership, issues the [75] teleport token, zeroes `AssemblyLinearVelocity` and `AssemblyAngularVelocity`, pivots the model upright at its yaw 3 studs higher (or to the last safe pose if that fails the [117] overlap test) and returns ownership 0.1 s later. Resets never repair damage ([231]-[250]); races reset to the last checkpoint ([391]-[415]). Bail (B, Y held 0.6 s above 10 km/h, or a 0.6 s mobile enter/exit long-press) raises the [74] `Bail` reason; re-entry locks for 2 s.

[119] `CameraController` owns `workspace.CurrentCamera` while riding: `CameraType` `Enum.CameraType.Scriptable`, updated in `RunService.PreRender` ([42]), setting `Camera.Focus` to the focus point each frame (it steers render detail); exit restores `Custom` with the `Humanoid` as `CameraSubject`. C and D-pad Down cycle Chase Far (4.2 m behind, 1.6 m up), Chase Near (2.6 m, 1.25 m), Helmet POV (rider `Head` 0.08 m forward, head and helmet hidden locally), Handlebar (0.15 m above `SteerPivot`, turning with the bars), Hood/Low (0.9 m above the hood on Hauler HD, Hauler Van and Dunecat XR Turbo; 0.6 m on quads and the snowmobile) and Cinematic/Drone (12 m out, 6 m up, orbiting 8 degrees per second, cutting angles every 8 s). Look Back (V or R3) is momentary Chase Near facing rearward. The last mode persists per vehicle class; values live in a new SI-unit `Shared/Config/Camera.luau`.

[120] Smoothing uses critically damped springs on scalars (focus X/Y/Z, yaw and pitch with errors wrapped to ±180 degrees, roll, distance, FOV), never raw `CFrame` components. For frame-rate independence use the exact closed form: y = x-target, j = (v+omega*y)*dt, e = exp(-omega*dt), then x = target+(y+j)*e and v = (v-omega*j)*e. Omega: Chase Far 8, Chase Near 10, Helmet POV 25 rad/s. Distance = base + grow*clamp(v/200 km/h, 0, 1), with grow 1.2 m Far and 0.6 m Near. FOV = BaseFOV + 15*smoothstep(20, 200, v in km/h), so the default 70 degree base reaches 85 at 200 km/h; the base slider spans 60-80. Roll = chassis lean times 0.25 Far, 0.35 Near, 0.6 Helmet (riders hold their heads nearer level than the bike) or 1.0 Handlebar, scaled by a 0-100% roll slider.

[121] Collision: each frame, `workspace:Spherecast` a 0.25 m (0.89 stud) sphere from 1.1 m above the seat toward the goal position; on a hit, sit 0.1 m short, snapping in instantly, easing out with omega 4. One reused `RaycastParams` (FilterType `Exclude`) lists the local vehicle, all characters and `CameraIgnore`-tagged foliage, chain-link and glass; Terrain stays included. With the camera within 0.8 m of the rider, fade the rider to `LocalTransparencyModifier` 0.7. Right mouse button held, mouse movement orbits instead of shifting weight: 0.25 degrees per pixel, pitch -15 to +40 degrees. Right stick X yaws up to 200 degrees/s, gamma 1.5; each right-stick axis has a 0.15 deadzone, 0.35 while the other exceeds 0.7, so hard weight shifts never orbit. Above 10 km/h, toggleable auto-recenter returns behind at up to 120 degrees per second after 1.5 s without orbiting.

[122] Helmet POV looks into curves: kappa = yawRate/max(v, 3 m/s), L = v*1.2 s, yaw offset = 0.5*L*kappa radians (chord angle to the point L ahead), clamped ±25 degrees, smoothed with omega 6, off below 10 km/h and while orbiting. Head bob: the eye is a critically damped spring (omega 12) chasing the seat, clamped to 0.08 m; fork dive still pitches the view through the chassis. Shake uses trauma 0-1: landings add clamp((vImpact-3)/7, 0, 1), vImpact = vertical touchdown speed, m/s; rough terrain holds at least 0.3*clamp(rmsSuspensionVelocity/1.5 m/s, 0, 1) over 0.5 s; trauma decays 1.5 per second. Rotation = trauma^2 * maxAngle (2 degrees chase, 3.5 POV) * `math.noise` at 18 Hz * the Camera Shake slider (0-100%, default 60%). Reduce Motion ([447], defaulting from `GuiService.ReducedMotionEnabled` read in pcall) zeroes shake, head bob and [120] FOV gain.

[123] Photo mode (P on PC; the phone's Camera app on gamepad and mobile) enables the `PhotoMode` context. Freeze is local-only: set `Archivable = true` on characters, clone every character and vehicle within 60 studs into a Folder under `workspace.CurrentCamera`, anchor the clones and hide originals with `LocalTransparencyModifier = 1`. The real vehicle gets throttle 0, 40% linked braking and full balance hold, so the server sees only legal motion. The free camera moves 4 m/s (3x boost) within 12 m of the rider, using the [121] Spherecast. Controls: FOV 10-110 degrees; roll ±45 degrees; camera-parented `DepthOfFieldEffect` (center-raycast autofocus or manual `FocusDistance`, `InFocusRadius`, `FarIntensity`); exposure -3 to +3 EV as an offset to the [324] Lighting mixer; a camera-parented `ColorCorrectionEffect` with filters Neutral, Warm, Cool, Mono, Film and Noir; Hide HUD; rider poses via `Animator:LoadAnimation` from 0-tolerant `Config/AssetIds`.

[124] Rumble uses `HapticService`: check `IsVibrationSupported` and `IsMotorSupported` for `Enum.VibrationMotor.Large`, `Small`, `LeftTrigger` and `RightTrigger`, then call `HapticService:SetMotor(pad, motor, value)`, `pad` being the last-used gamepad's `UserInputType` (usually `Gamepad1`). Engine: Small = 0.04 + 0.14 * (rpm / redline)^2 for gas engines, Large adds 0.10 at idle for V-twins and big singles, and electric motors buzz Small at 0.03 above 50% power. Rev limiter: Large pulses 0.45 at 8 Hz. Landings: Large = 0.25 + 0.75 * trauma ([122]) for 120-250 ms. Crash: 1.0 for 300 ms, fading linearly over 400 ms. ABS cycling pulses the front-brake trigger and traction-control cuts pulse the throttle trigger where supported. Update at 30 Hz, sending only changes above 0.02. Vibration Off/Low/Medium/High multiplies by 0, 0.4, 0.7 or 1.0. Stop all motors on exit, `GuiService.MenuOpened`, `UserInputService.WindowFocusReleased` and gamepad disconnect.

[125] Console navigation follows [448] (explicit `NextSelection*` links, `GuiService.SelectedObject` restored on close, LB/RB tabs, A confirm, B back), and the `Menu` context sinks all riding actions. Accessibility in Settings > Controls: Hold or Toggle for clutch and look back (`HoldToggle`, [447]); Throttle Latch (double-tap holds throttle as cruise; any brake cancels); Linked Brakes (one input, 70/30 front/rear); and One-Handed Mode, which forces auto-clutch, auto-shift and at least 60% balance assist. Gamepad: left stick Y throttle (up) and linked brake (down), X steer, LT/LB weight back/forward, L3 enter/exit (0.6 s hold bails), D-pad Up double-tap for horn (tap and hold keep lights and high beam, [199]); a right-hand variant mirrors this to the right side. Keyboard: WASD, S as linked brake, weight shift on 1 (back) and 2 (forward), unbound by default (Z/X stay indicators, [199]). Mobile: tilt steering plus a center-resting throttle/brake slider. Acceptance test: a tester completes a 2 km Old Town loop one-handed on each device.

## SECTION 6 - VEHICLE ROSTER I: PEDAL & ELECTRIC (Paragraphs 126-150)

[126] You will specify every vehicle in [126]-[205] with one stat block: one paragraph of semicolon-separated `Label: value` fields in this order: Name (VehicleId in backticks); Inspired by; Class; Price; Unlock (Rep level); Dealership; Mass (full battery or tank, no rider); Power/Torque (peak and rated kW; Nm marked motor, crank or wheel); Battery/Fuel; Top speed; 0-50 (seconds; 0-25 km/h when rated under 55 km/h); Travel; Wheels/Tires (size, outer diameter, [60] compound row); Wheelbase/Seat (mm, plus rake and trail); Handling; Model notes (L x W x H envelope in mm where given); Special. Top speed means motor or assist cutoff for electrics, standing-start sprint peak for pedal bikes. Tests run on flat dry Asphalt with the 75 kg rider of [47], full charge, default assists and the fastest ride mode: acceleration targets must land within 10%, top speeds within 3% ([68]).

[127] Map each stat onto `Shared/Config/Vehicles/<VehicleId>.luau` ([37]). Name fills `Identity.DisplayName`, `Brand` and `VehicleId`; Inspired by never reaches any game file, string or comment ([19]). Class fills `Identity.Class` (here `Pedal`, `LightElectric`, `ElectricMoto`); Price, Unlock and Dealership fill `Economy.Price` (FREE is 0), `UnlockLevel` and `Dealership` as a [38] ID (`ThrottleWorks`, `VoltTalonEV`). Mass fills `Mass.Mass_kg`; Power/Torque, Battery/Fuel, modes and regen fill the [76]-[87] `Powertrain` fields, plus `TopSpeed_kmh`, `DescentCap_kmh` (fastest plausible gravity-fed speed, read only by the [75] speed rule, never a limiter) and test-only `Target0to50_s` or `Target0to25_s`, declared in `Types`. Travel fills `Suspension.TravelF_mm`/`TravelR_mm`; diameters `Dimensions.WheelDiaF_mm`/`WheelDiaR_mm`; compounds `Tires.FrontCompound`/`RearCompound`; Wheelbase/Seat `Dimensions.Wheelbase_mm`/`SeatHeight_mm`; rake and trail `Chassis.Rake_deg`/`Trail_mm` (offset per [63]); envelope and bar width `Dimensions.Length_mm`/`Width_mm`/`Height_mm`/`BarWidth_mm`; passengers and cargo `Seats`. Handling is the playtest target for `Chassis`, `Suspension` and `Assists`; the rest of Model notes is the artist spec of [13] and [206]-[230].

[143] Volt Wasp X model, feel and sound. Envelope 1,880 x 780 x 1,040 mm: a bicycle-slim moto. A straight forged-aluminum backbone runs level from the steering head to a high flat seat; beneath it a vertical battery box (380 x 170 x 150 mm) drops in from above, the 140 mm-diameter motor bolts under it at peg height, and a finned controller case hides under the seat. Minimal plastics: flat battery side panels, a small front plate around a compact LED headlight, high front fender, rear fender with LED taillight. Left: 30/42 primary belt under a cover, then 11/48 420 chain (6.1:1 total); box-section swingarm, near-vertical linkage shock, inverted coil fork. Feel: silent launches, featherweight in the air, wallowing when pushed. Sounds per [259]: `PolePairs` 5 whine, belt hum at 30-tooth pass frequency, chain slap on landings.

[145] Talon Stryke MX model, feel and sound. Envelope 1,960 x 800 x 1,150 mm: a three-quarter-scale motocross bike. Plastic shrouds wrap the central battery into a tank shape flowing into a long flat MX seat reaching almost to the steering head, with side scoops over the battery cooling vents. The forged aluminum twin-spar frame shows below; the motor sits low at frame center behind a round left cover hiding a sealed oil-bath gear primary, then 428 chain. Box-section swingarm with snail adjusters and linkage shock; inverted air-oil fork; slim LED strip headlight in the front plate, fender-integrated taillight; finned controller case on the frame rails under the seat. Feel: firm race damping, strong regen bite. Sounds: helical gear whine sharper than the Wasp X belt hum, chain rattle, fork top-out clunk.

[149] Volt Tempest model, feel and sound. Envelope 2,190 x 830 x 1,260 mm, the size of a 250-class motocross bike. An aluminum perimeter frame splits around a tall battery under tank-shaped shrouds; where a cylinder would be, a finned round motor case (about 220 mm diameter) sits low and forward, driving a 520 chain. Full-length MX plastics: long flat seat, sharp side panels, high front fender, front plate holding a narrow vertically split LED headlight, rear fender with integrated taillight. The controller sits in a finned housing under the seat ahead of the shock. Long box-section swingarm with linkage shock; 48 mm inverted fork with disc guard. Feel: heavy at walking pace, glued at speed, launches needing forward weight. Sounds: deeper, louder motor whine than the smaller Volts, heavy chain drone, suspension clack on big landings ([251]-[270]).

## SECTION 7 - VEHICLE ROSTER II: GAS OFF-ROAD (Paragraphs 151-175)

[151] 2T versus 4T character must emerge from `GasEngine` data ([88]-[95]), never per-bike scripts. Powerband: 2Ts (`Kind` "Gas2T") ride the [95] pipe resonance: the Kensei K2-250 makes 55% of peak torque at 6,000 rpm, fires `PowerbandHit` entering its band and, with `Limiter` "None", fades through a steep over-rev that keeps top speed within 3% ([68]); 4T singles pull broad tables into a Hard or Soft limiter. Engine braking: 4Ts reach -0.25 x peak torque, 2Ts 40% of that ([89]), so 2Ts freewheel into corners and 4Ts settle the chassis. Sound ([256], [261]): 2Ts fire every revolution (rpm/60), ring-ding and spit; 4Ts fire every other revolution (rpm/120), thump and pop. `Fuel.FuelType` is `Gasoline`, `Premix` (`PremixRatio`) or `OilInjected` (`OilTank_L`); smoke follows [338]. `Engine.Maps` (`AirflowScale_x` fading to 1.0 by 70% throttle, `TorqueScale_x`, `ExtraLag_ms`) cycle on RideMode, replicated as the [36] mode index.

[152] These defaults hold unless a stat block overrides them; [380] sets tuning ranges. Sag: `SagTargetR_pct` 33 = 104-105 mm race sag on 315-317 mm MX shocks (30-40 mm static), `SagTargetF_pct` 30; enduro rear 35, trials 38, supermoto 30. `Tires.PressureF_kPa`/`PressureR_kPa`: MX 90 kPa (13 psi), enduro 83 (12 psi), range 83-97 (12-14 psi); trials 28-48. Each kPa below 90 adds 0.3% grip in the [60] Loose, Mud, Sand and Snow columns, costs 0.2% in Paved and Packed and adds 0.5% rolling resistance; above 90 the signs reverse. Under 76 kPa a [55] `Bottomed` above 9 m/s flats the tire ([244]); `Trials` radials are exempt but keep the 12 m/s rule. Clickers start 12 of 24 out. Shared: `SteerLock_deg` 40 (trials 45), `MaxLean_deg` 50, CdA 0.60 (supermoto 0.50), paved knobby Crr 0.025 ([68]), chain `Efficiency_x` 0.97, `AirPitchTorque_Nm` 25, `ShiftTime_s` 0.09.

[153] Name: Gopher 200 (`gopher_200`); Inspired by: Coleman CT200U minibike; Class: Minibike; Price: $2,200; Unlock: L2; Dealership: Dirt Depot; Mass: 59 kg; Power/Torque: 196 cc air-cooled OHV 4T single, carbureted, 6.5 hp (4.85 kW) at its 3,600 rpm governor; flat-topped curve, 13.5 Nm crank from 2,200 to 3,000 rpm, 12.9 Nm at 3,600; idle 1,800 rpm, inertia 0.015 kg m^2; torque-converter CVT engaging at 2,100 rpm, ratio 2.70 to 1.35, then 60/10 #35 chain (final 6.0, driveline efficiency 0.85); Battery/Fuel: `Gasoline`, 3.6 L, 1.0-1.6 L/h; Top speed: 40 km/h, governor at the top ratio; 0-25 km/h: 2.0 s; Travel: rigid, the tires are the springs (35 mm, 36/54 N/mm F/R, progression 1.2, sag 40%); Wheels/Tires: 19x7.00-8 balloons front and rear (483 mm OD), 69 kPa, `Touring`; Wheelbase/Seat: 990/610 mm, rake 24 deg, trail 60 mm; Handling: stable, slow-steering, skips over braking bumps; rear-only braking peaks near 0.35 g on Asphalt; Model notes: 1,420 x 740 x 860 mm, see [154]; Special: `StartType` "Pull"; `RiderStance` `Street`; 1 seat; no factory electronics.

[154] Gopher 200 model, feel and sound. Model a low, chunky minibike: 25 mm round-tube cradle frame, flat vinyl bench seat, exposed horizontal-shaft engine with recoil-starter housing and air-filter box, torque converter under a vented left cover, wide braced bar with thumb throttle, tube fenders, 8-inch split steel rims on turf-tread balloons; olive green, cream seat. Feel: its 35 mm of tire travel bottoms on any drop over 0.3 m, fast tire motion drives [122] camera trauma, drops over 4 m (9 m/s) flat the 69 kPa balloons ([152]), and the freewheeling converter gives almost no engine braking. Sound: a lawn-engine thud at 30 Hz on the governor (rpm/120), ±150 rpm hunting at light load, chain rattle. Special: Space and LT also apply the single rear brake (`BrakeTorqueR_Nm` 120). Acceptance test: 40-0 km/h in 17-21 m on dry Asphalt.

[155] Name: Rascal 110 (`rascal_110`); Inspired by: Kawasaki KLX110 / Honda CRF110F pit bike; Class: PitBike; Price: $3,500; Unlock: L4; Dealership: Dirt Depot; Mass: 76 kg; Power/Torque: 112 cc air-cooled SOHC 4T single, carbureted, 7 hp (5.22 kW) at 7,500 rpm; gentle hump, 8.0 Nm crank at 5,500 rpm, 6.65 Nm at 7,500, 5.8 Nm at the 8,100 rpm Soft limiter; idle 1,900 rpm, inertia 0.005 kg m^2; centrifugal clutch 2,300-3,800 rpm, rotary 4-speed 2.615/1.555/1.136/0.916, primary 4.055, final 41/14 (8,100 rpm in 4th = 65 km/h); Battery/Fuel: `Gasoline`, 3.8 L, 0.8-1.3 L/h; Top speed: 65 km/h on the limiter; 0-50: 5.2 s; Travel: 110 mm front 31 mm conventional fork, 110 mm rear single shock, coil, sag 33%; Wheels/Tires: 70/100-14 front (496 mm OD), 80/100-12 rear (465 mm OD), 97 kPa, `MXIntermediate`; Wheelbase/Seat: 1,090/665 mm, rake 25.5 deg, trail 70 mm, clearance 220 mm; Handling: forgiving and unstallable, darts through pump-track berms, bottoms on flat landings; Model notes: 1,615 x 745 x 950 mm, see [156]; Special: `StartType` "Electric" with kick backup; 110 mm drums front and rear; `RiderStance` `MX`; 1 seat; semi-automatic per [93].

[156] Rascal 110 model, feel and sound. Model a compact pit bike: steel backbone frame, high front fender, oval number plate, one-piece side panels, a finned cylinder canted forward, upswept muffler with heat shield on the right, wire-spoked 14/12 wheels on drum hubs; sky blue with white plastics. The adult rider fits via the [227] IK targets: knees above the tank, elbows wide, hips behind the pegs. Feel: slow-revving and unstallable, the Kids Track ([308]), pump-track and pit-race bike; clutch input is ignored, so wheelies come from a 1st-gear roll-on with weight back and the [65] pop. Sound: a soft putt-putt idle at 16 Hz rising to a buzzy 62 Hz drone at 7,500 rpm through a spark-arrestor muffler. Special: HUD gear N-1-2-3-4 ([93]). Acceptance test: a 1.2 m flat drop bottoms both ends (`Bottomed`); 0.5 m does not.

[157] Name: Ryujin Trail 230 (`ryujin_trail_230`); Inspired by: Honda CRF230F; Class: TrailBike; Price: $8,000; Unlock: L9; Dealership: Dirt Depot; Mass: 113 kg; Power/Torque: 223 cc air-cooled SOHC 4T single, carbureted, 18 hp (13.4 kW) at 8,000 rpm; flat, fat curve, 17.8 Nm crank at 6,000 rpm, above 15 Nm from 3,000 to 8,000, 13.5 Nm at the 8,800 rpm Soft limiter; inertia 0.009 kg m^2; 6-speed 2.769/1.882/1.400/1.130/0.960/0.840, primary 3.090, final 52/13 (8,800 rpm in 6th = 105 km/h); Battery/Fuel: `Gasoline`, 6.6 L, 1.5-2.5 L/h on trails; Top speed: 105 km/h on the limiter; 0-50: 3.4 s; Travel: 240 mm front 37 mm conventional fork, 240 mm rear linkage shock, sag 33% (80 mm), soft wheel rates 7.2/4.6 N/mm, progression 0.4; Wheels/Tires: 80/100-21 front (693 mm OD), 100/100-18 rear (657 mm OD), 96 kPa, `MXIntermediate`; Wheelbase/Seat: 1,380/880 mm, rake 27 deg, trail 108 mm, clearance 290 mm; Handling: plush, slow-steering and planted on singletrack, wallows on MX jumps; Model notes: 2,060 x 815 x 1,200 mm, see [158]; Special: `StartType` "Electric"; 240 mm front disc, 110 mm rear drum; `RiderStance` `MX`; 1 seat.

[158] Ryujin Trail 230 model, feel and sound. Model a tall trail bike: steel semi-double-cradle frame, a rounded tank flowing into small shrouds with no radiators, a fat finned cylinder, oval spark-arrestor muffler, rear drum with torque arm, black rims, blank number plate; Ryujin matte black with gold and the dragon-head monogram ([230]), never the inspired-by maker's red. Feel: a lazy, tractor-like pull for Pinecrest singletrack ([311]) and Red Mesa washes; full throttle lifts the front gently only in 1st; 2nd needs weight back or a clutch pop. Its soft springs bottom on flat landings or a cased 20 m double (`CasedLanding` above 7 m/s, [74]). Sound: a mellow 67 Hz thump at 8,000 rpm over a 16 Hz idle, with carburetor decel pops ([261]). Special: the gentlest manual-clutch trainer, rarely meeting the [90] stall condition.

[159] Name: Kensei K2-125 (`kensei_k2_125`); Inspired by: Yamaha YZ125; Class: Motocross; Price: $11,000; Unlock: L14; Dealership: Dirt Depot; Mass: 94 kg; Power/Torque: 125 cc liquid-cooled reed-valve 2T with exhaust power valve, 38 mm carburetor, 37 hp (27.6 kW) at 11,500 rpm; peaky curve, 11 Nm crank at 7,000, 24.5 Nm at 10,500, 22.9 at 11,500, 12.5 at 12,500, 8 at 13,000, zero by 13,500 (`Limiter` "None"); [95] band 9,500-12,500, A 0.55; inertia 0.006 kg m^2; 6-speed 2.385/1.933/1.643/1.444/1.263/1.136, primary 3.400, final 48/13 (12,500 rpm in 6th = 110 km/h); Battery/Fuel: `Premix` 32:1, 8.0 L, 3-4 L/h of motocross; Top speed: 110 km/h, the over-rev settling near 112; 0-50: 2.6 s; Travel: 300 mm front 48 mm spring fork, 315 mm rear linkage shock, sag 33% (104 mm), wheel rates 8.6/5.4 N/mm, progression 0.5; Wheels/Tires: 80/100-21 front (693 mm OD), 100/90-19 rear (663 mm OD), 90 kPa, `MXIntermediate`; Wheelbase/Seat: 1,445/970 mm, rake 26.3 deg, trail 117 mm, clearance 360 mm; Handling: featherweight and flickable, the quickest turner here; Model notes: 2,140 x 825 x 1,300 mm, see [160]; Special: `StartType` "Kick"; 270/245 mm discs; `RiderStance` `MX`; 1 seat.

[160] Kensei K2-125 model, feel and sound. Model the slimmest bike here: aluminium perimeter frame, narrow louvered shrouds over twin radiators, a fat expansion chamber bulging out right under the shroud and necking into a slim silencer, a visible power-valve cover, right-side kickstarter, flat seat; Kensei pearl white with violet graphics and the sword-guard ring ([230]), never the inspired-by maker's blue. Feel: it rotates fastest in the air (same 25 Nm air torque, least inertia), but is gutless below 9,000 rpm, so riders fan the clutch and carry corner speed to stay in the band. Sound: an angry 28 Hz ring-ding idle, a 158-208 Hz wail across the band (rpm/60), closed-throttle spitting, never 4T pops ([261]). Special: `Exhaust` health below 50 shows the chamber dent ([243]) and cuts resonance A by 0.10; auto-shift uses the band edges ([93]).

[161] Name: Trialtek TR-300 (`trialtek_tr_300`); Inspired by: GasGas TXT / Montesa trials bike; Class: Trials; Price: $13,000; Unlock: L18; Dealership: Dirt Depot; Mass: 70 kg; Power/Torque: 300 cc liquid-cooled 2T, carbureted, hydraulic clutch, about 25 hp (18.6 kW) at 7,000 rpm; low, fat curve, 33 Nm crank at 4,500 rpm, above 25 Nm from 2,000 to 7,000, Soft limiter 7,500; resonance A 0.15; 0.012 kg m^2 flywheel; ultra-low 6-speed 5.20/4.30/3.40/2.30/1.40/0.86, primary 2.95, final 42/10 (7,500 rpm: 14/17/21/32/52/85 km/h); Battery/Fuel: `Premix` 60:1, 2.6 L, 1.0-1.5 L/h; Top speed: 85 km/h; 0-50: 4.8 s, wheelie-limited; Travel: 175 mm front 39 mm fork, 170 mm rear linkage shock, sag 38%; Wheels/Tires: 2.75-21 front (663 mm OD) at 41 kPa, 4.00R18 tubeless radial rear (640 mm OD) at 31 kPa, `Trials`; Wheelbase/Seat: 1,320 mm, no seat (650 mm frame pad, pegs 340 mm), rake 23.5 deg, trail 80 mm; Handling: weightless, walking pace in 1st; Model notes: 2,010 x 830 x 1,140 mm, see [162]; Special: `StartType` "Kick"; `RiderStance` `Standing`, hips 0.15 m above MX standing; `Seats.WeightShift_m` 0.30; 185/150 mm discs; Soft map (`AirflowScale_x` 0.75, `ExtraLag_ms` 40), Hard stock; mechanics [163].

[162] Trialtek TR-300 model, feel and sound. Model a skeletal trials machine: twin-spar aluminium frame, foam pad instead of a seat, tank in the spars, short square swingarm, high wide bars, under-engine exhaust, squared radial rear; white with teal. Feel: a throttle crack lifts the front; the flywheel lugs at 1,500-2,500 rpm without stalling. Sound: a burbly 28 Hz idle, a sharp bap per clutch pop. Special, edges: three extra [52]-filtered rays per axle (forward, 45 deg down, 30 deg up; tire radius plus 0.05 stud) make faces steeper than 45 deg extra contacts: that wheel's [53]-[54] spring-damper along the face normal, [56] drive along the face at `Trials` grip, so the rear climbs walls; `Cylinder` hull parts on the full-compression axle points (tire radius minus 15 mm) replace the fork and tail boxes, a [48] exception.

[163] Trials mechanics (TR-300 only). Stance: the `Standing` rider never sits. Hops: a full forward-to-back weight snap fires the [65] pop, 75 kg x 0.30 m x |ds/dt| (about 375 N s at the 0.12 s smoothing limit); its cap rises from 60 to 420 N s, split 40/60 front/rear when both wheels touch, lifting the 145 kg bike and rider 0.30-0.45 m. Clutch pops: dumps from 4,000-5,500 rpm let the [91] flywheel spike loft the front onto obstacles. Pivot turns: below 5 km/h with one wheel airborne, steer yaws the bike about the grounded contact at up to 180 deg/s, the only scripted torque (rider body English). Track stands follow [64]. Acceptance test: 0.30 m hops, the [308] 1.6 m step in 3 of 5 tries, a 180 deg pivot under 1.2 s.

[164] Name: Kensei K2-250 (`kensei_k2_250`); Inspired by: Yamaha YZ250; Class: Motocross; Price: $15,000; Unlock: L18; Dealership: Dirt Depot; Mass: 103 kg; Power/Torque: 249 cc liquid-cooled power-valve 2T, 38 mm carburetor, 47 hp (35.0 kW) at 8,500 rpm; mid-range hit, 23 Nm crank at 4,000-6,000 rpm, 24 at 6,500, 41 at 7,500, 41.5 at 8,000, 39.4 at 8,500, 33 at 9,500, 25 at 10,000, 10 at 10,500, zero by 11,000 (`Limiter` "None"); [95] band 7,000-10,500, A 0.45; inertia 0.011 kg m^2; 5-speed per [92]: 2.07/1.63/1.31/1.05/0.85, primary 3.15, final 50/13; Battery/Fuel: `Premix` 32:1, 7.5 L, 4-5 L/h racing ([97]); Top speed: 125 km/h near 10,000 rpm in 5th, settling near 128; 0-50: 2.3 s, wheelie-limited; Travel: 300 mm front 48 mm spring fork, 317 mm rear linkage shock, sag 33% (105 mm), wheel rates 9.4/6.0 N/mm, progression 0.5; Wheels/Tires: 80/100-21 front (693 mm OD), 110/90-19 rear (681 mm OD), 90 kPa, `MXIntermediate`; Wheelbase/Seat: 1,475/970 mm, rake 26.4 deg, trail 116 mm, clearance 360 mm; Handling: flickable, loose rear on slick hard-pack; Model notes: 2,180 x 825 x 1,290 mm, see [165]; Special: `StartType` "Kick"; 270/245 mm discs; `RiderStance` `MX`; 1 seat.

[165] Kensei K2-250 model, feel and sound. Model it on the K2-125's frame and plastics with a fatter chamber, a wider cylinder, bigger radiators and a 110-section rear; black rims and deeper violet trim tell the Kensei 2Ts apart at 30 m. Feel: calm below 6,500 rpm, then the pipe hits like a switch, torque rising 70% by 7,500 rpm (`PowerbandHit`) and lofting the front in the first three gears unless weight is forward. Sound: a raspy 117-175 Hz bark across the band (rpm/60), a metallic 28 Hz ring-ding idle, a wailing over-rev dying past 10,500 rpm. Special: it runs the [91] clutch-dump test, and a cold pipe hits 9% lower in rpm ([95]). Acceptance test: a weight-neutral 2nd-gear roll-on keeps the front down below 6,500 rpm, then lifts past 15 deg within 0.5 s of the band.

[166] Name: Ryujin RX-250F (`ryujin_rx_250f`); Inspired by: Honda CRF250R; Class: Motocross; Price: $17,000; Unlock: L20; Dealership: Dirt Depot; Mass: 105 kg; Power/Torque: 249 cc liquid-cooled DOHC 4-valve 4T single, fuel-injected (46 mm throttle body), 44 hp (32.8 kW) at 11,500 rpm; rising, top-heavy curve, 20 Nm crank at 6,000, 28.5 Nm at 10,000, 27.2 at 11,500, 22 at the 13,500 rpm Hard limiter; inertia 0.010 kg m^2; 5-speed 2.071/1.714/1.444/1.240/1.080, primary 3.304, final 51/13 (13,500 rpm in 5th = 120 km/h); Battery/Fuel: `Gasoline`, 6.3 L, 3-4 L/h of motocross; Top speed: 120 km/h on the limiter; 0-50: 2.4 s; Travel: 310 mm front 49 mm spring fork, 317 mm rear linkage shock, sag 33% (105 mm), wheel rates 9.2/5.9 N/mm, progression 0.5; Wheels/Tires: 80/100-21 front (693 mm OD), 100/90-19 rear (663 mm OD), 90 kPa, `MXIntermediate`; Wheelbase/Seat: 1,485/965 mm, rake 27.1 deg, trail 116 mm, clearance 330 mm; Handling: precise and planted, holds ruts and berm lines, soft below 8,000 rpm; Model notes: 2,170 x 827 x 1,270 mm, see [167]; Special: `StartType` "Electric"; 260/240 mm discs; three maps; `RiderStance` `MX`; 1 seat.

[167] Ryujin RX-250F model, feel and sound. Model modern MX 4T proportions: aluminium twin-spar frame, shrouds with deep air scoops, one large radiator per side, a header wrapping into a slim upswept silencer high on the right, a tall flat seat running to the tank cap, the throttle body visible under the seat, an e-start button right and map pod left; Ryujin black and gold. Feel: racers hold it between 9,000 rpm and the limiter, set entries with strong engine braking (-0.25 x peak torque, [89]) and short-shift only in slick ruts. Sound: a tight 104-112 Hz shriek near the limiter (rpm/120), the 15-25 Hz limiter stutter of [89], intake honk, decel pops. Special: RideMode cycles Standard, Smooth (`AirflowScale_x` 0.80) and Aggressive (1.15); wide-open torque, and so peak power, is identical in all three ([151]).

[168] Name: Austral EX-300 (`austral_ex_300`); Inspired by: KTM 300 XC-W TPI; Class: Enduro; Price: $19,500; Unlock: L24; Dealership: Dirt Depot; Mass: 104 kg; Power/Torque: 293 cc liquid-cooled 2T with transfer-port fuel injection (35 ms lag), 51 hp (38.0 kW) at 7,750 rpm; broad, flat curve, 40 Nm crank at 3,000, 48 Nm from 4,000 to 7,000, 46.8 at 7,750, 42 at the 8,300 rpm ECU Soft limiter; resonance A 0.15; inertia 0.014 kg m^2; 6-speed 2.286/1.625/1.250/1.045/0.880/0.769, primary 2.769, final 52/14 (8,300 rpm in 6th = 135 km/h); Battery/Fuel: `OilInjected` (0.7 L oil tank), 9.0 L, 3.0-4.5 L/h of enduro; Top speed: 135 km/h; 0-50: 2.3 s; Travel: 300 mm front 48 mm open-cartridge fork, 310 mm rear linkless shock, sag 35% (108 mm), wheel rates 8.8/5.6 N/mm, progression 0.25; Wheels/Tires: 90/90-21 front (695 mm OD), 140/80-18 rear (681 mm OD), 83 kPa, `MXIntermediate`; Wheelbase/Seat: 1,482/960 mm, rake 26.5 deg, trail 108 mm, clearance 370 mm; Handling: calm in roots and rock, straight through braking bumps; Model notes: 2,180 x 820 x 1,280 mm, see [169]; Special: `StartType` "Electric"; 260/220 mm discs; two maps; `RiderStance` `MX`; 1 seat.

[169] Austral EX-300 model, feel and sound. Model a hard-enduro machine: exposed painted chromoly central-tube frame, slim tank shrouds, a deep-lug 18-inch rear, radiator guards, full skid plate, wrap-around hand guards, an enduro headlight mask carrying `Headlight`, a slim LED tail carrying `Taillight`, an oil filler under the seat and the chamber behind a pipe guard; Austral desert sand with charcoal and the southern-cross mark ([230]), never the inspired-by maker's orange. Feel: the hard-enduro tractor and [402] Enduro reference bike; it chugs up Pinecrest rock gardens at 2,500 rpm without stalling and lugs instead of hitting. Sound: a smoother, lower drone than the Kensei bikes (117 Hz at 7,000 rpm), with less ring-ding because injection meters idle fuel. Special: RideMode toggles Map 1 Standard and Map 2 Soft (`TorqueScale_x` 0.90, `ExtraLag_ms` 40) for wet roots.

[170] Name: Austral SM-700 (`austral_sm_700`); Inspired by: KTM 690 SMC R / Husqvarna 701 Supermoto; Class: Supermoto; Price: $21,000; Unlock: L26; Dealership: Dirt Depot; Mass: 157 kg; Power/Torque: 693 cc liquid-cooled 4T single, fuel-injected, 74 hp (55.2 kW) at 8,000 rpm; broad plateau, 60 Nm crank at 4,000, 74 Nm at 6,500, 65.9 at 8,000, 60 at the 8,500 rpm Hard limiter; inertia 0.030 kg m^2; 6-speed 2.500/1.750/1.300/1.091/0.957/0.856, primary 2.194, final 42/15, slipper clutch ([89]); Battery/Fuel: `Gasoline`, 13.5 L, 4.2-5.0 L/100 km; Top speed: 190 km/h on the limiter in 6th (supermoto CdA 0.50, [68]); 0-50: 2.1 s, wheelie-limited; Travel: 215 mm front 48 mm fork, 240 mm rear linkage shock, sag 30%, wheel rates 11.5/9.0 N/mm, progression 0.35; Wheels/Tires: 120/70-17 front (600 mm OD) at 214 kPa, 160/60-17 rear (624 mm OD) at 228 kPa, `StreetSport`; Wheelbase/Seat: 1,486/890 mm, rake 26.5 deg, trail 108 mm; Handling: light-steering hooligan, wheelies in the first three gears; Model notes: 2,160 x 850 x 1,200 mm, see [171]; Special: `StartType` "Electric"; 320/240 mm discs; cornering ABS with Supermoto mode, traction control 1-6, default 4 ([98]); two maps; `RiderStance` `Supermoto`; 2 seats.

[171] Austral SM-700 model, feel and sound. Model an enduro-derived silhouette on street wheels: chromoly trellis frame, tall slim bodywork, a small LED headlight mask carrying `Headlight`, mirrors, indicators on `IndicatorFL` to `IndicatorRR`, a plate holder with `Taillight` and `BrakeLight`, one large radiator, a stubby silencer under the right side panel (`Exhaust1` at its tip), black spoked 17-inch rims and a radial four-piston caliper on a petal-profile 320 mm disc; sand and charcoal. Feel: brutally torquey from 3,500 rpm, stoppies and slides on demand, at home on Harbor City asphalt and Canyon Road switchbacks, with a passenger at `PassengerSeatAttachment`. Sound: a deep hammering single at 42-71 Hz from 5,000 to 8,500 rpm, a gruff off-idle bark, overrun popping, slipper-clutch chatter on hard downshifts. Special: Street map `AirflowScale_x` 0.80, Sport stock; mechanics [172].

[172] Supermoto mechanics (SM-700). Backing it in: rear brake held, two downshifts on entry; the [89] slipper caps back-torque at 35% of forward capacity, so the rear neither hops nor locks but runs near peak longitudinal slip, shrinking its lateral share of the [58] friction circle until it steps out 10-30 deg. Supermoto ABS ([380] Off-Road) drops rear ABS only. Above 8 deg rear slip angle, the [61] controller derives R from front steer against the velocity vector, not chassis heading, keeping lean right mid-slide. Exits: traction control level 1-2 or Off allows 0.25-0.30 rear slip for power slides. The inside `LegOutL`/`LegOutR` extends on rear-brake entries above 35 km/h. Acceptance test: from 80 km/h into a 90 deg asphalt corner, rear brake plus two downshifts holds 12-25 deg rear slip for 0.8 s without `HighSide` at 50% balance assist.

[173] Name: Ryujin RX-450F (`ryujin_rx_450f`); Inspired by: Honda CRF450R / Yamaha YZ450F; Class: Motocross; Price: $22,000; Unlock: L28; Dealership: Dirt Depot; Mass: 111 kg; Power/Torque: 449 cc liquid-cooled 4T single, fuel-injected (46 mm throttle body), 59 hp (44.0 kW) at 9,500 rpm; thick, linear curve, 40 Nm crank at 4,000, 49.5 Nm at 7,500, 44.2 at 9,500, 36 at the 11,000 rpm Hard limiter; inertia 0.020 kg m^2; 5-speed 1.857/1.578/1.333/1.157/1.000, primary 2.739, final 49/13 (11,000 rpm in 5th = 135 km/h); Battery/Fuel: `Gasoline`, 6.3 L, 4-5 L/h of motocross; Top speed: 135 km/h on the limiter; 0-50: 2.1 s, wheelie-limited; Travel: 310 mm front 49 mm spring fork, 317 mm rear linkage shock, sag 33% (105 mm), wheel rates 9.8/6.3 N/mm, progression 0.55; Wheels/Tires: 80/100-21 front (693 mm OD), 120/80-19 rear (675 mm OD), 90 kPa, `MXIntermediate`; Wheelbase/Seat: 1,481/965 mm, rake 27.2 deg, trail 117 mm, clearance 330 mm; Handling: violent but controllable, stable at speed; Model notes: 2,180 x 827 x 1,270 mm, see [174]; Special: `StartType` "Electric"; 260/240 mm discs; launch control modes 1-3 ([98]); traction control Off/1/3/5; three maps; holeshot device; `RiderStance` `MX`; 1 seat.

[174] Ryujin RX-450F model, feel and sound. Model the flagship silhouette: the RX-250F frame scaled up, a fatter header into a larger silencer, bigger radiators, a 120-section rear, a three-button left-bar pod and a holeshot button on the fork leg; Ryujin black and gold. Feel: torque everywhere, lifting the front on careless throttle in the first three gears; above 55 km/h with weight back it skims the [303] whoops. Sound: a 62-79 Hz bark from 7,500 to 9,500 rpm and a 20 Hz ignition-cut stutter while launch control pins revs. Special: maps as on the RX-250F. Stopped, RideMode cycles Map 1-3 then Launch, arming the menu-selected launch mode and the holeshot device, which holds the fork at least 60 mm compressed until a compression past 80% travel. Launch disarms in 2nd ([98]) or after 15 s.

[175] Dirt Depot lists a beginner-to-pro ladder by unlock level, one teaches line per bike: Gopher 200 throttle and braking; Rascal 110 gears without a clutch; Ryujin Trail 230 the manual clutch; Kensei K2-125 staying on the pipe; Trialtek TR-300 or Kensei K2-250 precision or the hit; Ryujin RX-250F high-rpm 4T racing; Austral EX-300 enduro; Austral SM-700 pavement slides; Ryujin RX-450F power with electronics. The adjoining Throttle Park Academy turns each rung into a 60-90 s drill from an `AcademyStart` arch: clutch launch to 20 m, emergency stop from 50 km/h, cone figure-eight, 30 m wheelie, inside-rut berm exit ([304]), design-speed tabletop ([302]) and the [308] trials step. Drills score server-side from [75] samples as Bronze, Silver or Gold (one-time $300, $600, $1,200) and lend the stock bike to eligible non-owners; a clutch-drill Gold prompts one auto-clutch-off suggestion.

## SECTION 8 - VEHICLE ROSTER III: STREET, QUADS, UTV, SNOW, WATER & HAULERS (Paragraphs 176-205)

[176] Name: Corsa Zip 50 (`corsa_zip_50`); Inspired by: 50 cc moped; Class: Street; Price: $1,900; Unlock: L2; Dealership: Throttle Works; Mass: 82 kg; Power/Torque: 49 cc air-cooled reed-valve 2T single, 3.5 hp (2.6 kW) at 7,500 rpm, 3.4 Nm crank at 6,500 rpm, CVT engaging at 3,200 rpm ([94]); Battery/Fuel: 6.5 L tank plus 1.2 L injection-oil tank, 2.4-3.0 L/100 km; Top speed: 50 km/h, variator-restricted; 0-25 km/h: 3.6 s; Travel: 75 mm front telescopic fork, 65 mm rear single shock on the swinging engine unit; Wheels/Tires: 120/70-12 front (473 mm OD), 130/70-12 rear (487 mm OD), `Touring`; Wheelbase/Seat: 1,260/770 mm, rake 26 deg, trail 78 mm; Handling: scooter MaxLean 40 ([61]); the heavy unsprung engine unit patters over Old Town brick seams, and two-up it slows to about 30 km/h on an 8% grade; Model notes: 1,780 x 690 x 1,120 mm step-through plastic body, flat floorboard, round headlight in the apron, 190 mm front disc, 110 mm rear drum, under-seat helmet bay, rear rack; Special: 35 L top box (`Seats.Cargo_L` 35) takes Food Delivery bags and Parcel Courier parcels ([349], [351]); passenger seat ([204]).

[177] Name: Corsa Riva 150 (`corsa_riva_150`); Inspired by: Vespa Primavera 150 class; Class: Street; Price: $4,800; Unlock: L5; Dealership: Throttle Works; Mass: 130 kg; Power/Torque: 155 cc air-cooled 3-valve 4T single, 13 hp (9.7 kW) at 7,750 rpm, 12.8 Nm crank at 6,500 rpm, CVT 2.6 to 0.85 with an 8.6 final ([94]); Battery/Fuel: 8 L, 2.6-3.2 L/100 km; Top speed: 100 km/h; 0-50: 5.4 s; Travel: 80 mm front single-sided trailing link with one shock, 85 mm rear shock on the engine unit; Wheels/Tires: 110/70-12 front (459 mm OD), 120/70-12 rear (473 mm OD), `Touring`; Wheelbase/Seat: 1,334/790 mm, rake 27 deg, trail 88 mm; Handling: scooter MaxLean 40; the stiff steel body feels solid at 90 km/h, and the trailing link barely dives under braking but kicks sideways over potholes; Model notes: 1,860 x 735 x 1,140 mm pressed-steel monocoque body, curved leg shield, flat floor, engine and CVT hidden in a swollen right-hand side cowl, front wheel on a single-sided arm, handlebar-mounted headlight, 200 mm front disc; Special: single-channel front ABS ([98]); 16 L under-seat bay; fold-out pillion pegs; the Moto-Taxi workhorse, 100 km/h two-up ([204], [353]).

[178] Name: Ryujin Gremlin 125 (`ryujin_gremlin_125`); Inspired by: Honda Grom; Class: Street; Price: $5,500; Unlock: L6; Dealership: Throttle Works; Mass: 102 kg; Power/Torque: 124 cc air-cooled SOHC 4T single with a near-horizontal cylinder, 10 hp (7.5 kW) at 7,250 rpm, 10.5 Nm crank at 5,500 rpm, soft limiter 8,500 rpm; 5-speed 2.50/1.55/1.15/0.92/0.79, primary 3.35, final 34/15; Battery/Fuel: 6.0 L, 1.8-2.4 L/100 km ([97]); Top speed: 95 km/h; 0-50: 3.9 s; Travel: 90 mm front 31 mm inverted fork, 104 mm rear monoshock; Wheels/Tires: 120/70-12 front (473 mm OD), 130/70-12 rear (487 mm OD), `Touring`; Wheelbase/Seat: 1,200/761 mm, rake 25 deg, trail 81 mm; Handling: naked MaxLean 50, flicks instantly; full throttle in 1st gives only 0.43 g against the roughly 0.7 g a wheelie needs, so wheelies come from clutch-ups ([91]) or a weight-shift pop ([65]), which makes it the bikelife trainer; Model notes: 1,760 x 720 x 1,015 mm, steel backbone frame, short angular tank shrouds, exposed engine, stubby upswept muffler on the right, fat 12-inch cast wheels, LCD dash, 220/190 mm discs; Special: front ABS; passenger seat; accepted by every Wheelie challenge ([391]-[415]).

[179] Name: Liberty Bobber 1200 (`liberty_bobber_1200`); Inspired by: Harley Sportster / Triumph Bobber class; Class: Street; Price: $24,000; Unlock: L25; Dealership: Euro & Classic Motors; Mass: 250 kg; Power/Torque: 1,200 cc air-cooled 45-deg V-twin, 70 hp (52.2 kW) at 5,750 rpm, 100 Nm crank at 3,500 rpm, idle 950 rpm, soft limiter 6,200 rpm ([88]-[89]); 5-speed, belt final drive; Battery/Fuel: 12.5 L, 5.0-5.8 L/100 km; Top speed: 175 km/h; 0-50: 2.5 s; Travel: 92 mm front, 77 mm rear via a cantilever shock hidden under the seat; Wheels/Tires: 130/90-16 front (640 mm OD), 150/80-16 rear (646 mm OD), `Touring`; Wheelbase/Seat: 1,510/690 mm, rake 30 deg, trail 117 mm; Handling: cruiser MaxLean 32, pegs scraping from 29 deg ([202]); slow roll, heavy steering; Model notes: 2,270 x 870 x 1,080 mm steel cradle frame with a hardtail-look swingarm, floating solo saddle, bobbed rear fender with side-mounted plate, peanut tank, round headlight, low flat drag bar, mid pegs, wire-spoked 16-inch wheels, shotgun pipes on the right; Special: solo seat; a tuck behind the bars above 140 km/h lowers CdA to 0.66, which its 175 km/h target assumes ([68]).

[199] Street-legal equipment belongs to every Street-class bike and both Haulers. Indicators (rebindable in `Config/Controls`): Z left, X right, Z+X hazards on PC; D-pad Left/Right held 0.4 s, hazards D-pad Down held 0.4 s on gamepad; mobile arrow buttons. They flash amber at 1.5 Hz on `IndicatorFL/FR/RL/RR` via the [36] flags and auto-cancel after a 45-deg heading change once yaw rate stays under 5 deg/s for 1 s, or after 20 s and 300 m without a 15-deg turn. Tap L or D-pad Up for low beam, hold 0.4 s for high beam ([225]). H or L3 plays the `SoundIds` horn (6 s cap) via the [36] horn flag. Mirrors fold 70 deg on a same-side `HullImpact` until repaired ([245]). Plates: up to 8 characters matching `^[A-Z0-9 %-]+$`, set via `Custom_ApplyVisual` ([381]), filtered per [224].

[200] Sport-bike electronics. The quickshifter accepts upshifts above 30% throttle and 5,000 rpm, cutting ignition 80 ms at 6,000 rpm to 45 ms at 14,000, clutchless even in Sim Mode, with a 30 ms haptic pulse. Autoblip opens airflow on closed-throttle downshifts for 90-140 ms until rpm is within 300 of (v / r) x lower-gear overall ratio x 60/(2 pi). Slide control 1-3 trims `TorqueLimit` to cap rear slip angle at 14, 9 or 5 deg beyond 20 deg lean. `Audio.FiringIntervals_deg` feeds [251]-[270]: I4 180 x4 screams at rpm/30 Hz, triple 240 x3, 270-deg twin 270/450 and V-twin 315/405 lope, boxer 360/360 thrums, V4 90/200/90/340 rasps. Crank reaction -J x alpha: a boxer blip rocks the stopped bike 1-2 deg right (J 0.08 kg m^2); the V4's counter-rotating crank reverses its pitch sign and scales [61] w_n by 0.9.

[201] ZR-6S Stunt specifics ([182]). Its hull adds three "VehicleHull" blocks to the base three ([48]): front cage, one box over both rear subcages, and the 12 o'clock bar, whose skid puck sits 0.22 m behind and 0.60 m above the rear contact patch, touching down at 70 deg pitch, past the [66] 45-deg balance point. Puck friction 0.25 lets a 12 o'clock scrape hold at 15-25% throttle with feathered rear brake, spraying sparks; without the bar it loops out. Cages are the [388] part (0.6x side-impact damage). The left-thumb hand brake takes rear-brake input in every pose. Circle wheelies: front up over 20 deg, steer sets the lean target directly (Steer x 25 deg) and camber thrust 0.9 x F_z x lean (rad) gives r = v^2/(g tan(lean)): 13.5 m at 25 km/h and 20 deg.

[202] Cruiser and bagger specifics. Place `PegL`/`PegR` on the Glide Bagger's 330 x 110 mm floorboards (hinged feelers) and the Chopper's forward controls, 250 mm ahead of mid-peg positions, so foot IK follows. `Chassis.ScrapeLean_deg` (Bobber 29, Chopper 27, Bagger 28) arms a short `workspace:Raycast` from the low-side peg; contact calls `ParticleEmitter:Emit(n)`, n = speed_kmh / 10 sparks, and loops a scrape sound, while feelers only wear. Past MaxLean ([61]) hard parts touch: a 25,000 N/m virtual spring there levers load off both tires, so holding the lean lowsides. Bagger saddlebags: two 30 L lockable cases (`Seats.Cargo_L` 60, 12 kg each), four Parcel Courier parcels ([351]), mass via [47]. Its fairing radio plays licensed `Config/SoundIds` tracks ([251]-[270]) through a `Sound` with `RollOffMaxDistance` 50 studs and volume capped at 0.6, heard by others only with "Hear other players' radios" on.

[203] The 3-Rail Bike Trailer, Hitch Bike Carrier (welded rigidly to `Hitch`, blocking towing) and Hauler Van put ramps and rails in "VehicleDeck" ([48]); slot i sits at `TrailerBed` offset (i - (n+1)/2) x SlotPitch. Once [50] anchors the parked hauler (and its trailer), ride up the lowered ramp under 8 km/h into the chock and exit, killing the ignition. The server checks both wheel attachments inside the slot, speed under 0.3 stud/s, lean under 15 deg, slot limits, no overlap and permission; skips the [50] auto-anchor (an anchored `ChassisRoot` pins the rig); `PivotTo`s the bike upright (max 0.6 studs, 12 deg); welds deck to `ChassisRoot` with a `WeldConstraint`, sets `RootPriority` 0 and adds strap `Beam`s. An "Unload" `ProximityPrompt` fires `Vehicle_Unload`; the server re-validates, unwelds, restores 127 and parks the bike behind the ramp with a [75] teleport token.
```luau
-- hauler_3_rail_bike_trailer.luau (excerpt), Class "Trailer"
Economy={Price=3000,UnlockLevel=18,Dealership="HaulerTruckTrailer"},
Mass={Mass_kg=290,GVWR_kg=1600},Dimensions={Length_mm=3900,DeckHeight_mm=500,RampLength_mm=1500},
Seats={Riders=0,Passengers=0,BikeSlots=3,SlotPitch_mm=610,SlotLength_mm=2600,MaxBikeMass_kg=400},
-- hauler_hitch_bike_carrier.luau (excerpt)
Economy={Price=600,UnlockLevel=10,Dealership="HaulerTruckTrailer"},
Mass={Mass_kg=32},Dimensions={DeckHeight_mm=480,RampLength_mm=1600},
Seats={Riders=0,Passengers=0,BikeSlots=1,SlotLength_mm=2250,MaxBikeMass_kg=180},
```

[204] Passengers: `Seats.Passengers` is 1 on both Corsa scooters, Ryujin Gremlin 125, Kensei Street 900, Ryujin RS-600, Liberty Glide Bagger, Alpenrad GX-1300, Kensei Hayate 1300, Kensei ZR-1000, Dunecat XR Turbo and Wavecat 300, 2 on the Hauler Van, 4 in the Hauler HD cab, 0 elsewhere in [176]-[198]. Extra seats use `PassengerSeatAttachment2` to `4`. Owners allow Anyone, Friends (default), Crew or Nobody ([116]). Each passenger adds 75 kg through the [47] rewrite plus, every step, torque (world seat offset from `CoM`) x (0, -m_p g, 0), so a pillion unloads the front. Bike and Wavecat passengers ragdoll or splash off with the rider ([231]-[250]); Dunecat and Hauler occupants are belted. Moto-Taxi fares are server NPCs on the same seat and mass ([353]). Acceptance test: a pillion drops the RS-600's static front load from about 50% to 43% (within 2 points).

[205] Water and snow: loaded, the Wavecat 300 displaces 0.44 m^3, rides bow-up 6-8 deg through the 15-25 km/h hump and planes at 2-3 deg trim. Each client drops wake crests from every replicated watercraft every 0.25 s along a 19.5-deg Kelvin wedge, 0.08 m high per 10 km/h up to 0.4 m, decaying over 8 s; `Buoyancy` adds them to the surface, so wakes hit above 50 km/h launch the ski. Thrust stops while the intake is airborne; landing over 25 deg nose-down buries the bow (drag x4, 0.5 s). Swell (0.4 m high, 8 s, 100 m long) derives from `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()`. The Frostline 850 rides [71] on packed trail; in `DeepSnow` zones its track sinks to the zone's `Sink_m` (default 0.45 m) stopped, floats by 35 km/h, and trenches below 8 km/h at full throttle until rocked free.

## SECTION 9 - REALISTIC VISUALS & 3D ASSET PIPELINE (Paragraphs 206-230)

[206] You will be honest about realism: no script can generate a photoreal motorcycle, so visuals run on two tracks sharing one contract. Track A, `VehicleBuilder`, builds true-proportion Part placeholders from config, so everything is drivable from Phase 4; Track B, a Blender or vetted ([215]) MeshPart model at `Assets/VehicleModels/<VehicleId>`, replaces it with zero code changes via the same attachments, roles and attributes. Both carry grime: the server writes `Grime_Dust` and `Grime_Mud` (10-point buckets, session-only) from its [410] axle raycasts, rising on loose surfaces, Mud or wetness above 0.7 and falling in rain, fords ([313]) and at `WashBay` pressure washers ($25, 6 s); `Rig` adds splatter `Decal`s to lower panels and tires; clients set Transparency 1 - 0.85 x level/100. Acceptance test: with `VehicleModels` empty every VehicleId rides; restoring one model changes only its looks; both tracks muddy identically.

[207] `VehicleBuilder.Build(vehicleId: string): Model` is called by `VehicleService` for spawns and on the client for the anchored, local `workspace.GaragePreview` and dealership previews ([385], [444]); each side caches one template per VehicleId and returns `template:Clone()`. Resolution: if `Assets/VehicleModels/<VehicleId>` exists and `VehicleBuilder.Validate(model)` passes, clone it; otherwise generate the placeholder. `Validate` requires PrimaryPart `ChassisRoot`, every physics attachment the vehicle class needs ([48]) and zero `LuaSourceContainer` descendants (Script, LocalScript, ModuleScript); a failing real model is ignored with one `Logger` warning and the placeholder spawns instead, so the spawn refusal of [48] never triggers from art. Missing cosmetic attachments (Exhaust1..n, lights, indicators, SwingArmPivot) are synthesized from config with one warning. Mass, the `ChassisRoot` box and density follow [47]; every part you add is `Massless`. Asset IDs of 0 are skipped per [13].

[208] Two-wheeler placeholders convert config through `Units.mmToStuds`. Each wheel is a black `Rubber` tire `Part` with `Shape = Cylinder` (X axis along the axle) and `Size = Vector3.new(w, d, d)`, d from `Dimensions.WheelDiaF_mm`/`WheelDiaR_mm`, w from `Tires.WidthF_mm`/`WidthR_mm`, holding a `Metal` rim cylinder at 82% diameter and a 4 mm rotor (180 mm pedal, 220-270 mm dirt, 320 mm street). Fork tubes are two cylinders tilted to `Chassis.Rake_deg`, lowers 30% thicker; clamps are blocks; the handlebar is a `BarWidth_mm` cylinder. Frame tubes run from steering head to swingarm pivot; the swingarm is two blocks; tank, seat and side panels are `WedgePart`-tapered blocks carrying `Paint_Gloss` and [223] `PaintSlot` attributes, so paint works early. Add a motor, engine or crankset block, an exhaust cylinder ending at Exhaust1, and Neon lenses. Acceptance test: the volt_wasp_x placeholder wheelbase measures 1,260 mm (4.50 studs) within 0.02 studs.

[209] Quads, the UTV and haulers reuse the wheel builder at four corners: WheelFL/WheelFR spaced by `Dimensions.TrackF_mm`, WheelRL/WheelRR by `TrackR_mm`, axles `Wheelbase_mm` apart. Quads add a block frame, A-arm blocks per front corner, wedge fenders, seat, steel racks on the Ranchhand 570 and a handlebar under SteerPivot; front wheels steer by [46]-[75] Ackermann angles. The Dunecat XR Turbo adds a cage of 44 mm cylinders, bucket seats at SeatAttachment and PassengerSeatAttachment, and a steering-wheel cylinder carrying GripL and GripR at 9 and 3 o'clock. Hauler HD gets cab, hood wedge, bed, bumpers, mirrors and Hitch; Hauler Van a high-roof box, rear ramp and two bike chocks. The 3-Rail Bike Trailer is a single-axle deck with three rails and TrailerBed, the Hitch Bike Carrier a receiver arm with rails; ramps and rails join "VehicleDeck" ([203]). Windows are `Glass`, Transparency 0.35.

[210] The Frostline 850 uses two 1,000 mm ski blocks with upturned `WedgePart` tips on spindles that turn under SteerPivot at the 965 mm stance, a tunnel block, the 154-inch (3,912 mm) track as top and bottom run blocks carrying a scrolling lug `Texture` ([220]), hood wedges, a `Glass` windshield, running boards and a riser bar; `SkiL`, `SkiR` and `Track1`-`Track4` follow [48] and [71].
