The Wavecat 300 hull is mirrored wedges forming a 22-degree deadrise V, plus deck, seat, sponsons, a handlebar under SteerPivot and a pump nozzle at `Nozzle` that yaws up to 20 degrees with steering, showing thrust vectoring; `Buoy1`-`Buoy10` and `Intake` sit on the hull bottom per [72]. Both get Neon headlight lenses with `SpotLight`s and `SurfaceLight` taillights per [225]-[226]. Every placeholder's paint-slot parts take `Visual.FactoryColors[1]`; rubber, glass and metal parts keep fixed materials, never default SmoothPlastic.

[211] Every model, placeholder or real, uses this role hierarchy; each role is a Model whose PrimaryPart is its main part. `VehicleBuilder.Rig(model)` welds static parts to `ChassisRoot` and each role's extra meshes to its main part with `WeldConstraint`, joins moving roles with `Motor6D` whose `C0`/`C1` sit on the role's pivot attachment, so animation only writes `Motor6D.Transform`, and sets `Anchored = false` on every part except `ChassisRoot`. Placeholders put CoM at `Mass.CoMHeight_mm` over the wheelbase midpoint, SteerPivot at the steering head with its UpVector on the raked axis ([48]), SeatAttachment at `SeatHeight_mm`, GripL/GripR at the bar ends, PegL/PegR 330 mm above ground, Exhaust1 at the muffler tip and lights at lens centers.
```
VehicleModels/volt_wasp_x      -- Model, PrimaryPart = ChassisRoot
  ChassisRoot                  -- invisible; all canonical Attachments
  Hull                         -- 3-6 collision blocks, "VehicleHull"
  Body                         -- static meshes, WeldConstraint
  Steer                        -- Motor6D at SteerPivot
    ForkLower                  -- Motor6D, slides on fork axis
      WheelF                   -- Motor6D at axle (spin)
    LeverL, LeverR, Throttle   -- Motor6D at their perches
  SwingArm                     -- Motor6D at SwingArmPivot
    WheelR, ShockShaft
  ShockBody, ChainTop, ChainBottom, Sprockets, Kickstand, Footpegs
  Lights                       -- Neon lenses, SpotLight, SurfaceLight
```

[212] You will write the Blender standards as an artist checklist. Model every hero vehicle from orthographic side, front, top and rear blueprints scaled until the measured wheelbase equals the config value within 5 mm; never trace perspective photos. Work at real size (Scene Units Metric, Unit Scale 1.0, Length Meters), nose toward Blender's -Y, axles along X. Calibrate once: export a 1.000 m cube plus a cone pointing at the nose, import it with the Roblox 3D Importer, and adjust export scale, importer unit option or forward axis until the cube measures 3.571 studs within 0.005 (1 stud = 0.28 m) and the cone points along LookVector (-Z). Save the exporter preset as `TC_Export`, record importer values, and never change either. Acceptance test: a finished Volt Wasp X imports nose-forward with its 4.50-stud wheelbase and zero manual scaling or rotation.

[213] Before export, apply all transforms (Ctrl+A, All Transforms) so rotation is 0 and scale 1, recalculate normals outward, and set each object's origin at its real pivot: wheels at the axle, steering assembly on the steering axis, fork lowers on the fork axis, swingarm at its pivot bolt, shock body at its top eye and shaft at its bottom eye, kickstand, pegs and levers at their pins. Name objects `<VehicleId>_<Role>_<Detail>` (`volt_wasp_x_SwingArm_Main`), Role being a [211] role. Roblox centers each MeshPart on its bounding box, so pivots travel as markers: a 1 cm cube named `ATT_<Name>` per canonical attachment and moving-part pivot (`ATT_WheelF`, `ATT_GripL`, `ATT_SwingArmPivot`, `ATT_ShockTop`). `VehicleBuilder.ConvertMarkers(model)`, run once from the Studio command bar, replaces each marker with that exact-named Attachment on `ChassisRoot` at its position, axis-aligned to the model except SteerPivot, tilted back by `Chassis.Rake_deg` ([48]).

[214] FBX export uses: Selected Objects only, Object Types Mesh, Apply Modifiers on, Smoothing Face, Apply Scalings "FBX All", Forward -Z, Up Y (or whatever axes [212] calibrated), Apply Transform on, Bake Animation off, embedded textures off. Triangulate in Blender first (Triangulate modifier, Beauty) so the count you budget is the count Roblox reports. glTF 2.0 binary (`.glb`) with +Y Up and Apply Modifiers is the accepted alternative. In the Roblox 3D Importer keep every object as its own MeshPart (never merge meshes), import as one Model at the file's scene origin, and skip material and texture import because maps go through `SurfaceAppearance` ([221]); parts may stay anchored while editing because `Rig` unanchors them. After import, run `ConvertMarkers`, set fidelity properties per [216]-[217], tag paint slots per [223], rename the Model to the VehicleId and move it into `ReplicatedStorage/Assets/VehicleModels`.

[215] Creator Store meshes are allowed only after a vetting pass you document per asset. Confirm the asset may be used in experiences, delete every Script, LocalScript and ModuleScript inside it (hidden scripts are the most common backdoor and `Validate` rejects them anyway), and remove or retexture any real manufacturer logo, wordmark, sponsor decal or license plate. Count triangles against [216], split any oversized mesh, and rescale with `Model:ScaleTo` until the wheelbase matches the config within 1%. Re-parent its meshes into the [211] roles and add `ATT_` markers. Record the source asset ID and creator name as a comment beside its entry in `Config/AssetIds`; code never references it. Acceptance test: a deliberately broken sourced model (missing SteerPivot) logs one warning and spawns the placeholder without throwing.

[216] Triangle budgets for LOD0: hero motorcycles 25,000-40,000 triangles split across MeshParts that each stay under the 20,000-per-mesh limit, for example a Kensei ZR-1000 at about 32,500: fairings 9,000, frame and swingarm 6,000, engine 7,000, each wheel 3,000, steering assembly 3,000, exhaust 1,500. Pedal bikes and light electrics 12,000-20,000; quads and the UTV 30,000-45,000; Hauler HD and Hauler Van 35,000-50,000; AI traffic cars 5,000-8,000 in one or two meshes; props 50-2,000. Set `RenderFidelity = Automatic` on every vehicle and world mesh so the engine generates distance LODs itself, and `Precise` only on dealership showroom display copies. `RenderFidelity` and `CollisionFidelity` cannot be set by scripts, so set them in Studio before saving. Acceptance test: 30 parked hero bikes in view at the Old Town spawn plaza still hold the [476] frame targets (60 FPS mid-range PC, 30 FPS phone).

[217] Visual meshes never define physics. Set `CollisionFidelity = Box` on small cosmetic parts (levers, bolts, lenses, plates, pegs) and `Hull` on larger panels, never `PreciseConvexDecomposition` on anything attached to a vehicle. Every cosmetic part gets `CanCollide = false`, `CanQuery = false`, `CanTouch = false` and `Massless = true`; `CanQuery = false` is what keeps the suspension raycasts and spherecasts of [46]-[75], yours and other riders', from hitting your fender. Only `Hull` parts ([48]) and the [203] deck ramps and rails collide. Set `CastShadow = false` on parts under 0.3 studs, lens meshes, number boards and the GraphicsShell layer of [224]. Acceptance test: a `workspace:GetPartBoundsInBox` query around a parked bike returns only its Hull parts and the ground, and a downward `workspace:Raycast` through the tank never returns a cosmetic MeshPart.

[218] Model these as separate meshes with exact pivots inside the [211] roles: front and rear wheels (tire, rim, spokes or cast spokes, hub) with their brake rotors, which spin while calipers stay fixed; fork lowers carrying axle, front caliper and front fender; the steering assembly (both triple clamps, fork tubes, handlebar, both levers, throttle grip, mirrors, and the headlight and number plate when `Visual.HeadlightOnFork` is true, as on dirt bikes and naked bikes); the swingarm with rear caliper, chain guide and hugger; shock body and shock shaft; front and rear sprockets; chain or belt as separate top and bottom runs; kickstand; footpegs; brake pedal. Quads add A-arms, knuckles and tie rods, the snowmobile adds ski spindles and track runs, the jet ski adds the pump nozzle, and pedal bikes add the crankset.

[219] A `VehicleVisuals` child module of `VehicleController` poses nearby vehicles in `RunService.PreSimulation`, writing only `Motor6D.Transform`. Wheels: `angle += omega * dt`; the owner uses tire-state omega; remotes use [36] RPM x 2pi/60 over total gear ratio when in gear with clutch flag clear, else ground speed over radius, so burnouts show. Forks slide by front compression, measured remotely by one raycast per wheel. Swingarm rotation = asin((h0 + x)/L) - asin(h0/L): L pivot-to-axle length, h0 axle height relative to the pivot at full extension, x rear travel. Shock length = free eye-to-eye minus x / `Suspension.LeverageRatio` (about 2.4 on MX bikes); body and shaft aim at opposite eyes via `CFrame.lookAt` over a split coil; nothing resizes. Steer turns about SteerPivot, countersteer included. Rates per [480]: own vehicle every frame, remotes 30 Hz within 150 studs, 10 Hz to 500, frozen beyond.

[220] Spoked wheels carry two meshes: sharp spokes and a blur disc whose `SurfaceAppearance` (`AlphaMode = Transparency`) holds a radial smear. Above 22 rad/s show the disc, hiding spokes via `LocalTransparencyModifier`, reverting below 18 rad/s (hysteresis); the disc turns at 10% of omega. Cast wheels swap above 40 rad/s. Chains, belts and snowmobile tracks are top and bottom run Parts with a link or lug `Texture` on both sides; each frame `OffsetStudsU += chainSpeed * dt` modulo `StudsPerTileU`, top run forward, bottom rearward, with `chainSpeed = omegaRear * rearSprocketPitchRadius` in studs/s (tracks use ground speed). The front sprocket spins at `omegaRear * rearTeeth / frontTeeth`, pedal cranks at cadence. Levers rotate up to 15 degrees with brake and 25 with clutch, the grip twists 70 degrees at full throttle, and the kickstand drops 95 degrees in 0.3 s when riderless.

[221] Real meshes use `SurfaceAppearance` with `ColorMap` (sRGB albedo, no baked lighting or ambient shadow), `NormalMap` (tangent space, OpenGL green-up), `RoughnessMap` and `MetalnessMap` (linear grayscale; metalness 1.0 only on bare metal, 0 on paint, rubber and plastic). Bake normals from a high-poly model onto the LOD0 mesh with 8 px padding at 1024. Texel density targets: hero vehicles 1,024 px per meter (about 287 px per stud) on panels, engine and frame; small parts 512 px per meter; world props 256 px per meter; buildings and roads use tiling MaterialVariants. `SurfaceAppearance` maps cannot be written by scripts at runtime, so author every look in Studio and switch looks by cloning prebuilt `SurfaceAppearance` instances from `ReplicatedStorage/Assets`. Acceptance test: no ColorMap pixel on plastic, rubber or painted regions exceeds sRGB 240 or falls below 30.

[222] Textures cap at 1024x1024: use 1024 maps for hero parts (engine, frame, unpainted panels) and 512 for small parts (levers, pegs, lights, sprockets, plates), atlased so a hero two-wheeler uses at most two 1024 sets and four 512 sets; painted panels share the [223] MaterialVariants and add no maps. Budget about 1.4 MB per 1024 map and 0.35 MB per 512 map after GPU compression with mips, so four-map sets cost 5.6 and 1.4 MB and a hero bike about 17 MB; omit `MetalnessMap` on non-metal parts. Quads, UTV and haulers may use three 1024 sets; traffic cars share a 512 atlas set per body style. Verify in the Developer Console Memory tab (`GraphicsTexture`) in Harbor City with and without 30 distinct player vehicles: the difference stays under 520 MB on PC, and phones stay inside the [476] memory ceiling.

[223] Paint customization ([366]-[390]) needs paintable panels as separate MeshParts with no `SurfaceAppearance`, carrying the attribute `PaintSlot` = `Primary`, `Secondary`, `Accent`, `Frame` or `Wheels`. `CustomizationService` writes only `BasePart.Color`, `BasePart.Material` and `BasePart.MaterialVariant`, all script-writable, and always sets Material equal to the variant's `BaseMaterial`. Create these in `MaterialService` with near-white ColorMaps so Color drives hue; MaterialVariant has no scalar roughness, so the values below are flat-gray `RoughnessMap`/`MetalnessMap` levels: `Paint_Gloss` (SmoothPlastic, roughness 0.12), `Paint_Matte` (Plastic, 0.85), `Paint_Satin` (SmoothPlastic, 0.45), `Paint_MetallicFlake` (Metal, metalness 0.6, roughness 0.3, fine-noise NormalMap, StudsPerTile 0.5), `Carbon_Fiber` (SmoothPlastic, 2x2 twill map, StudsPerTile 0.35, Color forced white), `Aluminum_Brushed` (Metal, metalness 1, roughness 0.35, directional normal) and `Chrome` (Metal, metalness 1, roughness 0.04). The Pro Paint Lab pass gates Chrome, Satin and the [386] `Paint_Chameleon`.

[224] Graphics kits never touch paint panels directly: each paintable panel has a `GraphicsShell` twin MeshPart with identical UVs, offset 2 mm along normals, whose `SurfaceAppearance` (`AlphaMode = Transparency`) holds the kit art, so paint and finish show through. Unwrap each panel's visible face as its own non-overlapping UV island with consistent up-direction, so one 1024 kit sheet spans tank, shrouds and side panels cleanly, and hide shells beyond 200 studs via `LocalTransparencyModifier` to avoid z-fighting. Number plates and motocross number boards are flat Parts with a `SurfaceGui` (`SizingMode = PixelsPerStud`, 200 px per stud, `LightInfluence = 1`, `MaxDistance` 120) holding a `TextLabel`. Text (plates up to 8 characters per [199], boards 1-3 digits) is filtered on the server with `TextService:FilterStringAsync` then `GetNonChatStringForBroadcastAsync` before anyone else sees it ([451]-[475]); filter failures show a blank plate.

[225] Each Headlight attachment holds two child Attachments, `LowBeam` pitched 4 degrees down and `HighBeam` level, each parenting a `SpotLight` with `Face = Front`. Low beam: Brightness 3, Range 45 studs, Angle 70, warm white (255, 244, 229). High beam: Brightness 5, Range 60, Angle 45, cool white (230, 240, 255). `Range` caps at 60 studs (16.8 m) while a real low beam reaches about 50 m, so match beam shape, never stack lights. Per [199], tapping Lights (L, D-pad Up) toggles low beam and a 0.4 s hold toggles high beam, sent as bit 7 of the [36] mode byte so remotes show it. Only the local player's headlight has `Shadows = true`; remote headlights use Range 20 ([480]) and light only on the nearest 2/4/6/6 vehicles within 200 studs per [340] tier, others showing only Neon lenses.

[226] Rear and signal lamps are `SurfaceLight`s on the outward `Face` of their lens parts. Taillight: red (255, 25, 15), Brightness 1, Range 6, Angle 120, lit with the headlights. Brake light: the same lamp at Brightness 4 and Range 10 while the [36] brake-light flag is set (any brake input above 0.05). IndicatorFL/FR/RL/RR are amber (255, 140, 0), flashing at 1.5 Hz (90 per minute, inside the legal 60-120 band) from the [36] indicator and hazard flags; bindings and auto-cancel follow [199], and only Street-class bikes and both Haulers get indicators. Daytime running lights switch the DRL lens to `Neon` at 40% Color value whenever the vehicle is powered. Every lens is its own mesh switching `Material` between dark tinted `Glass` (off) and `Neon` (on). Remote SurfaceLights count toward the [480] light cap; past it, lenses alone signal.

[227] The seat joint's lifecycle belongs to [232]; its geometry is yours. It aligns a `RiderHip` attachment, inserted in the HumanoidRootPart at the avatar's measured hip offset so any R15 scale sits on the seat surface, with SeatAttachment; seated BaseParts are `Massless` per [47]. `RiderAnimationController` creates four `IKControl`s under each visible rider's Humanoid (Type `Transform`, Weight 1, SmoothTime 0.05): ChainRoot LeftUpperArm, EndEffector LeftHand, Target GripL; the mirror to GripR; ChainRoot LeftUpperLeg, EndEffector LeftFoot, Target PegL; the mirror to PegR. Orient grips so closed hands wrap the bar. Poles sit 0.3 m outboard of each elbow and 0.4 m ahead of each knee for an elbows-up stance. Pedal bikes retarget feet to `PedalL`/`PedalR` on the crank arms, leaving PegL/PegR on ChassisRoot for [48]; floorboards offset feet per [202]; the Glide E-Board and Gyro EUC-20 disable hand IK; crashes disable all four.

[228] Body motion layers procedurally on the [231]-[250] animation set. Each frame the Waist `Motor6D.Transform` gains a stance-signed roll, clamped to 12 degrees: 35% of vehicle lean into the turn for `Street` and `Sport`, 20% outward for `MX` and `Supermoto` (upright torso, [233]), zero otherwise; the Neck counter-rolls half the total torso roll. Weight shift moves `RiderHip` up to 0.79 studs (0.22 m) fore and aft and 0.18 studs (0.05 m) down per [65], pitching the waist up to 20 degrees. Acceptance test: at full lean and shift, hands stay within 0.05 studs of GripL/GripR. Rider gear is `Accessory` instances in `ReplicatedStorage/Assets/RiderGear` added with `Humanoid:AddAccessory`: helmets on HatAttachment, goggles on FaceFrontAttachment, chest protectors on BodyFrontAttachment, glove and boot pairs on grip and foot attachments. Helmets make the server park Hat and Hair accessories until removed. Gear is cosmetic ([366]-[390]).

[229] World art obeys the same rules on a tighter budget. Modular buildings snap to a 12.5-stud (3.5 m) bay and storey with 7.5-stud (2.1 m) doors and 3.2-stud (0.9 m) sills, from shared 200-1,500-triangle wall, corner, window, storefront, roof and trim pieces. Road MeshParts keep a `Material` matching their look so [59] grip resolves (`Asphalt` with worn variants like `WornAsphalt`, [275]). Enterable true-scale interiors (dealership showrooms and bays, property garages, Kickstand Diner, Pumpline Plaza stores, hangars, Snowfield Hut) get furniture, tool walls and fictional-brand posters; their `PointLight`s run only while the camera is in an `Interior`-tagged box, within the [480] cap. Other windows show one of six room-photo backplates 0.5 studs behind `Glass` (dark at ID 0). Weathering decals cover 30% of facades; no two neighbors match. Acceptance test: no drivable surface returns Plastic or SmoothPlastic.

[230] Every logo, wordmark and graphic is original vector art exported as 1024 PNG with alpha, its ID stored as 0 in `Config/AssetIds` until uploaded. Nothing may trace to an inspired-by maker or real sponsor: no wing emblems, tuning-fork marks, bar-and-shield shapes, quartered roundels, stylized-K river marks or three-claw slashes, no lime-green factory color on Kensei, no orange-white-black scheme on Austral, no shield badge on Veloce. Distinct marks: Ryujin a dragon-head monogram, Kensei a sword-guard ring, Austral a southern-cross star cluster, Volt a hexagonal cell, Talon a single hooked talon, Zephyr three swept wind lines, Liberty a bell silhouette, Veloce a split diamond, Alpenrad a mountain peak. Kits and gear show only in-world sponsors (Pumpline, VoltCharge, Dirt Depot). Acceptance test: zero real brand names, logos or sponsor marks in any asset; proportions may match; badges and headlight graphics must differ.

## SECTION 10 - RIDER ANIMATION, CRASHES, DAMAGE & MAINTENANCE (Paragraphs 231-250)

[231] You will make `RiderAnimationController` the only module that loads or plays rider animations; nothing else calls `Animator:LoadAnimation` on a rider. `Seats.RiderStance` picks the base pose: `MX`, `Supermoto`, `Standing` (Trialtek TR-300, [163]), `Sport`, `Street`, `Cruiser`, `Scooter`, `Deck`, `Pedal`, `Quad`, `UTV`, `Snow` or `Water`. Defaults when a stat block omits it: `MX` dirt bikes and e-moto; `Street` nakeds, adventure, cafe, minibike, Zephyr SR-E; `Sport` faired bikes; `Cruiser` Liberty; `Scooter` Corsa; `Deck` Glide E-Board, Lupo Twin, Gyro EUC-20; `Pedal` pedal bikes and e-bikes. IDs live in `Config/AssetIds.Animations` as 0 placeholders; a 0 ID skips its track and joins the [13] placeholder count. Required set:
```
Base (looped):  Pose_<Stance>, StandPegs, Attack, Tuck, PedalCycle
Blend (looped): LeanL, LeanR, WeightBack, WeightFwd, Absorb
Legs (looped):  LegOutL, LegOutR, DabL, DabR, FootDown
One-shot:       Kickstart, PullStart, PushOff, Mount, Dismount,
                IdleLook1-3, Celebrate1-3, GetUpFront, GetUpBack
```

[232] The seat joint is the [116] `Weld` from `HumanoidRootPart` to `SeatAttachment`, aligned per [227]; you own its lifecycle: created on enter, `Enabled = false` on ejection (never `Destroy` per frame, [42]), destroyed on exit. Load tracks once at mount, releasing them via the vehicle's `Trove` at dismount; while mounted, set the default `Animate` LocalScript's `Enabled` false and stop its tracks from `Animator:GetPlayingAnimationTracks()`. Base tracks use `Enum.AnimationPriority.Movement`; blend layers use `Action`, so weight w covers w of the base on the joints it keys; leg tracks use `Action2` and key only leg joints; one-shots `Action3`, get-up `Action4`. Blend layers are looped single-keyframe poses driven only by `AnimationTrack:AdjustWeight(w, 0.1)`; [228] procedural motion and [227] IK sit on top. Acceptance tests: a lone `LeanL` at weight 0.5 shows half its pose; with every ID at 0, all 53 vehicles ride error-free.

[233] Targets update every `RunService.PreRender`, smoothed by w += (target - w) x (1 - exp(-dt/tau)), tau 0.12 s (weight shift 0.06 s). Lean: `Street` and `Sport` hang off inside at smoothstep(15, 50 deg, |phi|); `MX` and `Supermoto` stay upright via the outside pose at smoothstep(10, 40 deg, |phi|); `Quad` and `Snow` lean inside at smoothstep(0.1, 0.6, lateral g); others follow the bike via IK. Weight shift s ([65]): WeightBack = min(1, max(0, -s) + 0.5 x clamp(pitch/60 deg, 0, 1)), WeightFwd = max(0, s). `MX`, `Supermoto`, `Quad` and `Pedal` riders cross-fade to `StandPegs` (0.3 s) when 0.5 s RMS suspension velocity tops 0.8 m/s or off-pavement speed 35 km/h; `Standing` always stands; standing Attack = clamp((RMS - 0.6)/0.6, 0, 1). `Sport` tucks above 0.8 throttle over 120-160 km/h. `Absorb` (deep crouch) weight = mean suspension compression fraction.

[234] Event layers follow physics signals. The [64] `FootDab {Side}` fades in `DabL`/`DabR` over 0.15 s; [64] FootDown plays `FootDown` on the left leg. `MX` riders extend the inside `LegOutL`/`LegOutR` at 12-80 km/h once steer exceeds 0.35 or roll 20 deg, retracting below 12 deg; `Supermoto` follows [172]. While a leg track, `Kickstart` or `PushOff` plays, that foot's `IKControl.Weight` fades to 0 over 0.15 s. The [90] `KickStart` plays `Kickstart`, reporting the kick at `GetMarkerReachedSignal("Kick")`, or after `task.delay(0.55, ...)` with a 0 ID; the Gopher 200's `PullStart` works alike. `PushOff` plays when `Pedal` and `Deck` riders accelerate from below 2 km/h; `PedalCycle` runs at `AdjustSpeed(cadence_rpm / 90)`. After 6 s idle, `IdleLook1-3` play every 8-14 s until any input. Race wins and stunts play `Celebrate1-3` when stopped, or the arm-only `Celebrate1` above 5 km/h with that hand's IK faded.

[235] Tracks the owning client plays on its own character's `Animator` reach every client through Roblox's built-in animation replication, so there is no animation remote. Keep that stream light: call `AdjustWeight` only when a target moves at least 0.05, at most 15 times per second per track, and leave idle looped layers playing at weight 0 instead of restarting them. `Motor6D.Transform` and IK results never replicate, so every client creates the four [227] `IKControl`s locally for riders within 150 studs ([480] Tier 1), disabling them beyond, and rebuilds the [228] waist roll and hip shift from the [36] lean and the replicated `WeightBack`/`WeightFwd` tracks' `WeightCurrent`. IK targets are the replicated bike's attachments, so contacts stay exact at zero bandwidth. Acceptance test: 30 riders cost under 3 KB/s of animation traffic per client (Developer Console), and observers see pose changes within 200 ms.

[236] `VehicleController`'s `CrashPolicy` decides eject or ride-on for each [74] `CrashSignal`, with m_total = `Mass_kg` + 75 kg rider and J = m_total x `ImpactSpeed_ms` in N*s. `HullImpact` in the front or top zone ([242]) always ejects (from 8 m/s: 1,048 N*s on a Volt Wasp X); side, rear and bottom hits eject from 11 m/s, else damage only. `LowSide`, `HighSide`, `Endo`, `UpsideDown`, `Submerged` and [86] `Cutoff` always eject; `Rollover` past 75 deg roll above 10 km/h; `LoopOut` past 100 deg pitch (the rear brake can save it); `CasedLanding` above 11 m/s; `Headshake` never alone. Rider rules: a 0.89-stud-radius `workspace:Spherecast` from `Head` along its velocity (own bike and character excluded) hitting anything above 3 m/s, or touchdown after 0.35 s airtime over 35 deg nose-down versus the slope above 4 m/s sink. Belted Dunecat and Hauler occupants never eject.

[237] Crash states: `Riding`, `Ejecting`, `Ragdoll`, `Recovering`. Bail ([118]) uses the same pipeline with reason `Bail` and a 3 m/s push away from the bike. A [74] `TipOver`, or roll past 45 deg below 10 km/h, never ragdolls: the rider steps off with a dab and the bike lies down. Wavecat ejections skip the ragdoll and place the rider swimming 2 studs beside it. On ejection the client zeroes throttle, disables balance, cuts ignition, keeps owning the tumbling bike until remount or 20 s, and fires `Vehicle_Crash` (`NetDefs` `Event`, 2 per s) with reason, J_Ns, `ChassisRoot`-space contact point, velocity and `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()`. Rules pause 1.5 s after spawn, reset or remount; in `FoamPit` zones [307] suppresses crashes and damage entirely. Thresholds ignore assists. Acceptance test: a 40 km/h head-on wall hit always ejects; a 5 km/h curb bump never does.

[238] Build the R15 ragdoll rig in `PlayerService` on `CharacterAdded`. For the 14 `Motor6D` joints other than `Root` (kept so `HumanoidRootPart` follows `LowerTorso`), add two attachments at the joint's `<Joint>RigAttachment` positions, one disabled constraint and a `NoCollisionConstraint` between the two limbs. Ball joints get a `BallSocketConstraint` (`LimitsEnabled`, `TwistLimitsEnabled`, `Restitution` 0, `MaxFrictionTorque` raised from 10 until a dropped arm settles within 0.3 s) with the attachments' primary (X) axis along the child limb, since cone and twist are measured about it; hip `Attachment0` is pre-rotated 30 deg toward flexion. Elbows and knees get a `HingeConstraint` (X axis lateral, `LimitsEnabled`, 0-130 deg toward flexion), never bending sideways or backward. `Config/Ragdoll.luau` (degrees, cone then twist):
```
Neck      35   -45/45
Waist     25   -30/30
Shoulder  95   -60/80
Wrist     40   -30/30
Hip       60   -25/25
Ankle     30   -10/10
Elbow     hinge 0/130
Knee      hinge 0/130
```

[239] Ragdoll runs on the owning client in the ejection step; the server mirrors joints, constraints and properties when `Vehicle_Crash` arrives, because those client changes never replicate, while Humanoid state is owner-authoritative and changes only on the client. Ragdoll is always honored; damage and payouts wait for [248]. Steps: disable the seat weld and the 14 joints, enable the constraints, clear `Massless` and `PlatformStand` ([116]), put limbs in the [48] `Ragdoll` group with `CanCollide` true (if the Humanoid resets it, weld a `Massless` collider to each limb), call `Humanoid:ChangeState(Enum.HumanoidStateType.Physical)` with `GettingUp` blocked via `SetStateEnabled`, and disable the four `IKControl`s. The server calls `HumanoidRootPart:SetNetworkOwner(player)`. Before the next step, set each limb's `AssemblyLinearVelocity` to `ChassisRoot:GetVelocityAtPosition(limb.Position)` plus a kick: front impact or `Endo` +2.5 m/s up (over the bars), `HighSide` +4 m/s up and outward, `LowSide` none. Passengers ([204]) eject alike.

[240] Ragdolled limbs use `PhysicalProperties.new(0.7, 0.55, 0, 2, 1)`; weighted 2:1 with Asphalt's 0.8, mu = 0.63, so a 100 km/h lowside slides about 62 m; defaults return on get-up. `CameraController`'s Crash mode springs ([120], omega 6) after `UpperTorso` from 10 studs back and 4 up, with [121] collision pull-in. Get-up: once `HumanoidRootPart` stays under 0.5 m/s for 0.5 s and 3 s have passed since ejection, show a Get Up prompt (F, Y or button); without input the rider rises 2 s later. On `Vehicle_GetUp` (`Event`, 1 per s) the server checks those 3 s, re-enables joints, disables constraints and restores collision; the client re-enables and enters `GettingUp`, playing `GetUpFront` when `UpperTorso.CFrame.LookVector.Y` is negative, else `GetUpBack`. Feedback stays Mild: thuds, scrapes, [124] rumble and a "Crashed!" or "Bailed!" banner; no blood, injury text, bone sounds or red vignette.

[241] Remount: after get-up, F or Y within 25 studs rights the bike by the cooldown-free [118] reset sequence, `PivotTo` upright on the normal of a downward `workspace:Raycast`, then remounts through [116]. During `Ragdoll` or `Recovering`, auto-respawn fires when a stuck rule holds 3 s: below Y = -20, in water over 1.2 m deep (Wavecat riders excepted), beyond +/-8,000 studs, rider and bike over 150 studs apart, or the righted pose failing [117] overlap. A stuck rider alone reappears beside the bike; otherwise both go to the nearest road-graph node ([271]-[295]; pre-Phase 6, the newest [117] safe position), facing the road. Nothing dies: the owning client disables `Enum.HumanoidStateType.Dead`, `BreakJointsOnDeath` is false and no script lowers `Humanoid.Health`. Acceptance test: riding off the Lake Serene dam into deep water respawns both on the nearest road within 4 s of splashdown.

[242] `VehicleService` keeps a `DamageState` per vehicle, health 0-100 for: `Bodywork`; `LeverL` (clutch, or rear brake on clutchless bikes); `LeverR` (front brake); `Handlebars` (`Steering` on steering-wheel vehicles); per wheel `Wheel_<Attachment>` and `Tire_<Attachment>` plus `Flat`; `Engine` or `Motor`; `Battery` (EVs, e-start engines); `Exhaust`; `Lights`. Zone = dominant axis of the contact normal in `ChassisRoot` space. Impacts deal D = 25 + 75 x clamp((J/m_total - 8)/17, 0, 1); slides deal D = 2 + 0.4 x speed_kmh to the low side, rollovers to top and both sides. Each part takes D x weight: front Lights 1.0, Bodywork 0.8, front wheel 0.5, bars 0.4, Engine 0.2; side Bodywork 0.8, that lever 0.7, same-side Exhaust 0.5, bars 0.4; bottom Engine/Motor 0.7, Battery 0.5, wheels 0.3; rear Bodywork 0.8, rear wheel and Lights 0.4; top Bodywork 0.8, bars 0.4. Damage persists until repaired ([451]-[475]).

[243] The server writes `Dmg_<Component>` attributes on the vehicle model in 10-point buckets, plus boolean `Flat_<Attachment>` and `BarBend` (-1 or 1, impact side), so only bucket changes replicate; clients react via `GetAttributeChangedSignal`. Bodywork below 80 scuffs: swap each panel's `SurfaceAppearance` for its pre-built `Scuffed` sibling (never edit maps at runtime) or set `MeshPart.TextureID`; with 0 IDs, darken `Color` 12%. Below 40, swap to `Cracked` and re-weld fairings 0.05 studs and 3 deg off their rest pose. A lever at 0 turns transparent, revealing a broken stub. Handlebars below 60 rotate about the bar clamp by (60 - H)/60 x 6 deg yaw and 2 deg roll toward `BarBend`, carrying `GripL`/`GripR` so IK hands follow. Dead lights disable their `SpotLight`/`SurfaceLight` and keep lenses dark `Glass` ([225]-[226]). Flat tires squash to 85% visual height. Exhaust below 50 shows the dented pipe ([160]).

[244] `VehicleController` turns `Dmg_` attributes into multipliers. Bars below 60 bias steering (60 - H)/60 x 3 deg toward `BarBend`, offsetting the [61] lean target. A broken brake lever leaves 25% of that brake; a broken clutch lever forces auto-clutch. `Engine` or `Motor` below 70 scales peak torque by 0.5 + 0.5 x H/70, cuts out 120 ms every 2-5 s below 20, and dies at 0 or after [95] seizure. `Battery` scales usable `Capacity_Wh` by 0.6 + 0.4 x H/100 (times [247] `PackSoH`); below 20, e-start fails (kick or bump-start). A dead `Exhaust` costs 5% power. Flats give that tire 0.45x peak grip, 6x rolling resistance and a 40 km/h cap; rims below 30 wobble, pulsing 3% lateral force per turn. A [55] `Bottomed` flats its tire above 9 m/s under 76 kPa ([152]), else above 12 m/s.

[245] One `Config/Economy` formula: FullRepair = clamp(0.06 x purchase price, $60, $5,000); Quote = FullRepair x sum(w_i x (100 - H_i)/100), weights Engine/Motor 0.25, Bodywork 0.22, Battery 0.12, Wheels 0.12, Exhaust 0.07, Handlebars 0.06, Tires 0.06, Lights 0.06, Levers 0.04, groups averaged; absent parts fold into Engine/Motor (Bodywork on pedal bikes). Flats add a tire at 0.8% of price ($30-$250). Example: a $14,000 Volt Wasp X at 50% everywhere quotes $420. Throttle Works and dealership service bays (tag `RepairBay`) charge 100% in 5 s; owned workshops ([360]) charge 60% in 15 s; Mechanic-job players bid 50-120% ([356]). The Field Repair Kit ($400 at Throttle Works and Pumpline, carry 3) works stopped and outside races in 10 s, lifts every component to at least 60, plugs flats and refits levers, but cannot raise Engine/Motor or Battery from below 20.

[246] Wear follows the per-player Realism setting `Off`/`Light`/`Full` (default `Light`, [344]; changeable every 10 minutes): `Off` freezes wear, `Light` runs 0.35x and floors items at 20% (no failures), `Full` runs 1.0x; crash damage always applies. Tread T drops by (tread km / Life_km) x (1 + 6 x combined slip), tread km integrating |omega| x r so burnouts count; Life_km: knobby 250, sport 500, touring/scooter 900, pedal 1,200. [59] wear factor: 0.80 + 0.20 x min(1, T/0.3); below T = 0.2 wet grip loses another 15%. Chain lube L lasts 120 km (60 in Mud or wetness above 0.5); L under 0.2 or stretch above 80% drops [77] chain efficiency to 0.93 with squeal. Stretch hits 100% at 2,500 km (3x faster unlubed), then skips teeth at full throttle on `Full`. Belt, shaft, CVT and hub drives are exempt.

[247] 4T oil lasts 4 engine-hours off-road, 10 on street; overdue on `Full`, `Engine` loses 1 health per 2 running minutes. Pads last 0.2 MJ of friction braking per kg of vehicle-plus-rider mass per axle (regen excluded); below 15%, brake torque falls to 65% with grinding. Air filters clog above 20 km/h on Sand, Ground or Gravel, filling in 90 minutes (2x in another rider's dust) and costing up to 12% power. EV `Wear.PackSoH` loses 0.1% per full cycle (1.5x when 60 kW charging past 80%), floored at 80%, scaling usable capacity. Costs: tire 0.8% of price ($30-$250), lube $15, chain kit 1.2% (min $40), oil $40 + $0.02/cc, pads $35/axle ($15 pedal), filter $25, pack 30%; workshops 60%. HUD: amber wrench below 25%, red below 10%, toasts at most every 10 minutes; the phone Garage app lists all ([431]-[450]).

[248] Never trust the client. Store [75] 10 Hz `ChassisRoot` position and velocity samples for 3 s per vehicle in a 30-entry ring `buffer`. For each `Vehicle_Crash`, locate the reported time plus `Player:GetNetworkPing()` in history and measure delta-v between samples 200 ms either side. Reject reports missing history by over 25% in pre-impact speed or 15+ studs in position; otherwise damage uses J = min(J_reported, m_total x (1.3 x measured delta-v + 2 m/s)). Unreported horizontal delta-v above 8 m/s within 0.2 s (braking reaches 2.5 m/s; landings are vertical) still applies as `HullImpact`; above 11 m/s the server disables the seat weld and the client ejects. No-crash rewards (stunt landings, clean races, delivery condition) pay only after 1 s without a validated crash. Acceptance test: a client hiding all reports while ramming walls still takes damage and ejects.

[249] Register `Characters`, `Passive` and `Ghost` in `VehicleService:Init` beside the [48] groups with `PhysicsService:RegisterCollisionGroup` (guarded by `IsCollisionGroupRegistered`), wire them with `PhysicsService:CollisionGroupSetCollidable` and assign parts only through `BasePart.CollisionGroup`; `PlayerService` puts every character part, later accessories included, in `Characters`. `VehicleHull`, `Characters` and `Ragdoll` collide with each other and the world, so player contact is real by default; `Passive` and `Ghost` collide only with `Default` and `VehicleDeck`, and a passive or ghosted rider's ragdoll stays in that group. Passive Mode is a free-roam phone toggle: after a 5 s countdown with nobody within 15 studs, character and vehicle move to `Passive`, a shield icon shows, and the toggle locks for 60 s. Races, events and Street Rules ([426]) disable it. Acceptance test: a passive and a normal rider passing through each other at 60 km/h both stay mounted.

[250] Anti-griefing lives in `AntiCheatService`. Hulls are `CanTouch` false ([48]), so a player contact is a [248]-validated impact interpolated within 8 studs of another rider's `ChassisRoot`. In free roam it is a ram when closing speed exceeds 8 m/s and the prior 1.5 s show the offender faster and aimed within 30 deg of the victim. Each ram adds closing speed / 8 points, decaying 1 point per 30 s. Three rams on one victim within 120 s, or 6 points, ghost the offender: vehicle and character join `Ghost` for 120 s (300 s, then 600 s on repeats), observers render them at `LocalTransparencyModifier` 0.5, with a toast. Victims' flagged-ram damage is repaired free; each ghosting calls `AnalyticsService:LogCustomEvent`. Acceptance test: the third ram ghosts the rammer and fully repairs the victim; 30 minutes of group riding raises zero flags.

## SECTION 11 - AUDIO & SOUND DESIGN (Paragraphs 251-270)

[251] You will build all game audio inside the client `AudioController` ModuleScript booted by `Main.client.luau`, split into the child ModuleScripts below, each `--!strict` with typed exports. `:Init()` never yields: it finds the [266] `SoundGroup`s with `FindFirstChild` (never `WaitForChild`) and reads `Config/SoundIds`. `:Start()` binds one `RunService.PreRender` callback wrapped in `debug.profilebegin("AudioController.Step")` that updates the local vehicle every frame and remote vehicles at the [480] tier rates (30 Hz within 150 studs, 10 Hz to 500), costing under 0.6 ms per frame on a mid-range phone. The server never creates or plays vehicle audio. Classic `Sound` objects are the reliable default; an `AudioPlayer`/`AudioEmitter`/`AudioListener`/`Wire` path is optional, only if you know that API thoroughly, behind `UseAudioApi = false`. Engine, event and tire audio ship in Phase 3, ambience and reverb in Phase 7, the radio in Phase 12.
```
AudioController
  EngineAudio   ElectricAudio   EventAudio
  TireAudio     WindAudio       AmbienceAudio
  RadioAudio    VoiceBudget     Doppler
  Mixer         OneShotPool
```

[252] You will never invent a sound asset ID: each lives in `Config/SoundIds.luau` as `0` with a descriptive comment, read only through `SoundIds.get(path): string?` ([38]); missing layers skip and neighbour weights renormalise, so silent vehicles never throw. One `Audio.Profile` may serve two vehicles of one layout and similar displacement (Ryujin RX-450F and Rattler 450R), split by `Audio.EqPreset`. Impacts (`VehiclesSFX`): crashes layer a body thud, a surface hit picked by the [59] SurfaceId (plastic crack, metal clang, dirt thump, splash) and a slide scrape, scaled by the [236] impulse J; ragdoll tumbles use soft gear-and-cloth hits, never bone or pain sounds ([240]). Foley: per-surface footsteps every 0.75 m, pump-nozzle click and flow, charge-connector clunk, workshop impact wrench, wash-bay spray. UI: click, confirm, error, cash-in, level-up and toast cues. Acceptance test: with every ID at `0`, all vehicles spawn and ride error-free.
```luau
Engines = { single4t_250 = { Layers = { -- 250 cc race 4T single
  { Sample_rpm = 1900, On = 0, Off = 0 }, -- idle; low 3000, mid 4800, high 7600, redline 12000 follow
} } },
Tires = { Asphalt = 0, Dirt = 0, Gravel = 0, Mud = 0, Sand = 0, Grass = 0, Snow = 0, Wood = 0 },
Horns = { Bell = 0, Beeper = 0, Moto = 0, AirHorn = 0 },
Radio = { Drive = { 0, 0 }, Dirt = { 0, 0 }, Chill = { 0, 0 } },
Impacts = { BodyThud = { 0, 0 }, Plastic = { 0, 0 }, Metal = { 0, 0 }, Dirt = { 0, 0 }, Splash = { 0 } },
Foley = { Steps = { Asphalt = 0, Dirt = 0, Wood = 0, Snow = 0 }, Nozzle = 0, ChargeClunk = 0, Wrench = 0, Wash = 0 },
UI = { Click = 0, Confirm = 0, Error = 0, CashIn = 0, LevelUp = 0, Toast = 0 },
```

[253] Each combustion profile holds 4 to 6 seamless loops recorded at steady idle, low, mid, high and redline RPMs, each with an on-load variant (pulling under throttle) and an off-load variant (closed-throttle engine braking at the same RPM), so 8-12 `Sound` instances per vehicle. When a vehicle model streams in, `EngineAudio` creates them once into that vehicle's `Trove` with `Looped = true` and `Volume = 0`, properties set before `Parent`, on the canonical `Exhaust1` attachment (fallback `ChassisRoot`), then in a `task.spawn` preloads them with `ContentProvider:PreloadAsync` inside `pcall` and starts them together; the local vehicle keeps all playing so crossfades never wait on playback start ([267] governs remote tiers). Loops are 1-3 s, cut at zero crossings, holding a whole number of firing cycles. Acceptance test: holding any steady RPM for 30 s yields no click, gap or phasing.

[254] Every frame `EngineAudio` turns RPM into layer gains. Layer i, sampled at r_i, plays at `PlaybackSpeed = rpm / r_i` clamped to 0.75-1.35, so it is audible only between 0.75·r_i and 1.35·r_i. The crossfade band between layers i and i+1 is [max(r_i, 0.75·r_(i+1)), min(r_(i+1), 1.35·r_i)]; with t the 0-1 position inside it, apply equal-power gains g_i = cos(t·π/2) and g_(i+1) = sin(t·π/2), so g_i² + g_(i+1)² = 1 and loudness never dips mid-sweep. Outside bands one layer plays at gain 1, holding the end layers with clamped speed. A load-time validator rejects adjacent ratios above 1.6 or bands narrower than 10% of r_i. Example, Ryujin RX-250F: layers at 1,900, 3,000, 4,800, 7,600 and 12,000 RPM; at its 13,500 limiter the top layer runs at 1.125.

[255] Engine load L blends the variants: L_target = throttle × (0.4 + 0.6·drive), where drive is clutch engagement in gear (0 in neutral, so free revs sound 40% loaded), smoothed with a 60 ms attack and 150 ms release. On-load layers receive sqrt(L) and off-load layers sqrt(1 − L), multiplied by the RPM gains of [254]. Profile volume = `Audio.BaseVolume` × (0.55 + 0.45·L) × (0.75 + 0.25·rpm/redline). Each on-load layer carries one `EqualizerSoundEffect` set from `Audio.EqPreset` (dB; Thump: LowGain +4, MidGain 0, HighGain −3; Scream: LowGain −2, MidGain +1, HighGain +3) and one `DistortionSoundEffect` with `Level` = 0.05 + 0.25·L. Exhausts ([372]) add +2 dB LowGain and +0.05 `Level` at Pro, scaled by the tier's 0.30/0.55/0.80/1.00 effect fraction. Acceptance test: rolling off at constant RPM changes timbre over 150 ms with no click or gap.

[256] Check each recording's firing frequency: f = rpm/60 for a 2-stroke single, f = rpm/120 × cylinders for 4-strokes, matching `Audio.FiringIntervals_deg` ([200]). 2-strokes (Kensei K2-125 and K2-250, Austral EX-300, Trialtek TR-300, Corsa Zip 50) idle with an uneven ring-ding near 1,680 RPM (28 Hz, [151]-[175]) from four-stroking misfires: add ±40 RPM jitter at 3-6 Hz to the idle layer only. Off the pipe they sound thin; inside the powerband (K2-125 9,500-12,500, K2-250 7,000-10,500 RPM) the chamber braaps, so each 2T profile centres one layer in the band and adds +3 dB HighGain from `PowerbandHit` until RPM exits it. 4-stroke singles (Gopher 200, Rascal 110, Ryujin Trail 230, RX-250F, RX-450F, Gremlin 125, Corsa Riva 150, Austral SM-700, every Rattler, Ranchhand 570) need distinct idle thumps, 16 per second at 1,920 RPM. Acceptance test: testers tell K2-250 from RX-250F blind.

[257] Multi-cylinder character comes from firing intervals, so record engines of the same layout and never pitch-shift singles into twins. Thornton Cafe 1200: 270-degree parallel twin, 270/450 gaps, loping 1,050 RPM idle ([181]). Liberty Bobber 1200, Chopper Custom and Glide Bagger: 45-degree V-twin, 315/405 gaps, potato-potato idle at 950 RPM with LowGain +5 dB and a ±30 RPM wobble at 1.5 Hz. Alpenrad GX-1300: boxer twin, even rocking 1,000 RPM idle, shaft whir tied to wheel speed instead of chain noise. Kensei Street 900: triple, 240-degree gaps, hoarse induction howl. Inline-4s (Ryujin RS-600, Kensei ZR-6S Stunt, Hayate 1300, ZR-1000): silky 1,200 RPM idle ([88]) rising to a scream; the RS-600 at 16,000 RPM fires 533 Hz, so top layers take HighGain +3 dB and minimal distortion. Veloce Furia V4: 90/200/90/340 big-bang growl under a high-RPM wail.

[258] Hauler HD and Hauler Van: diesel idle near 700 RPM, injector clatter at gain 1 − 0.8·L, a turbo whistle at `PlaybackSpeed` 0.6 + 0.9·b and volume 0.5·b, where b = smoothed throttle × clamp((rpm − 1,200)/800, 0, 1) (τ 0.6 s up, 0.3 s down), a lift-off whoosh, never pops, and an exhaust-brake drone in tow mode ([89]). Dunecat XR Turbo: the same whistle driven by its [96] boost, plus a blow-off on `BlowOff`. Wavecat 300: supercharger whine pitched by crank RPM × drive ratio, volume following the [96] boost 0.9 × (rpm/8,000)², hull-muffled engine (HighGain −8 dB), impeller rush by pump RPM, idle exhaust bubbling, cavitation hiss when the intake breaches. Frostline 850: 2T drone held near 8,000 RPM by the CVT under speed-scaled track slap and ski hiss. Remotes recompute boost from replicated RPM and throttle.

[259] `ElectricAudio` serves every electric vehicle from the `CoM` attachment. Motor whine uses two loops recorded at 500 Hz and 1,400 Hz: target f = motorRPM/60 × `Audio.PolePairs` × `Audio.WhineHarmonic` (default 6, the 3-phase torque-ripple order), each loop at `PlaybackSpeed` = f / f_sample clamped 0.5-2.0, equal-power crossfaded across their 700-1,000 Hz overlap, volume (0.2 + 0.8·|torque|/peak) × min(1, f/250). Controller: 100 Hz hum plus PWM hiss scaled by battery current. Regen above 5% of peak power keeps pitch on motor RPM but crossfades over 0.2 s to a rougher regen-whine variant scaled by regen power. Drivetrain: belt hum, helical gear whine or chain whine per its [126]-[150] entry, pitched by tooth-pass frequency; hub e-bikes stay near silent, the Ridgeline Summit E adds chainring hum. Gyro EUC-20 ([86]): 2 Hz beeps from 90% of tilt-back speed, 4 Hz past it.

[260] `EventAudio` plays `Powertrain.Events` locally and `PowertrainReplica` events remotely ([100]), plus edges of the [36] clutch and horn bits, never raw input; one-shots come from `OneShotPool` (48 pooled Sounds, round-robin, [479]). `Shift`: upshifts dip on-load gain to 0.3 for 90-130 ms with a class-sized clunk, downshifts a lighter clunk, neutral to first at standstill a heavy clunk; `MissedShift` a hollow false-neutral clack. Clutch-bit edges click the lever at 0.3 volume. Bikes whose stat block lists a quickshifter ([176]-[205]) mute on-load gain for the [200] cut (80 ms at 6,000 RPM to 45 ms at 14,000), no clunk, 35% chance of one crackle; `Blip` plays a 90-140 ms on-load flare. Horn ([199], 6 s cap): bell on pedal bikes, beeper on light EVs, moto horn, air horn on Haulers. Acceptance test: remote shifts are heard within 150 ms.

[261] Decel crackle: with throttle below 0.05 and RPM above 45% of redline, schedule pops as a Poisson process, rate λ = `Audio.PopRate` × (rpm/redline)² per second (6 stock 4T, 12 with Race or Pro exhaust, 0 for diesels and 2Ts, which spit instead), each gap −ln(u)/λ from a per-vehicle `Random.new(seed)`, picking among 4-6 pop samples at ±8% pitch; `Backfire` adds one loud bang. `Limiter`: each [89] `"Hard"` cut plays a short stutter and gates on-load gain to 0.2 for 25 ms; remotes repeat it at 20 Hz while the limiter bit is set; `"Soft"` limiters just flatten. Per [90], `KickStart` plays a kick thunk then a catch or cough, `Start` a starter whirr, `PullStart` a recoil-cord pull. `Stall`: a sputter while layers fade over 0.4 s. Acceptance test: 60 s of stock-4T overrun at 80% redline yields 180-280 pops.

[262] `TireAudio` reads each wheel's SurfaceId from [59] through `WeatherQuery.cosmeticSurface` (soaked dirt reads Mud, [333]) plus `VehicleController`'s slip values; it never raycasts. Loops on the wheel attachments map to 8 rolling sounds: Asphalt (also Concrete, Pavement, Brick, Cobblestone, Metal, Rock), Dirt (PackedDirt, Ground, unknown), Gravel (Pebble), Mud, Sand, Grass (LeafyGrass), Snow (Ice, Glacier) and Wood (WoodPlanks). Per axle, volume = clamp(v/25 m/s, 0, 1)^0.8 × load/staticLoad and `PlaybackSpeed` = 0.8 + 0.4·clamp(v/35 m/s, 0, 1); surface changes crossfade over 0.15 s, and paved loops add a wet hiss above wetness 0.2. Paved squeal s = max(smoothstep(0.08, 0.25, |slip ratio|), smoothstep(6°, 12°, |slip angle|)) sets volume, with pitch 0.9 + 0.3·s; loose surfaces scrub instead. Roost on Dirt, Mud, Sand, Gravel and Snow: slip ratio above 0.2 with throttle above 0.5 adds a debris-spray loop and pebble ticks every 40-150 ms.

[263] Suspension: above 1.5 m/s wheel compression velocity, play a damper clunk at volume clamp((v − 1.5)/3, 0, 1); when travel passes 95% of `Travel_mm`, play a bottom-out thud scaled by impact speed plus a frame clank above 4 m/s, debounced 250 ms per wheel. Chain slap: on Dirt and Gravel, swingarm vertical acceleration above 25 m/s² fires slaps at most every 80 ms, chain drive only. Peg scrape: from `Chassis.ScrapeLean_deg` ([202]; default `MaxLean_deg` − 3) loop a metal scrape on the low-side peg, volume rising over the next 4 degrees, then a hard-part grind past MaxLean. Pedal bikes: freehub ticks while coasting at rear-wheel RPM/60 × `Audio.EngagementPoints` per second (36 on the Ridgeline Gravity DH, none on the Ridgeline Fixie). Crash impacts ([231]-[250]) use `VehiclesSFX`. Acceptance test: a cased 20 m double plays exactly one bottom-out per wheel.

[264] Wind scales with dynamic pressure: volume = clamp((v / 160 km/h)², 0, 1), so 0.25 at 80 km/h and full at 160, on low-buffet and high-whistle loops with `PlaybackSpeed` = 0.85 + 0.35·clamp(v / 160 km/h, 0, 1), where v is airspeed. Naked and pedal bikes add a 3-7 Hz buffet modulating volume ±15%; faired sportbikes use 0.8× wind with less buffet. Helmet POV ([119]): tween an `EqualizerSoundEffect` on the `Engines`, `VehiclesSFX` and `Ambience` groups over 0.25 s to HighGain −12 dB, MidGain −4 dB, LowGain +2 dB and swap to a helmet-wind loop; leaving the mode reverses both. Rain on helmet: in Helmet POV with rain intensity r above 0 ([336]), loop rain taps at volume r × (0.6 + 0.4·clamp(v / 60 km/h, 0, 1)). Acceptance test: at 200 km/h in Helmet POV, wind masks engine detail.

[265] Every positional `Sound` uses `RollOffMode = Enum.RollOffMode.InverseTapered` with `RollOffMinDistance`/`RollOffMaxDistance` in studs from `Audio.RollOffClass`: Pedal 4/80, LightEV 6/120, EMoto 10/220, GasSmall 14/400, Quad and UTV 16/450, Truck 18/450, GasLoud, Street, Snow and Water 20/500, never past the [267] silence line. Set `SoundService.DopplerScale` to 0 in Studio and simulate Doppler for remote vehicles and traffic only (the local vehicle keeps factor 1): with d the unit vector from source to listener (the camera), v_s = v_source·d and v_l = v_listener·d (velocities from 0.1 s position deltas), factor = (c − v_l)/(c − v_s) with c = 1,225 studs/s (343 m/s). Clamp to 0.8-1.25, smooth with an 80 ms time constant, and multiply into every layer's `PlaybackSpeed` after the [254] clamp. Acceptance test: a 200 km/h pass-by falls from about 1.19 to 0.86.

[266] Create five `SoundGroup` instances under `SoundService` in Studio: `Engines`, `VehiclesSFX`, `Ambience`, `Music` and `UI`, base volumes 0.8, 0.7, 0.6, 0.5 and 0.6; `Mixer` creates any missing group at boot with one warning. Set `Sound.SoundGroup` at creation; an ungrouped Sound trips a debug assertion. The [446] Audio tab offers Master plus one 0-100% slider per group; apply volume = base × (slider/100)² × (master/100)² for a perceptual curve, saved with the profile settings ([451]-[475]) and applied before the first vehicle spawns. Mix rules: race countdowns duck `Music` by 6 dB; the map and pause menu duck `Engines` and `Ambience` by 6 dB over 0.3 s; `UI` is never ducked. Non-diegetic sounds (radio, UI, stingers) play 2D from Sounds parented to `SoundService`. Acceptance test: every slider at 0 silences its group instantly.

[267] `VoiceBudget` ranks vehicles by camera distance every 0.25 s with 10% hysteresis. Local tier: the player's vehicle runs every layer, event, tire, wind and effect. Near tier: the nearest 7 other vehicles (6 on mobile), NPC riders ([429]) included, play only nonzero-gain layers (at most 4), events and one tire loop, without distortion. Far tier: anything else within 500 studs, and traffic cars ([416]-[430]) always, plays one loop, the mid on-load layer at rpm/r_mid clamped 0.5-2.0. Beyond 500 studs ([480] Tier 3) set `Playing = false`, not volume 0. Cap: 96 playing Sounds (64 on mobile), dropping the farthest first. Remotes use only [36] `Vehicle_State` data via `PowertrainReplica` ([100]); their throttle decays to 0 after 1.5 s without packets, since steady senders drop to 2 Hz. Acceptance test: 30 bikes at the Port meet lot never exceed the cap.

[268] `AmbienceAudio` tests the camera position every 0.5 s against parts tagged `AmbienceZone` (attributes `ZoneId`, `Priority`; point-in-box math, never `Touched`), crossfading over 3 s, at most two beds plus four spot emitters every 8-30 s, 40-150 studs away. Day/night variants crossfade over 4 real minutes by the replicated clock: plaza walla ([430]) and Harbor City traffic hum, Port horns and container clanks, Throttle Park distant engines, Pinecrest birds by day and crickets and owls by night, Red Mesa wind, Sunset Beach waves and gulls, Lake Serene lapping water; weather adds rain or sandstorm beds and [332] thunder. `ReverbZone` parts (Interstate 90 tunnel, Harbor City parking garages) put a `ReverbSoundEffect` on `Engines` and `VehiclesSFX`: tunnel `DecayTime` 2.8 s, garage 1.6 s with `Density` 0.9, `WetLevel` −80 to −6 dB over 0.4 s. Acceptance test: entering the tunnel audibly lengthens engine tails.

[269] The radio uses only Creator Store audio that Roblox marks as licensed for use in experiences, or tracks the user created and uploaded; never user-entered IDs, boombox inputs or ripped music. Ship three stations (Drive, Dirt, Chill), each a `SoundIds.Radio` playlist of `0` placeholders, played 2D through `Music` while seated or from the phone Radio app ([431]-[450]), shuffled without repeating the last 3 tracks, crossfaded over 1.5 s, the next track preloaded with `ContentProvider:PreloadAsync` inside `pcall`; a track that fails to load is skipped after 5 s with one warning. Settings offer Radio On/Off, applied instantly. Only the Liberty Glide Bagger fairing speaker is heard by others ([202]: 50-stud `RollOffMaxDistance`, volume cap 0.6), and only by listeners with "Hear other players' radios" on. Acceptance test: Radio Off leaves zero playing `Music` Sounds.

[270] Tell the user how to get engine audio legally: record a machine they own or may record with the owner's permission, ridden by an adult on private land or a dyno, never on public roads; or buy a sound library licensed for games and keep the receipt; never rip games, videos or films. Recording: one windscreened microphone 1 m behind the exhaust, 45 degrees off-axis, one near the intake, 48 kHz 24-bit WAV; hold each layer RPM 10 s loaded (dyno or uphill in gear), then on closed throttle (downhill in gear). Cut loops at zero crossings, match loudness per profile, export `.ogg` (MP3 padding breaks loops). Upload in Creator Hub under the account or group owning the experience, or grant it permission; audio must pass moderation within the monthly upload quota; then paste approved IDs into `Config/SoundIds.luau`.

## SECTION 12 - OPEN WORLD MAP I: LAYOUT, SCALE, ROADS & URBAN REGIONS (Paragraphs 271-295)

[271] You will build the urban half of the world in two passes: Phase 6 blockout (anchored Parts at final road geometry, the road graph, streaming flags) and Phase 7 art pass (meshes, materials, props); natural regions follow in [296]-[320]. Compass: +X east, -X west, -Z north, +Z south (an unrotated CFrame looks toward -Z), used by minimap, GPS and signage. The world is 16,000 x 16,000 studs centered on the origin, all gameplay inside +/-8,000, sea level Y = 0; the south coast runs near Z = +6,100 from X -1,500 eastward, the east coast near X = +6,800, and ocean continues past both edges. Author every real-world dimension in meters in `ReplicatedStorage/Shared/Config/RoadStandards.luau` (`LaneWidth_m = 3.5`) and convert only through `Units`. Acceptance test: no drivable part lies outside +/-8,000 studs.

[272] Urban footprints (studs): Old Town X -800..800, Z -700..700 at Y 15; Harbor City X 1,500..3,500, Z -600..1,200 at Y 15; Maple Heights X -1,100..1,100, Z -4,200..-2,800 at Y 40 (Hillside Estate Y 190); Port X 4,900..6,700, Z 300..2,300 at Y 8; Skyline Airfield X 5,200..6,400, Z -4,850..-1,450 at Y 10; each canon center lies inside its box. Main Street runs east into Harbor Avenue; Maple Parkway runs north on X = 0 over Interstate 90 to Maple Heights and Highway 9; Park Road runs west past Throttle Park to Highway 9; Boardwalk Boulevard runs south over Highway 9 to Sunset Beach; Port Boulevard crosses the Gateway Bridge to the Port; Serene Drive spurs to Lake Serene. Outer regions front Highway 9 or a short spur; Old Town and Harbor City reach it via these roads.

[273] Landmarks visible across the map: the Old Town clock tower, 45 m (161 studs); Meridian Tower, downtown's tallest at 174 m (621 studs), where Harbor Avenue ends east of the river, with red aviation beacons flashing 30 times per minute; the Gateway Bridge pylon, 85 m (304 studs); four ship-to-shore cranes, 72 m (257 studs); the Maple Heights water tower, 40 m (143 studs); the airfield control tower, 30 m (107 studs); plus the summit, lighthouse and dam of [296]-[320]. Sight lines: southbound Maple Parkway ends on the clock tower, eastbound Harbor Avenue on Meridian Tower, eastbound Interstate 90 crests near X = -1,200 to reveal the skyline, and westbound roads end on Pinecrest. Acceptance test: an edit-mode script raycasting from highway-edge points every 200 studs sees two unobstructed landmark tops from 95% of points outside the tunnel.

[274] Road hierarchy, right-hand traffic; `SpeedLimit_kmh` takes only 24.14, 40.23, 56.33, 72.42, 88.51 or 104.61 (signed 15/25/35/45/55/65 mph). Interstate 90: 3 lanes each way (2 west of Exit 1), 65 mph, Concrete, 3.0 m (10.7-stud) outside and 1.2 m (4.3-stud) inside shoulders, Jersey median barrier 0.81 m high on a 0.61 m base, 107 studs wide. Highway 9 (expressway): 2 lanes each way, 55 mph, Asphalt, 2.4 m (8.6-stud) outside and 0.6 m inside shoulders, a 4.8 m (17.1-stud) raised grass median with left-turn bays at at-grade junctions, 89 studs wide. Arterials: 2 lanes each way, 3.5 m center turn lane, 1.8 m (6.4-stud) bike lanes, 35-45 mph, 75 studs curb to curb. Locals: 1 lane each way plus 2.4 m parking lanes, 25 mph, 42 studs. Alleys: 4.5 m (16 studs), unmarked, 15 mph. Ramps: 1-2 lanes, 25-45 mph.

[275] Urban cross-sections: lanes 3.5 m (12.5 studs); sidewalks 3.0 m (10.7 studs) downtown, 2.5 m (8.9) in Old Town, 2.0 m (7.1) in the suburbs plus a 1.5 m (5.4) grass verge; curbs 15 cm (0.54 studs) high, separate anchored Parts so suspension rays see a true step. Pavement carries a 2% crown: two halves tilted 1.15 degrees toward the gutters. Every corner gets two perpendicular 1:12 curb ramps (0.54 studs of rise over 6.4) and every driveway an apron, so wheels roll up while BMX riders can still hop the full curb. Paved surfaces use only Asphalt, Concrete, Pavement, Brick, Cobblestone, Granite, WoodPlanks or Metal (grips in [59]); urban `MaterialVariant` names never equal a SurfaceId (`OldTownBrick`, not `Brick`), so `Surface.resolve` falls through to base-material grip. Acceptance test: a lint flags any other material in a `Roads` folder.

[276] Geometry: maximum grade 4% highways, 6% ramps and parking ramps, 8% arterials, 12% residential hills. Superelevation e max: 0.08 highways, 0.04 arterials, 0 locals; bank = atan(e) (4.57 degrees at 0.08), run out over L = w x e / 0.005 (400 studs for 25 rotated studs). Minimum radius R = v^2 / (g(e + f)) in meters: 110 km/h, f 0.10 gives 529 m (1,888 studs) for Interstate 90; 90 km/h, f 0.13 gives 303 m (1,084 studs) for Highway 9; 40 km/h loops, f 0.20 give 45 m, so build 50 m (179 studs). Crest curves use stopping-sight K values (radius = 100 x K meters): 74 Interstate, 39 Highway, 17 arterial; since staying planted needs only R > v^2/g (703 m at 299 km/h), no such crest launches anyone. Overpass clearance: 5.0 m (17.9 studs).

[277] Intersections: curb-return radii are 4.5 m (16 studs) on locals, 9 m (32 studs) on arterials, 15 m (54 studs) on Port truck routes. Keep a 10 m (36-stud) corner sight triangle clear above 0.9 m and set stop bars 1.2 m (4.3 studs) before crosswalks. Build three roundabouts: single-lane Founders Circle at Old Town's north gateway, where Maple Parkway begins, and Maple Circle at the Maple Heights entry (36 m / 129-stud inscribed diameter, 5.5 m circulatory lane, 4 m truck apron raised 7.5 cm, splitter islands, yield lines, 30-40 km/h entry), plus two-lane Harbor Circle on Harbor Avenue at Harbor City's west gateway (55 m / 196 studs). Ring nodes use `Kind = "Roundabout"`. Acceptance test: the Hauler HD towing the 3-Rail Bike Trailer ([176]-[205]) completes every Port turn and roundabout touching no curb except truck aprons.

[278] Lane markings: white RGB 235,235,230 for lane lines and right edges, yellow 245,185,30 for centerlines and left edges of divided roads, 0.15 m (0.54 studs) wide; dashes follow the US 10 ft line plus 30 ft gap (3.05 + 9.14 m = 10.9 + 32.7 studs); stop bars are 0.6 m (2.1 studs). On straight segments, paint markings as a transparent-background `Texture` on each road Part's Top face, `StudsPerTileU` equal to that Part's width and `StudsPerTileV` = 43.5, with zero extra geometry and no z-fighting. Curves, ramps and roundabouts are Blender `MeshPart` road pieces with markings baked into their `SurfaceAppearance` color map while `Material` stays Asphalt or Concrete for grip. Arrows, bike symbols and gore chevrons are `Decal`s on 0.05-stud plates raised 0.02 studs, with `CanCollide`, `CanQuery`, `CanTouch` and `CastShadow` off. Texture IDs are 0 placeholders in `Config/AssetIds`.

[279] Signage uses MUTCD-style shapes and sizes, never real route shields or logos: stop signs are 750 mm octagons (2.7 studs), speed limit signs 600 x 750 mm, bottom edges 2.1 m (7.5 studs) above the sidewalk; overhead guide signs hang on gantries with 5.3 m (18.9-stud) clearance and 400 mm (1.4-stud) capitals. Render text with a `SurfaceGui` (`SizingMode = PixelsPerStud`, `PixelsPerStud = 50`, `MaxDistance = 400`) so names stay editable. Highway 9 uses a county-style pentagon marker and Interstate 90 a custom shield shape, never the trademarked Interstate shield. Number exits 1-5 west to east as in [281]; each gets a 1/4 MILE advance sign 1,437 studs before the gore, a 1/2 MILE sign at 2,874 studs where spacing allows, and a gore sign; both termini get END signs naming the connecting roads. Sign posts use `CollisionFidelity = Box`.

[280] Traffic lights: each signalized intersection is one Model with mast arms carrying `IntersectionId`, `Cycle_s` (60-90), `Offset` and `MainSplit_pct`. Signal heads (300 mm lenses = 1.07 studs, bottom 4.6 m / 16.4 studs above the road) are tagged `TrafficLight` with `Approach` (N/E/S/W) and `Phase` (1-4) attributes and lens Parts `Red`, `Amber`, `Green` and optional `LeftArrow`. Amber lasts 1.0 + v / 6.1 s, v the limit in m/s (3.6 s at 35 mph, 4.3 s at 45 mph), then 2.0 s all-red; `Offset` = distance / progression speed makes green waves on Harbor City avenues. State is a pure function of `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` in `Shared/SignalClock.luau`, so no per-change remotes; rendering and AI obedience live in [416]-[430]. Crosswalks use continental bars 0.6 m wide, 0.6 m apart, 3.0 m long; `PedSignal`-tagged heads count down crossing length / 1.07 m/s.

[281] Highway 9 is a rounded rectangle about 35,300 studs around (6.7-minute lap at 55 mph): legs at Z -4,400, Z 5,200 (coastal), X -4,300 and X 4,800, corner radius 1,200. Interstate 90 runs west along Z = -1,200 from Exit 5 (terminus trumpet, X 5,600: Airfield Road north, Port Boulevard south) through Exits 4 Highway 9 East (cloverleaf, X 4,800), 3 Harbor City (diamond, X 2,400), 2 Maple Parkway (diamond, X 0) and 1 Highway 9 West (cloverleaf, X -4,300), then climbs at 3% into a 1,500-stud twin-bore tunnel under the Pinecrest foothills (X -5,500 to -7,000; 2 lanes per bore, 5.0 m clearance) and ends at X -7,200 in a signalized T: Canyon Road north, Mesa Road south. Ramps get 536-stud (150 m) decel and 1,250-stud (350 m) accel lanes; closer ramps merge into auxiliary lanes.

[282] Author the road graph exactly as below. Place nodes at every intersection, lane-count change, ramp gore, bridge end and tunnel portal, plus shape nodes on curves spaced L = sqrt(8 x R x 0.5) studs so the polyline strays under 0.5 studs (27 studs at R 179, 69 at R 1,200). Every edge polyline runs along the left edge of its forward lanes, so lane i's center lies (i - 0.5) x 12.5 studs to its right and backward lanes mirror to the left; divided roads, turn-lane arterials, ramps and roundabout rings are therefore one-way edges (`LanesBack` = 0), one per carriageway. Beams stay enabled in Studio for visual editing and are disabled at runtime; `Graph` carries `NodeCount` and `EdgeCount` so clients confirm full replication before parsing.
```
Workspace/RoadGraph               Folder
  Graph                           Model, ModelStreamingMode = Persistent
    NodeHost                      Part: Anchored, Transparency 1, no collide/query/touch
      N_OT_0001 ...               Attachment: Kind, Region, IntersectionId
                                  Kind = Intersection|Shape|Gore|Roundabout|Portal|BridgeEnd|LaneChange
    Edges                         Folder
      E_00001 ...                 Beam: Attachment0, Attachment1, RoadClass, RoadName,
                                  LanesFwd (A0 to A1), LanesBack, SpeedLimit_kmh,
                                  Surface, Bridge, Tunnel, Closed (set by RaceService)
```

[283] `ReplicatedStorage/Shared/RoadGraph.luau` (`--!strict`) parses the graph once into typed arrays (`export type RoadClass = "Interstate" | "Highway" | "Ramp" | "Arterial" | "Local" | "Alley" | "Garage" | "Trail"`; each Beam yields one directed `Edge = { From: number, To: number, Class: RoadClass, Lanes: number, SpeedLimit_kmh: number, Length_studs: number }` per direction that has lanes), adjacency lists and a 256-stud spatial hash serving `nearestEdge(position)`. GPS A* uses cost = length / speed limit ([341]-[365]); traffic spawns on and follows lanes ([416]-[430]). `RoadGraph.validate()` reports missing attachments, duplicate IDs, edges shorter than 4 studs, limits outside the [274] table, nodes whose downward ray misses a road part within 3 studs, and a directed graph that is not strongly connected. Acceptance test: validate returns zero errors and GPS routes from the spawn plaza to the node nearest each of the ten region centers.

[284] Old Town core: the spawn plaza at (0, 15, 0) is a 160 x 160-stud Brick square with eight `SpawnLocation`s around a central fountain. The brick clock tower stands on its north edge at (0, 15, -95) with four 3 m (10.7-stud) faces whose hands turn client-side from in-game time, the hour hand twice and the minute hand 24 times per 48 real minutes ([321]-[340]). Throttle Works fills the east side: a converted two-story brick-and-steel factory 40 x 25 m with a glass showroom (24 display plinths, 4.5 m ceiling), four service bays with 3 x 3.5 m roll-up doors and lift tables, one with a roller dyno ([366]-[390]), and a 40-slot used-market lot, tagged `Dealership` (`DealershipId = "ThrottleWorks"`, the [38] ID). The free Old Town Studio Garage (3 bays, `GaragePoint`, `PropertyId = "old_town_studio_garage"`) sits one block south on Rivet Lane.

[285] Old Town streets: Euro & Classic Motors occupies a 1920s brick building with arched windows and a checkerboard floor at about (-420, 15, 0) on Main Street (`DealershipId = "EuroClassic"`, 12 display bays). Main Street is Brick, the lanes ringing the plaza Cobblestone (both grip 0.85), through-streets Asphalt. Flush streetcar rails run down Main Street (Metal 0.70, 0.25-stud heads, 1,435 mm gauge = 5.12 studs), so a wheel crossing them at a shallow angle in full rain reads 0.49 grip. The Kickstand Diner, a stainless 1950s diner with a 30-space lot, anchors Main Street's west end at Park Road as the default meet spot. Blocks are 230 x 200 studs with 2-5-story buildings (ground floors 4.5 m, upper floors 3.5 m); 4.5 m alleys carry dumpsters, fire escapes, 1.2 m loading docks and one ledge-to-dock gap for BMX lines.

[286] Harbor City core: a 5 x 5 grid of 300 x 260-stud blocks; north-south avenues are arterials (Grand Avenue reaches Exit 3), east-west streets are locals, and Harbor Avenue, the east-west spine, is a median-divided boulevard. Build 22 high-rises of 20-45 floors (floor-to-floor 3.8 m = 13.6 studs, lobby 6 m = 21.4 studs) and about 90 mid- and low-rise buildings. Each high-rise lobby is modeled 15 m deep behind clear glass (reception desk, elevators, seating); six, Meridian Tower's included, have 3 m sliding doors riders can roll through onto polished-stone Concrete. Civic Plaza: four granite levels with 3, 6, 9 and 12-stair sets (17 cm risers = 0.61 studs, 28 cm treads = 1.0 stud), round 0.9 m handrails, 0.45 m Metal-edged ledges tagged `Grindable`, manual pads, planter gaps and 1:3 banks; scoring lives in [391]-[415].

[287] Harbor City garages: Harbor Center Garage (6 levels plus open roof, 70 x 50 m) and Riverside Garage (4 levels). Floor-to-floor is 3.2 m (11.4 studs), clear height at least 2.2 m (7.9 studs), stalls 2.7 x 5.5 m (9.6 x 19.6 studs), aisles 7.3 m (26 studs), Concrete with Metal expansion joints every 30 m. Up-ramps run at 5% (228 studs per level) with half-grade transitions at both ends so long-wheelbase bikes never scrape. The down-ramp is an external two-lane helix of 12 m (43-stud) centerline radius making one full turn per level (75.4 m per 3.2 m = 4.2%), which riders will loop endlessly. The rooftop lot (near Y 83, 1.07 m / 3.8-stud parapets, skyline views) is reachable only by ramp, a natural night meet spot. Rubber humps (7.5 cm) appear only in exit lanes.

[288] Dealers and river: Volt & Talon EV Center is a two-story glass cube (40 x 30 m) at about (1,900, 15, 150) with rooftop solar, an indoor test lane and four 20 kW plus two 60 kW VoltCharge stalls (`DealershipId = "VoltTalonEV"`). Ryujin-Kensei Motorsports (`DealershipId = "RyujinKensei"`), east of the river at about (3,350, 15, -300), has 10 plinths, four service bays and a glass-walled dyno room ([366]-[390]). Harbor River flows south in a concrete flood channel under Interstate 90, through downtown near X 3,150 (60 m = 214 studs wide), then east at Z 2,500 under Highway 9 to the sea. Bridges: a Union Street steel-grate truss (Metal, deliberately squirmy), a Harbor Avenue concrete arch and the cable-stayed Gateway Bridge on Port Boulevard. A riverwalk 1.5 m above the water, ramped every 150 m, is a bike path.

[289] Maple Heights: about 140 houses on curvilinear streets with six cul-de-sacs (14 m / 50-stud bulb radius); lots 18 x 36 m, 7.5 m setbacks, 3.6 m concrete driveways, street trees every 10 m. 12 archetypes x 3 palettes x mirroring give 72 variants; identical neighbors are a defect. Maple Heights House is 18 Birch Court at (300, 42, -3,650): two stories, 6-bay garage, workshop (`GaragePoint`, `PropertyId = "maple_heights_house"`). Maple Heights High has a 400 m track (36.5 m radius, 84.39 m straights, red Asphalt-base variant); Maple Park a pond, 3 m loop path and skate spot. Crestview Drive climbs at 10% with 15 m hairpins to Hillside Estate (-900, 190, -4,000; `PropertyId = "hillside_estate"`): villa, 15-bay garage and a gated 600 m PackedDirt track built with [299] `JumpDesigner` (two tabletops, step-up, rhythm lane, four berms, timing `Checkpoint`).

[290] Port & Industrial: the quay wall runs near X 6,700 at Y 8 under four rail-mounted ship-to-shore cranes. About 1,400 ISO containers share two meshes (40 ft = 43.5 x 8.7 x 9.25 studs; 20 ft = 21.6 long), stacked four high around 4 m lanes and a maze. Eight tilt-up warehouses (120 x 60 m) have 1.2 m loading docks that serve as kickers and Parts Runner `JobPickup` sites. Warehouse 7 (5,300, 8, 1,900) is the Port Warehouse: 20 bays, paint booth, dyno cell (`PropertyId = "port_warehouse"`). Hauler Truck & Trailer, with a loading-ramp demo lot, guards the Port Boulevard gate (5,050, 8, 700). The rail yard: eight Metal tracks (5.12-stud gauge), gated crossings, a spur bridging Port Boulevard. Pier 4 Lot (600 x 400 studs, 30 m sodium high-masts) hosts night street meets ([391]-[415]).

[291] Skyline Airfield: abandoned runway 18/36 at X = 6,000 from Z -1,600 to -4,800 (896 m), 30 m (107 studs) wide, faded Asphalt, cracked Concrete patches. Drag strip, south end: start line Z -1,800, two 7.3 m (26-stud) lanes, 1/8-mile finish at 718.4 studs (Z -2,518.4), 1/4-mile at 1,436.8 (Z -3,236.8), then 1,563 studs (438 m) of shutdown, enough to stop from 280 km/h at 0.8 g (385 m), ending in a sand trap and catch net. Pre-stage and stage beams sit 0.64 studs (7 in) apart, with a Christmas tree between lanes, a 71.9-stud (66 ft) speed trap before each finish, a burnout box and six 214-stud staging lanes on the parallel taxiway. Add a 400-seat grandstand, three arched 40 x 30 m hangars (Hangar 2 is the meet spot) and the control tower; race logic: [391]-[415].

[292] Fuel and charging: on every Interstate, Highway and Arterial edge, the forward road-graph distance to the next Pumpline and VoltCharge site never exceeds 1,800 studs (tunnel edges count zero), and same-brand sites on one road sit at least 1,200 apart; expect about 40 of each, 70% co-located. Highway 9 sites sit at median openings so both directions reach them; Interstate 90 uses exit-side sites and median service plazas with their own ramps. Kits: Pumpline Plaza (4.6 m / 16.4-stud canopy, four two-dispenser islands, store, air pump), Pumpline Express (two dispensers), VoltCharge Hub (four 20 kW, two 60 kW stalls), VoltCharge Curbside (two 20 kW sidewalk posts downtown, exempt from the minimum). Tag dispensers `GasPump` and stalls `ChargingStation` (`Power_kW`), each with a `ProximityPrompt` (`MaxActivationDistance = 8`); pricing in [341]-[365]. Acceptance test: `StationSpacingLint` (Dijkstra over those edges) reports zero violations.

[293] Collectibles: urban regions hold 36 graffiti tags (fictional `Decal` art tagged `GraffitiTag`) and 24 lost parts (carb, sprocket or piston meshes tagged `LostPart`), each with a unique `CollectibleId`; add both tags to `Util.Tags` (nature regions: [296]-[320]). Clients detect proximity and fire RemoteEvent `Collectible_Claim` (add to NetDefs); the server checks the character is within 12 studs and the item unclaimed before awarding ([341]-[365]). At least 30% require a riding skill (gap, wall ride, stair set); none require clipping or sit on private property. Hidden spots: an abandoned streetcar tunnel under Old Town via a 6% alley ramp, the Underpass DIY skate spot beneath the Exit 2 overpass, the 1:2 concrete banks of the Harbor River flood channel, a container-top line in the Port maze, the ramp-reached Hangar 3 mezzanine, and a Riverside Garage rooftop-to-office gap.

[294] Construction: put each urban region in `Workspace/World/<Region>` (OldTown, HarborCity, MapleHeights, Port, SkylineAirfield, plus Highways) with subfolders `Roads`, `Buildings`, `Props`, `Signals`, `Stations`, `Collectibles` and `Spawns`. Build from modular kits published as Packages (RoadKit, CurbKit, SidewalkKit, StreetPropKit, FacadeKit, StationKit, SignalKit) so one edit propagates everywhere; road pieces snap at 64-stud multiples via attachments `SnapA` and `SnapB`, and all static geometry is `Anchored`. Ship no `UnionOperation` in these folders (blockout unions become Blender MeshParts in Phase 7; the lint fails on any); MeshParts stay under 20,000 triangles with `CollisionFidelity` Box for props, Hull for simple shapes and PreciseConvexDecomposition only for drivable curved meshes. Set in Studio: `ModelStreamingMode` Atomic on every building, station, sign and signal assembly, Persistent only on `Graph` here; `LevelOfDetail` StreamingMesh on high-rises, bridges, cranes and towers so the skyline survives streaming out. StreamingEnabled values belong to [476]-[500].

[295] Density targets: Old Town about 140 buildings, Harbor City 22 high-rises plus about 90 others, Maple Heights about 140 houses, Port about 1,400 containers. Streetlights (tagged `StreetLight`) stand every 30 m (107 studs) on arterials and highways and every 45 m on locals; hydrants every 90 m; one bench, bin or newspaper-box cluster per 60 studs of downtown sidewalk; unbadged fictional parked cars fill 40% of parking lanes. Budgets: at most 15,000 BaseParts and 1.2 million rendered triangles within 600 studs of any street-level point, at most 150 unique MeshIds per region, textures at most 1024 x 1024, identical meshes reused so the renderer instances them. Acceptance test: at Civic Plaza a three-year-old mid-range phone at default quality holds at least 30 FPS with 30 players; traffic and pedestrian densities come from [416]-[430].

## SECTION 13 - OPEN WORLD MAP II: OFF-ROAD & NATURE REGIONS (Paragraphs 296-320)

[296] You will build every off-road and nature region at true scale: meters x 3.5714 = studs and feet x 1.0886 = studs, always through `Units` ([21]-[45]); real-world dimensions come first, studs in parentheses where layout depends on them. Each region gets its own folder in the world hierarchy of [271]-[295] with subfolders `Terrain`, `Tracks`, `Props`, `Foliage`, `Zones` and `Spawns`. Zones are invisible anchored Parts (`CanCollide`, `CanQuery` and `CanTouch` false) tagged `DeepSand`, `DeepSnow`, `RiverFlow`, `FoamPit`, `KidsTrack` or `WaveZone`, carrying SI attributes such as `ExtraCrr`, `Sink_m`, `FlowDir` and `FlowSpeed_ms`; each small `Zones` model is set to `ModelStreamingMode` Persistent in Studio. `VehicleController` bakes zones into a 64-stud grid as they arrive (`CollectionService:GetInstanceAddedSignal`, never `WaitForChild`), so each wheel resolves its zone in O(1) without per-frame spatial queries. Acceptance test: zone lookup costs under 0.05 ms per vehicle per frame in the MicroProfiler.

[297] Terrain has one global look per material, so give every surface its own terrain slot, reskinned by a `MaterialVariant` (PBR `ColorMap`, `NormalMap`, `RoughnessMap`, `MetalnessMap`, 1024 x 1024 max, `StudsPerTile` 8-16, `Pattern` Organic) set as that slot's base-material override in the `MaterialService` Properties panel; tune tints with `Terrain:SetMaterialColor`. Overrides reskin Parts too, so urban Parts never use the five repurposed slots ([275]). `Surface.luau` ([59]) maps them exactly as below; Mud, Grass, LeafyGrass, Rock, Basalt and Ice keep their [59] values.
```
Slot         Variant      SurfaceId   Grip  Used for
Ground       ForestLoam   Ground      0.70  trail dirt, forest floor
Slate        PackedDirt   PackedDirt  0.80  MX/SX tracks, singletrack (remapped)
CrackedLava  Gravel       Gravel      0.60  fire roads, washes (remapped)
Limestone    GroomedSnow  Snow        0.35  groomed loop (remapped)
Salt         PlayaCrust   PackedDirt  0.80  dry lakebed (remapped)
Snow         PowderSnow   Snow        0.35  powder bowls
Sand         DuneSand     Sand        0.40  dunes, beach, MX sand
```
