```
Sandstone    MesaStrata   fallback    0.70  mesas, canyon floors (remapped)
```

[298] Author each nature region's landform as 8-bit grayscale heightmaps from a terrain generator, in 512 x 512 px tiles covering 2,048 x 2,048 studs, so one pixel equals one 4-stud voxel. Keep each tile's height range within 1,020 studs (255 steps x 4) so no height step exceeds one voxel; visible terracing is a defect. Import each tile with its colormap (painted in the documented material color legend) through the Terrain Editor Import tool at exact Position and Size, then hide seams with the Smooth brush. Sculpt features with `Terrain:FillBlock`, `FillBall`, `FillWedge` and `FillCylinder` (`Enum.Material.Air` carves) and repaint with `Terrain:ReplaceMaterial`. These builders are ModuleScripts under `ServerScriptService/Server/DevTools` ([45]) run from the Studio command bar in Edit mode, since edits made during Play vanish on Stop. After every pass, save one `Terrain:CopyRegion` snapshot per region in `ServerStorage/ServerAssets/TerrainBackups`.

[299] Voxels are 4 studs (1.12 m), too coarse for jump faces, so `DevTools/JumpDesigner` builds every jump face, landing, berm and roller from anchored Parts segmented every 0.5 m along a circular transition (no kink over 4 degrees), Material Slate so they wear the track's PackedDirt variant, each with a `Surface` attribute ([59]) and `CanTouch` false. An optional Blender MeshPart skin (ruts, roost) may cover them with collision and queries off, the segments then turning Transparency 1. It solves design speed from y = x tan(theta) - g x^2 / (2 v^2 cos^2 theta) at the sweet spot and sets the landing within 5 degrees of the arrival angle, keeping equivalent fall height under 0.3 m. `SpeedBoard` adds a SurfaceGui showing design speed 40 m before the lip.
```
JumpProfile = {
  Kind = "Tabletop", -- Double|Triple|StepUp|StepDown|Kicker|Drop|Roller|Berm
  Gap_m = 24.4, LipHeight_m = 2.7, LipAngle_deg = 32, TransitionRadius_m = 12,
  SweetSpotDrop_m = 1.0, LandingLength_m = 14, Width_m = 10,
  SegmentLength_m = 0.5, Surface = "PackedDirt", SpeedBoard = true,
}
```

[300] Throttle Park occupies 2,300 x 1,900 studs (644 x 532 m) centered on (-3000, 20, 600), its west fence beside Highway 9's west leg (X -4,300, [281]), terrain rising to Y 62 there so the pro track gains 12 m. Pro MX track: x -4,100 to -3,150, z -200 to 1,500. Supercross stadium: (-2,350, 20, 1,100). Freeride Zone (pump track, dirt jumps, foam pit, FMX ramps): around (-2,400, 20, 350). Trials area and kids track: (-2,450, 20, -150). Park Road ([272]) enters at the northeast gate and follows the north fence to the paddock at (-3,000, 20, -150): Dirt Depot (`Dealership`, 15-vehicle showroom, `RepairBay` service bay), 40 pit stalls of 8 x 12 m, 24 trailer bays of 4 x 18 m, a wash bay and a fuel island with 4 `GasPump` and 4 `ChargingStation` (two at 60 kW).

[301] The pro track, Throttle Park National, is a 2.0 km lap (7,143 studs), 9-12 m wide, in Slate PackedDirt. Its 40-gate start sits on a 12 m concrete pad at 1.05 m pitch (150 studs total). Each 0.5 m steel gate Part, hinged at its base, falls back toward the rider, flat in 0.25 s. `RaceService` sets attribute `DropAt` (`workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` time) a random 5-10 s after the 30-second board turns sideways; every client animates the gates at that instant, so bikes push against real collision until it drops. Practice drops repeat every 60 s. The 140 m (500 studs) straight narrows to 15 m into a 180-degree left berm, inside radius 18 m, banked 30 degrees; the first front wheel over the holeshot line 5 m before its apex takes the holeshot.

[302] Tabletops ([299]) of 60, 75 and 90 ft (65/82/98 studs) have 2.4-3.0 m faces at 28-34 degrees. The 80 ft (24.4 m) finish tabletop, 32-degree lip, sweet spot 1.0 m below lip height, needs 15.8 m/s (57 km/h) and arrives at 35 degrees, so its landing is 30 degrees. Add a step-up (3.0 m rise, 15 m gap), a step-down (4 m drop, 25 m), doubles of 40 and 55 ft, and triples of 85 and 110 ft; the 110 ft triple off a 30-degree lip needs 19.5 m/s (70 km/h). The step-up and one triple sit uphill (third gear on a Ryujin RX-450F). Acceptance test: a Ryujin RX-250F clears every jump at design speed; casing the finish table at 85% speed lands at 7.1 m/s vertical, registering `CasedLanding` ([74]) on bottoming.

[303] All whoops, rhythm and braking bumps are sub-voxel [299] Roller profiles. Whoops: 14 whoops, 1.0 m tall at 5.0 m spacing, on a downhill straight; above 55 km/h with weight back a Ryujin RX-450F skims them, while below 30 km/h riders double them. Rhythm: 9 obstacles 1.2-2.0 m tall, 9-12 m peak to peak, ridden triple-triple-triple, quad-triple-double (the quad spans 33 m) or double-triple-double-double. Deep sand: 180 m of Sand filled 2 m deep in a `DeepSand` zone (`ExtraCrr` 0.10 over the [68] sand Crr of 0.20, `Sink_m` 0.08 below 25 km/h), with 0.3 m braking bumps at 1.5 m spacing before its berm. Finish: a timing `Checkpoint` at the finish tabletop's base beside a 4 m flag tower. Mechanics area: a fenced 60 m lane after the finish with 40 pit-board stands and a `RepairBay` ([245]).

[304] Berms are `JumpDesigner` Kind "Berm": 1 m-segmented banked arcs, bank 20-35 degrees rising to 50 at a 1.2-2.0 m lip. With mu 0.80, the 30-degree, 18 m Turn 1 holds 77 km/h from v^2 = g R (sin theta + mu cos theta) / (cos theta - mu sin theta). Ruts are never voxel grooves: `RutLine` folders hold ordered Attachments for three lines per berm, 0.20-0.30 m deep. A wheel within 0.25 m and 25 degrees of a line is captured: `VehicleController` adds a 2,000 N/m centering spring and up to 0.5 F_z of extra rut-wall side force; steer input above 0.6 across the line for 0.15 s hops it out (0.2 s wobble). Visuals: flat `Beam`s (`FaceCamera` false) with a rut texture. Acceptance test: a Kensei K2-250 exits Turn 1 at least 10% faster in the inside rut.

[305] Throttle Park Stadium is an open-air bowl (shell 760 x 520 studs) around a 150 x 75 m dirt floor. The 700 m (2,500-stud) supercross lap is 6-8 m wide: a 22-gate start ([301]) on a 75 m straight into a 180-degree berm; a 70 ft (21.3 m) triple with 2.4 m, 38-degree faces needing 14.5 m/s (52 km/h) onto a 36-degree landing; 12 whoops 1.2 m tall at 4.5 m spacing; rhythm lanes A (triple-triple-double) and B (quad-quad); an on-off tabletop; a sand corner; and an over-under: a 6 m wide wooden bridge 4 m up over a 3.2 m-clearance tunnel. Seating: 400 crowd-card MeshParts sharing 4 textures. Night events ([321]-[340]): four 30 m towers with Neon lamp heads, the floor lit by 12 Shadows-false SpotLights (Range 60, the maximum) hung 40 studs up, within the [480] cap.

[306] Pump track: 260 m of 2.0 m wide asphalt, 0.6-1.0 m rollers at 4-6 m wavelength, six 1.5-2.0 m berms banked to 55 degrees, two crossovers. The [65] rider model has no vertical push, so `VehicleController` adds pumping: forward shift s on a downslope phi adds 0.5 s m_r g sin(phi) forward, back shift cancels that share of gravity on upslopes, capped at 450 W. Acceptance test: a Ridgeline Slope DJ rider holds 22 km/h for 3 laps without pedaling; a passive rider stops within 2 laps. Dirt-jump lines start from 2.5, 3.5 and 4.5 m roll-in towers. Beginner: 4 tabletops of 2.5-3.5 m, 0.9 m lips, 28 degrees (20-23 km/h). Intermediate: 5 doubles of 4-5 m, 1.5 m lips, 38 degrees (25 km/h). Pro: 6 gaps of 5.5-7 m, 2.2 m lips steepening to 52 degrees (29 km/h).

[307] Foam pit: 24 x 14 m, 3.5 m deep, fed by 2.4 m wooden pedal and 3.6 m steel moto kickers. In the `FoamPit` zone `VehicleController` applies pure drag F = -c v with c = m x max(v_entry / 3 m, 10 s^-1), stopping entries within 3 m, sinking under 1 m/s; `Simulator` suppresses `CrashSignal` and damage there ([237]). Visuals: 400 anchored, shadowless foam cubes ([320]), the 30 nearest scattering on entry. After 1.5 s below 1.2 m/s, fade and `PivotTo` the queue pad. FMX: 75 and 100 ft steel kickers (3.0 m lips, 42 degrees) onto dirt mounds with knuckles 3 m above lip height, 40-degree faces and 12 m run-out radii; sweet spots 1.5 m below the knuckles need 56 and 64 km/h. Acceptance test: 50 foam entries at 30-80 km/h yield no crash or damage.

[308] Trials area: six taped 60 m sections ([391]-[415]) of logs 0.4-0.9 m thick (cylinder Parts, `Surface` "WoodPlanks", earning the [60] trials-tire x1.20), granite boulders of 0.5-1.6 m (Rock, `CollisionFidelity` PreciseConvexDecomposition), pallets of 1.2 x 1.0 x 0.144 m stacked 2-8 high, 1.5 m concrete pipes, tractor tires and a final 1.6 m vertical concrete step. Acceptance test: a skilled Trialtek TR-300 clears the step; a Ryujin RX-450F crashes trying. Kids track: a 350 m loop with 3-6 m tabletops, 0.3 m whoops and gentle berms inside a `KidsTrack` zone where pedal bikes, the Gopher 200, Rascal 110 and Rattler Trail 90 ride freely; other vehicles are limited to 25 km/h with a HUD notice ([431]-[450]). The Hillside Estate private track ([289]) reuses these kits: a 600 m loop with two tabletops (one of 60 ft), a step-up, a rhythm lane and four berms.

[309] Pinecrest spans x -7,900 to -2,900 and z -7,900 to -2,700, peaking at the summit (-5,600, 1,400, -5,400). Tree bands: maple, birch and fir at Y 150-450; Douglas fir, ponderosa and spruce to Y 1,050; krummholz and granite above, Summit Snowfield on the north face ([314]). Pinecrest Village (-4,100, 180, -3,500), inside Highway 9's northwest curve where Canyon Road ([281]) ends, holds Summit Outdoor (`Dealership`), the lift base, a timber lodge, 2 `GasPump`, 6 `ChargingStation` (two at 60 kW) and 48 trailer-sized bays. The Ranger Station (-4,700, 260, -3,850) is the trailhead: map board, `GaragePoint`, `RepairBay`. A 25 m fire lookout crowns Lookout Ridge (-6,700, 900, -4,200); six log cabins line 14 km of 4-5 m gravel fire roads (max 15% grade, 10 m switchback radius, 0.25 m water bars every 60 m) climbing to the Snowfield Hut.

[310] The Pinecrest Express, a detachable six-seat chairlift, runs from (-4,000, 185, -3,600) to the summit (-5,500, 1,340, -5,250): 700 m at 5.0 m/s (1.0 m/s in terminals), 36 chairs 9 s apart, 150 s per ride. Chairs ride 8-15 m above shaped ground, crossing Highway 9 on a netted 14 m span. Clients pose the chairs (a small Persistent model) from `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()`. Rear racks carry pedal and light-electric vehicles up to 35 kg. Stopped 1 s in the loading box, the client invokes `Lift_Board` (a RemoteFunction; add it and RemoteEvent `Lift_Exit` to NetDefs) and `VehicleService` returns a validated chair index; then each `PreSimulation` zeroes assembly velocities and `PivotTo`s bike and rider onto it, `Simulator` suspended. Unloading onto a 10-degree ramp is automatic; bail is off, reset returns to base. The server resets riders 8 studs off-pose for 2 s.

[311] Enduro singletrack (9.6 km): Ranger Loop (green, 1.6 km), Cedar Run (blue, 2.2 km), Root Canal (black, 1.8 km), Ridge Traverse (black, 2.6 km, 15-degree off-camber) and Rockstair (double black, 1.4 km, 0.3-0.8 m steps). `DevTools/TrailCarver` paints a one-voxel Slate tread along a spline, benches side-hills with `FillWedge` (under 10 degrees cross-slope), keeps 0.8 m trunk clearance and 2.5 m headroom, and edges it with brush; 0.08-0.2 m roots are cylinder colliders. Sawtooth Hill Climb (300 m slope, 130 m rise): 120 m at 30%, 90 m at 50%, 65 m at 70%, then 25 m of ledges at 90-100% (42-45 degrees). Climbing needs 0.95 mu cos(theta) > sin(theta) (95% rear load): past 37 degrees on packed dirt, only momentum works. Acceptance test: a Ryujin RX-450F hitting the ledges at 40+ km/h summits; a Ryujin Trail 230 stalls short.

[312] DH trails start at the summit. Pine Flow (blue, 2.4 km): 45 berms, 30 tabletops of 2-6 m, no drop over 1 m. Rockfall (black, 1.9 km): rock gardens 30-80 m long of 0.3-0.9 m boulders spaced 0.5-2 m, root webs, 1-2 m drops. Timber Shore (double black, 1.6 km): WoodPlanks north-shore decks, ladder bridges 0.6-1.0 m wide up to 3.5 m high, a 0.3 m skinny log, a 70-degree wall ride, a 4 m drop and a 12 m step-down off a wooden kicker. Pinecrest Pro DH (2.6 km, 325 m vertical, 50% max) is the timed race line ([391]-[415]). Drops follow [299]: a 3 m drop at 30 km/h lands x = v sqrt(2h/g) = 6.5 m out at 43 degrees onto a 40-degree landing. Acceptance test: a Ridgeline Gravity DH rides every feature in Sim Mode.

[313] Pinecrest Creek drops from a spring below the summit over Bridal Falls to Beaver Pond, crossing trails at six fords 0.2-0.6 m deep with depth posts and bridge bypasses. In water cells of the zone grid each wheel adds a second raycast with `IgnoreWater` false; a hit on `Enum.Material.Water` gives depth (surface Y minus ground Y). Submerged frontal area A adds drag 0.5 x 1000 x A x v_rel^2 N (Cd 1.0), v_rel relative to the `RiverFlow` current (`FlowDir` x `FlowSpeed_ms`, 0.3-1.5 m/s). Add `Powertrain.WadeDepth_mm` (MX/enduro 750, pit/minibike 450, street 550, ATV/UTV 650, hauler 700, light EV 300, e-moto 500): gas engines stall above it, electric drives limp at 20% until 10 s dry. Bridal Falls is 22 m of three `Beam`s (`TextureSpeed` 2) over a 3 m plunge pool, with mist and a trail behind the curtain.

[314] Summit Snowfield covers 2,000 x 1,400 studs of the north face above Y 1,050 on slopes under 40 degrees (steeper ground stays Rock), painted by a DevTools `Terrain:ReadVoxels`/`WriteVoxels` pass. Groomed: a 1.6 km, 8 m wide loop in Limestone GroomedSnow with marker poles every 25 m. Powder: North Bowl and Cornice Bowl in Snow inside `DeepSnow` zones ([205]), with a 6-12 m cornice drop, a 38-degree climb chute and a 120 m Ice tarn. The Snowfield Hut (-5,350, 1,180, -5,750) has a `GasPump` and a `GaragePoint` (`AllowedClass` "Snow") that is Summit Outdoor's delivery point ([358]): Frostline 850 purchases spawn there, buyer alongside. Tunnels: carve the Interstate 90 bore under the foothills (alignment in [281]) with `FillBlock` Air 4 studs oversize around modular concrete lining; the fire-road Miner's Tunnel is 140 m of timber-shored bore with a Mud floor.

[315] Red Mesa spans x -8,000 to -2,600 and z 2,400 to 7,900; west of Sunset Beach the coast ([271]) swings south to its sea cliffs. Dunes: 2,400 x 1,600 studs at (-5,800, 30, 6,300), transverse ridges 6-20 m tall, 60-180 m apart, 10-15 degree windward slopes, 32-34 degree slip faces; a DevTools 4-stud raycast scan fails Sand slopes over 35 degrees. tan 33 = 0.65 beats even SandPaddle grip (0.40 x 1.60), so slip faces yield only to momentum; brinks launch onto natural 33-degree landings. Three washes 8-30 m wide have 1.5-4 m banks, Sand or Gravel beds. Needle Slot: 600 m long, 2.5-5 m wide, 25-40 m deep, mesh sandstone walls. Big Red mesa (-6,300, 30, 3,600), 140 m tall with a 600 x 400-stud top and switchback jeep trail, and two buttes use modular strata cliff MeshParts.

[316] Red Mesa 7.2 is a 7.2 km Baja-style loop west of Highway 9: 140 km/h graded roads, silt (`DeepSand`, `ExtraCrr` 0.15), 0.6-1.2 m whoops at 8-12 m, washes, a rock garden, two 15-25 m crest jumps at 100 km/h and `Checkpoint` gates. Pumpline Oasis (-4,050, 25, 4,350) on Highway 9's southwest curve: 6 `GasPump`, 4 `ChargingStation` (two 60 kW). Copper Gulch ghost town (-5,000, 30, 4,500): 14 timber buildings and an 80 m mine tunnel. Red Mesa Playa (dry lakebed): a 150 m-wide Salt pan on x -7,550, flat within 0.05 m, from z 7,700 (launch pad) north to z -1,800: 2.7 km, trap at 1,400-1,600 m ([408]), then 1.1 km shutdown (stopping from 299 km/h on 0.64 street-tire grip takes 550 m). Mesa Road runs south from the Interstate 90 west terminus T (X -7,200, Z -1,200), just east of the playa near its north end, and skirts its east shore.

[317] Sunset Beach runs 1.4 km from x -1,500 to 3,500: 60-90 m of dry Sand above a 25 m wet-sand racing band of large flat Parts (`MaterialVariant` WetSand, `Surface` "Ground", grip 0.70), backed by 3-8 m marram-grass foredunes. The 1.2 km Boardwalk (WoodPlanks on pilings, Y 8-10) has shops under a 15 km/h advisory; the 350 m pier runs south from x 1,000 beside a launch ramp with a "Water" `GaragePoint`; the 32 m Kestrel Point Lighthouse on the east headland sweeps every 12 s. Wave area: in a west-beach `WaveZone`, `VehiclePhysics/WaveField.luau` gives `Buoyancy` ([72]) h = sum of H_i ((1 + sin phi_i) / 2)^1.5 over 100, 62 and 35 m deep-water waves (8.0, 6.3, 4.7 s) on `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()`, up to 1.6 m over the sandbar. `WeatherFXController` renders it as a bone-skinned mesh above the flat terrain water.

[318] Lake Serene fills an irregular basin (x 2,800 to 5,100, z -6,400 to -4,800, clear of Skyline Airfield), surface Y 22, 12 m deep, shores shelving 1:8. Lakeside Marine (`Dealership`, south shore, (4,000, 25, -4,700)) has a floating fuel dock (`GasPump`) and two 14% launch ramps whose `GaragePoint` (`AllowedClass` "Water") spawns the Wavecat 300 afloat. Serene Pines Campground: 24 sites with tent pads, fire rings and RV posts. The 3.4 km Lakeside Trail (Slate) has a 300 m marsh boardwalk and three timber footbridges. Serene Dam, concrete gravity on the east outlet, carries a crest road (Y 28) 7 m above its stilling basin; its 60 m ogee spillway (crest Y 22) pours `Beam` sheets down sloped concrete into Serene River, flowing east to the sea. A 160 m girder bridge spans the narrows with 8 m jet-ski clearance.

[319] Foliage is instanced MeshParts, never unions: at most 8 tree and 6 rock meshes per biome, sharing identical `MeshId`, `TextureID` or SurfaceAppearance for engine instancing, varied by yaw, 5 degrees of lean and scale 0.8-1.3, with two baked colorways per species. Triangles: conifers 2,500-4,000, hero trees 8,000, rocks 300-1,500. Set in Studio: `RenderFidelity` Automatic, `DoubleSided` only on leaf cards, `CastShadow` false under 2 studs ([480]). Group foliage into 256-stud tile Models (`ModelStreamingMode` Atomic, `LevelOfDetail` StreamingMesh) so unstreamed tiles show imposters. Forest density: one tree per 400 sq studs within 150 studs of trails and roads, one per 2,500 beyond, at most 4,000 trees within the 1,024-stud streaming radius ([477]). `Terrain.Decoration` is Studio-only: the user enables it; it covers only Grass, so dune grass, ferns and shrubs are MeshParts. Acceptance test: no bare area over 200 x 200 studs ([1]-[20]).

[320] Performance: foliage and decor set `CanCollide`, `CanQuery` and `CanTouch` false so [52] probes skip them, while trees keep invisible trunk cylinders; rocks use Hull and only slot-canyon walls and trials and crawl boulders use PreciseConvexDecomposition; jump sets stream `Atomic`; landmarks use `LevelOfDetail` StreamingMesh. Nature regions add 40 collectibles to [293]: 6 `LostPart` and 2 `GraffitiTag` each, half skill-gated. Bog Hollow, a quad and UTV park at (-3,600, 150, -3,100), has a 120 x 40 m mud-drag lane of Mud 0.5-1.0 m deep in a new `DeepMud` zone (`ExtraCrr` 0.12, `Sink_m` 0.25, handled like `DeepSand`) and a 150 m rock-crawl staircase of 0.6-1.4 m boulders to 35 degrees, both timed from `TimeTrialStart` arches. Acceptance test: 30-player races at the MX gate, Pro DH start and stadium meet [476]; a 4WD-Low Ranchhand 570 crawls the staircase that stops it in 2WD.

## SECTION 14 - LIGHTING, ATMOSPHERE, WEATHER & VISUAL EFFECTS (Paragraphs 321-340)

[321] The server owns facts only: `TimeService` publishes the clock epoch and `WeatherService` publishes per-zone weather, wetness, temperature and wind as `Workspace` attributes. `WeatherFXController` computes every visual on each client, because `Lighting`, post-effect, `Clouds` and particle changes made in LocalScripts stay local; no server script ever writes `Lighting`. Child ModuleScripts: `LightingMixer`, `TimeOfDayCurve`, `RegionLook`, `Precipitation`, `Lightning`, `SurfaceFX`, `NightLights`, `VehicleVFX`, `TireMarks`, `QualityScaler`. `LightingMixer` alone writes `Lighting`, `Atmosphere`, `Sky`, `Clouds`, `Workspace.GlobalWind` and post effects, at 30 Hz in `PreRender`: time-of-day base, then region blend, weather blend and transients (lightning; +0.6 tunnel exposure eased over 1.5 s after 1 s under the [333] cover ray; the [123] photo offset). Colors lerp in linear space, clamped to legal ranges. Never tween these properties with `TweenService`; ease layer weights instead. Acceptance test: outside lightning, no property moves over 2% of its range per frame.

[322] Set these `Lighting` baseline values in Studio; `LightingMixer` snapshots them at `:Init()` as the 09:00-15:30 day plateau of [327]. `Technology` Future is Studio-only (Properties panel, never scripts). `Brightness` 3, `ExposureCompensation` 0, `EnvironmentDiffuseScale` 1 and `EnvironmentSpecularScale` 1 so PBR `SurfaceAppearance` metals, chrome tanks and wet asphalt reflect the sky, `GlobalShadows` true, `ShadowSoftness` 0.12 (the real sun's 0.53-degree disc throws crisp shadows), `Ambient` (18, 18, 22) so garage and tunnel interiors never crush to pure black, `OutdoorAmbient` (118, 122, 132), `ColorShift_Top` (255, 247, 235), `ColorShift_Bottom` black and `GeographicLatitude` 34 for a southern-California sun arc. Acceptance test: at `ClockTime` 12 a chrome test sphere at the spawn plaza shows a readable sky reflection, a rider's shadow edge stays sharp at 10 studs, and the Old Town Studio Garage interior reads above RGB 15 in a screenshot.

[323] Create one `Atmosphere` in `Lighting` with baseline `Density` 0.28, `Offset` 0.30 (keeps nearby terrain crisp and pushes haze toward the horizon), `Color` (200, 208, 220), `Decay` (106, 112, 125), `Glare` 0.35 and `Haze` 1.2. Create one `Sky` with `SunAngularSize` 12, `MoonAngularSize` 9, `StarCount` 3000 and `CelestialBodiesShown` true; skybox, sun and moon textures come from `Config/AssetIds` and stay on the Roblox defaults while those IDs are 0. Create one `Clouds` parented to `Workspace.Terrain`, the only parent where it renders, with baseline `Cover` 0.45, `Density` 0.55 and `Color` white. Acceptance test: from the Pinecrest summit, terrain at the 1,024-stud streaming edge ([477]) melts into the horizon color with no visible fog band or pop, `StreamingMesh` landmark imposters beyond it read as soft silhouettes, and no hard edge appears where the cloud layer meets the haze.

[324] Author the post stack in `Lighting` with these exact names: `Bloom` (`BloomEffect`, `Intensity` 0.45, `Size` 28, `Threshold` 1.8, so only the sun, chrome, `Neon` and lamps bloom), `ColorGrade` (`ColorCorrectionEffect`, `Brightness` 0, `Contrast` 0.08, `Saturation` 0.04, `TintColor` white; time-of-day and region layers), `WeatherGrade` (a second `ColorCorrectionEffect`, neutral by default; weather and wetness layers) and `SunRays` (`SunRaysEffect`, `Intensity` 0.06, `Spread` 0.55, so shafts appear only near the sun, never a screen-wide wash). Depth of field exists only in photo mode as the camera-parented `DepthOfFieldEffect` of [123]; never place one in `Lighting` and never run it while riding. The [123] filter stacks on top of `ColorGrade`, and the mixer adds the photo exposure offset instead of overwriting it. Acceptance test: closing photo mode returns every Lighting and post value to the mixer's live output within one frame.

[325] Store regional looks in `Config/Weather` under `RegionLooks` as partial overrides of the baseline. Warm desert (Red Mesa): `Atmosphere` `Density` 0.22, `Haze` 2.4, `Glare` 1.4, `Color` (232, 206, 168), `Decay` (165, 122, 82); `ColorGrade` `TintColor` (255, 243, 226), `Saturation` 0.08; `ExposureCompensation` +0.15. Cool forest (Pinecrest): `Density` 0.34, `Haze` 1.6, `Color` (188, 204, 216), `TintColor` (240, 248, 255), `Saturation` -0.03, `SunRays` `Intensity` 0.10. Hazy city (Harbor City, Port): `Density` 0.40, `Offset` 0.15, `Haze` 2.8, `Color` (202, 200, 194), `Saturation` -0.06. Golden beach (Sunset Beach): `Density` 0.25, `Glare` 1.8, `Color` (236, 216, 186), `TintColor` (255, 241, 222), `Saturation` 0.10. Other regions use the baseline. `RegionLook` weights each look by the camera's distance to region bounds (shared with `MapController`), feathered across a 300-stud border and eased over 8 s with 150-stud hysteresis so boundaries never flicker.

[326] `TimeService` (server) owns the canonical clock: one day lasts 2,880 real seconds, so a game hour is 120 s and a game minute is 2 s. At boot it writes `Workspace` attributes `DayEpoch` (a `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` value), `ClockAtEpoch` (random 7.0-16.0 on live servers) and `TimeScale` (1, or 0 to freeze). Each client computes clock = (`ClockAtEpoch` + (`workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` - `DayEpoch`) x `TimeScale` / 120) mod 24 and `LightingMixer` writes `Lighting.ClockTime`; nothing replicates per frame, yet all 30 players see the same sun. Server API: `TimeService:GetClock(): number` and a `PhaseChanged` `Signal` with Dawn 05:30-07:00, Day 07:00-17:45, Dusk 17:45-19:15 and Night 19:15-05:30, consumed by jobs and events ([341]-[365], [391]-[415]). The `/time` command of [45] and fixed-time race lobbies rewrite all three attributes together. Acceptance test: two clients' `ClockTime` values differ by under 0.01 hours.

[327] `TimeOfDayCurve` holds keyframes interpolated per channel with monotone cubic (Fritsch-Carlson) splines across the 24-hour wrap, so plateaus never overshoot. 00:00 night: `Brightness` 0.8 (moonlight), `ExposureCompensation` +0.35, `OutdoorAmbient` (34, 40, 58), `TintColor` (205, 215, 255), `Saturation` -0.25, `Atmosphere` `Color` (60, 70, 95), `Bloom` `Threshold` 1.1 so neon and lamps glow. 05:45 blue hour: `OutdoorAmbient` (70, 78, 105), `Saturation` -0.12. 06:30 sunrise: `Brightness` 2.0, `Atmosphere` `Color` (255, 196, 150), `Decay` (230, 130, 90), `Glare` 2.0, `TintColor` (255, 226, 200). 09:00-15:30: the [322]-[324] baseline. 17:00 golden hour: `TintColor` (255, 234, 205), `Glare` 1.2. 17:50 sunset: `Atmosphere` `Color` (255, 170, 120), `Decay` (210, 95, 70), `Glare` 2.6, `Saturation` 0.12, `SunRays` 0.12. 18:45 blue hour: `OutdoorAmbient` (60, 66, 98), `TintColor` (200, 210, 255). 19:30: night values. Acceptance test: a full 48-minute timelapse capture shows no visible step in any channel.

[328] Each `StreetLight` model holds a head `Bulb` plus, because [480] caps lamp `Range` at 20 studs (short of the road from an 8-9 m head), an Attachment `LampPool` hung 14 studs above the road with a downward `SpotLight` `Lamp` (`Shadows` false, `Brightness` 3, `Range` 20, `Angle` 120) painting an 8 m pool, sodium (255, 205, 150) in Old Town and Port, LED (232, 240, 255) elsewhere. `NightLights` lights lamps 18:10-06:05, each after a seeded 0-8 s photocell offset. Only the nearest lamps within 300 studs of the camera are enabled ([340] counts), re-ranked every 0.5 s from a `CollectionService`-fed spatial hash. Every switched-on `Bulb` turns `Neon` even unbudgeted, so distant streets read lit. `NeonSign` and `NightWindow` parts (65% of windows) turn `Neon` at dusk, 200 per frame; `Flicker` signs flicker at 0.5-3 Hz unless Reduce Flashing is on.

[329] Night must make headlights matter. The night curve leaves unlit ground beyond about 25 studs as moonlit silhouettes, so Pinecrest singletrack at 01:00 is rideable unlit only at walking pace, while the [225] beams light a readable cone. Auto Lights (setting, default on) switches the local headlight to Low from Dusk to Dawn, in tunnels, and in Fog, Rain, Storm, Snow or Sandstorm; riding unlit at night shows a HUD warning ([431]-[450]). In Fog at night high beam backscatters: `LightingMixer` adds +0.06 `Atmosphere.Density` and +1.0 `Haze` while it is on, so low beam is better. Wet asphalt mirrors headlights through the [334] wet variants; the remote headlight budget follows [225] and [340]. Acceptance test: at 00:00 without lights a rider cannot read a trail fork 40 studs ahead; with low beam they can.

[330] `WeatherService` runs one state machine per zone: `Lowland` (elsewhere), `Mountain` (Pinecrest, X < -3600 and Z < -2800) and `Desert` (Red Mesa, X < -3600 and Z > 2800). Snow exists only in Mountain, Sandstorm only in Desert; Rain and Storm follow only Overcast or Rain; Fog weight triples 04:00-09:00. Each change blends over a random 60-120 s. Each `Config/Weather` state defines `Zones`, `Duration_s`, `Next` weights, `WetRate_per_s`, `Wind_ms`, `BrightnessMul`, `ShadowSoftness`, `Clouds`, `Atmosphere`, `Grade` and `Precip`. Every 2 s it writes `Workspace` attributes `State_<Zone>`, `Next_<Zone>`, `Blend_<Zone>`, `Forecast_<Zone>` (120 s ahead), `Wind_ms_<Zone>` (Vector3) and `Temp_C_<Zone>` (Lowland 14 °C at 05:00 to 27 °C at 15:00; Desert +10, Mountain -10, Rain -4, Snow pins -2; [84] subtracts 6.5 °C per 1,000 m). `LightingMixer` sets `Workspace.GlobalWind` from the camera zone's wind via `Units`. `/weather` ([45]) and race lobbies pin states.

[331] Per-state targets, written as `Clouds` `Cover`/`Density`, `BrightnessMul`, `ShadowSoftness`, then extras. Clear: 0.25/0.40, 1.0, 0.12. Cloudy: 0.60/0.60, 0.85, 0.25, `Saturation` -0.03. Overcast: 0.92/0.80 tinted (175, 180, 190), 0.60, 0.60 for diffuse shadows, `SunRays` and `Glare` 0. Rain: 0.95/0.88 tinted (130, 135, 145), 0.45, 0.80, `Atmosphere` `Density` +0.15, `Haze` 3.0, `Saturation` -0.15. Storm: 1.0/1.0 tinted (85, 90, 100), 0.30, 0.90, `Haze` 4.5, wind 10-16 m/s. Fog: 0.70/0.60, 0.70, 0.55, `Atmosphere` `Density` 0.62, `Offset` 0, `Haze` 0.5, `Color` (205, 210, 215), about 250 studs visibility. Snow: 1.0/0.85 tinted (215, 220, 228), 0.65, 0.75, `TintColor` (235, 242, 255), `Saturation` -0.20. Sandstorm: 0.40/0.50, 0.55, 0.70, `Atmosphere` `Density` 0.70, `Haze` 6, `Color` (196, 150, 100), `TintColor` (255, 230, 195), wind 14-22 m/s. Durations in seconds: Clear 600-1500, Cloudy 300-900, Overcast 300-600, Rain 240-600, Storm 180-360, Fog 240-480, Snow 300-900, Sandstorm 120-300.

[332] In Storm, `WeatherService` schedules a strike every 6-25 s at a random zone point at least 250 studs from every player (never on riders) and fires `Weather_Lightning` (a `NetDefs` `RemoteEvent`, 12-byte `buffer`: x f32, z f32, seed u32) to all. The client `Lightning` module computes distance d in studs and flashes: `ExposureCompensation` +1.6 / (1 + d / 1500) in a seeded pattern of 2-3 pulses over 0.35 s, plus, when d < 3,000, a 0.2 s bolt of 6-10 `Beam` segments from 900 studs above the strike's ground (one downward ray, Y 0 if unstreamed). Thunder is cued to `AudioController` after d x 0.28 / 343 s (343 m/s; 1,000 studs = 0.82 s): a crack under 1,200 studs, rolling rumble beyond, skipped past 12 s ([251]-[270]). Reduce Flashing ([431]-[450]) cuts the flash to 25% and one pulse.

[333] Wetness w (0-1) integrates per zone on the server at `WetRate_per_s`: Rain +0.004 (dry to soaked in about 4 min), Storm +0.008, Fog +0.0005 capped at 0.25, Snow 0; drying runs -0.0015 in Clear daylight, -0.0006 at night, -0.0010 Cloudy, -0.0004 Overcast, -0.02 Sandstorm; other rates triple in Desert. The server writes `Wetness_Lowland`, `Wetness_Mountain` and `Wetness_Desert`, which nothing reads directly: `Surface` ([59]) and all visuals call `Shared/WeatherQuery.wetnessAt(position)`, which blends zones across a 400-stud border and returns 0.15w under cover (an upward ray hits a roof within 80 studs, cached 0.25 s). Above w 0.7, `WeatherQuery.cosmeticSurface` reports Ground and PackedDirt as Mud to audio ([262]), VFX and tire marks, and `WeatherQuery.extraCrr` ramps 0 to 0.03 for [68]; grip stays on the [59] multipliers, which already pull dirt toward Mud's 0.45 (loose 0.42, packed 0.48 at w 1.0).

[334] `SurfaceFX` renders wetness locally. Give every road and dirt `MaterialVariant` a wet twin named `<Name>Wet` with the same `BaseMaterial` (`WornAsphaltWet`, `OldTownBrickWet`, plus `ForestLoamWet` and `PackedDirtWet` beside [297]), a ColorMap about 35% darker and RoughnessMap 0.15-0.25 instead of 0.7-0.8, so `EnvironmentSpecularScale` 1 paints mirror streaks of lights. `MaterialService:SetBaseMaterialOverride` restyles every part and terrain cell of a material in one call; parts with an explicit variant, including newly streamed ones, swap locally in batches of 200 per frame, and `Surface` ([59]) strips the `Wet` suffix. Swap paved twins above local w 0.35, back below 0.25; dirt twins above 0.7, back below 0.55, also darkening `Grass` 15% via `Terrain:SetMaterialColor`. Puddles are flat parts tagged `Puddle` in road dips (`CanCollide`, `CanQuery`, `CanTouch` false) whose `Decal` transparency runs from 1 at w 0.4 to 0.2 at w 0.9, lingering until w < 0.2.

[335] `Precipitation` attaches to the camera, never the world: a client-only anchored `PrecipRig` part (`CanCollide`, `CanQuery`, `CanTouch` false) follows `workspace.CurrentCamera` in `PreRender`, 35 studs overhead and led by 0.6 s of camera velocity. Rain sheets: near streak emitters (`Orientation` VelocityParallel, `Speed` 30-34 studs/s, a raindrop's real 9 m/s terminal velocity, `Lifetime` 1.1-1.3 s to die at ground level, 300 particles/s) plus a far emitter of wide, faint sheets 40-90 studs out at 60/s; Storm doubles rates and slants streaks with wind. Occlusion: every 0.2 s cast 9 upward rays in a 3 x 3 grid at 12-stud spacing (120 studs long, excluding characters and vehicles); the rate multiplier eases over 0.4 s to the unblocked fraction, so tunnels, garages and bridges stay dry. Acceptance test: 30 studs past either Interstate 90 tunnel portal, zero rain particles render.

[336] Snow (Mountain only): flakes fall 3-4 studs/s (1 m/s real) at 300 particles/s, swirling with `Workspace.GlobalWind`, above the snow line at Y 600, crossfading to cold rain below Y 450. Sandstorm (Desert only) drives 250/s grit streaks at 50-70 studs/s downwind plus a close dust sheet that cuts visibility to 150 studs. POV droplets appear only in Helmet POV ([119]): a `ScreenGui` pool of 24 `ImageLabel` drops spawned at 2/s x intensity (Rain 0.6, Storm 1.0) x (1 + speed_kmh / 60), sliding down when slow, streaking outward above 40 km/h, fading over 1.5-3 s; under cover, spawning stops and live drops fade within 1.5 s; Low tier skips them. Drop textures come from `Config/AssetIds` (ID 0: a translucent `Frame` with `UICorner`). Acceptance test: riding into a parking garage during Storm clears every POV drop within 2 s.

[337] `VehicleVFX` reads wheel contacts from a `VehicleController` `Signal` and fires pooled `ParticleEmitter`s with `:Emit(n)` via a frame-rate-safe accumulator, spending a per-vehicle token bucket (40 particles, refilled 40/s, [480]), splash first and exhaust last. Roost: rear slip above 1.5 m/s on loose ground throws 4 flipbook clods/s per m/s, 25 degrees up at 0.6x slip speed, `Acceleration` (0, -35, 0), `Drag` 1.2, lifetime 0.6-1.4 s, tinted by `Terrain:GetMaterialColor` or part `Color`, darkened 25% x w; mud adds 1-3 chunks per 0.1 s at `Drag` 0.3. Landings after 0.4 s airtime puff 15-30 dust particles, impact-scaled. Snow/sand spray is roost at 2x size and 1.5x lifetime, doubled in `DeepSand`/`DeepSnow` zones. Water, from a separate 10 Hz wheel ray with `IgnoreWater` false hitting `Enum.Material.Water` or a hashed `Puddle`, emits clamp(0.4 x speed_kmh, 5, 30) drops/s and a rooster tail above 30 km/h.

[338] Exhaust: every `Gas2T` vehicle (Kensei K2-125, K2-250, Trialtek TR-300, Austral EX-300, Corsa Zip 50, Frostline 850) emits blue-grey smoke (175, 188, 212) at each `Exhaust1..n`: 3 particles/s idling, 6-12 per throttle blip, transparency 0.55 to 1 over 1.5-2.5 s, tripled for 60 s after a cold spawn; the fuel-injected EX-300 emits 40% as much. Four-strokes show a faint cold-start puff, electrics nothing, and the `Diesel` Hauler HD and Hauler Van a grey puff above 80% throttle below 1,500 rpm; remotes use [36] data. Burnouts (rear slip above 8 m/s on paved) emit white-grey smoke at up to 25 particles/s, each growing 2 to 14 studs over 3-5 s, `WindAffectsDrag` true, replaced by spray above w 0.5. Sparks (orange, `LightEmission` 1, 0.2-0.5 s) fly when pegs, crash cages or the hull slide on paved or Metal above 3 m/s.

[339] `TireMarks` draws pooled `Beam`s between pooled `Attachment`s parented to `Workspace.Terrain`. Each wheel adds a segment per 2.5 studs while marking, raised 0.04 studs against z-fighting, `FaceCamera` false with attachments aligned to the normal so the strip lies flat, tread texture from `Config/AssetIds` (solid dark at ID 0). Paved surfaces mark only above slip ratio 0.2 or slip angle 8 degrees; dirt, mud, sand and snow mark whenever rolling. Marks fade out over 120 s on paved, 90 s on dirt and sand, 60 s on snow or above w 0.6, updating 1/20 of the pool per frame. Hard cap per client: Low 250, Medium 600, High 1,200, Ultra 2,000 segments, recycling the oldest. Remote vehicles within 200 studs mark from replicated motion plus one downward ray per wheel at 10 Hz.

[340] `QualityScaler` picks a tier from the custom Graphics setting ([431]-[450]: Auto, Low, Medium, High, Ultra). Auto maps `UserSettings():GetService("UserGameSettings").SavedQualityLevel` (QualityLevel1-3 Low, 4-6 Medium, 7-8 High, 9-10 Ultra, Automatic Medium) and listens to `GetPropertyChangedSignal("SavedQualityLevel")`; a governor drops a tier when 3 s average frame time tops 20 ms, restoring it after 30 s under 15 ms. Low/Medium/High/Ultra: particle multiplier 0.25/0.5/0.8/1.0 ([335]-[338] rates assume 1.0), then a global clamp keeps total emission under 1,200/s on PC and 400/s on mobile ([480]); post FX grades only/+`Bloom`/full/full; `Clouds.Enabled` false on Low; lights: streetlights 6/8/14/16, remote headlights 2/4/6/6 and 2 local, capped at 24 (12 on mobile, streetlights trimmed first); remote-vehicle VFX within 60/100/150/200 studs, none beyond. The tire-mark and post-FX toggles zero those budgets. Acceptance test: on a 3 GB phone in a Harbor City night Storm, `WeatherFXController` costs under 2 ms per frame.

## SECTION 15 - PROGRESSION, ECONOMY & JOBS (Paragraphs 341-365)

[341] `EconomyService` owns Rep progression, driven by `Config/Economy.Rep`. Across levels 1-100, the XP from L to L+1 is XPToNext(L) = round(250 + 120 x L^1.45). DevTools `/selftest` must reproduce: L1 370, L2 578, L5 1,488, L10 3,632, L20 9,490, L25 13,020, L50 35,139, L75 63,059, L99 94,190. Cumulative XP is 14,401 to reach L10, 129,980 to L25, 706,906 to L50, 1,105,092 to L60 and 3,867,789 to L100. Excess XP carries over (one grant can raise several levels), and XP keeps counting past the L100 cap for leaderboards. At planned all-source rates (jobs, challenges, races, stunts, collectibles) of about 3,500 XP/hour at L1-11, 8,000 at L12-39 and 14,000 at L40+, an active player reaches L10 in about 4 hours, L25 in 20, L50 in 76, L60 in 104 and L100 in 300. Rep never decreases and is never sold.

[342] Unlocks are data-driven: vehicles' `Economy.UnlockLevel`, jobs' `MinLevel` in `Config/Jobs` and properties' or features' `MinLevel` in `Config/Economy` are the only sources; UI derives the unlock table at boot (`/unlocks` prints it), and `ConfigValidator` flags any gap over 5 levels between unlocks below L50. Level-ups fire one toast, a phone list of what opened and `New` dealership badges. Rentals are unowned, `Rental`-flagged spawns that replace the active vehicle and, like [359] test rides, save no wear and run no jobs, races or loading: bike-share docks at VoltCharge Hubs lend the Ridgeline Commute E ($1 plus $0.25 per minute); from L10, the Wavecat 300 on Lake Serene and Frostline 850 on the Snowfield rent at their dealers for $40 per 5 minutes, via `Shop_Rent`. Acceptance test: an L17 profile cannot buy the Hauler HD; at L18 it can.

[343] Long-tail progression never adds performance. Levels 61-100 give one cosmetic every 5 levels (plate frames, gear colorways, titles), ending with "County Legend" at L100. Vehicle Mastery: each owned VehicleId earns points from distance (10 per km), clean deliveries, race finishes and banked stunts, saved in `Progress.Mastery`; tiers 1-10 unlock exclusive kit colorways, a number-board style, a garage plaque and, at 10, a gold Build Sheet badge ([366]). `Stats` keeps records per roster `Class`: top speed, longest wheelie, stoppie and airtime, maximum lean, longest jump. Free-roam PR zones (tags `SpeedTrap`, `JumpZone`, `SlideZone`): 20 highway and Canyon Road gate pairs 20 m apart timed by [396] crossing math, 12 jump ramps measured lip to first [75] ground sample, and 8 slide lanes scored per [410], each with a [414] board under its zone id and three stars worth $250 each.

[344] A new profile holds exactly $2,500, Rep L1 at 0 XP, the unsellable Kurb Street BMX in the Old Town Studio Garage (1 of 3 slots) and Realism `Light` ([246]). The first 30 minutes extend [3], logging the eight [449] steps via `AnalyticsService:LogOnboardingFunnelStepEvent`. Minutes 0-2: spawn, mount, riding lesson. Minutes 2-10: a scripted 0.6 km Kickstand Diner delivery (fixed $250 and 250 XP), then one or two orders capped at 0.6 km, reaching L2. Minutes 10-15: test ride and first purchase, Glide E-Board or Corsa Zip 50 at Throttle Works (or a Dirt Depot Gopher 200). Minutes 15-18: first refuel or charge (free once), GPS lesson. Minutes 18-30: four deliveries, completing the first-day daily challenge (always "Deliver 3 orders") and reaching L3. Acceptance test: a scripted tester completes all 8 steps by minute 30, at L3 with at least $2,000.

[345] Every job pays Payout = round((Base + Rate x D_km + PerDrop x drops) x TB x VM x LM x QM x DR), D_km = the server's A* route length ([348]) via `Units`. TB = clamp(1 + 0.6 x (T_target - t) / T_target, 0.75, 1.30), with t the server time from `Loaded` to `Complete` and T_target = 30 s + D_m / VRef. LM = 1 + 0.015 x (L - 1): 1.165 at L12, 1.585 at L40, 2.485 at L100. VM (vehicle), QM (quality) and DR ([357] diminishing returns) are set per job. Targets for an active pass-free player on the best open job: L1-11 $4-6k/hour, L12-29 $15-25k, L30-39 a ramp from $25k to $40k, L40+ $40-60k. The 2x Cash pass doubles final job, race, event and challenge Cash ([459]) and never counts toward these targets.

[346] Seed `Config/Jobs.luau` with the values below. Job XP = XPBase + XPPerKm x D_km and is never scaled by LM or passes. Expected earnings: Food about $4k/hour (BMX, L1) to $5.9k (e-bike, L5); Parcel $5-6k (L5-11); Moto-Taxi $16k (L12) to $33k (L40); Parts Runner $14-23k (L12-20) and $33k (full Hauler HD, L40); Bike Transport $23k (L18, 4 km, two bikes), $40k (L40, 6 km, three bikes) and $47k (L60). Other activities stay inside the same bands: races per [399], challenges per [363], achievements and collectibles per [364]. DevTools `/econsim` replays each job on real `RoadGraph` routes with the speed, handling-time and deadhead assumptions stored in `Config/Jobs.Sim`, and must land every figure within 10%; on a miss, tune `Base_usd` and `Rate_usd_per_km`, never the formula or the targets.

```lua
-- Config/Jobs.luau (excerpt)
FoodDelivery={MinLevel=1, Base_usd=110, Rate_usd_per_km=190, VRef_kmh=30, DRAfter=20, XPBase=80, XPPerKm=120},
ParcelCourier={MinLevel=3, Base_usd=120, Rate_usd_per_km=160, PerDrop_usd=30, VRef_kmh=40, DRAfter=10, XPBase=150, XPPerKm=120},
MotoTaxi={MinLevel=12, Base_usd=200, Rate_usd_per_km=340, VRef_kmh=45, DRAfter=15, XPBase=200, XPPerKm=150},
PartsRunner={MinLevel=12, Base_usd=250, Rate_usd_per_km=300, VRef_kmh=50, DRAfter=10, XPBase=250, XPPerKm=150},
BikeTransport={MinLevel=18, Base_usd=500, Rate_usd_per_km=300, ExtraBike=0.5, VRef_kmh=50, DRAfter=8, XPBase=500, XPPerKm=250},
Mechanic={MinLevel=10, PartsShare=0.40, Callout_usd=150, CalloutPct=0.10, XPBase=300},
```

[347] Jobs run through the phone Jobs app (`PhoneController`, layout in [431]-[450]). Every 45 s `JobService` offers each player up to 6 jobs from tagged sites within 3,000 road studs, filtered by `MinLevel` and the active vehicle (`Seats.Passengers`, `Seats.Cargo_L`, `Seats.Payload_kg`, `Seats.BikeSlots`, Class). Each offer shows pickup, distance, pay range and T_target, expiring after 120 s. The client fires `Job_Action` (RemoteEvent, 5 per s per [35]) with `Accept`, `Interact` or `Cancel`. The server moves one job per player through `ToPickup`, `Loaded`, `ToDropoff`, then `Complete`, `Failed` or `Cancelled`, pushing state on `Job_Update`. Sites are parts tagged `JobPickup` or `JobDropoff` with `SiteId`, `JobTypes` and `DisplayName` attributes. The client builds one local `ProximityPrompt` on the current target (`HoldDuration` 1.0, `MaxActivationDistance` 10), used on foot so E and ButtonX never clash with shifting; the server accepts `Interact` only within 14 studs.

[348] `Shared/GPS.luau` runs A* over the [282]-[283] `RoadGraph`, server-side for payout distance and client-side for display. Cost = Length_studs / SpeedLimit_kmh; the heuristic, straight-line distance / 104.61 km/h (the [274] maximum), stays admissible. Respect one-way edges, snap endpoints with `nearestEdge`, add a straight last leg to off-road sites, use a binary heap and a 256-pair LRU cache, and reroute after 2 s beyond 40 studs off the line. `GPSRenderer` pools 150 `Beam`s on one local anchored non-collidable part, resamples every 24 studs, raycasts points onto the road, lifts them 0.35 studs and draws only the next 1,800 studs. Beams: `Width0`/`Width1` 2.2, `FaceCamera` false with attachments rotated so ribbons lie flat, `Segments` 1, cyan, `LightEmission` 0.5, scrolling chevron texture from `Config/AssetIds` (solid strip at 0). Acceptance test: spawn plaza to Port Warehouse resolves in under 10 ms on mobile.

[349] Food Delivery (L1): about 40 restaurants tagged `JobPickup` with `Food` in `JobTypes` (Kickstand Diner, Harbor City food courts, Boardwalk stands) send orders to doors 0.4-1.5 km away. Dismount, walk in and hold "Pick up order"; the 2-6 kg insulated bag welds to the rider's back, or into the delivery box when `Seats.Cargo_L` is at least 20 (Ridgeline Commute E, Corsa Zip 50), its mass added through the [47] rewrite. Hand over at the door with a 1.0 s hold. VM is 1.10 for every LightElectric vehicle and both Corsa scooters, 1.0 for other two-wheelers and ATVs; haulers, the UTV, snowmobile and jet ski cannot take orders. From L6, two orders from one restaurant can stack with sequential drops. The Dusk-phase ([326]) dinner rush raises offer count 50% and Base 20%.

[350] Order condition C starts at 100 and uses only server data ([248]). A validated crash costs 50. A landing, found in [75] history when vertical speed rises from below -3 to above -0.5 m/s within 0.3 s, costs 12 x (|v_z| - 3) (|v_z| = impact speed, m/s), so a 1.5 m drop (5.4 m/s) costs 29; impacts above 6 m/s add 10 for bottoming. Server-detected airtime over 0.6 s costs 8, and a wheelie or stoppie (sampled `ChassisRoot` pitch beyond 15 deg) over 2 s costs 5. QM = 0.4 + 0.6 x C/100. At C 95+ the customer tips 10%; below 20 they refuse the order (no cash, 25% XP). The HUD predicts C locally, snapping to the server value at handover. Acceptance test: a clean ride delivers at 100; one validated crash leaves C at most 50.

[351] Parcel Courier (L3): routes of 3-8 drops (up to 20 in the Hauler Van, 16 in the HD bed), sized to the active vehicle, start at three `JobPickup` depots: Old Town post office, Harbor City sorting hub, Port parcel dock. Parcels: Small (2 kg, 5 L), Medium (6 kg, 12 L) or Large (15 kg, 30 L). Capacity: a 30 L, 12 kg backpack plus `Seats.Cargo_L` (Glide Bagger saddlebags hold four, [202]); mass uses the [47] rewrite, loading takes 0.5 s per parcel, and VM matches Food (haulers 1.0). The server pays on its own nearest-neighbor plus 2-opt tour over road distances, so drop order never changes pay. Each drop is a 1.0 s on-foot hold at a `JobDropoff` door. Fragile parcels (15%) take [350] penalties at half weight. Cancelling after k of n drops pays k/n with TB 1.0.

[352] Parts Runner (L12) hauls crates 0.9-3.6 km from eight Port docks to dealership service bays, the Ranger Station and Pumpline Plazas. Crates: Small 8 kg (backpack, one per motorized two-wheeler), Medium 25 kg and Large 60 kg (racks, ATVs), Pallet 250 kg (haulers, 4 s forklift prompt). Capacity is `Seats.Payload_kg` (Ranchhand 570 racks 41 kg front + 82 kg rear, Hauler HD 1,200, Hauler Van 1,000). Welded crates add mass ([47]) and offset torque ([204]), so handling changes emerge from physics. VM = 1 + load_kg/100 for bikes and ATVs (cap 2.3), 1 + load_kg/1,000 for haulers (cap 2.2). A validated crash costs every crate 40 condition; QM = 0.5 + 0.5 x C/100. Acceptance test: loaded with 123 kg, the Ranchhand 570 takes at least 20% longer for 0-50 km/h than empty, and no handling code reads crate count.

[353] Moto-Taxi (L12) needs a passenger seat (`Seats.Passengers`, [204]). Fares run 1.5-5 km from 25 `JobPickup` stands; haulers take groups (+40% per extra rider). VM: 1.25 Liberty Glide Bagger and Alpenrad GX-1300, 1.10 other street bikes, 1.0 scooters and haulers. Stop within 12 studs: a server NPC (`ServerStorage/ServerAssets/NPCPassenger`, helmeted, `Massless` parts) walks over via `Humanoid:MoveTo`, then welds at `PassengerSeatAttachment` with `PlatformStand` true, adding 75 kg ([47]). Comfort starts at 100, scored from [75] samples (3-sample average, 1 g = 35 studs/s^2): horizontal acceleration above 0.45 g costs 25 per excess g-second, landings 10 x (|v_z| - 2), airtime over 0.4 s 10, each wheelie or stoppie 15, lean past 40 deg 4/s, riding 25+ km/h over the posted limit 2/s. QM = 0.6 + 0.5 x comfort/100, plus a 15% tip at 90+. Crashes void the fare; passengers stand unhurt.

[354] Bike Transport (L18) needs an owned rig: Hauler HD plus 3-Rail Bike Trailer (3 slots), Hauler HD or Van plus Hitch Bike Carrier (1), or Hauler Van alone (2, or 3 with a carrier). Contracts carry up to 2 bikes and 4 km below L30, 3 bikes and 6 km from L30, drawn from the roster within each slot's `MaxBikeMass_kg` and length limits ([203]), from all eight dealerships, Throttle Park paddock, Maple Heights driveways, Pinecrest Village and Pumpline Oasis. The server spawns each bike unowned with `CustomerBike` = OfferId; only the job holder may ride it, at 25 km/h or less within 150 studs of pickup or dropoff. At handover the server snapshots its `DamageState` ([242]). Loading reuses the [203] ramp, slot check, weld and strap flow. Acceptance test: breaking either limit returns the bike to its pickup bay.

[355] While towing, each validated hauler impact ([248]) passes 40% of its [242] damage to each loaded bike's `Bodywork` and `Handlebars`; a roll past 60 deg snaps the straps, unwelding the bikes. Unload (`Vehicle_Unload`) each bike into its 10 x 4.5-stud (2.8 x 1.26 m) `JobDropoff` bay. The server diffs `DamageState` against the snapshot, deducting 1.5 x the [245] repair quote for added damage; zero added damage earns a 10% White Glove bonus. Payout = (Base + Rate x D_km) x (1 + ExtraBike x (n-1)) x TB x LM x DR less deductions (floor $0); T_target adds 45 s per bike. Exceeding 3 x T_target, abandoning the rig (500 studs) or logging off voids the contract and despawns the bikes, with a 10-minute cooldown. Acceptance test: after a 30 km/h jackknife, each deduction equals 1.5 x that bike's added-damage quote.

[356] Mechanic (L10), toggled On Duty in the phone, is player-to-player and server-mediated. A customer requests Roadside Service; the server quotes from [245] and [247], on-duty mechanics within 4,000 studs bid 50-120% of it, and the accepted bid is escrowed via `EconomyService:Hold`. Service needs both players within 12 studs of the stopped, non-racing vehicle, then a 3 s hold plus 1 s per 10% damage (cap 15 s). The server restores health and settles: 40% of the quote is destroyed as parts (a sink), the mechanic keeps the rest of the bid, and the game adds a $150 + 10%-of-quote callout bonus (6 per hour, one per customer per 30 min, only above 15% of FullRepair). Tune requests install customer-owned upgrades ([366]-[390]) for bid labor. No-shows refund escrow after 10 minutes; an account pair shares XP at most 3 times hourly.

[357] Anti-farming is server-side; clients never choose endpoints. A job pays $0 and adds an `AntiCheatService` strike if elapsed time is under 0.9 x straight-line distance / (top speed x 1.15), if the [75] jump rule fired during it, or if the sampled path is shorter than 0.8 x the straight-line distance; three strikes in 24 h lock jobs for 30 min. Cooldowns: 5 s between accepts, 15 min before a pickup-dropoff pair repeats, 120 s after 3 cancels in 15 min, 60 s after a failure. Each same-type job past `DRAfter` per rolling hour multiplies DR by 0.85 (floor 0.5, saved in the profile); switching type restores full pay. Moving under 20 studs in 120 s cancels the job. Over $90,000 job income per hour (pass-adjusted) flags a review, never an auto-ban. Acceptance test: a teleporting bot earns $0.

[358] `Shop_Buy` is a client-to-server RemoteFunction taking `VehicleId` and a color index. `ShopService` first takes a per-player lock (1 call per 2 s), then validates in order: the vehicle exists in `Config/Vehicles`; the player stands within 80 studs of a `Dealership`-tagged part whose `DealershipId` sells it; Rep level is at least `Economy.UnlockLevel`; Cash is at least `Economy.Price`; owned vehicles are fewer than total garage slots. On success, one `EconomyService:Charge` runs, the server adds a vehicle record (`Serial` from `HttpService:GenerateGUID(false)`, `VehicleId`, `TotalSpent` = price, odometer 0, full `DamageState`, empty upgrades) to the first property with a free bay, requests `profile:Save()` ([452]), and spawns the vehicle at the dealership's delivery `GaragePoint`, replacing any active one ([361]). It returns `{Ok, Serial}` or a reason: `LevelTooLow`, `InsufficientCash`, `GarageFull`, `NotHere` or `Busy`. Acceptance test: two simultaneous calls with cash for one vehicle buy exactly one.

[359] Test rides (`Shop_TestRide`) cover any vehicle at that dealership up to 10 levels above the player: 180 s within 2,500 studs (warning at 2,250, ending after 5 s outside), flagged `TestRide`, full tank, no saved damage or wear, no jobs, races, loading or sales, 120 s cooldown, then back to the showroom. The Throttle Works 40-slot used lot ([284]) lists 12 vehicles per in-game day (48 min), seeded by day index, at 55-80% of new with 300-9,000 km odometers, 60-95% component health and 30-80% tread; buyers still need the level and a slot. Resale at any dealership pays floor(0.60 x `TotalSpent`): price plus upgrades plus paid cosmetics (game-pass finishes add $0), never reduced for damage. Selling is blocked while spawned, in a job, loaded, or for the last owned vehicle. v1 has no player-to-player trading.

[360] Properties: Old Town Studio Garage (free, 3 slots, [284]); Maple Heights House ($120,000, L10, 6 slots, workshop, [289]); Port Warehouse ($400,000, L30, 20 slots, paint booth, dyno, workshop, [290]); Hillside Estate ($750,000, L45, 15 slots, private dirt track, workshop, [289]). Garage+ adds 5 (49 maximum). Buy at the property sign via `Property_Buy`, validated like [358]; properties are unsellable in v1. Workshops repair and service at 60% ([245]-[247]) and install upgrades without labor; the paint booth and dyno open their [366]-[390] tools; the Hillside track gate admits the owner plus whitelisted friends or crew, with a lap timer. Every property charges free at 3.3 kW and offers phone "Go Home" travel (60 s cooldown, blocked in jobs, races and police heat). Ownership is per player: co-owners of one building see only their own vehicles on its display bays (client-side).

[361] Garage logistics extend [49]: `Vehicle_Spawn` needs an owned, unloaded, unsold serial and a `GaragePoint` within 40 studs that is owned (`PropertyId`) or public (dealership bays, Ranger Station, Snowfield Hut, marine ramps) and fits any `AllowedClass`; one active vehicle (plus trailer or carrier) each; 1 spawn per 5 s; blocked bays try 4 sub-bays before "Bay blocked"; damage, energy, odometer and upgrades restore. Vehicles left 10 minutes beyond 300 studs of owners despawn. "Deliver to me" brings the vehicle to the nearest road node within 80 studs after 20 s ($100 above a $10,000 price). Garage visits: each property is open to Nobody, Friends (default), Crew or Anyone and displays its first 6 bay serials; same-server visitors ride over or Visit via `Garage_Visit` (validated like [443]) and render the host's displays as anchored, paint-accurate clones with Inspect ([366]), overriding [360].

[362] Pumpline `GasPump`s bill $2.00 per liter at 38 L/min compressed 10x; `ChargingStation`s bill $0.40 per kWh at 20 or 60 kW, tapering to 40% above 80% SoC ([87]). The server owns `Fuel_L` and `Charge_Wh`, accrues each session's bill per 0.25 s tick, capped at Cash, charging once. Running costs take 5-12% of L12+ income. Running dry: the HUD pulses below 0.5 L or 3% SoC; at zero combustion stops (`Stall`) and EVs coast at the [80] voltage floor. Riders can push (`Vehicle_Push`: the owner's client rolls it at 4 km/h, balance held, hands on grips per [227]), pour a 5 L Fuel Can ($30 at Pumpline, carried like the [245] kit) or call Roadside Rescue (`Rescue_Call`): on-duty Mechanics ([356]) first, then a $150 tow to the nearest matching station after 60 s. Acceptance test: $3 buys exactly 1.5 L.

[363] Three daily challenges reset at 00:00 UTC, drawn from a 60-template pool filtered to unlocked content and seeded by UserId plus day index, with one free daily reroll (examples: 5 orders at 95% condition, 20 km electric, a 30 m Throttle Park jump). Each pays $400 + 40 x L and 15% of XPToNext(L); finishing all three pays one more. Three weekly challenges reset Monday 00:00 UTC, paying $3,000 + 250 x L and 50% of XPToNext(L). `EventService` runs four 21-day seasonal events yearly from UTC windows in `Config/Economy.Seasons`: Winter Snowfest (Summit Snowfield, Frostline races), Spring Mud Run (Pinecrest enduro, trails held at wetness 0.6+ by `WeatherService`), Summer Boardwalk Bash (Sunset Beach, Wavecat) and Fall Moto Mayhem (Throttle Park night supercross). Each has a 30-tier play-earned track of cosmetics ($0 resale) and Cash, never sold; modes in [391]-[415].

[364] Build 120 achievements in eight categories (Jobs, Riding, Stunts, Racing, Explorer, Collector, Property, Crew); Bronze, Silver and Gold pay 500, 2,000 and 6,000 Rep plus $1,000, $5,000 and $15,000. Examples: "Hot and Fresh" (100 deliveries at 95% condition), "Five Stars" (50 fares at comfort 90+), "White Glove" (25 damage-free transports), "Full House" (20 owned vehicles). Collectibles ([293], [296]-[320]) pay $250 and 100 XP per `GraffitiTag`, $500 and 200 XP per `LostPart`. Twenty marquee achievements also call `BadgeService:AwardBadge` through `Util.retryAsync` ([43]), IDs in `Config/AssetIds` (skipped at 0). The login streak never punishes absence: one claim per UTC day in the phone Rewards app advances `StreakDay`, which never resets or drops. The 7-day cycle pays $500, $750, $1,000, $1,250, $1,500, $2,000, then $3,000 plus 1,000 Rep. Acceptance test: claim days 1-3, skip 5, and the next claim pays day 4.

[365] `EconomyService` is the only writer of Cash and Rep. API: `Grant(player, amount, reason, ref)`, `Charge(player, amount, reason, ref)` (fails with no partial debit when Cash is short), `Hold`/`Release` for escrow, and `GrantRep(player, xp, reason)`. Amounts are positive integers, Cash stays between 0 and 1,000,000,000, and `reason` comes from an enum in `Config/Economy`. A `ref` already present in the Ledger is rejected, so retries never pay twice. Each mutation appends {Id, T, Kind, Delta, Balance, Ref} to `Profile.Data.Ledger` (the 300-entry audit ring of [456]; lifetime totals go to `Stats`), replicates `Cash`, `RepLevel` and `RepXP` as Player attributes, and calls `AnalyticsService:LogEconomyEvent(player, flowType, "Cash", amount, endingBalance, transactionType, itemSku)` with `Enum.AnalyticsEconomyFlowType.Source` or `Sink`, `Enum.AnalyticsEconomyTransactionType.Gameplay.Name` (or `Shop`, `IAP`, `TimedReward`, `Onboarding`) and SKUs like `Job_FoodDelivery` or `Fuel`. Level-ups call `LogProgressionEvent(player, "RepLevel", Enum.AnalyticsProgressionType.Complete, level)`. Escrow open at shutdown refunds on the next load.

## SECTION 16 - CUSTOMIZATION & TUNING (Paragraphs 366-390)

[366] You will build customization and tuning in Phase 9 as one deterministic pipeline: base config plus installed parts plus the active tune yields an `EffectiveConfig` from the pure function `UpgradeApplier.apply(base, parts, tune)`, run identically on server and owning client. `CustomizationService` is the only writer of parts, paint, tunes and gear; `GarageService` owns garage stages and spawn hand-off; `UIController` hosts garage screens; `VehicleController` rebinds `ChassisParams` from the effective config. Every number lives in `Config/Upgrades`, never in a Service. `BuildSheet` lets any client inspect any spawned vehicle remote-free, rebuilding its effective config from replicated `UpgradeSet`, `TuneBlob` and `PI` attributes ([381]): PI class, parts by tier, paint zones, kit, mass, power, top speed, [383] 0-100 time, mastery tier ([343]) and any shared tune code, via a client-made `Inspect` prompt (0.3 s hold, 10 studs, parked vehicles) or the Players app. Files:
```
ReplicatedStorage/Shared/Config/Upgrades.luau
ReplicatedStorage/Shared/Tuning/
  UpgradeApplier.luau  PerformanceIndex.luau
  DynoSim.luau         DragEstimator.luau
  Fitment.luau         TuneCodec.luau
  BuildSheet.luau
ServerScriptService/Server/Services/CustomizationService.luau
StarterPlayerScripts/Client/Controllers/UIController/Garage/
```

[367] Every performance part has a category and one of five tiers: Stock ($0), Street, Sport, Race, Pro. Price = vehicle price x `CategoryShare` x tier multiplier (Street 0.5, Sport 1.0, Race 1.8, Pro 3.0), rounded to $50, minimum $50: the Volt Wasp X controller (share 0.055) costs $400, $750, $1,400 and $2,300, the Veloce Furia V4 Pro titanium exhaust (share 0.05) $11,700. Unlocks: Street at purchase, Sport at Rep max(vehicle unlock + 2, 5), Race max(unlock + 6, 15), Pro max(unlock + 12, 30). Tiers deliver 0.30, 0.55, 0.80 and 1.00 of the category's Pro effect. Dealer and Throttle Works bays add 15% labor; owned workshops install free ([360]); Mechanics install owned parts for bid labor ([356]). `TotalSpent` includes parts and labor, so resale returns 60% of everything ([341]-[365]); a full Pro build costs 1.2-1.5x the vehicle.

[368] `Config/Upgrades.Parts` holds one typed record per part: `PartId` (snake_case, such as `ferrox_full_ti_pro`), `Brand`, `DisplayName`, `Category`, `Tier`, `Kinds` (allowed `PowertrainKind`s), optional `VehicleIds`, `Requires` (categories at a minimum tier), `MassDelta_kg`, `MassHeight_mm` and `Effects`, a list of `{Field, Op, Value}` with Op `Set`, `Add` or `Mul` on dotted paths like `Powertrain.Electric.PhaseCurrent_A`. `UpgradeApplier` deep-copies the base, applies every Set, then Add, then Mul, walking categories in fixed `CategoryOrder`, and clamps to `Caps`: naturally aspirated gas power at most 1.30x stock (1.15x for triples, fours and V4s), electric peak power 2.6x, total weight reduction 8% of config `Mass_kg`. Mass changes rewrite `ChassisRoot` density ([47]) and shift CoM height by sum(dm x (h - h_CoM)) / M. Shares include controller 0.055, battery 0.10, motor 0.08, exhaust 0.05, ECU 0.03, big-bore 0.07, suspension 0.07, weight 0.06, brakes 0.03. `ConfigValidator` rejects names on a real-brand denylist.

[369] Ampforge controllers fit every Talon, Volt and Zephyr moto plus the Lupo Twin; e-bikes, Glide E-Board and Gyro EUC-20 get no power or speed parts. Tiers multiply `BatteryCurrent_A` by 1.2, 1.5, 2.0 and 2.6 and `PhaseCurrent_A` by 1.25, 1.5, 1.85 and 2.15: the Wasp X's 100/300 A becomes 260/645 A on Pro, 15.6 kW electrical at 60 V, the 2.6x cap. Race and Pro add better FETs (`Fet_ohm` x 0.7, controller heat capacity x 1.6) and double the `RegenCurrent_A` ceiling. Copper loss scales with I_phase^2 ([84]): Pro amps heat the stock motor 4.6x faster, and the pack's `MaxDischarge_A` (Wasp X 120 A) still caps current. Acceptance test: Pro on the stock pack is pack-limited near 7 kW; with the Race pack, 0-60 km/h improves 30% or more and one Canyon Road climb derates the stock motor within 45 s.

[370] Voltaro packs replace the `Battery` block (series count setting `Pack_V`, `PackFull_V`, `PackEmpty_V` per [80], `Capacity_Ah`, `Capacity_Wh` within 2% of V x Ah, `Pack_ohm`, `MaxDischarge_A`), +4.5 kg per kWh. Wasp X from 16S 60 V 40 Ah (2.4 kWh, 0.05 ohm, 120 A): Street 16S 50 Ah (3.0 kWh, 150 A, +2.5 kg); Sport 20S 74 V 45 Ah (3.33 kWh, 180 A, +4.5 kg); Race 20S 60 Ah high-drain (4.44 kWh, 0.035 ohm, 260 A, +9 kg); Pro 22S 81 V 60 Ah (4.86 kWh, 0.03 ohm, 300 A, +11 kg). Other Talons and Volts: Sport/Race +4S, Pro +6S (max 26S); Zephyrs and light electrics: capacity-only (Ah x 1.25 or 1.5). Back-EMF divides by `MotorRef_V` ([78]): 74 V lifts base speed 23% and top speed to the 4,300 rpm `MaxSpeed_rpm` governor (83 km/h). Higher voltage needs a Sport+ controller.

[371] Coilwerk motors raise the saturation limit `MotorMaxPhase_A` (stock 1.6x `PhaseCurrent_A`; amps beyond it add heat, not torque). Sport rewind (speed constant +15%): `PeakTorque_Nm` (so Kt) x 0.87, base speed x 1.15. Race big-stator: saturation 2.0x, `Winding_ohm` x 0.8, heat capacity x 1.5, +3.5 kg. Pro: saturation 2.4x, heat capacity x 1.9, `MaxSpeed_rpm` x 1.15, +6 kg. Cryon cooling scales the [84] motor R_0: Street finned cover 0.92, Race ducted shroud 0.80, Pro oil-cooled stator 0.65; the Sport heatsink sets controller R_0 to 0.75x. Linkdrive sprockets (chain drives, not hubs) set `Reduction_x` = stock x (rear / rear_stock) x (front_stock / front), rear -4 to +12 teeth, front -1 to +2; 8% more reduction gives 8% more wheel torque and 7.4% less rpm-limited top speed. Acceptance test: Pro controller plus stock motor gains no dyno torque above 480 A.

[372] Breathing parts scale each `TorqueCurve` WOT row by g = 1 + G_lo (1 - x) + G_hi x, x = clamp((rpm - Idle_rpm) / (Redline_rpm - Idle_rpm), 0, 1); gains multiply. Pro: Ferrox four-stroke exhaust G_lo 0.03, G_hi 0.08, -5.5 kg on multi-cylinders, -2.5 kg on singles; Brisa intake G_lo 0.01, G_hi 0.03; Mapline ECU map G_lo 0.03, G_hi 0.04, throttle-body tau ([88]) x 0.85, +300 rpm on `Soft` limiters. Carbureted engines (Gopher 200, Rascal 110, Ryujin Trail 230, Kensei K2-125 and K2-250, Trialtek TR-300, Corsa Zip 50, Rattler Trail 90) take a Mapline jet kit in the ECU slot. ECU gains halve unless exhaust and intake are Sport or better; breathing gains fall to 70% without ECU or jet kit. Exhausts apply the [255] audio offsets; Race and Pro lift [100] `Backfire` odds from 25% to 40%.

[373] Corvane four-stroke big-bore kits scale WOT torque by (cc_new / cc_stock)^0.9, only for the Rascal 110 to 143 cc (+25%), Ryujin Gremlin 125 to 143 cc (+14%), Trail 230 to 250 cc (+11%), RX-450F to 478 cc (+6%) and Austral SM-700 to 750 cc (+7%); each adds 5% crank inertia, 10% engine heat ([97]) and 1.25x wear ([231]-[250]). Cams: Stage 1 G_lo -0.02, G_hi +0.04, peak-power rpm +250; Stage 2 G_lo -0.05, G_hi +0.07, +500 rpm, idle +150, springs allowing +300 rpm on `Hard` limiters. Two-strokes edit the [95] resonance: Sidewind Pipeworks torque pipe rpm_res -400, width x 1.15, A +0.03; race pipe rpm_res +300, width x 0.9, A +0.08; porting (Sport/Race/Pro) A +0.04/+0.08/+0.12, rpm_res +300 to +600, below-band rows x 0.97 to x 0.90, A capped at 0.75. Trialtek: torque porting only (A -0.05, rows under 5,000 rpm +8%).

[374] Dentra clutches: Sport springs raise `Capacity_Nm` 20%; Race plates lift fade onset from 250 to 290 °C, heat capacity to 2,100 J/K and cooling to 8 W/K ([91]); Pro is a centrifugal auto-clutch for manual dirt bikes (engages 2,400-3,600 rpm, unstallable, lever overrides) or a street slipper clutch capping back-torque at 35% ([89]). Race-tier Mapline quickshifters retrofit fuel-injected manual bikes over 200 cc ([93] cut curve, Sport ECU required); Pro adds 100 ms autoblip on four-strokes. Linkdrive sprockets set `FinalDrive_x` = rear / front teeth (front -2 to +1, rear -4 to +8): torque scales with it and limiter speed Redline_rpm x 2 pi R / (60 x Primary x Ratio_top x Final) inversely, so a K2-250 on 13/52 versus 13/50 gains 4% thrust and drops from 125 to 120 km/h. Shaft and belt finals are fixed.

[375] CVT vehicles tune the variator: Dentra roller weights from 0.7x to 1.2x stock scale held engine speed by (stock / new)^0.5, and clutch springs raise `Engage_rpm` 300 or 600 rpm ([94]), Frostline 850 included. Forced induction stays canon-only: `ConfigValidator` fails the build if any part adds `BoostCurve_Nm` elsewhere. Dunecat XR Turbo Mapline tunes raise the [96] wastegate from 1.3 to 1.45 bar (Sport) or 1.6 bar (Race, larger intercooler), scaling `BoostCurve_Nm` by the absolute-pressure ratio (+6.5% and +13%), EGT-derated above 950 °C; the Hauler HD tow tune adds 10% torque under a 1,600 Nm transmission cap; the Wavecat 300 Pro impeller adds 6% thrust and keeps its 108 km/h governor. The Kensei Hayate 1300 Drag Kit ($8,500, L45) installs the exact [187] package as one removable bundle locking the rear tire, lowering link and wheelie bar slots.

[376] Talvik suspension. Street springs: five steps, 0.8x to 1.2x stock `SpringRate_Nmm`, preload re-solved for sag ([53]). Sport revalve: digressive knee 0.3 to 0.35 m/s ([54]) plus full clickers where the new `Suspension.Adjusters` field (`None`, `Preload`, `Full`) lacks them. Race cartridge: the [380] high-speed compression slider and a firmer bottoming cone (the [55] 3 c_b v term becomes 4 c_b v). Pro factory kit: a `Progression` slider (0-0.6 linkage, 0.8-1.5 air), -0.8 kg. Lowering links drop the rear 25 or 45 mm: rake grows atan(drop / wheelbase), about 1.0 or 1.8 deg at 1,450 mm, `Trail_mm` is recomputed from the fixed [63] offset, the CoM falls half the drop, progression rises 0.08 and `MaxLean_deg` shrinks 1.5 deg per 10 mm. Calma steering dampers write `Chassis.SteeringDamper` ([63]) over 18 clicks; Race adds speed sensing: 0.3x below 40 km/h, full above 100 km/h.

[377] Brakhaus parts scale stock `Chassis.BrakeTorqueF_Nm`/`BrakeTorqueR_Nm` ([56]) by ratios of T = 2 mu_pad F_clamp r_eff per caliper (r_eff = rotor radius - 22 mm), so configs need no pad data. Street sintered pads: mu 0.42 to 0.48 (x1.14). Sport oversize rotors: dirt front 260 to 320 mm (x1.28), street 320 to 330 (x1.04). Race: radial master cylinder (x1.10), race pads (mu 0.52) and fade onset 600 instead of 450 °C, rotors being [84]-style 1,200 J/K nodes heated by T_brake x omega, losing 30% mu past onset. Pro: monobloc 4-piston calipers (x1.12), floating rotors, onset 700 °C. Nerofibra carbon parts save 0.8, 1.6, 3.0 or 4.5 kg on dirt bikes, double on street bikes; street Race adds a lithium battery (-2.4 kg), Pro forged magnesium wheels (-2.8 kg, inertia -22%); pedal bikes get carbon wheels (-0.6 kg, inertia -25%), within the [368] cap.

[378] Grivo tires map onto [60] rows: Asfalto `StreetSport`, Tour `Touring`, Terra MI `MXIntermediate`, Terra S `MXSoft`, Paddle `SandPaddle` (rear only), Bog `Mud`, Trial `Trials`, Circuit R `Slick`, Polar Stud `SnowStudded`, Claw `KnobbyATV`. `Fitment.luau` parses moto sizes (110/90-19: OD mm = rim x 25.4 + 2 x width x aspect / 100) and bicycle 29x2.20, allowing the same rim, one width step and 3% OD change, then rewrites radius and mass. By stock row: street rows take Asfalto, Tour, Circuit R (`Street`, `Supermoto` classes) and Terra MI (Alpenrad GX-1300, Zephyr FX-E); MX rows take Terra MI, Terra S, Bog, and Paddle on dirt motorcycles; `KnobbyATV` takes Claw, Bog, and Paddle on quads, UTV; Trials keeps Trial; Polar Stud fits all but the Glide and Gyro. Each tire costs the [247] 0.8% of vehicle price ($30-250; Circuit R, Polar Stud 1.5x) and resets wear.

[379] Tuning exposes only what installed hardware can adjust. Each `Config/Upgrades.Sliders` record has `Field`, `Min`, `Max`, `Step`, `Default` and `RequiresPart`; client and server clamp, quantize and reject non-finite values. Final drive (Linkdrive set): the [374] or [371] tooth range into `Gearbox.FinalDrive_x` or `Reduction_x`. Per-gear ratios (Corvane close-ratio gearbox, Race): each `Ratios_x[g]` within 6% of stock, ratio[g+1] at most 0.94 x ratio[g]. Throttle response (any Mapline ECU or Ampforge controller): s from -1 to +1 sets exponent e = 1.7^(-s), Progressive (1.7) to just past Aggressive (0.59), on gas bikes and electric Sport and Turbo modes; Eco stays Progressive ([83]). Amp limit: 50-100% of installed currents. Regen: 0 to the `RegenCurrent_A` ceiling, times the [82] player setting. Launch rpm (launch-control bikes, Drag Kit): 45-90% of the limiter in 250 rpm steps. Single-speed bicycles: chainring stock +/-3 teeth, cog -1 to +2.

[380] Chassis sliders: preload 0-15 mm shock, 0-10 mm fork (`Preload_mm`) with live sag against `SagTargetF_pct`/`SagTargetR_pct`; compression and rebound clickers 0-24 out (stock 12) map to zeta = zeta_default x 1.5^((12 - clicks) / 12), full-in 1.5x, full-out 0.67x ([54]); Race high-speed compression sets the post-knee slope 0.30-0.60. Tire pressure writes `PressureF_kPa`/`PressureR_kPa`: MX and enduro 83-97, trials 28-48 ([152] effects); street 210-290, each 10 kPa under 250 adding 1% peak grip, 4% rolling resistance and 3% peak slip; road bikes 550-760, MTB 140-240, fat bikes 50-140, ATV, UTV and minibike balloons 20-70 kPa. Steering damper (Calma): 0-1 in 18 clicks. Brake bias, linked systems only (Corsa scooters, Liberty Glide Bagger, UTV, haulers): 50-80% front into `Chassis.BrakeBiasF_x`. Factory electronics where `Assists` lists hardware: TC Off/1-6, ABS On/Off/Off-Road, wheelie control Off/1-3, engine-brake control 1-3; player assists stay global, stronger wins ([113]-[115]).

[381] `CustomizationService` validates everything; clients never send prices or effects. Remotes from `NetDefs`: `Custom_BuyPart`, `Custom_Install`, `Custom_ApplyVisual`, `Custom_BuyGear`, `Tune_Apply`, `Tune_Save`, `Tune_Publish`, `Tune_Import` and `Dyno_Run`, each with a client `RequestId` cached 60 s so retries never double-charge. Checks in order: vehicle owned; player within 30 studs of a `GaragePoint` or `Dealership` service bay; vehicle under 2 km/h and not in a race; Rep level; `Kinds`, `VehicleIds`, `Requires` and fitment; price recomputed from config; funds. Then, in one non-yielding step, `EconomyService:Charge` ([365]) deducts Cash and logs the sink, and CustomizationService records `OwnedParts` and adds `TotalSpent` ([456]). On success the server recomputes effective config and PI, sets model attributes `UpgradeSet`, `TuneBlob`, `PI` and `PIClass`, and fires the reliable `Vehicle_Notice` "Rebind" ([34]) so `VehicleController` rebuilds `ChassisParams` without respawning; the [75] speed rule uses effective top speed. Limit: 4 requests per second per player.

[382] Dyno rooms: Port Warehouse (owner, free), Throttle Works and Ryujin-Kensei ([288], $150 per pull). `Dyno_Run` anchors the bike on the roller while server `DynoSim` steps the same [76] `Powertrain` modules at h = 1/240 s in 2 ms slices per frame; the client replays it with roller spin and audio. Gas: engine at 90 °C, gear nearest 1:1, clutch locked, full throttle, eddy brake holding a 600 rpm/s sweep from idle + 500 rpm to the limiter. Electric: 0 to `MaxSpeed_rpm` at 90% SoC and 40 °C, logging amps and volts; CVTs and electrics plot against km/h. Each 100 rpm sample stores wheel power and torque plus crank power (wheel / `Driveline.Efficiency_x`); the model ignores air density, so curves are SAE J1349 standard as-is. A `buffer` (max 160 samples) feeds a two-axis graph of rotated Frames overlaying stock, previous and current runs.

[383] `DragEstimator` runs a 1D launch model after every change: m = effective mass + 75 kg; drive force = min(wheel torque / R, mu x rear load including transfer m a h / L, wheelie limit m g b / h from [66]); tucked drag 0.5 rho CdA v^2, Crr 0.015 street or 0.025 knobby ([68]); shift times or quickshifter cuts ([92]-[93]); launch at launch-control rpm or 0.75 x peak-torque rpm with 0.35 s clutch slip; 1/240 s steps, dry Asphalt. Outputs: 0-60 mph, 0-100 km/h, 1/8 mile (201.17 m) and 1/4 mile (402.34 m) times and trap speeds (last 20 m). Acceptance tests within 3%: stock Kensei Hayate 1300 9.9 s at 238 km/h and Drag Kit 6.0 s and 9.3 s at 246 km/h ([187]); stock Kensei ZR-1000 9.9-10.4 s; Skyline Airfield passes ([391]-[415]) within 5%.

[384] `PerformanceIndex.compute` reruns on every change; c() clamps to 0-1. Inputs: DynoSim peak crank power P (kW; pedal 0.9 sprint, [99]), top speed V (km/h, lowest of governor, limiter and drag balance), m = mass + 75 kg. s_pw = c(ln(P / (0.004 m)) / ln(187.5)); s_v = c((V - 25) / 275); s_g = c((mu - 0.6) / 0.7), mu = best Paved or Packed compound grip ([60]); s_b = c((a_b - 0.4) / 0.8), a_b (g) = min(total brake torque / (R m g), mu, stoppie limit x_f / h); s_h = 0.45 c((MaxLean_deg - 25) / 30) + 0.35 c(1 - (m - 85) / 415) + 0.20 c((1,700 - Wheelbase_mm) / 750), four-wheelers leaning atan(min(mu, track / 2h)). S = 0.36 s_pw + 0.22 s_v + 0.14 (s_g + s_b + s_h); PI = round(clamp(100 + 1,199 (S - 0.24), 100, 999)).

[385] The garage (at a `GaragePoint`, dealer bay or dyno room) puts the vehicle on a turntable; the client clones it into `workspace.GaragePreview` (anchored, local) and previews every change instantly, sending nothing until Buy or Apply, reverting on cancel or failure. `CameraController` sets `Enum.CameraType.Scriptable`: orbit by drag, right stick or swipe, pitch -5 to 60 deg, zoom 1.6-4.5 vehicle lengths, easing 0.25 s to per-zone presets, plus `DepthOfFieldEffect` focus. The side panel shows price, Rep lock, mass delta, stat bars and a live PI badge with delta (class D under 400, then C, B, A, S per 100, X from 800). PI calibration (+/-30): Kurb Street BMX 150, Volt Wasp X 375, Kensei K2-250 530, Kensei Street 900 725, Veloce Furia V4 855. Acceptance test: hue drags recolor the preview within one frame at 60 FPS on mid-range phones.

[386] Paint zones: each paintable MeshPart keeps its [223] `PaintSlot` and adds a `PaintZone` attribute (`Tank`, `Frame`, `Plastics`, `Fairing`, `Rims`, `Swingarm`, `ForkLowers`, `Accent`); unzoned parts fall back by slot (Primary to Tank, Secondary to Plastics, Wheels to Rims, others same-named) and empty zones hide. The picker offers an HSV hue ring and saturation-value square (V floor 5%), hex entry, 24 presets including `Visual.FactoryColors` and 8 recent swatches, saved via `Color3:ToHex()`. Finishes ([223]): `Paint_Gloss`, `Paint_Matte`, `Paint_MetallicFlake` anywhere; `Aluminum_Brushed` on Frame, Swingarm, Rims; `Carbon_Fiber` on `CarbonCapable` parts. Pro Paint Lab adds `Chrome`, `Paint_Satin` and `Paint_Chameleon` (Metal; metalness 0.7, roughness 0.18 maps), hue-shifted client-side up to 60 deg by 1 - abs(view dot normal) at 10 Hz on the 6 nearest within 80 studs. Per zone: $300 solid, $450 metallic or brushed, $600 carbon or pass finishes, half price in the Port Warehouse booth.

[387] Graphics kits are `SurfaceAppearance` templates in `ReplicatedStorage/Assets/VehicleModels/_Kits/<VehicleId>/<KitId>/<PanelName>`, outside the model so spawn clones stay lean; `CustomizationService` and the garage preview clone them into the [224] `GraphicsShell` twins. Scripts never write texture properties at runtime, and kits whose `Config/AssetIds` entries are 0 stay hidden. Up to 12 sponsor stickers (fictional brands only) go on flat faces as `Decal`s. Number boards (dirt, pit, electric moto) take 1-3 digits matching `^%d%d?%d?$`, a white, yellow, black, red or blue background and `Enum.Font` Sarpanch, Michroma, Oswald or Bangers; street plates take up to 8 characters matching `^[A-Z0-9 %-]+$` ([199]); both pass the server filter flow of [224]. Spokes are a separate `Spokes` MeshPart in any color with `Paint_Gloss` or `Aluminum_Brushed`, plus 8 anodized nipple and hub colors. Sidewall lettering is a `TireLettering` shell in white, yellow, red or off, $150 a pair.
