[388] Accessories mount on canonical attachments; `VehicleBuilder` makes primitive stand-ins while asset IDs are 0. Cosmetic: grips on `GripL`/`GripR`; anodized bars and levers (rise moves grips up to 20 mm; IK follows); seat covers; exhaust tips on `Exhaust1..n`; fender eliminators moving plate and `Taillight` to a tail-tidy bracket. Functional, priced like Sport parts: stunt cages add "VehicleHull" boxes, +4 kg and 0.6x side-impact damage ([231]-[250]); a drag wheelie bar for street bikes over 600 cc adds a roller hull 1.2 m behind the axle; saddlebags add 6 kg, adventure panniers with top box 12 kg and CdA +0.06. Lights Pack: LED headlights recolor `SpotLight.Color` and lenses to cool white (235, 242, 255), [225] beam shapes unchanged; underglow is two downward `SurfaceLight`s (Brightness 1.2, Range 8, Angle 150, any color, Static/Breathe/Cycle) drawn for the 8 nearest within 150 studs.

[389] Rider gear follows [228] accessory rules and is cosmetic only. Helmets: full-face (street; visor clear, smoke or iridium), MX with peak, open-face (scooters, cruisers, chopper), plus half-shell and full-face DH for pedal bikes; goggles fit MX helmets only (clear, smoke, iridium or gold lens, optional tear-offs). Matching jersey-and-pant kits are classic `Shirt` and `Pants` templates with IDs in `Config/AssetIds`; the server saves the avatar's own templates, parks layered clothing like [228] hair and restores both on exit. Also boots (MX, street, sneakers), gloves and a hydration backpack on `BodyBackAttachment`. Dirt, Street and Pedal loadouts auto-equip by vehicle class on enter; "Match bike" recolors untextured helmet and backpack accent MeshParts to the bike's Tank paint. Prices: helmets $250-1,800, kits $150-600, boots $200-650, goggles $60-220, gloves $40-120, backpack $90, sold at Throttle Works and Dirt Depot via `Custom_BuyGear`, saved in `Profile.RiderGear`.

[390] Each vehicle keeps up to 5 named tunes (24 characters, filtered per [224]) and an active index. `Tune_Publish` writes the active tune to the raw DataStore `TuneCodes_v1` (global data, pcall with retry and backoff) under a random 8-character Crockford base32 code shown as XXXX-XXXX, via `UpdateAsync` whose transform returns nil if the key exists, then retries with a new code (5 tries); 10 publishes per player per day. Records hold `VehicleId`, author UserId, raw name (re-filtered with the author's UserId on each read) and `TuneCodec`-quantized slider values, never parts; `Tune_Import` accepts only a matching `VehicleId`, clamps to the importer's installed parts and lists what changed. Customization slice of the [454] `OwnedVehicle`:
```luau
export type PaintZone = {Hex: string, Finish: string} -- "RRGGBB", MaterialVariant name
export type Tune = {Name: string, Values: {[string]: number}} -- slider Field -> value
export type OwnedVehicleCustom = {
  Upgrades: {[string]: string},          -- Category -> installed PartId
  OwnedParts: {[string]: boolean},       -- every PartId bought, installed or not
  Paint: {[string]: PaintZone},          -- PaintZone name -> paint
  Visual: {[string]: string},            -- Kit, Spokes, Grips, Underglow... -> option id
  Plate: string?, NumberBoard: string?,  -- raw text, filtered before display
  Tunes: {Tune}, ActiveTune: number,     -- up to 5 tunes, 1-based index
  TotalSpent: number,                    -- parts + labor, basis of 60% resale
}
```

## SECTION 17 - GAME MODES, RACES & EVENTS (Paragraphs 391-415)

[391] You will build every mode in Phase 10 on top of free roam, the default state of the main place: everyone spawns into free roam, jobs, meets and stunt scoring run there, and a race or event is a temporary overlay that returns each rider to the venue paddock afterward with vehicle, damage and fuel intact. `RaceService` is the only authority for race state, timing, results, rewards and ratings; `EventService` schedules server events and seasons; clients only render. Modules: `Shared/Config/Races.luau`, `Shared/Race/` (`Standings`, `GhostCodec`, `StuntScore`, `DragTree`), `Server/Services/RaceService/` (`init`, `Lobby`, `Validator`, `Drag`, `Ghosts`, `Stunts`, `Rewards`, `Boards`) and `HUDController/Race`. Players carry attributes `RaceId` (empty in free roam) and `RaceRole` (`Racer` or `Spectator`). A server runs at most 3 race instances at once and never two on one venue.

[392] Every race is data in `Config/Races`, keyed by `RaceId`; several races may share one `TrackId` (MX1 and MX2 both use the National). `Races.validate()` errors at boot on an unknown `Type`, `MinPlayers` above `MaxPlayers` or a `TrackId` over 32 characters, and `RaceService:Start` disables, with a Logger ERROR, any track whose checkpoints have a duplicate or missing `Index`. `Type` is one of Motocross, Supercross, Enduro, HillClimb, Trials, Rally, Street, Canyon, Drag, TopSpeed or TimeTrial. Checkpoints are Parts tagged `Checkpoint` with attributes `TrackId`, `Index` (0 = start/finish) and `Kind` (`Gate` by default, `TC`, `WPS`, `WPM` or `Speed`) plus a child `Respawn` Attachment; geometry lives in [271]-[320]. `Phases` uses the day phases of [326].
```lua
export type RaceDef = {
  RaceId: string, TrackId: string, Type: string, Name: string, Venue: string,
  Laps: number, Start: "Gate" | "Grid" | "Tree" | "Interval" | "Solo", Interval_s: number?,
  PIClasses: {string}, VehicleClasses: {string}, SimOnly: boolean,
  Spec: string?, MinPlayers: number, MaxPlayers: number,
  EntryFee: number, DNF_s: number, Length_m: number, KillY_studs: number,
  Phases: {string}?, Discipline: string, Floor_ms: number,
  Reward: {Base: number, XP: number},
}
```

[393] Riders join from the phone Races app or by stopping under 5 km/h in a venue's `RaceStart`-tagged zone; the client fires `Race_Join(raceId)` (1 per 2 s); the server checks eligibility ([394]) and holds `EntryFee` in escrow ([365]). Instances run `Idle -> Open -> Locked -> Staging -> Countdown -> Running -> Results -> Idle`. Open lasts 60 s or until `MaxPlayers`; at Locked, entrants, vehicles and tunes freeze and later joiners queue for the next. Below `MinPlayers` (2; 1 for solo types) escrow releases and Open restarts. Staging disables Passive Mode ([249]), calls `Player:RequestStreamAroundAsync` at each slot, then `PivotTo`s each vehicle to its slot under a [75] teleport token, reclaiming and returning ownership as in [118]. `Race_Leave` is free before Locked and a DNF afterward. `Race_State` broadcasts phases with a monotonic `Seq`; `game:BindToClose` cancels live instances and releases escrow.

[394] The server computes eligibility at `Race_Join` and again at Locked from `UpgradeApplier.apply` and `PerformanceIndex.compute` ([366]-[390]), never a client value. A race lists PI classes (D 100-399, C 400-499, B 500-599, A 600-699, S 700-799, X 800-999, or Open) and roster `Class` values ([126]-[205]): Motocross, Supercross and Enduro take Motocross, Enduro, TrailBike and ElectricMoto; Quad MX takes Quad; Mini MX takes PitBike, Minibike and the Rattler Trail 90; Hill Climb, the motocross set plus Trials; Trials takes Trials and Pedal; Pinecrest Pro DH, Pedal only; Street and Canyon take Street and Supermoto; Rally splits bikes, Quad and UTV; Drag and Top Speed take every wheeled land class. Under 30% condition prompts a repair ([245]). `SimOnly` races need Sim Mode at Locked ([115]). `Spec` races lend identical stock, server-owned loaners (no damage bills). Garage swaps and tune edits lock until Results.

[395] Starts use five modes. `Gate`: the Throttle Park 40-gate drop-gate ([301]; 22 gates in the stadium, [305]); gate pick is one simultaneous 15 s window where conflicts go to the higher discipline rating and the loser takes the nearest free gate. Gates are real collision, so riders hold revs against them; `RaceService` writes `DropAt` = `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` + random 5-10 s after the 30-second board turns sideways. `Grid`: two-wide staggered `GridSlot01`-`GridSlot30` attachments at 8 m row pitch; five red lights come on 1 s apart and go out together at `GoAt`, 0.5-2.0 s after the fifth. `Tree` serves drag ([406]), `Interval` enduro, rally and Canyon pairs (`Interval_s`), `Solo` single runs. Clients schedule lights against `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()`. A grid rider whose samples leave the slot by over 1.5 studs before `GoAt` takes a 5 s jump-start penalty.

[396] Checkpoints are scored on the server only. `Validator` stamps each [75] 10 Hz `ChassisRoot` sample with `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` and tests the segment between consecutive samples against the next expected checkpoint only: signed distances d0 and d1 to the gate plane must change sign and the intersection must fall inside the gate rectangle plus 4 studs of tolerance. Crossing time t* = t0 + (t1 - t0) x d0 / (d0 - d1), taken at the front axle point (`WheelF`, or the front axle midpoint on four-wheelers), minus half of `Player:GetNetworkPing()` (a round-trip figure), capped at 0.25 s. Skipped gates earn nothing; a split faster than the straight-line distance between the two gates / (top speed x 1.15) is rejected and logged. Client-predicted crossings show instantly, then server times overwrite them. Any [75] violation during a race voids that rider's result.

[397] Each track stores the arc length `S_m` of every checkpoint along a 2-stud centerline polyline, so progress P = lap x lap length + S(last checkpoint) + the rider's projection along the polyline toward the next checkpoint. A lap counts only when Index 0 is crossed after every index in order. Standings sort finishers by finish time, then others by P descending; gaps are crossing-time differences at the latest shared checkpoint. `Validator` recomputes at 5 Hz and sends a packed `buffer` (u8 sequence as in [36], under 300 bytes for 30 riders) on UnreliableRemoteEvent `Race_Standings`; final results use reliable `Race_Results`. Photo finish: times resolve to the millisecond ([396]) and display as m:ss.mmm; an identical millisecond is a dead heat sharing position and payout, and a top-two gap under 0.050 s shows both recorded poses at the line.

[398] In a race, reset (R or RB held 1.5 s, [118]) keeps its 15 s cooldown but returns the rider to the last validated checkpoint's `Respawn` attachment, facing the track at zero velocity under a fresh teleport token; damage persists. The server auto-respawns riders, ignoring that cooldown, 4 s after an ejection ragdoll settles ([231]-[250]), after 10 s over 60 studs off the centerline (not in Rally or on Enduro transfers), below the race's `KillY_studs`, or in water over 1.2 m deep. Respawned vehicles stay in the `Ghost` collision group ([249]) for 3 s. Progress falling for 3 s flashes WRONG WAY. DNF: once the winner finishes, the rest get `DNF_s` (default max(30 s, 0.25 x winner time), capped at 180 s); 90 s without progress, 20 s off the vehicle, or disconnecting is also a DNF.

[399] `RaceService/Rewards` pays Cash = `Reward.Base` x position factor x field factor x level factor, rounded to $10, via `EconomyService:Grant`, ref `<InstanceId>:<UserId>` ([365]). Position: 1st 1.00, 2nd 0.80, 3rd 0.65, 4th-10th linear 0.50 to 0.30, other finishers 0.20, DNF 0. Field factor = min(1, entrants / 8); level factor = 1 + 0.03 x (L - 1), capped at 3.0. Bases: Motocross moto $1,500, Supercross main $1,000, Enduro $2,000, Rally $1,800, Street or Canyon $1,200, Trials $800, Hill Climb $600, drag round $350 ([346] bands); XP uses the same factors. A +15% clean bonus needs zero [248]-validated crashes. Anti-farm: each rerun of one `RaceId` within 60 minutes pays 15% less (floor 40%); finishes under `Floor_ms` pay nothing and log to `AnalyticsService`. Entry fees (5% of base) are a sink; a Time Trial personal best pays $200 per track per day.

[400] Discipline ratings live in the profile ([451]-[475]) as `Ratings[discipline] = {R, Races, Wins, Peak}` for Motocross, Supercross, Enduro, HillClimb, Trials, Rally, Street, Canyon and Drag, starting at R 1000. Each result is scored as pairwise duels: E_ij = 1 / (1 + 10^((R_j - R_i) / 400)); S_ij = 1 if i finished ahead, 0.5 for a dead heat or two DNFs, else 0; R_i += K / (N - 1) x sum over j of (S_ij - E_ij). K is 40 for the first 10 races, 24 until R 2000, then 16. Leaving after Locked scores as last. Tiers: Rookie under 1100, Amateur 1100, Expert 1300, Pro 1500, Elite 1700, Legend 1900, shown beside names. Ratings set gate-pick priority and heat seeding; private-server races never change them.

[401] Motocross runs on Throttle Park National ([301]-[304]): 2.0 km laps, 40-gate start, 4-lap motos (about 8 minutes at a 60 km/h average) for up to 30 riders, plus a $250 holeshot bonus ([301]). Classes: MX2 (Kensei K2-125, Ryujin RX-250F, electric moto up to 15 kW peak), MX1 (Open up to PI S; the Kensei K2-250 races here, as under AMA rules) and Quad MX (Rattler 450R, Rattler 700); Mini MX runs 6 laps of the kids track ([308]). Supercross runs in Throttle Park Stadium ([305]) with `Phases` Dusk and Night: 700 m laps, 22-gate start, an 8-lap main; above 22 entrants, two 4-lap heats each send their top 11. A lapped rider gets a blue-flag HUD icon when the leader closes within 2 s; holding the leader up within 1 s for 10 s costs 3 s.

[402] Enduro runs in Pinecrest ([311]) as a roughly 30-minute day for up to 20 riders started individually at 15 s intervals: three special tests (Root Canal, Ridge Traverse, Rockstair) linked by transfers on Ranger Loop, Cedar Run and fire roads. Each transfer allows `Transfer_s` (a 25 km/h average) and ends at a time control (`Checkpoint` with `Kind = "TC"`): early arrivals wait in the TC zone until their due time, and each started minute late adds 60 s. Special tests are timed to the millisecond from start beam to flying finish; results sum test times plus penalties, and a missed test checkpoint adds 5 minutes instead of a DNF. Transfers stay open to free roam, while non-entrants inside a taped test move to `Passive` ([249]). Refuelling and charging happen only in TC zones, so range ([76]-[100]) becomes strategy.

[403] Hill Climb uses the Sawtooth Hill Climb ([311]): two solo runs each, best counts; summiters rank by time, everyone else by the farthest validated sample along the centerline, to 0.1 m. A run ends on a crash, after 3 s rolling backward, or at 60 s. Trials uses the six 60 m sections of [308] with observed scoring: 0 clean, 1 one dab, 2 two dabs, 3 three or more, 5 fail. A dab is any [64] `FootDab` the owning client reports on `Race_Dab`; the server also counts one when its samples show roll beyond 12 degrees under 5 km/h followed by recovery, so suppressed reports change nothing. A fail is a crash, a stall that rolls back, crossing the tape, passing a gate backward, or 90 s elapsed. Two laps; lowest total wins, ties go to most cleans.

[404] Desert Rally runs Red Mesa 7.2 ([316]) as a 2-lap loop for up to 30 bike, Quad and UTV entrants, launched at 20 s intervals so dust clears. A waypoint validates when the path between samples passes within 40 studs; `WPS` safety waypoints show on the map, `WPM` masked ones only in the roadbook, and each miss adds 2 minutes. The HUD scrolls a roadbook: each note gives distance to the next in km to 0.01, a tulip diagram and a cap heading, while a 3D chevron 60 studs ahead points at the next `WPS` turning red beyond 45 degrees off heading. `Speed` zones through Copper Gulch cap 50 km/h, adding 10 s per started 10 km/h of peak excess. Public rallies use live weather, so a Desert-zone `Sandstorm` ([330]) can arrive mid-race; private lobbies may pin it.

[405] Street Circuit is a closed 2.0 km night loop through Harbor City ([286]-[288]) using the Harbor Avenue start straight, the steel-grate Union Street truss bridge, a Civic Plaza chicane and a hairpin by Riverside Garage, with `Phases` Night only and 8 laps from a grid. While it runs, `RaceService` marks the route's road-graph edges `Closed` so client traffic ([417]) reroutes, spawns barrier and cone models, and moves non-entrants on the loop to `Passive`. Canyon Sprint runs point-to-point up Canyon Road from the Interstate 90 west terminus T to Pinecrest Village ([281], [309]) with pairs launched every 10 s. Both lanes close too: traffic is per-client ([416]), and no racer may meet cars rivals never see; crossing the centerline for over 1 s still adds 2 s. Both use street PI classes; Sim-only weekly versions feed separate boards ([414]).

[406] Drag racing runs on the Skyline Airfield strip ([291]): two lanes, pre-stage and stage beams 7 in (0.64 studs) apart and a Christmas tree between lanes. `RaceService/Drag` evaluates the beams for the two active riders every `RunService.PostSimulation` step from the replicated front-tire contact point, interpolated to the millisecond and latency-corrected as in [396]. When both riders are staged the tree fires after a random 0.6-1.1 s; a rider not staged 7 s after the first is disqualified. `TreeMode`: Pro .400 (three ambers together, green 0.400 s later), Pro .500, Sportsman .500 (ambers 0.500 s apart, green 0.500 s after the third) and Sportsman .400 for practice. Leaving the stage beam before green lights the red foul light and loses; if both foul, the earlier foul loses. Crossing the lane line also loses.

[407] Reaction time RT = stage-beam exit - green (.000 perfect, negative = red light). The ET clock starts at stage-beam exit, excluding RT and the tire's roughly 0.29 m (1.05 studs) of rollout; incrementals fall at 60 ft (65.3 studs), 330 ft (359.2 studs), 1/8 mile (718.4 studs) and 1/4 mile (1,436.8 studs). Trap speed = 66 ft / time through the 66 ft trap ending at each finish. Heads-up, the first front tire to the line (lowest RT + ET) wins. Bracket mode: each rider enters a dial-in of 4.000-30.000 s and the slower dial leaves earlier by the difference; first to the line wins, but breaking out (running under the dial) loses unless both break out, then the smaller breakout wins. Acceptance test: a Drag Kit Kensei Hayate 1300 lands within 5% of [187] and [383].

[408] Top Speed Run uses the dry lakebed ([316]): a standing solo launch from the south end, scored as average speed through the 200 m trap between 1,400 and 1,600 m from [396] crossings, so one-frame spikes earn nothing; boards keep the best per roster `Class`. Time Trials exist on every circuit plus Pinecrest Pro DH ([312]), started through a `TimeTrialStart` arch, no lobby. `RaceService/Ghosts` records every timed run on the server from the [75] samples plus the latest `Vehicle_State` cosmetic values, so clients never upload ghosts. `GhostCodec` packs each 10 Hz frame into a `buffer`: position as int16 deltas in 1/100 stud (absolute int32 keyframe every 50 frames), orientation as a quantized quaternion in 4 x int16, speed as uint16 km/h x 100, plus throttle, steer, rider-pose and gear bytes: 20 bytes per delta frame, 26 per keyframe.

[409] A run that beats the rider's best on a track, with every checkpoint validated and no teleport token after the start, saves to the raw DataStore `Ghosts_v1` under key `<TrackId>/<UserId>` (32-character TrackId cap, 50-character key limit) as a base64 string from `GhostCodec`: a 3-minute run is 1,800 frames, about 36 KB raw and 48 KB encoded, under the 4 MB limit. Each track also keeps `<TrackId>/WR` for the record holder. Writes use `Util.retryAsync`, at most one per player per track per 60 s; reads cache 10 minutes. Playback: a validated `Race_GhostGet` answers with 8 KB `buffer` chunks on `Race_GhostChunk` at 2 per second, within the bandwidth budget; the client interpolates frames with `CFrame:Lerp` in `RunService.PreRender` and poses an anchored, 50%-transparent clone with `CanCollide`, `CanQuery` and `CanTouch` false. The HUD shows a split against the ghost at every checkpoint.

[410] `RaceService/Stunts` scores stunts in free roam and events from the replicated `ChassisRoot` pose at 10 Hz plus a server raycast under each axle, never from client claims. Wheelie: rear within 0.3 studs of ground, front 0.5 studs clear, pitch 8 degrees or more; 1 point per metre, x1.5 within 5 degrees of the balance point ([66]). Stoppie: rear clear, entered at 30 km/h or more; 3 points per metre. Supermoto slide on pavement above 25 km/h: slip angle beta of 12-60 degrees scores beta x speed (m/s) x 0.1 per second, x1.5 above 35 degrees. Combos: each different stunt starting within 2.5 s of the last adds +0.5x, max x5; 2.5 s idle banks it; a validated crash ([248]) wipes the unbanked chain. `StuntZone` lanes (Airfield taxiway wheelies, Pier 4 Lot stoppies) keep top-10 boards.

[411] Pedal tricks at Civic Plaza ([286]) and the Freeride Zone ([300], [306]) join the chain: manuals 2 points per metre, peg grinds on `Grindable`-tagged edges (pegs within 0.6 studs, above 8 km/h) 3 points per metre, barspins (Trick A airborne) 150 on the Kurb Street BMX; every vehicle earns 10 points per 0.1 s of air beyond 0.3 s, x2 on pedal bikes. Landings: Clean = pitch within 15 degrees of the slope, roll under 15, yaw slip under 10 and any trick finished 0.15 s before contact (x1.0); Sketchy = outside those limits without a [74] crash (x0.5); Crash = 0, chain lost. Validation rejects over one trick start per 0.3 s, pose jumps over 30 degrees between samples lacking matching angular velocity, and totals above `StuntCapPerMin` (25,000). Free-roam points pay $1 per 20, max $8,000 per hour.

[412] FMX tricks need 0.4 s of air on a class with `AirPitchTorque_Nm` of 18 or more ([67]). Airborne, manual shifts are ignored, so E/Q (gamepad B/X, mobile air buttons) become Trick A/B, combined with steer and weight shift. `RiderAnimationController` ([231]) plays each trick through `Animator:LoadAnimation` from `Config/AssetIds` with `IKControl` hands (ID 0 uses a procedural IK pose). A backflip commits by holding Back + Trick B over the lip's last 0.25 s, adding a pull impulse of up to 3.5 rad/s (under the [67] 6 rad/s clamp): in 1.4 s that turns about 280 degrees, what a 42-degree lip to a 40-degree landing needs, and weight shift and rear brake ([67]) meter the rest. Repeats in one chain pay 25% less; trick plus backflip x1.5.
```lua
Tricks = { -- Config/Races.Tricks; A/B = Trick A/B, "@Lip" = held over the lip's last 0.25 s
  Whip = {Base = 150, MinAir_s = 0.5, Input = "SteerFull"},
  Scrub = {Base = 120, MinAir_s = 0.4, Input = "Lean@Lip"},
  NacNac = {Base = 300, MinAir_s = 0.8, Input = "Steer+A"},
  HeelClicker = {Base = 350, MinAir_s = 0.9, Input = "Fwd+A"},
  Superman = {Base = 450, MinAir_s = 1.0, Input = "Back+A"},
  Cliffhanger = {Base = 600, MinAir_s = 1.3, Input = "Fwd+B"},
  Backflip = {Base = 1000, MinAir_s = 1.4, Input = "Back+B@Lip"},
}
TrickHoldBonus = {Per01s = 0.10, Max = 0.50} -- per 0.1 s held at full pose
```

[413] `EventService` starts a server event every 15 minutes from `Config/Races.Events`, announced 2 minutes ahead; completion pays $400 + 15 x L. Rideout (90 km/h-capable vehicles): a 12-18 km road-graph route behind a 70-90 km/h pace marker. Meet-Up at Pier 4 Lot or Hangar 2 ([290]-[291]): 8 minutes on numbered show pads rated 1-5 via `Event_Vote` (top three: $1,500, $1,000, $500), then a Burnout Battle in a painted box: 10 s turns, scoring seconds of rear-wheel slip above 8 m/s ([338]) from relayed `Vehicle_State` RPM and gear ([92]) with ground speed under 3 km/h, the server clamping relayed RPM to the config redline and rejecting RPM that changes faster than crank inertia allows ([88]); exits or crashes score 0; winner $750. King of the Hill: 5 minutes scoring seconds alone in a 40-stud Throttle Park hilltop ring. Convoy: one Highway 9 lap, each rider within 120 studs of the next. Seasons ([363]) add snowcross, Wavecat slaloms, night supercross, wet enduro.

[414] Leaderboards use raw `DataStoreService:GetOrderedDataStore`, never ProfileStore, with stores `LB_<TrackId>` (all time), `LB_<TrackId>_W<YYYYWW>` (ISO week, resetting Monday 00:00 UTC) and `_Sim` variants for Sim Mode runs, keyed by UserId. Times are integer milliseconds read with `GetSortedAsync(true, 100)` for the global top 100; top speed and stunt boards store integer km/h x 100 or points and sort descending. Only server-validated runs write, through `UpdateAsync` keeping the better value; anything under `Floor_ms` is held for review. Pages cache 120 s per server. The friends board covers `Players:GetFriendsAsync` friends: in-server ones from loaded `PersonalBests[TrackId]`, the first 50 offline ones by `GetAsync`, one call per 2 s, cached 10 minutes. In-world boards are `SurfaceGui`s on `LeaderboardBoard`-tagged Parts at each venue, showing the top 10 with display names from `UserService:GetUserInfosByUserIdsAsync`.

[415] Private race lobbies: the host picks a race in the Races app; the server calls `TeleportService:ReserveServer(game.PlaceId)`, stores the settings in a `MemoryStoreService` hash map keyed by the returned private server ID (2-hour TTL; teleport data is never trusted), then sends the host and accepted invitees from this server (30 maximum) with `TeleportService:TeleportAsync` using `TeleportOptions.ReservedServerAccessCode`. A reserved server (`game.PrivateServerId` set, `game.PrivateServerOwnerId` 0) runs only that lobby, may pin weather, pays 25% Cash, writes no global boards and changes no ratings; paid private servers ([461]) get the same tools under the same limits. Spectators get Follow, Trackside (`SpectatorCam` Parts) and Director (cuts to the closest battle) cameras in `CameraController` ([101]-[125]), and the server sets `Player.ReplicationFocus` to the watched `ChassisRoot` so streaming follows. Acceptance test: two bots crossing the line 3 ms apart finish in the correct order on every client.

## SECTION 18 - AI TRAFFIC, PEDESTRIANS, POLICE & WORLD LIFE (Paragraphs 416-430)

[416] You will build this section in Phase 11 ([495]) with a strict split. `TrafficService` (server) publishes facts only: Workspace attributes `SignalEpoch`, `TrafficSeed` and `TrafficDensity` (1.0, or the private-server owner's 0-100% from [461]), plus Street Rules heat, pursuit anchors and fines. `TrafficController` (client) simulates and renders cars, pedestrians, police visuals, NPC riders and wildlife locally, with zero per-car remotes. Traffic is cosmetic-authoritative: no Cash, Rep, job payout, race result or anti-cheat verdict may read a traffic car or pedestrian. Clients derive each car's class, paint and driver factor from `TrafficSeed`, edge index and spawn counter. These additions extend the [23] tree:
```
Server/Services/TrafficService      SignalEpoch, seeds, heat, pursuit anchors
Client/Controllers/TrafficController   Init wires, Start binds PreRender
  TrafficSim      IDM following, lane changes, intersections
  TrafficPool     spawn ring, pooling, distance-rate scheduler
  SignalRender    lamp and PedSignal states
  Pedestrians     sidewalks, crosswalks, plazas
  PoliceVisuals   renders Heat_State pursuers
  NpcRiders       spline playback riders
  WorldLife       wildlife, boats, planes, Port crew
Shared/SignalClock.luau     phase math shared by server and client
Shared/Config/Traffic.luau  classes, densities, budgets, heat rules
Assets/Traffic/             Cars Peds Police Animals Boats Planes
```

[417] `TrafficSim` parses `RoadGraph` ([283]) at most 2 ms per frame once `Graph` reports full `NodeCount` and `EdgeCount` ([282]). Each directed edge yields one polyline per lane, lane i offset (i - 0.5) x 12.5 studs right of the edge polyline, plus an arc-length table; cubic Bezier connectors join stop bars to exit lanes along the [277] curb returns. Cars are anchored kinematic models with typed records {LaneId, S_studs, V_ms, A_ms2, LatOffset_studs, ClassId, Seed}, simulated within 600 studs of the camera. Cars spawn on Interstate, Highway, Arterial and Local edges and drive Ramps; Alley, Garage, Trail and `Closed` ([405]) edges carry none. `Density_perKm` per lane: Interstate 18, Highway 14, Arterial 21 (Harbor City 29), Local 7, times a [326] clock curve (03:00 0.25, 08:00 1.0, 13:00 0.7, 17:30 1.0, 22:00 0.4), `DensityMul`, `TrafficDensity` and the [446] slider, capped by [423].

[418] Longitudinal motion is the Intelligent Driver Model in SI, converted only through `Units`: a = a_max x [1 - (v/v0)^4 - (s*/s)^2], with s* = s0 + v T + v dv / (2 sqrt(a_max b)), s the bumper gap and dv the closing speed. Parameters (a_max m/s^2, b m/s^2, T s, s0 m): sedan 1.6/2.5/1.3/2.0, hatchback 1.8/2.5/1.3/2.0, SUV 1.5/2.5/1.4/2.0, pickup 1.4/2.3/1.4/2.5, bus 0.9/1.8/1.8/3.0, delivery truck 0.8/1.8/1.7/3.0. Desired speed v0 = `SpeedLimit_kmh` (via `Units` to m/s) x a per-car factor of 0.92-1.08, capped on curves at sqrt(2.5 m/s^2 x R), R in meters. Clamp braking at 8 m/s^2; v never goes negative. The leader is the nearest of: car ahead within 120 m, a red stop bar, an occupied crosswalk, or a projected player vehicle ([421]). Acceptance test: a 10-car queue released at green averages 1.8-2.2 s headway.

[419] Lane changes use MOBIL: change when a'_self - a_self + p x (a'_newFollower - a_newFollower + a'_oldFollower - a_oldFollower) exceeds 0.2 m/s^2 with politeness p = 0.3, and only if the new follower's deceleration stays under 4 m/s^2. Indicate 2 s before moving (1.5 Hz), blend laterally over 3.0 s with smoothstep, wait 6 s between changes, and never change within 30 m of a stop bar or inside an intersection. Turns are weighted straight 60%, right 25%, left 15%, chosen 150 m ahead as a mandatory change. Permissive lefts accept 5.0 s oncoming gaps; right on red follows a 1.5 s full stop; all-way stops serve first arrivals; roundabout entries (`Kind = "Roundabout"`) take 4.0 s gaps; on-ramp cars merge within the [281] acceleration lanes. A car blocked for 20 s despawns once unseen.

[420] `Shared/SignalClock.luau` holds the phase math. The server writes `Workspace` attribute `SignalEpoch` = `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` once; replication is the broadcast ([280]). Per intersection, t = (`workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` - `SignalEpoch` + `Offset`) mod `Cycle_s`. The main street owns `MainSplit_pct` of the cycle: phase 2 protected left (8 s; 0 s without `LeftArrow` heads), then phase 1 through; the cross street runs phases 4 and 3. Greens end with amber 1.0 + v/6.1 s (v = limit, m/s) and 2.0 s all-red. Lanes obey heads whose `Approach` matches their heading. `PedSignal` WALK lasts 7 s with the parallel through, then the [280] countdown. From 01:00-05:00, `NightFlash` intersections flash amber (main) and red (cross: stop sign) at 1 Hz. `SignalRender` swaps lens `Material` (`Neon` lit, dark tinted `Glass` unlit, as in [226]) on phase changes within 400 studs, 1 Hz beyond. Acceptance test: two clients' lamps change within 50 ms.

[421] Every 0.1 s `TrafficSim` projects the local vehicle and every other player's vehicle or on-foot character within 150 studs (replicated `ChassisRoot` or `HumanoidRootPart` position and `AssemblyLinearVelocity`) into nearby cars' lane frames. Within half a lane plus 1.5 m laterally and ahead, it becomes an IDM leader at its along-lane speed; crossing a car's path with time-to-conflict under 2.5 s triggers braking up to 8 m/s^2 and one horn (per car per 10 s, within 80 studs). Cars stop for anyone in or within 1.5 m of a crosswalk and never accelerate toward a rider. This is mandatory because remote bikes would otherwise clip through local cars. Only the local vehicle physically hits traffic: crashes follow [236], and the struck car waits 8 s with hazards. Acceptance test: a rider stopped mid-lane for 60 s is never touched.

[422] The fleet is six fictional, unbadged classes in `Assets/Traffic/Cars` (real-average L x W x H, spawn weight): Arden Sedan 4.85 x 1.85 x 1.45 m, 30%; Pip Hatch 4.10 x 1.80 x 1.48 m, 20%; Bastion SUV 4.90 x 1.95 x 1.75 m, 22%; Bramble Pickup 5.90 x 2.03 x 1.95 m, 15%; County Transit Bus 12.2 x 2.59 x 3.20 m, 5%, arterials only, pausing 12 s at shelters; Parcelline Box Truck 7.40 x 2.40 x 3.40 m, 8% (25% in the Port). Each has at most 12 parts: body MeshPart (cars 3,500 triangles, bus and truck 6,000, 512 px atlas), glass, five lens parts (head, tail, brake, two indicators) at the canon light attachments, four wheels spun via `Motor6D.Transform` inside 150 studs, and an invisible Box hull in collision group `TrafficCar`, collidable only with `VehicleHull`, `WheelProbe`, `Ragdoll` and `Characters`; visual parts never collide. Paint: white 25%, black 20%, gray 18%, silver 12%, blue 9%, red 9%, other 7%. Lenses go `Neon` 18:10-06:05 ([328]), brake lenses when a < -0.5 m/s^2; only the nearest cars get `SpotLight`s, inside the [340] remote-headlight budget. ID 0 yields a box placeholder.

[423] `TrafficPool` pre-clones every class over the first 10 s (2 per frame) and parks idle models at `Parent = nil`. Spawn 350-600 studs from the camera (150-600 right after join or teleport, so streets are never empty), outside the horizontal view cone (half-angle atan(tan(FieldOfView/2) x aspect ratio) + 15 degrees) or behind a camera raycast hit; despawn once unseen beyond 650. Update by camera distance on the [480] tier boundaries: under 150 studs every frame, 150-500 at 10 Hz, 500-600 at 2 Hz, bucket-staggered, interpolated each `PreRender` and moved in one `workspace:BulkMoveTo` call. Pedestrians live within 250 studs; police (max 5) bypass budgets. When the 60-frame average exceeds `FrameBudget_ms`, shed the farthest cars and peds first. Tiers Low/Medium/High/Ultra ([340]):
```luau
Budgets = {
	DensityMul = {0.35, 0.6, 0.85, 1.0},
	MaxCars = {10, 20, 32, 40},
	MaxPeds = {12, 24, 40, 50},
	MaxNpcRiders = {3, 6, 9, 12},
	MaxWildlife = {6, 12, 18, 24},
	FullAnim_m = {12, 24, 34, 43}, -- all tracks inside; one loop to 2x; static pose beyond
	FrameBudget_ms = {1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 2.5},
}
```

[424] `Pedestrians` walks a sidewalk graph generated from `RoadGraph` (each carriageway's outer curb plus half the [275] sidewalk width) plus Studio-placed `PedNode` attachments in plazas, linked by a `Links` attribute. Rigs are R15-proportioned Models with an anchored root and `AnimationController` plus `Animator` (no Humanoid), 8 body meshes x 12 outfit palettes, playing Walk, Jog, Idle, PhoneLook, SitBench, ChatPair, TakePhoto, StepAside and Glance via `Animator:LoadAnimation` from `Config/AssetIds.Peds`; a 0 ID joins the [13] placeholder count and the rig glides in a static pose. Animation LOD follows `FullAnim_m` ([423]). Peds walk at 1.1-1.5 m/s, grounded by a downward raycast every 0.5 s, wait for WALK (or a 6 s gap where unsignalized) and cross at 1.3 m/s. Idle slots fill the Old Town fountain, Civic Plaza, the Sunset Beach Boardwalk, Serene Pines Campground and Kickstand Diner: benches, chatting pairs, photographers, food-truck queues.

[425] Pedestrians are never harmed. `TrafficService:Init` registers `TrafficCar`, `Pedestrian`, `Police` and `NpcRider` (guarded by `PhysicsService:IsCollisionGroupRegistered`) and wires them with `CollisionGroupSetCollidable`; the last three collide with no group. Ped parts also set `CanCollide`, `CanQuery` and `CanTouch` false. Every 0.1 s each ped within max(60 studs, 2 s of travel) of a moving vehicle computes time to closest approach; under 1.8 s with a miss distance under 6 studs it plays StepAside and moves 4-7 studs perpendicular to the path onto walkable ground. With no safe spot it back-steps and fades `LocalTransparencyModifier` to 1 over 0.15 s, reappearing 2 s after the vehicle passes; no ped ever ragdolls or falls. Any horn within 40 studs (local horn or the [36] horn flag) triggers Glance. Acceptance test: a Kensei Hayate 1300 at 100 km/h along a crowded Harbor Avenue sidewalk makes zero contacts.

[426] Street Rules is opt-in, default OFF. The Gameplay toggle ([446]) fires `Heat_Toggle` (enum On/Off); the server enforces a 60 s cooldown, refuses Off above heat 0, or On if the private-server owner disabled heat ([461]), and writes `Settings.StreetRules` ([453]) itself. On disables Passive Mode ([249]). `TrafficService` scores opted-in riders' heat 0-100 (20 per star) at 10 Hz from validated [75] samples and pitch; races, events, Throttle Park and properties freeze it. In `CityZone`-tagged boxes, each km/h above edge `SpeedLimit_kmh` + 16 adds 0.15 heat/s; entering an intersection 0.5+ s into red above 8 km/h adds 20 (once per 30 s); a highway wheelie (12+ degrees over grade, 30+ km/h, 1 s) adds 10, then 4/s. Heat decays 2/s after 10 clean unpursued seconds, never saved. Acceptance test: two riders run one red; only the opted-in one gains heat.

[427] Heat 20 summons the fictional Throttle County Sheriff: one SUV, plus a bike at 2 stars, a second SUV at 3, a second bike at 4, a third SUV at 5. Units are green-and-white Bastion SUVs and patrol-trimmed Alpenrad GX-1300s with an invented star badge. `TrafficService` keeps a 60 s, 10 Hz breadcrumb ring per heated player; each unit is a server-side anchor spawning 250 studs back along it. Off-road, SUVs hold at the last breadcrumb within 25 studs of an edge; bikes follow anywhere. Rubber-banding: unit speed = player speed x 1.10 beyond 200 studs, x 1.00 at 80-200, x 0.97 inside 80, never under 40 km/h until 30 studs behind, capped at 200 km/h (SUV) and 230 km/h (bike). Units (group `Police`) collide with nothing and never ram; `PoliceVisuals` raycast-grounds them with siren and 2 Hz lightbar.

[428] `Heat_State` (UnreliableRemoteEvent, 4 Hz while heat > 0, `Net.FireNear` within 600 studs of the heated player) is a fixed 57-byte `buffer`: seq u8, target UserId f64, heat u8, evade u8, unit count u8, then five 9-byte slots {kind u8, x/y/z i16 at 0.5 studs, yaw u8, speed u8 km/h}. Pull-over (under 3 km/h for 3 s within 100 studs of a unit, a crash within 150, or a reset or teleport while pursued): a deputy walks up; the server charges $100 + $8 x heat (max $900) via `EconomyService:Charge(player, math.min(fine, Cash), "StreetRulesFine", ref)` ([365]; skipped at $0), then grants 120 s heat-free. Escape: every 0.5 s `workspace:Raycast` each anchor to `ChassisRoot` (Include: `Workspace.World`, Terrain); while all are blocked or beyond 500 studs, an evade timer (10/15/20 s at 1-2/3-4/5 stars) runs, pausing on sight, then heat drops to 0.

[429] `NpcRiders` plays spline riders: 6 on Throttle Park National, 4 in the supercross stadium, 2 on the pump track, 3 on Ranger Loop and Cedar Run, 2 on Pine Flow; the nearest `MaxNpcRiders` render. DevTools ([45]) `/recordpath <PathId>` samples the developer's `ChassisRoot` CFrame and speed at 10 Hz, printing 100-sample chunks to paste into `Config/NpcPaths/<PathId>.luau` (`Source` is plugin-only). Catmull-Rom positions and slerped rotations replay airtime, whips and berm lean. Path time = (`workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` x pace + phase) mod duration, with seeded pace 0.97-1.03 and phase. Models: Kensei K2-250 and Ryujin RX-450F on MX, Ridgeline Slope DJ on the pump track, Austral EX-300 on enduro, Ridgeline Gravity DH on Pine Flow, with [231]-[250] poses and `VoiceBudget` audio ([267]). Group `NpcRider` collides with nothing; riders fade to 60% within 10 studs of a player and hide during races on their track.

[430] `WorldLife` runs client-side, collision-free, capped by `MaxWildlife`, on the [326] clock. Deer: herds of 3-5 in Pinecrest meadows below Y 1,050 bound away at 12 m/s from vehicles within 60 studs (100 for `GasLoud` engines). Birds: flocks of 8-20 in Old Town and Maple Heights scatter within 25 studs of a vehicle; gulls circle Sunset Beach and the Port quay by day. Lake Serene: a sailboat, fishing boat and pontoon loop splines at 2-5 m/s with wakes, slowing to 0.5 m/s near vehicles (40 studs), and dock at Lakeside Marine at Night. A light aircraft overflies Skyline Airfield at 600-900 studs every 3-5 minutes. Port: 10 hard-hat workers loop scaffold and forklift tasks; a crane moves a container every 90 s. Plaza walla volume = 0.15 + 0.02 x peds within 80 studs (max 0.6) via `AmbienceAudio` ([268]).

## SECTION 19 - UI/UX, HUD, MENUS & SETTINGS (Paragraphs 431-450)

[431] You will build every screen on one token module, `ReplicatedStorage/Shared/UI/Theme.luau` (`--!strict`, deep-frozen, a leaf that requires nothing; this adds `Shared/UI` to the [23] tree). UI code never hard-codes a color, size, font or duration: Find All for `fromHex(` or `fromRGB(` across UI code must hit only `Theme.luau`, which converts each hex once with `Color3.fromHex`. Spacing follows a 4 px scale. Body text keeps at least 4.5:1 contrast against panels, and the [447] colorblind palettes replace the whole `Color` set. Token schema:
```luau
Space  = { xs=4, sm=8, md=12, lg=16, xl=24, xxl=32, xxxl=48 },
Color  = { Surface="#101216", Raised="#1C1F26", Text="#F0F2F5", TextDim="#969CA8",
  Accent="#FF7A1A", Positive="#2ECC71", Warning="#FFC400", Danger="#EB4034", Info="#3DA9FC" },
Alpha  = { panel=0.18, raised=0.10, stroke=0.85, scrim=0.45 },
Motion = { fast=0.12, base=0.18, slow=0.25 },
Font   = { ui="BuilderSans", numerals="RobotoMono" },
```

[432] Typography uses `Font` objects assigned to `FontFace` on `TextLabel`, `TextButton` and `TextBox`, never the legacy `Font` enum property: `Font.new("rbxasset://fonts/families/BuilderSans.json", Enum.FontWeight.Bold)`, with Regular for body, Medium for labels, Bold for headings and ExtraBold for rarely changing hero numbers (level badge, results). Live values (speed, lap time, delta, cash) use `rbxasset://fonts/families/RobotoMono.json` at Bold so digits never jitter sideways. Type scale at the 1080p reference before `UIScale`: Display 56, H1 32, H2 24, Body 18, Label 16, Caption 14, `LineHeight` 1.1. Never use `TextScaled`; use a fixed `TextSize` with `TextWrapped` and `AutomaticSize` so translated text grows ([450]). Rendered floors: 12 px anywhere, 14 px body on Touch, 24 px body in 10-foot mode ([448]); UIKit enforces them as `TextSize = max(token, floor / layerScale)` on every scale change. Acceptance test: Find All for `.Font =` in UI code returns zero hits.

[433] Panels are `Frame`s in `Color.Surface` at `BackgroundTransparency` 0.18 with a 12 px `UICorner` and a 1 px `Text`-colored `UIStroke` at transparency 0.85; list rows use `Raised` at 0.10, scrims black at 0.45, and translucent layers never stack more than two deep. Panel and row background transparencies are multiplied by `GuiService.PreferredTransparency` (Roblox's accessibility setting; 0 makes them opaque). Motion runs only through `TweenService`, easing Out: panel entry 180 ms `Quint` (fade plus 12 px slide), hover and press 120 ms (a child `UIScale` from 1.0 to 0.96), page changes 250 ms, exits 120 ms. Gameplay never waits on a tween. Icons come from one 1024 x 1024 atlas on a 64 px grid, addressed by `ImageRectOffset` and `ImageRectSize`, ID in `Config/AssetIds.UI.IconAtlas` (0 placeholder); at 0 each icon falls back to a 1-3 letter text glyph.

[434] `Shared/UI/UIKit.luau` is the only widget factory; no UI framework is needed. `UIKit.new(className, props, children)` creates the instance, assigns every property, adds children and sets `Parent` last, registering the result in the caller's `Trove` ([31]). Typed builders wrap it: `Panel`, `Label`, `Button`, `IconButton`, `Toggle`, `Slider` (drag, arrow keys or D-pad in 5% steps), `Dropdown`, `Tabs`, `List` (virtualized: visible rows plus 4, pooled), `Modal`, `Toast`, `StatBar` and `Badge`. Labels take a localization key, never literal English ([450]). Every interactive builder sets `Selectable`, enforces a 44 px minimum `AbsoluteSize` (offset 44 / layerScale), wires the gamepad links of [448], clicks through the `UI` `SoundGroup` and exposes `Activated` as a `Signal`. Each screen is a module exporting `mount(parent, props): Trove`, so closing it is one `:Clean()`. Acceptance test: opening and closing the dealership 100 times returns the `PlayerGui` descendant count to baseline.

[435] `UIController:Init()` creates one `ScreenGui` per layer with `ResetOnSpawn = false`, `ScreenInsets = Enum.ScreenInsets.DeviceSafeInsets`, `ZIndexBehavior = Enum.ZIndexBehavior.Sibling` and a fixed `DisplayOrder`: `HUD` 10, `Menus` 20, `Phone` 30, `Modal` 40, `Toasts` 50, `Tutorial` 60, `Loading` 100 ([104] touch controls: 12). Top-anchored blocks also clear `GuiService.TopbarInset`. Each layer root holds one `UIScale`: Scale = clamp(min(V.X / Rx, V.Y / Ry), 0.5, 1.5) for viewport V; reference (1920, 1080) on keyboard and gamepad or (1280, 720) on Touch, times 1.15 in 10-foot mode; `HUD` also multiplies HUD Scale (0.75-1.25). Recompute on `GetPropertyChangedSignal("ViewportSize")` of the current camera (debounced 0.2 s, rebound when `CurrentCamera` changes) and on [106] input switches. Acceptance test: at 1280 x 720, 1920 x 1080, 3840 x 2160, 852 x 393 (phone) and 1180 x 820 (tablet) nothing clips, covers the topbar or leaves the safe area.

[436] `HUDController` anchors blocks 24 px (`Space.xl`) inside the safe area. Speed and tach cluster: bottom-right, `AnchorPoint` (1, 1). Minimap: bottom-left on PC and console, top-left on Touch, clear of the [104] thumb zone. Cash and Rep: top-right (cash counts up over 0.6 s; Rep is a level badge over a 2 px XP bar), left of the 56 px Touch buttons. Notifications: top-center, at most 3 stacked toasts of 4 s, priority-queued (race and police above economy). Race and job info: top-left on PC and console, under the minimap on Touch. On Touch the cluster anchors to the bottom-right corner of the free area left of the right thumb zone, read from `InputController:GetReservedRects()`, so no control is ever covered, even after [105] edits. Acceptance test: a debug overlay of every rect shows zero intersections on all [435] viewports.

[437] The cluster is a 260 px dial. Digital speed: three right-aligned digits plus unit, from `AssemblyLinearVelocity` along the chassis forward axis via `Units.studsPerSecToMph` or `Units.studsPerSecToKmh` (MPH default), with 0.4-unit hysteresis. Analog needle: a 240-degree sweep to top speed rounded up to the next 20, on the [120] critically damped spring, omega 18. Tach: an inner arc to `Redline_rpm` with its red zone; the shift light glows amber at 88% and red at 95% of the full-throttle [93] upshift rpm (0.95 x redline; 2T: powerband top) and flashes at 8 Hz while the [89] limiter is cutting. Gear: N, 1-10 or R; CVTs show D, H, L, N or R; an "A" tag marks auto-shift. Pedal bikes swap the tach for cadence (rpm) and rider power (W); EVs for a kW meter whose regen arc turns `Positive` below zero.

[438] EVs show battery % and range (remaining Wh / [81] rolling 3 km Wh/km), gas vehicles `Fuel_L` / `Tank_L`; both turn `Warning` under 20% and `Danger` under 10%; below 15% the minimap marks the nearest `GasPump` or `ChargingStation`. A warning slot shows [84] heat (amber at 110 °C motor or 75 °C controller; pulsing red "Power limited" while derating) and the [71] slide-overheat, [247] wrench and [329] lights-off warnings. A 32 px row shows ride mode or assist level ([83]), 2WD/4WD, H/L, applicable [113] assists (TC, ABS, wheelie control, steering, auto-clutch, auto-shift, balance %), a Sim Mode badge and the [249] Passive shield. Police stars (1-5, [426]) sit under Cash. The combo meter under the toasts shows trick, points, x1.0-x5.0 multiplier and a 2.5 s drain ring ([410]); in wheelies a pitch bar marks the [66] balance point ±5°.

[439] While the player's `RaceId` attribute is non-empty ([391]), `HUDController/Race` owns the top-left block: position (3/12) from `Race_Standings`, lap (2/3), current lap (m:ss.cc while running) and best lap (m:ss.mmm), a next-checkpoint arrow and the 3-2-1 countdown. Delta to ghost is live: when the ghost loads, precompute each ghost frame's cumulative distance along the track's checkpoint polyline; each update, binary-search it for the rider's projected distance d, interpolate, and show delta = t_rider - t_ghost(d) to 0.01 s (+0.42 `Danger`, -0.31 `Positive`) at 10 Hz; the official [409] split replaces it for 3 s at each gate. Drag trees, rally roadbooks and blue flags render in this block ([391]-[415]). Outside races it shows the active job card: destination, distance, timer and the [350] cargo condition meter. Acceptance test: riding exactly the ghost's line holds the delta within ±0.05 s.

[440] `HUDController:Start()` binds one `RunService.PreRender` connection wrapped in `debug.profilebegin("HUD")` and `debug.profileend()`. Needle and bar springs integrate every frame; text, icons and colors update at 30 Hz from an accumulator (one catch-up maximum). The loop allocates nothing: instances are cached at mount, each widget writes a property only when its value changes, and all text comes from prebuilt string tables (speed 0-400, percent 0-100, position 1-30, gear labels, "00"-"99" timer fields, "0.00"-"9.99" deltas beside a sign glyph), so timers are three labels and only deltas beyond 9.99 s build strings. Bar fills pick one of 101 cached `UDim2.fromScale` values and colors come cached from `Theme`. Acceptance test: the MicroProfiler HUD label stays under 0.15 ms per frame on a mid-range phone, and over 60 s of riding `gcinfo()` grows less than 2 KB/s faster with the HUD visible than hidden.

[441] `MapController`'s 220 px round minimap is a `CanvasGroup` with a full-radius `UICorner` clipping up to 400 pooled `Frame` segments, one per undirected `RoadGraph` edge ([283]) within 1.5 view radii, rebuilt after moving 30% of the radius. Colors: Interstate and Highway amber, Ramp and Arterial white, Local and Alley grey, Trail brown, Garage hidden; lowest classes drop first over budget; the [348] GPS route draws on top. Heading-up rotates the `CanvasGroup` itself, so segments never move on turns; North-up (-Z, [271]) is a toggle. Content moves at 30 Hz to cap redraws. Zoom is one content `UIScale`: 1 px = 3 studs below 30 km/h, easing to 10 at 150 km/h. An unrotated overlay holds the player arrow, players within 600 studs (crew in `Info`), waypoint and job target, pinning off-radius markers to the rim. Budget: 0.3 ms per frame.

[442] `PhoneController` opens a 360 x 760 px phone (Tab, View or the [104] button; M jumps to Map), sliding in over 200 ms (right edge on PC, centered on console, 92% screen height on Touch). Home is a 4 x 4 grid of `PhoneController/Apps/<Name>App` modules on the [434] `mount` contract: Map, Garage, Jobs ([347]), Races ([393]), Shop, Crew, Photos, Settings, Players ([102]), Social ([465]), Radio ([269]), Rewards ([364]), Camera ([123]), Passive ([249]) and the owner-only Server app ([461]); input while open follows [448]. Garage lists owned vehicles with condition, energy and maintenance ([247]), plus Spawn (`Vehicle_Spawn`, enabled near a valid `GaragePoint`), Deliver to me ([361]) and Go Home ([360]). Shop lists only canon passes and Cash bundles via `MarketplaceService:PromptGamePassPurchase` and `PromptProductPurchase`; only server grants count. Photos keeps the session's last 24 `CaptureService` photo-mode captures with Roblox's save-to-gallery prompt.

[443] The Map app draws the world from 16 tiles (4 x 4, 512 px, 7.8 studs/px, `Config/AssetIds.UI.MapTiles`, 0 placeholders) loaded while open; at ID 0, or above 4x zoom, it draws `RoadGraph` vectors. Zoom 1x-16x in 5 steps (wheel, pinch, triggers); pan by drag or left stick. Layers: region labels, tagged POIs (`GasPump`, `ChargingStation`, `Dealership`, `GaragePoint`, `JobPickup`, `Checkpoint`), properties and crew. Selecting a point sets one waypoint and a [348] route. Fast travel links only discovered hubs: one public `GaragePoint` per region ([271]-[320]) with a `FastTravelHub` attribute, discovered within 150 studs per [75] samples, saved in the profile (Old Town preset). New RemoteEvent `Map_FastTravel` is server-validated (under 5 km/h, not in a job, race, test ride or police heat, 180 s cooldown); the server then teleports rider and vehicle with a [75] token, garaging vehicles the hub's `AllowedClass` excludes.

[444] Each `Dealership`-tagged counter's `ProximityPrompt` opens the [385] turntable showroom (auto-rotating 0.25 rad/s until dragged) on a local `workspace.GaragePreview` clone. A left list filters the roster by category, sorted by price, level or PI; locked rows show "Unlocks at L22" yet preview. The right panel shows the in-game name (never inspired-by names), price, class-colored PI badge and five 0-10 bars: Speed, Acceleration, Handling and Braking = 10 x s_v, s_pw, s_h and s_b ([384]); Off-road = 5 x (a + b) (c() clamps 0-1), a = c((mu_L - 0.35) / 0.5), mu_L = 0.70 x mean Loose multiplier ([60]), b = c((Travel_mm - 100) / 200) on mean travel. Compare overlays an owned vehicle's ticks and signed deltas. Test Ride calls `Shop_TestRide` ([359]); Buy opens a modal with price, Cash after purchase and slot use, closed by the `Shop_Buy` reply.

[445] The [385] garage uses three zones on PC and console: a 300 px left tab rail (Performance, Tires, Suspension, Tuning, Paint, Parts, Rider Gear, Builds), the live preview in the center and a 380 px detail panel on the right; on Touch the preview fills the screen and tabs become a bottom sheet at 45% height, swiping up to 85%. LB/RB or Q/E switch tabs. Rows show name, price difference against the installed part ("+$1,450"), Rep lock and a mini stat delta. Selection previews instantly; a cart totals pending changes against current Cash with a running PI line ("B 568 -> A 604") and signed per-stat deltas. Apply sends the [366]-[390] remotes (`Custom_BuyPart`, `Custom_Install`, `Custom_ApplyVisual`, `Tune_Apply`) and waits for the server; Undo keeps 20 steps, Revert All clears the cart, and leaving with unapplied changes asks to confirm.

[446] `SettingsController` owns one Settings screen (pause menu or phone) with tabs Graphics, Audio, Controls, Gameplay, Camera, HUD, Privacy and Accessibility; changes apply live and save via `Settings_Save` ([108]), except Street Rules, which fires `Heat_Toggle` ([426]). Graphics: [340] tiers, traffic density 0-100%, tire marks, post FX. Audio: Master plus one slider per [266] `SoundGroup`, radio, other players' radios ([269]), mute on focus loss. Controls hosts [105]-[110], [124] and [125]. Gameplay: units, presets and Sim Mode ([113]-[115]), Realism ([246]), Street Rules, Auto Lights ([329]), Allow Friend Join ([465]). Privacy (defaults `Nameplates = "Everyone"`, `StreamerMode = false`): `BillboardGui` nameplates for Everyone, Friends and Crew or nobody (display name, Rep level, crew tag, rating tier; 120-stud `MaxDistance`, fading from 80, hidden behind walls), with `Humanoid.DisplayDistanceType` None; Streamer Mode, showing others as "Rider N" with plates blanked and Cash hidden; garage-visit access ([361]).

[447] Camera: base FOV 60-80 degrees, roll ([120]), auto-recenter ([121]) and shake 0-100% ([122]). HUD: Scale 75-125% in 5% steps plus toggles for minimap, cluster, combo meter, race/job block, notifications, nameplates and hints. Accessibility: Default, Deuteranopia, Protanopia and Tritanopia palettes swap `Theme.Color` (deutan and protan: Positive `#3D9BFF`, Danger `#FF8A00`; tritan: Positive `#00B3B3`, Danger `#E0218A`; Accent `#FFFFFF` in all three), and no state relies on color alone: deltas carry signs, warnings carry icons. Reduce Motion (`System` follows `GuiService.ReducedMotionEnabled`) makes UI tweens instant and zeroes camera shake, FOV speed gain, head bob and speed lines. Defaults:
```luau
Settings = { Version=1, Units="MPH", Graphics="Auto", Palette="Default",
  Audio={ Master=100, Engines=100, VehiclesSFX=100, Ambience=100, Music=100, UI=100,
    Radio=true, OtherRadios=true },
  Camera={ BaseFOV=70, Roll=100, Shake=60, AutoRecenter=true },
  HUD={ Scale=100, Hidden={} }, ReduceMotion="System", Vibration="High",
  HoldToggle={ Clutch="Hold", LookBack="Hold" }, Realism="Light", StreetRules=false,
  AutoLights=true, AllowFriendJoin=true, TrafficDensity=100, TireMarks=true, PostFX=true,
  Nameplates="Everyone", StreamerMode=false, Assists={}, Controls={} }
```

[448] Any `Menus`, `Phone` or `Modal` screen pushes the [125] `Menu` context, which sinks riding input and suspends the [111] mouse lock until all close; with a gamepad active it sets `GuiService.SelectedObject` to the primary control, restored on close. UIKit wires `NextSelectionUp`, `NextSelectionDown`, `NextSelectionLeft` and `NextSelectionRight` explicitly in lists, grids, tab bars and modals keeping focus off the HUD; `GuiService.AutoSelectGuiEnabled` stays false ([103]). Selection shows a 2 px `Accent` ring via `SelectionImageObject` and a 1.04 scale. A confirms, B backs out, LB/RB switch tabs, LT/RT page lists, X and Y run hint-bar actions with [106] glyphs. Touch targets stay at least 44 x 44 px, 8 px apart. 10-foot mode ([106]) sets 24 px body text, a 5% title-safe margin and no hover-only affordances. Acceptance test: finish a dealership purchase and a rebind with only a gamepad, then only touch.

[449] The tutorial covers the first ~10 minutes: mount, ride, first job, first purchase (pacing per [344]). `UIController/Tutorial` draws on the `Tutorial` layer: one card under the toasts with device-correct glyphs, a world arrow, a GPS route and, on menu steps only, a cutout scrim. Steps complete from server-observed gameplay, never a Next button: 1 Joined (first spawn), 2 MountedBike, 3 RidingLesson (500 m from [75] samples with one stop from 15+ km/h), 4 FirstJobAccepted, 5 FirstJobDelivered, 6 FirstPurchase (Glide E-Board, Corsa Zip 50 or Gopher 200), 7 FirstRefuelOrCharge, 8 FirstDailyChallenge. The server logs each once per profile, in order, via `AnalyticsService:LogOnboardingFunnelStepEvent(player, step, stepName)`; this list overrides [484]'s summary. Skip (card or Settings, confirmed) sets `Flags.OnboardingSkipped`, logs `AnalyticsService:LogCustomEvent(player, "OnboardingSkipped", step)` and hides cards; steps still log. Acceptance test: a fresh profile logs steps 1-6 in order within 15 wall-clock minutes.

[450] Every user-facing string is a key in one `LocalizationTable` named `GameStrings` under `LocalizationService`, keyed by dot path (`HUD.Unit.MPH`, `Shop.Confirm.Title`) with English source text, also uploaded as CSV to the cloud localization table with automatic translation on. UIKit translates through `LocalizationService:GetTranslatorForPlayerAsync(Players.LocalPlayer)` (in `Start`, pcall with retry) and `Translator:FormatByKey(key, args)` with named arguments ("{cash} earned"), never concatenation, which breaks word order; a missing key falls back to source text with one warning. Text set this way, and filtered player text ([451]-[475]: names, plates, crew tags, tune names), uses `AutoLocalize = false`. No text is ever baked into images or icons. Layouts tolerate 40% expansion through `AutomaticSize`, `UIListLayout` and `TextWrapped`. Numbers and money pass through `Util.formatNumber`, which follows the translator's `LocaleId`. Acceptance test: a DevTools pseudo-locale flag lengthens every string 40% inside brackets, and every screen stays unclipped at 852 x 393.

## SECTION 20 - DATA, MULTIPLAYER, SOCIAL, SAFETY, SECURITY & MONETIZATION (Paragraphs 451-475)

[451] `DataService` owns every player save through ProfileStore by loleris at `ServerScriptService/Server/Packages/ProfileStore`; raw `DataStoreService` is reserved for global data (crews, leaderboards, tune codes, moderation and admin logs). In :Init() create `ProfileStore.New("PlayerData_v1", ProfileTemplate)`, switching to its `.Mock` store in Studio when the `UseMockData` attribute is set. In :Start(), handle `Players:GetPlayers()` and `Players.PlayerAdded`, each in `task.spawn`: call `StartSessionAsync("Player_" .. player.UserId, {Cancel = function() return player.Parent ~= Players end})`, which retries internally. Nil means the player left or DataStore access failed: kick with "Your data could not be loaded, please rejoin". If the player left meanwhile, `EndSession` at once. Otherwise call `profile:AddUserId(player.UserId)` for data-erasure requests, connect `profile.OnSessionEnd` to clear the cache and kick if the player is still present, migrate and reconcile per [455], then fire `ProfileLoaded`. ProfileStore autosaves every 300 s; `Players.PlayerRemoving` calls `profile:EndSession()`, which saves and releases the lock.

[452] `DataService:Start()` binds `game:BindToClose`: set `DataService.IsShuttingDown = true` (receipts defer; ShopService, CustomizationService and JobService refuse new transactions), call `EndSession` on every active profile, then poll with `task.wait(0.25)` until all sessions are inactive or 25 s pass, inside Roblox's 30 s close window; skip the wait on the Mock store. `DataService:RequestSave(player)` is the only caller of `profile:Save()`: used after receipts and vehicle, property or upgrade purchases, it saves at most once per 6 s per profile, merging extra requests into one trailing save, and never runs on a timer of your own. `PlayerService` waits for `ProfileLoaded` before `player:LoadCharacter()`; if loading takes over 30 s it kicks with a retry message rather than allow play on unsaved data. Acceptance test: spend cash, press Shut Down All Servers in Creator Hub, rejoin, and the change is there.

[453] Types live in `ReplicatedStorage/Shared/Types` as `PlayerProfile` and `OwnedVehicle`, extending [29]; a `ProfileTemplate` ModuleScript under DataService holds defaults: current `DataVersion`, `Cash` 2,500, `Level` 1, empty collections and `CrewId` "" (never nil, because Reconcile cannot restore nil). `Settings` holds `Controls` keybinds ([108]), units, assists, audio sliders, `Realism` ([246]), `StreetRules` ([426]) and `AllowFriendJoin` ([465]). The starter Kurb Street BMX is granted once with a fresh `Serial` while `Flags.StarterGranted` is false ([344]), never baked into the template. Top level:
```luau
export type PlayerProfile = {
	DataVersion: number, Cash: number, RepXP: number, Level: number,
	OwnedVehicles: {[string]: OwnedVehicle}, Garage: {[string]: {string}},
	Properties: {[string]: number}, Settings: PlayerSettings, Stats: {[string]: number},
	Achievements: {[string]: number}, Challenges: ChallengeState, Progress: ProgressState,
	Ratings: {[string]: Rating}, PersonalBests: {[string]: number}, RiderGear: GearState,
	CrewId: string, Receipts: {string}, Ledger: {LedgerEntry}, LedgerHead: number,
	Strikes: {number}, Flags: {[string]: boolean},
}
```

[454] Each `OwnedVehicles` entry is keyed by its `Serial` (`HttpService:GenerateGUID(false)`, [358]) and holds `VehicleId`, `Upgrades` (PartId per category), `OwnedParts`, `Visual` options, `Tunes` (up to 5 with raw names of 24 characters, plus `ActiveTune`, [390]), `Paint` (zone to `RRGGBB` hex plus finish name), raw `Plate` and `NumberBoard` text, `Odometer_km`, `DamageState` (component health 0-100, [245]), `Wear` (tread, chain lube, pads, oil hours, air filter, `PackSoH`, [246]-[247]), `Fuel_L` or `Charge_Wh`, `TotalSpent` and `AcquiredAt`. `Garage` maps PropertyId to the ordered Serials in its bays; capacity is computed from owned properties plus Garage+, never stored. Never store Instances, CFrame, Vector3, Color3, EnumItems or functions; use hex and `.Name` strings, string keys and no sparse arrays. Measure `#HttpService:JSONEncode(profile.Data)` on each forced save: a maxed 49-vehicle account stays under 150 KB; warn at 300 KB, alert at 1 MB (25% of the 4 MB cap).

[455] Versioning: `CURRENT_DATA_VERSION` starts at 1, and `Migrations[n]` is a pure function (no yields, no web calls) upgrading a version-n table to n+1, each covered by a DevTools `/selftest` fixture. Steps run in order. (1) A missing `DataVersion` becomes 1, so Reconcile never stamps the current version onto old data. (2) A version above current means a newer build saved it: end the session untouched and kick with "This server is outdated, please rejoin". (3) Migrate a `Util.deepCopy` and write it back key by key only if every step succeeds; otherwise kick and log ERROR, original intact. (4) `profile:Reconcile()`. (5) Reconcile each GUID-keyed `OwnedVehicles` entry, invisible to Reconcile, against `OwnedVehicleTemplate`; `Settings.Controls` migrates its own version here ([108]). (6) Clamp Cash to 0..1,000,000,000; for any `VehicleId` removed from Config, refund 100% of `TotalSpent` (Ledger reason `Refund`) and delete the entry.

[456] Only the owning Service mutates its slice: EconomyService writes `Cash`, `RepXP`, `Level` and `Ledger` ([365]); ShopService and GarageService write `OwnedVehicles` and `Garage`; CustomizationService writes upgrades, paint and tunes; CrewService writes `CrewId`; MonetizationService writes `Receipts`. Others read through `DataService:GetProfile(player)` (nil until loaded) or `DataService:WaitForProfile(player, 30)`. Mutations never yield between check and write, so two requests can never spend the same cash. After `Player_Ready` the client receives a sanitized snapshot (no `Receipts`, `Ledger` or `Strikes`) via the `Data_Snapshot` RemoteEvent, then `Data_Patch` events of {path, value} batched every 0.25 s into a read-only mirror; the client never writes. `Ledger` is the 300-entry audit ring of [365], overwritten at `LedgerHead`, with entries {Id, T, Kind, Delta, Balance, Ref}; receipts use reason `IAP` and admin actions `Admin`, so support can rebuild any balance.

[457] `MonetizationService` assigns `MarketplaceService.ProcessReceipt` exactly once, in :Start(); nothing else may set it. Handler steps: (1) find the player by `receiptInfo.PlayerId` (absent: return `Enum.ProductPurchaseDecision.NotProcessedYet`); if still loading, `DataService:WaitForProfile(player, 30)`; on timeout, `profile:IsActive()` false or `DataService.IsShuttingDown`, return NotProcessedYet. (2) If `receiptInfo.PurchaseId` is already in `Receipts`, skip to step 4. (3) Look up `receiptInfo.ProductId` in `Config/Economy.Monetization.Products` (unknown IDs log ERROR and return NotProcessedYet), call `EconomyService:Grant(player, amount, "IAP", PurchaseId)` inside pcall and, in the same non-yielding block, append the PurchaseId (newest 100 kept) and log `receiptInfo.CurrencySpent`; a failed grant returns NotProcessedYet. (4) Call `DataService:RequestSave(player)` and wait on `profile.OnAfterSave` until `profile.LastSavedData.Receipts` contains the PurchaseId, then return `PurchaseGranted`; if the session ends first, return NotProcessedYet so Roblox retries on the next join. Acceptance test: replaying one receipt three times grants cash once.

[458] Game passes are checked once per join, never per use. `MonetizationService` calls `MarketplaceService:UserOwnsGamePassAsync(player.UserId, passId)` for all five passes through `Util.retryAsync`, stores results in a server-only `OwnedPasses[player]` table read through `MonetizationService:OwnsPass(player, passName)`, and mirrors them to attributes (`Pass_VIP`, `Pass_GaragePlus`, `Pass_ProPaint`, `Pass_Lights`, `Pass_DoubleCash`) for UI only; gameplay never reads the attributes. A failed check counts as not owned and retries after 60 s instead of sticking for the session. `MarketplaceService.PromptGamePassPurchaseFinished` (player, gamePassId, wasPurchased) updates the cache and applies perks immediately when `wasPurchased` is true. The store UI calls `PromptGamePassPurchase` and `PromptProductPurchase`; products are granted only by ProcessReceipt, never by `PromptProductPurchaseFinished`. All IDs live in `Config/Economy.Monetization` as 0 placeholders with comments; a 0 ID hides its store tile and joins the [13] placeholder count.

[459] Canon catalog; revisit the suggested prices after 30 days of analytics. VIP 399 R$: gold chat tag and nameplate frame, 3 exclusive solid paint colors and a decorative rooftop lounge over the Old Town spawn plaza (no gameplay advantage). Garage+ 249 R$: +5 vehicle slots, raising the maximum from 44 to 49 ([360]). Pro Paint Lab 299 R$: chrome, chameleon and satin finishes. Lights Pack 199 R$: LED strips, underglow and light colors. 2x Cash 499 R$: doubles Cash from jobs, races, events and challenges, never resale, refunds, Rep, admin grants or bundles. Cash bundles (developer products): $10,000 for 49 R$, $30,000 for 129 R$, $75,000 for 299 R$, $200,000 for 699 R$ and $500,000 for 1,499 R$, never bypassing Rep unlocks or garage slots. Private servers: 100 R$ per month. Pass finishes add $0 to resale ([359]).

[460] Fairness rules are acceptance criteria. No paid random items, loot boxes, crates, spin wheels, gacha or random reward rolls (the [363] reroll redraws tasks, not rewards); every vehicle, part and tune costs in-game Cash; pass cosmetics change zero physics, PI ignores them, and ratings, matchmaking and boards ignore ownership. Purchase prompts open only from a tapped store tile, never automatically, mid-race, mid-job or under countdown pressure. Try before you buy: anyone may preview Pro Paint Lab finishes and Lights Pack items on the local [385] `GaragePreview` clone beside the pass tile; nothing unowned reaches the server. Premium Payouts need no code; Premium members (`Enum.MembershipType.Premium`, rechecked on `Players.PlayerMembershipChanged`) may get a plate frame, and `MarketplaceService:PromptPremiumPurchase` appears only in the store. Acceptance test: a free account can buy and fully upgrade every vehicle, selling older ones once its 44 slots fill.

[461] Enable private servers in Creator Hub monetization settings at the [459] price. A paid private server has `game.PrivateServerId ~= ""` and `game.PrivateServerOwnerId ~= 0`; a reserved race lobby has an ID with owner 0 and is fully specified in [415]. When `player.UserId == game.PrivateServerOwnerId`, the phone's owner-only Server app ([442]) appears for that player and locks time of day, sets weather, sets traffic density 0-100%, toggles police heat availability and kicks (never bans) guests, all through a `Server_Config` remote validated per [471]; settings live only in server memory. Jobs pay normally; race payouts and leaderboard writes follow [415]. Tag analytics events with `ServerType` (Public, Private, Reserved) so economy dashboards separate them. Acceptance test: a non-owner firing `Server_Config` with a valid payload changes nothing and logs one WARN.

[462] Servers hold 30 players ([21]). In place settings, set Server Fill to customize with 4 slots reserved so friends can follow in from the Roblox friends list. With `Players.CharacterAutoLoads` false, PlayerService gates entry: profile loaded and migrated ([451]), passes cached ([458]), crew tag and pass attributes set, then `player:LoadCharacter()` at the Old Town spawn plaza (0, 15, 0) or the player's chosen home property. Per-client network stays under 50 KB/s with a 20 KB/s average target: one batched `Vehicle_State` relay per recipient per tick ([36]), full rate within 600 studs, 5 Hz from 600 to 1,500 studs and nothing beyond; slow state rides on attributes ([41]). Acceptance test: a Studio local server with 8 clients plus 22 `/stress` dummy riders ([45]) keeps every client's receive rate under 50 KB/s in Studio's network stats (View > Stats > Network).

[463] `CrewService` stores crews as global data in raw DataStore `Crews_v1` (key = CrewId GUID) with record {Name, Tag, Color, LeaderId, Members = {[userIdString] = Role}, MotdRaw, MeetupPointId, CreatedAt, Rev}; the profile holds only `CrewId`, and the crew record wins any disagreement, checked on load. Founding requires Rep L10 and costs $25,000 (an `EconomyService:Charge` sink). Names are 3-24 characters and tags 2-4 characters matching `^[A-Z0-9]+$`; both pass the [467] filter and are rejected if the filtered text differs from the raw text. Tag uniqueness is claimed in `CrewTags_v1` with `UpdateAsync` that aborts when the key exists, and released with `RemoveAsync` on disband or tag change. Every write uses `UpdateAsync` incrementing `Rev`, through `Util.retryAsync`. Caps: 50 members, one rename per 7 days. Each server caches online crews' records and refreshes on a `CrewEvents` message ([466]) or every 120 s.

[464] Roles: Leader (one; disband, transfer, rename), Officer (up to 5; invite, kick Members and Recruits, set meetups, schedule events), Member (invites if the Leader allows) and Recruit (first 48 h; no invites). Invites reach same-server players or online friends elsewhere, expire after 120 s, are capped at 10 per hour per sender and never arrive from a blocked user ([468]). The filtered tag shows on a `BillboardGui` above the nameplate and as a chat prefix set in the client's `TextChatService.OnIncomingMessage`; each crew gets a private `TextChannel` `Crew_<CrewId>`, members added via `TextChannel:AddUserAsync`. Meetups: an Officer pins a `GaragePoint`, Pier 4 Lot or Hangar 2 ([290]-[291]); members get a map pin and phone notice, and those in other servers a Join button ([465]). Crew events reuse the [413] Rideout and Convoy formats; weekly crew Rep feeds ordered DataStore `CrewRep_W<YYYYWW>`.

[465] The phone Players app ([442]) lists online friends from `Player:GetFriendsOnline(200)`, called by PhoneController on the client. Join sends `Social_JoinFriend` with the friend's UserId; the server checks `player:IsFriendsWith(friendId)`, resolves the friend's server with `TeleportService:GetPlayerPlaceInstanceAsync`, requires the same `game.PlaceId` and the friend's `Settings.AllowFriendJoin` (default on, published in the [466] presence map), then teleports with `TeleportOptions.ServerInstanceId`; full servers and `TeleportService.TeleportInitFailed` show a toast, never a hang. Rideout groups: any player forms one of up to 8, picks a map destination and invites players in-server (expiry 60 s, one invite per 10 s); members share the GPS route and see each other on the minimap. Off-server friends get `SocialService:PromptGameInvite` (after `SocialService:CanSendGameInviteAsync`), whose `ExperienceInviteOptions.LaunchData` carries only the group GUID, validated on arrival from `player:GetJoinData()` and resolved to a server through the [466] `Rideouts` map.

[466] `MessagingService` topics: `Announce` (admin and EventService broadcasts), `CrewEvents` (crew updates, invites and meetups, filtered by CrewId on receipt) and `AdminCmd` (cross-server kicks). Payloads are JSON under 1 KB with {MsgId, Kind, SentAt}; receivers drop MsgIds seen in the last 256 messages or older than 60 s; publish and subscribe run through `Util.retryAsync` and carry only filtered text. `MemoryStoreService:GetHashMap` holds short-lived state: `Presence` (UserId to {JobId, AllowFriendJoin}, 120 s expiry, one write per player per 60 s), `Rideouts` (group GUID to JobId and members, 30 min expiry) and the lobby configs of [415]. A cross-server `MemoryStoreService:GetQueue("RaceQueue_<RaceType>", 30)` is optional, enabled only if in-server grids keep starting under 4 riders: the server whose `ReadAsync` claims a batch (hidden from others for 30 s) calls `RemoveAsync` only after the teleport succeeds, so failures requeue. Missing keys are normal, never errors.

[467] Chat runs only on `TextChatService` (set `ChatVersion` in Studio), whose channels filter automatically; never relay chat through custom remotes. Every other player-entered string (plates up to 8 characters, number boards, crew names, tags and MOTDs, tune names, rideout group names) goes through `PlayerService:FilterForBroadcastAsync(author, raw)`: trim, reject invalid UTF-8 and control characters, enforce the field's length cap, then `TextService:FilterStringAsync(raw, author.UserId, Enum.TextFilterContext.PublicChat)` and `GetNonChatStringForBroadcastAsync()` via `Util.retryAsync`. Fail closed: a filter error shows blank text ([224]). Profiles and crew records store raw text only; re-filter with the author's UserId on every load or read, because filter rules change, caching each (author, raw) result in server memory. Only filtered strings replicate to other clients. New submissions are capped at 6 per player per minute. System messages use the client's `RBXSystem` channel `DisplaySystemMessage`.

[468] Keep Roblox's default proximity voice from `VoiceChatService` untouched: never reparent, anchor or hide the character while riding, so voice travels with the bike, and never require voice for any feature. Blocking: on start the client reads `StarterGui:GetCore("GetBlockedUserIds")`, re-reads whenever the `PlayerBlockedEvent` or `PlayerUnblockedEvent` core BindableEvents fire, and sends the list via `Social_SetBlocked` (max 500 numbers); the server stops crew invites, rideout invites, join prompts and meetup pings between blocked pairs, and clients hide blocked players' plates and tags. Because [102] hides the default player list, each Players app card offers Block (`StarterGui:SetCore("PromptBlockPlayer", player)`) and Report, which points to the Report tab of the Roblox menu; never a custom report form. No off-platform links: no URLs, social handles, QR codes or "join our server" text anywhere. No gambling: no wagers, betting, pooled entry fees or casino props.

[469] Complete the Maturity and Compliance questionnaire in Creator Hub before the first public release and again whenever a phase adds content, with answers that keep the label Mild. Violence: non-graphic vehicle crashes that ragdoll the rider, with no blood, gore, injury, death or weapons; police pursuits ([416]-[430]) end in fines, never force. Blood, fear, crude humor and romance: none. Alcohol, tobacco and drugs: none (the boardwalk sells food and soda; no bar props). Gambling and paid random items: none. Strong language: none, all text filtered. Free-form user creation: no (presets, colors and short filtered text only). Social features: declare text chat, eligible voice and crews. In the Phase 12 delivery notes, list each answer beside its justifying paragraph. Acceptance test: a feature that would change any answer is redesigned or cut, never shipped with a higher label.

[470] Clients send intents, never values ([358], [396], [357], [248]); economic handlers lock per player, mutate without yielding and return `Busy` while locked. Riders own their `ChassisRoot` ([50]), so the [75] sampler also reclaims ownership on angular speed above 30 rad/s or a 60 studs/s jump between samples without a `Vehicle_Crash` within 0.5 s. Each client guards its character: if another player's vehicle changes its velocity in one frame by more than twice that vehicle's speed plus 20 studs/s, it restores the last grounded pose, zeroes velocity and reports it to [250]. Unanchored world parts stay server-owned (`SetNetworkOwner(nil)`). AFK: 15 idle minutes move a public-server rider to Passive ([249]) and park the vehicle; "Still riding?" appears at 18, before Roblox's 20-minute idle kick. Acceptance test: `/fuzz` plus an injected 500 studs/s spin changes no Cash, results or other riders' positions.

[471] Per-remote validation extends [35]. After the `Net.T` type, range and rate checks pass, every handler runs domain checks in order: the profile is loaded; the referenced Serial, part, property or crew belongs to the player or their crew role permits the action; the player's state allows it (not in a race, test ride or teleport); and proximity to the relevant tagged object is measured from server-side positions. `Net.Violation(player, name, reason)` feeds AntiCheatService with weights: wrong type or argument count 3 points (a genuine client never sends one), out-of-range or ownership failure 2, rate-limit overflow 1 per burst. Logger writes `[AC] userId remote reason`, capped at 3 lines per player per remote per minute, and the AnalyticsService custom event `AC_RemoteViolation` counts per remote, so a buggy client build shows as a cross-player spike rather than a punishment.

[472] AntiCheatService extends the [75] speed, jump and hover rules with lenient airborne heuristics. Airborne (no ground within 8 studs below by server `workspace:Raycast`), horizontal speed may rise at most 5% and vertical velocity must change at 35 studs/s^2 within 25%, both over 0.5 s windows; freefall of any length is legal; the speed rule pauses 3 s after drops over 20 studs.
